## `/AST/`

### `IResidual` — *interface*

Marks an AST node that represents oracle text the parser recognised but has not yet fully structured — the typed residual arms of the free-text doctrine (ADR 0001): a structured node whose role is to carry "not yet structured" honestly (e.g. `OtherCharacteristic`, `OtherHistoryPredicate`, `UnparsedEffect`).

Implemented purely so the residual-debt metric can find these nodes without a hardcoded type list: a new residual arm opts into the count by implementing this marker. Does NOT cover `UnparsedAbility` — a total ability-level parse failure is already tracked by `ParseMetrics.FailedAbilities`; residual debt is what hides *inside* otherwise-parsed ASTs.

[Source](AST/Residual.cs)

### `IUnparsed` — *interface*

Marks an AST node that represents a genuine parse FAILURE — the parser could not structure this clause or effect at all, and recovered by emitting a node that carries the raw text (e.g. `UnparsedAbility`, `UnparsedEffect`).

Distinct from `IResidual` (a recognised construct whose sub-part structuring is deferred): an `IUnparsed` node is a hole, not a coarse description. Under ADR 0001 its presence ANYWHERE in the tree means the parse is not complete — it must fail triage and is banned from gold fixtures (a gold fixture carrying one would assert the parser's current failure as truth, the test-overfit anti-pattern). Real error-recovery parsers keep the partial tree for diagnosis but propagate a "has errors" signal to the root; this marker is that signal.

[Source](AST/Residual.cs)

### `CardOracle` — *sealed record*

Root node of the parsed AST representing all abilities on a card.

[Source](AST/CardOracle.cs)

### `FreeTextFieldAttribute` — *sealed class* : Attribute

Marks a string-valued property that carries free text inside an otherwise structured node — interior free text under the ADR 0001 doctrine (e.g. `HistoryPredicate.Timeframe`, `SpellAbility.Instructions`, `AbilityAdder.AbilityText`). The residual-debt metric counts non-empty occurrences so this bounded-but-unstructured debt stays visible and trends down rather than quietly accumulating.

[Source](AST/Residual.cs)

### `Parenthetical` — *sealed record*

Represents parenthetical content in oracle text, typically reminder text. Rule 207.2: Reminder text is italicized text in parentheses that summarizes a rule. Parentheticals have no rules meaning but provide clarification.

[Source](AST/Parenthetical.cs)

## `/AST/Abilities/`

### `Ability` — *abstract record*

Base type for all ability nodes in the AST. Abilities represent the functional components of card text.

- **Polymorphic base, discriminator property:** `Kind`

[Source](AST/Abilities/Ability.cs)

### `Condition` — *abstract record*

A predicate over game state — "you control a Forest", "seven or more cards in your graveyard", "enchanted creature is black". Modelled as one discriminated union that <i>composes the existing primitives</i> (`ObjectFilter`, `Comparison`, and — in later arms — `HistoryPredicate`) rather than introducing a new one. The single home for every "if …", "as long as …", and "unless …"-style predicate, replacing the former stringly `Condition { Text }` record and the bare `string Condition` fields.

Encoded as written; the engine evaluates it against game state — a `CountCondition` is "you control a Forest", never a pre-resolved boolean (ADR 0004, reference-not-resolution). Seeded worst-first (ADR 0001): `CountCondition` for the dominant shape and the `OtherCondition` residual for the rest; history, object-state, and compound arms are added as the card families that need them land. See ADR 0007.

- **Polymorphic base, discriminator property:** `ConditionType`

[Source](AST/Abilities/Condition.cs)

### `ActivatedAbility` — *sealed record* : Ability

Represents an activated ability: "[Cost]: [Effect.] [Instructions]" Rule 113.3b, Rule 602

- **Ability discriminator:** `activated`

[Source](AST/Abilities/ActivatedAbility.cs)

### `CardTypeDiversityCondition` — *sealed record* : Condition

"there are N or more card types among cards in [your|a] graveyard" — the Delirium mechanic's activation gate (CR 207.2c: Delirium is an ability word with no special rules meaning; the condition is the engine-evaluated predicate).

Distinct from `CountCondition`: a `CountCondition` counts the NUMBER of objects matching a filter; this condition counts the number of DISTINCT CARD TYPES among a set of objects. A graveyard with seven cards but all Instants satisfies a CountCondition of seven or more but fails this condition's diversity test if the threshold is &gt;= 4.

CR 205.2 lists the card types: artifact, battle, conspiracy, creature, dungeon, enchantment, instant, kindred, land, phenomenon, plane, planeswalker, scheme, sorcery, vanguard. In practice Delirium counts the creature/artifact/enchantment/ instant/sorcery/land/planeswalker subset that appears in a player's graveyard.

<b>Example:</b> Shifting Woodland — "Activate only if there are four or more card types among cards in your graveyard." → Count: { Operator: GreaterThanOrEqual, Value: 4 }, Zone: Graveyard, Owner: You.

[Source](AST/Abilities/CardTypeDiversityCondition.cs)

### `CastThisObjectCondition` — *sealed record* : Condition

"if you cast it" — an intervening-if (CR 603.4) on a self ETB trigger that gates on whether the source object entered the battlefield by being CAST (CR 601), as opposed to being created by a copy effect, reanimated, blinked, or otherwise put onto the battlefield (CR 707.10 — a copy isn't cast). The structured form of The One Ring's "if you cast it" — NOT a free-text `OtherCondition` residual. A marker (no fields): the affirmative "you cast it" is the only form; a copy/reanimate entry fails this gate so the consequent (the protection) does not apply.

[Source](AST/Abilities/Condition.cs)

### `CountCondition` — *sealed record* : Condition

A count of objects matching a filter, compared to a threshold — the dominant condition shape ("you control two or more artifacts", "seven or more cards in your graveyard", "two or fewer other lands"). Composes `ObjectFilter` (which objects — controller, zone, types) and `Comparison` (the threshold).

[Source](AST/Abilities/Condition.cs)

### `DelayedTriggeredAbility` — *sealed record*

A triggered ability created by a <i>resolving effect</i> rather than printed on the card (CR 603.7) — "Sacrifice it at the beginning of the next end step", "Whenever you cast a creature spell this turn, draw a card". Effect-owned: it is reached only through `CreateDelayedTriggerEffect`, never appears in `CardOracle.Abilities`, and is deliberately <b>not</b> an `AbilityKind` (which maps to CR 113.3's printed categories). See ADR 0002/0004.

Reuses `TriggerCondition` so its firing point is the same `event | GameTime` union a printed trigger uses — covering both the "at" (clock) and "when/whenever" (event) delayed forms. An optional `Window` bounds a repeating delayed trigger to a span ("…this turn").

[Source](AST/Abilities/DelayedTriggeredAbility.cs)

### `DiedThisWayCondition` — *sealed record* : Condition

"if [object] dies this way" — a causation-gate condition that is true when the referenced object died <i>as a direct result of this ability's own preceding destroy effect</i> (CR 701.7a: "To destroy a permanent, move it to its owner's graveyard"). Distinct from an arbitrary death-trigger condition (`Dies`): this condition is <i>effect-scoped</i>, not trigger-scoped — it gates the follow-on effect within the same spell/ability resolution, checking whether indestructible (CR 702.12) or a replacement effect prevented the move-to-graveyard that the destroy effect would otherwise cause.

"Its controller creates two tokens that are copies of that creature, except …" (Saw in Half, CLB) is the canonical example: the copy-token effect fires only if the creature was not indestructible and no replacement suppressed the death. MAST describes the condition as written; the engine evaluates it at resolution (ADR 0004 — reference-not-resolution).

`Reference` names the object being checked (typically `It` for "that creature" — the immediately preceding destroy target).

[Source](AST/Abilities/DiedThisWayCondition.cs)

### `KeywordCostPaidCondition` — *sealed record* : Condition

"if its [keyword] cost was paid" — true when the additional/alternative cost a keyword grants was paid as the spell was cast: Kicker/Multikicker ("if it was kicked", CR 702.33d; a multikicker cost is a kicker cost, 702.33c), Evoke ("if its evoke cost was paid", 702.74a), Dash (702.109a), Blitz (702.152a). The consumer half of the keyword production/reference duality (ADR 0003/0004): reference-not-resolution — keyed on the producing keyword's typed `KeywordAbility` identity (a linked ability, e.g. CR 702.33e), NOT a pre-resolved boolean threaded from the cost ability. The matching producer (`KeywordSource` + its `AlternativeCastEffect` / `AdditionalCastCostEffect`) rides on the same card. Count sibling: `KeywordCostPaidCountQuantity`.

[Source](AST/Abilities/Condition.cs)

### `LevelStanza` — *sealed record*

One "LEVEL N-M" or "LEVEL N+" stanza on a Leveler. While the creature has level counters within this range, the listed P/T and abilities apply.

[Source](AST/Abilities/LevelUpAbility.cs)

### `LevelUpAbility` — *sealed record* : Ability

Represents the Level Up superstructure on Leveler creatures (Rule 711, Rise of the Eldrazi). The level-up cost is an activated ability that adds a level counter to the creature; the LEVEL N-M stanzas describe the creature's characteristics (P/T and inner abilities) at each level range.

- **Ability discriminator:** `levelUp`

[Source](AST/Abilities/LevelUpAbility.cs)

### `ModalAbility` — *sealed record* : Ability

Represents a modal ability where the player chooses between options. e.g., "Choose one —", "Choose two —", "Choose one or both —"

- **Ability discriminator:** `modal`

[Source](AST/Abilities/ModalAbility.cs)

### `ModalOption` — *sealed record*

A single option in a modal ability.

[Source](AST/Abilities/ModalAbility.cs)

### `ModeSelection` — *sealed record*

Describes how modes are selected for a modal ability.

[Source](AST/Abilities/ModalAbility.cs)

### `ModeSelectionOverride` — *sealed record*

A conditional override for mode selection.

[Source](AST/Abilities/ModalAbility.cs)

### `ObjectHasSubtypeCondition` — *sealed record* : Condition

"if it's a Unicorn" / "if it's an Elf" — a condition that checks whether a designated game object (the "it" pronoun from the enclosing ability context, typically the creature that entered the battlefield in an ETB trigger) has a specific creature subtype.

CR 205.3m (Creature subtypes): creature subtypes are listed after the type-line dash and are checked at resolution by looking at the object's current characteristic. MAST records the condition as written — reference-not-resolution (ADR 0004): the engine reads the object's type line; MAST does not pre-evaluate it.

The `Subject` field disambiguates the pronoun ("It" for the standard "if it's a [Subtype]" oracle form, the most common case). "It" refers to the same object as the preceding effect's target — in Emiel the Blessed, the creature that just entered the battlefield and received the counter.

CR 205.3m (verbatim): "Creature subtypes are always a single word and are listed after a long dash on the card's type line. ..." CR 603.2: "Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability's trigger event, that ability automatically triggers."

[Source](AST/Abilities/Condition.cs)

### `OtherCondition` — *sealed record* : Condition, IResidual

Typed residual for a condition that does not yet have a structured variant — carries the literal oracle phrase. A deferral, not a destination (ADR 0001): counted by the residual-debt metric, structured when the shape recurs.

[Source](AST/Abilities/Condition.cs)

### `QuantityComparisonCondition` — *sealed record* : Condition

"if X is greater than or equal to [quantity]" — a condition that compares two `Quantity` values. Covers the Thassa's Oracle win-condition shape: "If X is greater than or equal to the number of cards in your library, you win the game." (where X is the devotion-derived look count).

CR 700.5 (devotion); MAST records the comparison as written; the engine resolves both operands against game state (ADR 0004: reference-not-resolution). Neither operand is pre-evaluated.

[Source](AST/Abilities/Condition.cs)

### `SagaAbility` — *sealed record* : Ability

Represents the chapter-triggered structure on a Saga card (Rule 715). Each `SagaChapter` is an implicit triggered ability that fires when the Nth lore counter is added to the saga; the SagaAbility itself is the container that holds them in order.

- **Ability discriminator:** `saga`

[Source](AST/Abilities/SagaAbility.cs)

### `SagaChapter` — *sealed record*

One chapter of a Saga. The chapter is fired when the saga has accumulated the indicated number of lore counters.

[Source](AST/Abilities/SagaAbility.cs)

### `SourceCombatStateCondition` — *sealed record* : Condition

"this [permanent] is attacking" / "this [permanent] is blocking" — a game-state predicate on the <em>source</em> object's current combat role (CR 508 / 509).

Models the "Activate only if this creature is attacking" restriction family (Glint-Horn Buccaneer, Spectral Sailor, Boltbender). The `State` field carries the required combat role; `Attacking` is the dominant form. "This creature" is always the ability's own source (Self); no reference field is needed.

Distinct from `CombatStateCharacteristic` (an `ObjectFilter` predicate that selects <em>other</em> objects in a given combat role): this is a `Condition` on the source object itself, not a filter axis. Reference-not-resolution (ADR 0004): MAST records which state is required; the engine evaluates it against the actual combat stack at activation time. CR 508.1 (Declare Attackers step) / CR 602.5c (activation restrictions).

[Source](AST/Abilities/SourceCombatStateCondition.cs)

### `SpellAbility` — *sealed record* : Ability

Represents the effect text of an instant or sorcery spell. Rule 113.3a: Spell abilities are followed as instructions while resolving.

- **Ability discriminator:** `spell`

[Source](AST/Abilities/SpellAbility.cs)

### `StaticAbility` — *sealed record* : Ability

Represents a static ability: a statement that is simply true. Rule 113.3d, Rule 604

- **Ability discriminator:** `static`

[Source](AST/Abilities/StaticAbility.cs)

### `TriggeredAbility` — *sealed record* : Ability

Represents a triggered ability: "[When/Whenever/At] [trigger condition], [effect]" Rule 113.3c, Rule 603

- **Ability discriminator:** `triggered`

[Source](AST/Abilities/TriggeredAbility.cs)

### `TriggeringAbilityIsManaCondition` — *sealed record* : Condition

"if it isn't a mana ability" / "if it's a mana ability" — an intervening-if (CR 603.4) on an `AbilityActivated` trigger that gates on whether the triggering ability is a mana ability (CR 605.1: a mana ability is an activated/triggered ability that could add mana, doesn't target, and isn't a loyalty ability). The structured form of Rings of Brighthearth's "if it isn't a mana ability" — NOT a free-text `OtherCondition` residual. `IsManaAbility` carries the polarity: `false` = "isn't a mana ability".

[Source](AST/Abilities/Condition.cs)

### `TriggeringObjectCounterCondition` — *sealed record* : Condition

"if it had a +1/+1 counter on it" / "if it had no +1/+1 counters on it" — the triggering object (the dying/affected permanent a leaves-the-battlefield trigger refers to) had, or had not, a counter of the given kind immediately before the event (CR 603.10 — dies-triggers look back in time). The structured form of the recurring Persist / Undying / Basri's-Lieutenant counter-gate, replacing the free-text residual. Reference-not-resolution: the engine reads the dying object's last-known counters, not a pre-evaluated boolean.

[Source](AST/Abilities/Condition.cs)

### `UnparsedAbility` — *sealed record* : Ability, IUnparsed

Represents an ability that could not be fully parsed. Always contains the raw text and diagnostics explaining the failure.

- **Ability discriminator:** `unparsed`

[Source](AST/Abilities/UnparsedAbility.cs)

### `AbilityKind` — *enum*

The category of ability as defined by Comprehensive Rules 113.3.

[Source](AST/Abilities/AbilityKind.cs)

### `ActivationRestriction` — *enum*

Restrictions on when an activated ability can be used.

[Source](AST/Abilities/ActivatedAbility.cs)

### `StaticTimingKind` — *enum*

Replacement-timing qualifier for a static ability — the "when" half of the composite "At &lt;When&gt;, do &lt;Effect&gt;". Kept deliberately small: timing is a separate axis from the effect, so an effect node never bakes its own timing in.

[Source](AST/Abilities/StaticAbility.cs)

### `TriggeredAbilityRestriction` — *enum*

Restrictions on when or how a triggered ability can resolve.

[Source](AST/Abilities/TriggeredAbility.cs)

## `/AST/Costs/`

### `Cost` — *abstract record*

Base type for all costs in Magic. Costs are what must be paid to cast spells or activate abilities.

- **Polymorphic base, discriminator property:** `CostType`

[Source](AST/Costs/Cost.cs)

### `AdditionalCost` — *sealed record*

Represents an additional cost defined in oracle text. "As an additional cost to cast this spell, [cost]"

[Source](AST/Costs/AdditionalCost.cs)

### `AlternativeCost` — *sealed record*

Represents an alternative cost that can be paid instead of the mana cost.

[Source](AST/Costs/AdditionalCost.cs)

### `CompositeCost` — *sealed record* : Cost

Multiple costs combined with commas. e.g., "{2}{B}, {T}, Sacrifice a creature"

- **Cost discriminator:** `composite`

[Source](AST/Costs/Cost.cs)

### `CostReduction` — *sealed record*

Represents a cost reduction effect. e.g., "This spell costs {1} less to cast for each creature you control"

[Source](AST/Costs/AdditionalCost.cs)

### `DiscardCost` — *sealed record* : Cost

"Discard a card" or "Discard X cards".

- **Cost discriminator:** `discard`

[Source](AST/Costs/Cost.cs)

### `ExileCost` — *sealed record* : Cost

"Exile [filter] from [zone]".

- **Cost discriminator:** `exile`

[Source](AST/Costs/Cost.cs)

### `ForageCost` — *sealed record* : Cost

"Forage" keyword action cost (CR 701.61a): "Exile three cards from your graveyard or sacrifice a Food." Printed as a bare keyword on the cost side of an activated ability, e.g. "{2}, Forage: [effect]".

CR 701.61a (verbatim): "To forage means 'Exile three cards from your graveyard or sacrifice a Food.'" MAST records the keyword-action invocation here; the two alternative payment modes (exile-3 or sacrifice-Food) are engine territory — the node names the action, not the execution (per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine). This mirrors how other keyword-action costs are modelled (e.g. Saddle).

- **Cost discriminator:** `forage`

[Source](AST/Costs/Cost.cs)

### `ManaCost` — *sealed record* : Cost

A mana cost like "{2}{G}{G}" or "{X}{R}".

- **Cost discriminator:** `mana`

[Source](AST/Costs/Cost.cs)

### `ManaSymbol` — *sealed record*

Represents a single mana symbol like {W}, {2}, {G/U}, {2/W}, {G/P}.

[Source](AST/Costs/ManaSymbol.cs)

### `PayEnergyCost` — *sealed record* : Cost

"Pay N {E}" — pay N energy counters as an activation cost. Rule 107.14: The energy symbol is {E}; each represents one energy counter. "Pay eight {E}" removes eight energy counters from the activating player. MAST records the verb + count; the player-state bookkeeping (removing the counters) is engine territory.

- **Cost discriminator:** `payEnergy`

[Source](AST/Costs/Cost.cs)

### `PayLifeCost` — *sealed record* : Cost

"Pay N life".

- **Cost discriminator:** `payLife`

[Source](AST/Costs/Cost.cs)

### `PutCountersCost` — *sealed record* : Cost

"Put N [type] counter(s) on [target]" — a cost that places counters as part of activating an ability. Used by cards like Devoted Druid: "Put a -1/-1 counter on this creature: Untap this creature."

CR 122.1: "A counter is a marker placed on an object or player that modifies its characteristics and/or interacts with a rule, ability, or effect." CR 602.1a: "The activation cost is everything before the colon (:)." The placement of the counter is itself the cost; the engine resolves the counter placement at activation time.

- **Cost discriminator:** `putCounters`

[Source](AST/Costs/Cost.cs)

### `RemoveCountersCost` — *sealed record* : Cost

"Remove N counters from [target]".

- **Cost discriminator:** `removeCounters`

[Source](AST/Costs/Cost.cs)

### `ReturnToHandCost` — *sealed record* : Cost

"Return this [permanent] to its owner's hand" activation cost (a self-bounce) — e.g. Grinning Ignus "{R}, Return this creature to its owner's hand: Add {C}{C}{R}". CR 701.x: the permanent is moved to the Hand zone as part of paying the cost, so the ability can't repeat without recasting it.

- **Cost discriminator:** `returnToHand`

[Source](AST/Costs/Cost.cs)

### `SacrificeCost` — *sealed record* : Cost

"Sacrifice a [filter]" or "Sacrifice X [filter]s".

- **Cost discriminator:** `sacrifice`

[Source](AST/Costs/Cost.cs)

### `SelfManaCost` — *sealed record* : Cost

"the card's mana cost" — a self-referential cost that stands for the mana cost of the card on which the ability is printed (or granted to), rather than a specific fixed mana-cost value.

Used by Underworld Breach (THB, CR 702.139): "The escape cost is equal to the card's mana cost plus exile three other cards from your graveyard." Each nonland graveyard card's escape cost has a mana component equal to that card's own printed mana cost — not to the enchantment's cost. MAST records this as a reference; the engine resolves the card's mana cost at cast time (CR 202.1 — "The mana cost of an object represents what a player must spend from their mana pool to cast that card."). No fields are needed: the reference is always "the card's own mana cost" and the card is always the context object.

Serialised as `{ "CostType": "selfMana" }`. Sits alongside other alternative-cost primitives under `Cost`.

- **Cost discriminator:** `selfMana`

[Source](AST/Costs/SelfManaCost.cs)

### `TapCost` — *sealed record* : Cost

The tap symbol {T}.

- **Cost discriminator:** `tap`

[Source](AST/Costs/Cost.cs)

### `TapPermanentsCost` — *sealed record* : Cost

"Tap N untapped [filter]s you control".

- **Cost discriminator:** `tapPermanents`

[Source](AST/Costs/Cost.cs)

### `UnattachNamedCost` — *sealed record* : Cost

"Unattach [card name]" — the activation cost of undetaching a specifically named Equipment from the creature it is currently attached to. Unlike the parameterless `UnattachEffect` (the effect of unattaching), this is a cost paid as part of activating an ability (KHM Toralf's Hammer: "{1}{R}, {T}, Unattach Toralf's Hammer: It deals 3 damage to any target. Return Toralf's Hammer to its owner's hand."). The named Equipment must be detached from the equipped creature before the ability resolves.

Rule 201.4: "If an object's oracle text refers to the object it's applying to by name, that reference applies to any object with that name." The card name `CardName` is retained verbatim from oracle text; the engine resolves which physical permanent is being referred to.

- **Cost discriminator:** `unattachNamed`

[Source](AST/Costs/Cost.cs)

### `UntapCost` — *sealed record* : Cost

The untap symbol {Q}.

- **Cost discriminator:** `untap`

[Source](AST/Costs/Cost.cs)

### `ManaColor` — *enum*

The five colors of Magic. Uses single-letter codes (W/U/B/R/G) for JSON serialization.

[Source](AST/Costs/ManaSymbol.cs)

### `ManaSymbolKind` — *enum*

The kind of mana symbol.

[Source](AST/Costs/ManaSymbol.cs)

## `/AST/Effects/`

### `ContinuousEffect` — *abstract record* : Effect

Base for effects that establish a continuous effect (CR 611) — modify P/T, grant an ability, restrict combat, etc. These persist, so they may carry a `Duration`; one-shot action effects (CR 608) extend `Effect` directly and cannot. The former IDurativeEffect.Duration lives here now, defined once, only where it can be borne (ADR 0005). Abstract, so the polymorphic converter skips it and discovers concrete continuous effects (each with its own [OracleEffect] discriminator) under the Effect base.

[Source](AST/Effects/ContinuousEffect.cs)

### `Duration` — *abstract record*

How long an effect lasts.

- **Polymorphic base, discriminator property:** `DurationType`

[Source](AST/Effects/Duration.cs)

### `Effect` — *abstract record*

Base type for all effects in Magic. Effects are what happens when spells and abilities resolve.

- **Polymorphic base, discriminator property:** `EffectType`

[Source](AST/Effects/Effect.cs)

### `AsLongAsDuration` — *sealed record* : Duration

"as long as [condition]"

- **Duration discriminator:** `asLongAs`

[Source](AST/Effects/Duration.cs)

### `PermanentDuration` — *sealed record* : Duration

Effect is permanent (no duration specified).

- **Duration discriminator:** `permanent`

[Source](AST/Effects/Duration.cs)

### `TokenDefinition` — *sealed record*

Defines a token that can be created.

[Source](AST/Effects/TokenDefinition.cs)

### `UnlessClause` — *sealed record*

Represents an "unless [player] pays [cost]" clause.

[Source](AST/Effects/Effect.cs)

### `UntilLeavesBattlefieldDuration` — *sealed record* : Duration

"until [object] leaves the battlefield"

- **Duration discriminator:** `untilLeavesBattlefield`

[Source](AST/Effects/Duration.cs)

### `UntilTimeDuration` — *sealed record* : Duration

"until [a clock point]" — the single canonical representation of a continuous effect's expiry at a point on the turn timeline (ADR 0002): "until end of turn" → { Part: Turn, Edge: End }, "until end of combat" → { Part: Combat, Edge: End }, "until your next turn" → { Part: Turn, Edge: Beginning, When: Next, Whose: You }. There is no flat per-point variant — every clock-bounded duration is a `GameTime`. (The former "at the beginning of the next …" durations were never durations: they are delayed triggered abilities, CR 603.7, now in `DelayedTriggeredAbility`.)

- **Duration discriminator:** `untilTime`

[Source](AST/Effects/Duration.cs)

## `/AST/Effects/Battle/`

### `SiegeEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Marker for the Siege mechanic (the March-of-the-Machine "Battle — Siege" subtype). Recorded on a `StaticAbility` whose `Reminder` carries the verbatim standalone Siege type-reminder parenthetical ("(As a Siege enters, choose an opponent to protect it. You and others can attack it. When it's defeated, exile it, then cast it transformed.)").

- **Effect discriminator:** `siege`

[Source](AST/Effects/Battle/SiegeEffect.cs)

## `/AST/Effects/CardFlow/`

### `AbundanceRevealEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Abundance's "choose land or nonland and reveal cards from the top of your library until you reveal a card of the chosen kind. Put that card into your hand and put all other cards revealed this way on the bottom of your library in any order."

This is a single atomic action specific to the Abundance draw-replacement family: the controller first declares whether they want a land or nonland card, then reveals cards from the top of their library one at a time until the first card of the chosen kind is found. That card goes to the controller's hand; all other revealed cards go to the bottom of the library in player-chosen order (any order, not a random order).

"In any order" (player-chosen ordering of the remainder) distinguishes this from `RevealUntilEffect` (which always places the remainder on the bottom in a random order, per CR 400.4). The two shapes are distinct oracle templates and must not be collapsed.

The "choose land or nonland" step is baked into this effect's discriminator: the binary land/nonland choice is the only dimension of the filter, and it is made by the controller at resolution — unlike `RevealUntilEffect`, where the filter is a static `ObjectFilter` printed on the card. MAST records the instruction to choose, not the chosen value (descriptive-not-engine, ADR 0004).

The two-sentence oracle text is one coupled action — "a card of the chosen kind" and "all other cards revealed this way" in the second sentence are back-references to the first. This MUST NOT be decomposed into separate effects.

CR 614.11 (draw replacement effects); CR 701.12 (reveal); CR 400.4 (random order), compared with the Abundance template which says "in any order" (player-chosen).

- **Effect discriminator:** `abundanceReveal`

[Source](AST/Effects/CardFlow/AbundanceRevealEffect.cs)

### `AdditionalCastCostEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"You may pay an additional [cost] [any number of times] as you cast this spell" — the shared permission a CR-static additional-cost keyword grants: Kicker (CR 702.33), Multikicker (702.33c), Buyback (702.27), Entwine (702.42), Escalate (702.120), and the repeatable copy/token keywords Squad / Replicate / Conspire (the cost half). (CR numbers verified against rules-structure.json — 702.32 is Fading, 702.121 is Melee.)

These keywords are static abilities per their CR, so the keyword combinator emits a `StaticAbility` carrying this effect with its `KeywordSource` label — NOT a card-level `AdditionalCostsAttribute`, which loses the keyword identity and so cannot express the production/reference duality ADR 0003 requires ("Kicker abilities cost {1} less"). Sibling of `AlternativeCastEffect` (alternative cost) on the additional-cost side.

Riders keyed to whether/how-many-times the cost was paid (Buyback's return-to-hand replacement, Conspire/Replicate's copy, Squad's ETB tokens) are SEPARATE abilities on the card — they reference "if the [keyword] cost was paid", and the topology of two abilities is faithful (ADR 0004). This effect models only the additional cost itself.

- **Effect discriminator:** `additionalCastCost`

[Source](AST/Effects/CardFlow/AdditionalCastCostEffect.cs)

### `AlternativeCastEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"You may cast this card from [zone] by paying [cost] rather than its mana cost" — the shared permission a CR-static alternative-cast keyword grants: Flashback (CR 702.34, from graveyard), Escape (702.139), Aftermath, Jump-start, Retrace; Madness (702.35, from exile); and the cast-from-hand conditional family Surge, Spectacle, Freerunning.

These keywords are static abilities (per their CR), so the keyword combinator emits a `StaticAbility` carrying this effect — not a card-level attribute — which keeps keyword identity on a correctly-categorized ability and is testable through the keyword-expansion harness.

The alternative `Cost` is the shared polymorphic `Cost` primitive, so a keyword that pays by exiling or discarding filtered cards reuses the existing cost vocabulary: Escape's "exile N other cards from your graveyard" is a `CompositeCost` bundling the mana cost with an `ExileCost`{Filter, FromZone, Quantity}; Jump-start's "discard a card" a `DiscardCost`. The card itself is the implicit subject ("this card"); the zone it is cast from is `FromZone`. The post-cast exile (Flashback) and stack/zone bookkeeping are engine territory (ADR 0003/0004 describe-not-execute).

- **Effect discriminator:** `alternativeCast`

[Source](AST/Effects/CardFlow/AlternativeCastEffect.cs)

### `DiscardCardsEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"discard [count] cards"

The discarding player is `Player`; the optional `Filter` narrows which card(s) may be discarded ("a nonland card"). CR 701.9a: "To discard a card, move it from its owner's hand to that player's graveyard."

The targeted reveal-choose-discard family (Thoughtseize / Coercion / Thought Erasure — "Target opponent reveals their hand. You choose a nonland card from it. That player discards that card.") is ONE coupled game action, not three independent sentences: the chosen card from the reveal is the very card discarded. It is modeled here as a single effect with three distinct structured axes —

`Player` — the discarder ("that player", i.e. the targeted opponent); `Chooser` — who selects the card ("you"), distinct from the discarder; `Filter` — which card qualifies ("a nonland card").

The preceding hand reveal is the disclosure that makes the choice possible; it is recorded by `RevealHand` rather than as a separate decomposed effect, so the coupling between "what is revealed" and "what is chosen and discarded" is not lost.

- **Effect discriminator:** `discardCards`

[Source](AST/Effects/CardFlow/DiscardCardsEffect.cs)

### `DrawCardsEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"draw [count] cards"

- **Effect discriminator:** `drawCards`

[Source](AST/Effects/CardFlow/DrawCardsEffect.cs)

### `ExploreEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Explore (Rule 701.44a). "[Permanent] explores. (Reveal the top card of your library. Put that card into your hand if it's a land. Otherwise, put a +1/+1 counter on this creature, then put the card back or put it into your graveyard.)"

MAST records the keyword action's invocation and the exploring subject; the reveal / land-to-hand / +1/+1-counter / graveyard sequence is engine territory per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine (mirroring the SurveilEffect pattern — Surveil similarly records only the keyword and count, not the look/put-into-graveyard machinery).

Filed under `CardFlow` alongside `SurveilEffect`: the keyword's primary descriptive axis is library-reveal followed by put-into- zone, with the +1/+1 counter being a fallback path. The Counter directory hosts effects whose <em>primary</em> shape is counter placement; here that is conditional and secondary.

- **Effect discriminator:** `explore`

[Source](AST/Effects/CardFlow/ExploreEffect.cs)

### `ImpulseEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

"Look at the top N cards of your library. Put one of them into your hand and the rest into [RestDestination]." Models the Impulse/Strategic Planning family: a single atomic action where the controller looks at the top N cards, chooses one to keep in hand, and the remaining cards go to a fixed destination. Rule 701.12 (look).

The two-sentence oracle text is a single game action — "the rest" in the second sentence is a back-reference to the cards revealed by the first. This must not be decomposed into a flat [lookAtCards, …] list; a single ImpulseEffect preserves that semantic coupling.

Variants by RestDestination:

`Graveyard` — Strategic Planning, Rummage `BottomOfLibrary` — Impulse (Visions), Anticipate

- **Effect discriminator:** `impulse`

[Source](AST/Effects/CardFlow/ImpulseEffect.cs)

### `LookAtCardsEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"Look at the top N cards of your library" or "Look at target player's hand"

- **Effect discriminator:** `lookAtCards`

[Source](AST/Effects/CardFlow/LookAtCardsEffect.cs)

### `LookAtTopCardAnyTimeEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"You may look at the top card of your library any time." A continuous static permission that lets the controller inspect the top card of their library whenever they choose, rather than only at times when they could cast or play something. This is a persistent visibility grant, not a one-shot look or a triggered look.

MAST records this as a static ability (the permission persists as long as the source is on the battlefield — Rule 604.2). The "You may" preamble makes the effect permissive (`IsOptional` = `true`); the controller is never forced to look. There are no parameters: the subject is always "You" (the controller), the zone is always the top of the library, and the timing grant is "any time" — all of which are captured by the discriminator alone.

- **Effect discriminator:** `lookAtTopCardAnyTime`

[Source](AST/Effects/CardFlow/LookAtTopCardAnyTimeEffect.cs)

### `MayCastTargetFromGraveyardEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"You may cast [target] from your graveyard [paying its mana cost]." — a one-shot permission granted by an activated ability that allows the controller to cast a targeted card from their graveyard, paying its normal mana cost.

Distinct from `MayPlayFromGraveyardEffect` (a continuous static permission for lands) and `CastWithoutPayingEffect` (which waives the mana cost). This effect is a one-shot permission — it grants the option to cast the targeted card at its printed mana cost from the graveyard.

CR 601.3e: "Some rules and effects state that an alternative set of characteristics or a subset of characteristics are considered to determine if a card or copy of a card is legal to cast…" Here the permission grants a zone exception — graveyard rather than hand — without altering the cost. MAST describes the permission; the engine handles the legality check, zone-change, and stack placement (ADR 0003/0004 describe-not-execute).

- **Effect discriminator:** `mayCastTargetFromGraveyard`

[Source](AST/Effects/CardFlow/MayCastTargetFromGraveyardEffect.cs)

### `MayPlayFromExileEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"[During your turn,] you may [play|cast] cards exiled with [object] [as though they had flash]. [Mana of any type can be spent to cast those spells.]" — a static permission to play cards from exile (Azula, Cunning Usurper).

The permission is the linked second ability of a CR 406.6 pair: it refers to "cards exiled with [object]" via the `Cards` filter's `ExiledWith` reference, NOT by threading a binding from the exile ability (ADR 0004 "reference not resolution"). It is a separate static ability rather than a one-shot bundled inside the exile trigger (ADR 0004 "topology not annotation").

- **Effect discriminator:** `mayPlayFromExile`

[Source](AST/Effects/CardFlow/MayPlayFromExileEffect.cs)

### `MayPlayFromGraveyardEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"You may play [lands/cards] from your graveyard." — a static permission that allows the controller to play cards matching `Cards` from their graveyard (Crucible of Worlds, Ramunap Excavator).

CR 305.1: "A player who has priority may play a land card from their hand during a main phase of their turn when the stack is empty. Playing a land is a special action; it doesn't use the stack." This ability extends that permission so the eligible land cards may come from the graveyard rather than (or in addition to) the hand. MAST describes the permission; when and whether the player actually plays a land is engine territory (ADR 0003/0004 describe-not-execute).

- **Effect discriminator:** `mayPlayFromGraveyard`

[Source](AST/Effects/CardFlow/MayPlayFromGraveyardEffect.cs)

### `MayPlayFromLibraryEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"You may cast [filter] spells from the top of your library." — a static permission that allows the controller to cast matching spells from the top of their library (Mystic Forge: "artifact spells and colorless spells"; Bolas's Citadel: "nonland cards").

This is a continuous static ability (CR 604.2): the permission persists as long as the source permanent is on the battlefield. MAST models what the oracle text says — which cards may be cast and from which zone — not the execution (when the player actually casts, the engine handles priority, timing restrictions, etc.). The `Cards` filter identifies which subset of the library's top card qualifies; Zone=Library is implied by the effect type.

CR 604.2: "Static abilities create continuous effects, some of which are prevention effects or replacement effects. These effects are active as long as the permanent with the ability remains on the battlefield and has the ability, or as long as the object with the ability remains in the appropriate zone …"

- **Effect discriminator:** `mayPlayFromLibrary`

[Source](AST/Effects/CardFlow/MayPlayFromLibraryEffect.cs)

### `MayPlayFromTopOfLibraryEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"You may play lands and cast spells from the top of your library." — a static permission allowing the controller to play cards from the top of their library rather than from hand, optionally with an alternative cost for spells cast this way.

Bolas's Citadel (WAR) grants this permission with an attached alternative cost: "If you cast a spell this way, pay life equal to its mana value rather than pay its mana cost." (CR 118.9b: paying life is an alternative cost; CR 202.3: a spell's mana value is the total cost of its mana symbols.) The `SpellAltCost` field records the alternative cost kind for spells cast this way — null when no alternative cost applies.

MAST describes the permission as written: the controller may play lands and/or cast spells from the top of their library. What "the top" means and when the controller may exercise this permission are engine territory (CR 305.1 for playing lands, CR 601.2 for casting spells). The effect's `Actions` list records which permissions are granted (play lands, cast spells, or both). Describing, not executing (ADR 0003).

- **Effect discriminator:** `mayPlayFromTopOfLibrary`

[Source](AST/Effects/CardFlow/MayPlayFromTopOfLibraryEffect.cs)

### `OracleTopLookEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"Look at the top N cards of your library. Put up to one of them on top of your library and the rest on the bottom of your library in a random order." — the Thassa's Oracle / oracle-look family.

Semantics: the controller looks at N cards from the top of their library, then chooses up to one card to remain on top, and the remaining cards are placed on the bottom of the library in a random (unordered) arrangement. CR 701.12 (look); CR 701.14 (in a random order, the owner randomizes the placement).

This is a single atomic action — the "put up to one on top" clause and the "rest on the bottom in a random order" clause back-reference the same looked-at pile. It must not be decomposed into separate look + move effects; the coupling between the two dispositions (the chosen one to top, the unchosen to random-bottom) is part of the action's meaning.

- **Effect discriminator:** `oracleTopLook`

[Source](AST/Effects/CardFlow/OracleTopLookEffect.cs)

### `PlayWithTopRevealedEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"Play with the top card of your library revealed." — a continuous static effect that requires the controller to keep the top card of their library face-up and visible to all players at all times while the source permanent is on the battlefield.

CR 401.5: "Some effects tell a player to play with the top card of their library revealed … If the top card of the player's library changes while a spell is being cast, the new top card won't be revealed and can't be looked at until the spell becomes cast." CR 401.6: "If an effect causes a player to play with the top card of their library revealed, and that particular card stops being revealed for any length of time before being revealed again, it becomes a new object." CR 701.18c: "Some effects instruct a player to 'play' with a certain aspect of the game changed, such as 'Play with the top card of your library revealed.' 'Play' in this sense means to play the Magic game."

MAST encodes this as a continuous static effect (no `Duration` stated — the effect persists as long as the source permanent is on the battlefield, per CR 604.2). The imperative "Play with …" form is NOT optional: the controller has no choice. No `OptionalEffect` wrapper is applied. There are no parameters; the discriminator alone carries the full meaning (subject = You, zone = top of library, visibility = revealed to all). Covers Oracle of Mul Daya and similar library-transparency effects.

- **Effect discriminator:** `playWithTopRevealed`

[Source](AST/Effects/CardFlow/PlayWithTopRevealedEffect.cs)

### `RevealUntilEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"Reveal cards from the top of your library until you reveal a [filter] card. Put that card into your hand and the rest on the bottom of your library in a random order."

This is a single atomic action (CR 701.12 — look/reveal): the controller reveals cards one at a time from the top of their library until they find the first card matching `Filter`, which then goes to their hand. All other revealed cards are placed on the bottom of the library in a random (shuffled) order.

The two-sentence oracle text is one game action — "that card" in the second sentence is a back-reference to the matching card found in the first. This MUST NOT be decomposed into separate reveal + zone-change effects; the coupling between the found card and the rest is part of the action's meaning (mirroring the coupling in `ImpulseEffect` and `TopLookPutOntoBattlefieldEffect`).

CR 701.12 (look/reveal from library); CR 400.4 (random order on the bottom).

- **Effect discriminator:** `revealUntil`

[Source](AST/Effects/CardFlow/RevealUntilEffect.cs)

### `ScryEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"scry [count]"

- **Effect discriminator:** `scry`

[Source](AST/Effects/CardFlow/ScryEffect.cs)

### `SurveilEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"Surveil N" - look at top N cards, put any number into graveyard, rest on top in any order. Rule 701.42

- **Effect discriminator:** `surveil`

[Source](AST/Effects/CardFlow/SurveilEffect.cs)

### `TopLookPutOntoBattlefieldEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"Look at the top N cards of your library. You may put a [filter] card from among them onto the battlefield. Put the rest on the bottom of your library in a random order." — the Kinnan, Bonder Prodigy activated ability family.

This is a single atomic action: the controller looks at the top N cards, optionally chooses one matching `CardFilter` to put onto the battlefield, and then the remaining cards go to the bottom of the library in a random order. The "from among them" back-references the same looked-at pile; the action must not be decomposed into separate look + zone-change effects.

Structural position: similar to `ImpulseEffect` (look top N, keep one, rest elsewhere) but with distinct semantics:

The kept card goes to the battlefield (not hand). The kept card must match `CardFilter` (e.g. non-Human creature). The choice is optional ("You may"). The rest go to the bottom in a random order (not any order).

CR 701.12 (look); CR 400.7 (putting a card onto the battlefield); CR 400.4 (random order).

- **Effect discriminator:** `topLookPutOntoBattlefield`

[Source](AST/Effects/CardFlow/TopLookPutOntoBattlefieldEffect.cs)

### `WishEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"You may reveal a [filter] card you own from outside the game or choose a face-up [filter] card you own in exile. Put that card into your hand." — the Karn, the Great Creator −2 / Glittering Wish / Mastermind's Acquisition family. The controller retrieves a card matching `CardFilter` from either outside the game (sideboard) or exile and puts it into their hand.

CR 400.11: "Some effects bring cards into a game from outside the game. Those cards remain in the game until the game ends, their owner leaves the game, or a rule or effect removes them from the game, whichever comes first." The two source zones (outside game and exile) are stated explicitly on the card; this node records both as the `Sources` list. MAST records what the oracle text says (reference-not-resolution, ADR 0004) — which sources are available and which filter qualifies the card — not the runtime evaluation.

The "You may" optionality is encoded by an `OptionalEffect` wrapper around this node at the parse level (ADR 0005: clause-level modifiers are composition). The `WishSource` enum records the legal retrieval zones so a consumer can distinguish the broad form (outside game + exile) from a narrower form (outside game only, or exile only).

"Face-up" qualifies the exile-zone source: only face-up exiled cards may be chosen (Karn can't retrieve face-down morphs or other hidden cards). This is captured by `FaceUpOnly` on the exile branch.

- **Effect discriminator:** `wish`

[Source](AST/Effects/CardFlow/WishEffect.cs)

### `ImpulseRestDestination` — *enum*

Where the unchosen cards go in an `ImpulseEffect`.

[Source](AST/Effects/CardFlow/ImpulseEffect.cs)

### `ManaSpendRelaxation` — *enum*

How the mana restriction is relaxed when casting cards under a `MayPlayFromExileEffect`.

[Source](AST/Effects/CardFlow/MayPlayFromExileEffect.cs)

### `PlayFromExileAction` — *enum*

What a `MayPlayFromExileEffect` permits doing with the cards.

[Source](AST/Effects/CardFlow/MayPlayFromExileEffect.cs)

### `PlayFromTopAction` — *enum*

Actions permitted by a `MayPlayFromTopOfLibraryEffect`.

[Source](AST/Effects/CardFlow/MayPlayFromTopOfLibraryEffect.cs)

### `TopOfLibrarySpellAltCost` — *enum*

Alternative cost kinds for spells cast via `MayPlayFromTopOfLibraryEffect`.

[Source](AST/Effects/CardFlow/MayPlayFromTopOfLibraryEffect.cs)

### `WishSource` — *enum*

A legal retrieval zone for a `WishEffect`.

[Source](AST/Effects/CardFlow/WishEffect.cs)

## `/AST/Effects/Combat/`

### `AllMustBlockEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

"All creatures able to block [Target] do so." — Lure-type forcing requirement. Rule 509.1c (block requirements that the defending player must satisfy when possible).

- **Effect discriminator:** `allMustBlock`

[Source](AST/Effects/Combat/AllMustBlockEffect.cs)

### `AssignDamageAsToughnessEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

Combat damage assignment substitution: creatures assign combat damage equal to their toughness rather than their power. Rule 510.1a (verbatim): "Each attacking creature and each blocking creature assigns combat damage equal to its power." This effect records the static replacement that substitutes toughness for power when assigning combat damage — the High Alert / Doran, the Siege Tower family.

- **Effect discriminator:** `assignDamageAsToughness`

[Source](AST/Effects/Combat/AssignDamageAsToughnessEffect.cs)

### `AssignDamageAsUnblockedEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

Combat damage-assignment substitution (attacker-side): oracle text states that a blocked creature may "assign its combat damage as though it weren't blocked." Rule 510.1c (combat-damage step; a blocked creature normally assigns its combat damage to the creatures blocking it — "If exactly one creature is blocking it, it assigns all its combat damage to that creature. If two or more creatures are blocking it, its controller divides that creature's combat damage…"). This effect records the substitution that lets the creature instead assign that damage as though no creature were blocking it (i.e., to the player or planeswalker it is attacking).

- **Effect discriminator:** `assignDamageAsUnblocked`

[Source](AST/Effects/Combat/AssignDamageAsUnblockedEffect.cs)

### `BlockAdditionalEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Combat-block permission (blocker-side): oracle text states that a creature "can block an additional creature each combat" or "can block any number of creatures." Rule 509.1a (the defending player may declare more than one blocker for the same or additional attackers when an effect explicitly grants the extra-blocker permission).

- **Effect discriminator:** `blockAdditional`

[Source](AST/Effects/Combat/BlockAdditionalEffect.cs)

### `CanAttackIgnoringDefenderEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

Permission for creatures to attack as though they did not have the Defender keyword ability. Rule 702.3b (verbatim): "A creature with defender can't attack." This effect records the static override that removes the can't-attack restriction normally imposed by Defender — the High Alert / Assault Formation family.

- **Effect discriminator:** `canAttackIgnoringDefender`

[Source](AST/Effects/Combat/CanAttackIgnoringDefenderEffect.cs)

### `CanBlockOnlyEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Combat-block restriction (blocker-side): oracle text states that a creature "can block only [filter]." Rule 509.1c (declare-blockers step; blocking restrictions constrain the set of legal blocker declarations the defending player can make).

- **Effect discriminator:** `canBlockOnly`

[Source](AST/Effects/Combat/CanBlockOnlyEffect.cs)

### `CantAttackEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

Combat-attack restriction (attacker-side): oracle text states that a creature "can't attack." Rule 508.1c (declare-attackers step; attacking restrictions constrain the set of legal attacker declarations the active player can make).

- **Effect discriminator:** `cantAttack`

[Source](AST/Effects/Combat/CantAttackEffect.cs)

### `CantBeBlockedEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

Combat-block evasion (attacker-side): oracle text states that a creature "can't be blocked" (unconditionally), "can't be blocked by [filter]" (color-/power-restricted), or "can't be blocked by more than one creature" (blocker-count restriction). Rule 509.1b (declare-blockers step; evasion abilities constrain the set of legal blocker declarations the defending player can make against the named object).

- **Effect discriminator:** `cantBeBlocked`

[Source](AST/Effects/Combat/CantBeBlockedEffect.cs)

### `CantBlockEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

Combat-block restriction (blocker-side): oracle text states that a creature "can't block." Rule 509.1c (declare-blockers step; blocking restrictions constrain the set of legal blocker declarations the defending player can make).

- **Effect discriminator:** `cantBlock`

[Source](AST/Effects/Combat/CantBlockEffect.cs)

### `CombatDamageTimingEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Combat damage timing effect: modifies when this creature deals combat damage. Covers: First Strike, Double Strike

- **Effect discriminator:** `combatDamageTiming`

[Source](AST/Effects/Combat/CombatDamageTimingEffect.cs)

### `EnchantRestrictionEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"Enchant [quality]" - defines what an Aura can legally target and attach to. Rule 702.5

- **Effect discriminator:** `enchantRestriction`

[Source](AST/Effects/Combat/EnchantRestrictionEffect.cs)

### `ExertEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"You may exert [this creature] as it attacks." — the optional cost to attack that defines the Exert mechanic (CR 701.43d).

CR 701.43d (verbatim): "'You may exert [this creature] as it attacks' is an optional cost to attack (see rule 508.1g). Some objects with this static ability have a triggered ability that triggers 'when you do' printed in the same paragraph. These abilities are linked. (See rule 607.2h.)"

CR 701.43a: "To exert a permanent, you choose to have it not untap during your next untap step." The untap consequence is engine-side; MAST records the oracle text's mechanic, not the enforcement.

This is a parameterless marker — the subject is always the source creature itself (Self). It sits on a `StaticAbility` whose `KeywordSource` is "Exert" and pairs with a linked `TriggeredAbility` (Event: Exerted) that fires "When you do" — i.e., when the controller pays the optional exert cost.

- **Effect discriminator:** `exert`

[Source](AST/Effects/Combat/ExertEffect.cs)

### `FightEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Fight keyword action (CR 701.14): "Target creature you control fights target creature you don't control." MAST records the two participants as target references and does not model the mutual-damage exchange — that is engine territory per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine. Each creature deals damage equal to its power to the other; MAST describes <em>who fights whom</em>, not the resulting damage events.

- **Effect discriminator:** `fight`

[Source](AST/Effects/Combat/FightEffect.cs)

### `MustAttackEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

Combat-attack restriction: oracle text states that a creature "attacks each combat if able." Rule 508.1d (declared attackers must include any creature with such a requirement when possible).

- **Effect discriminator:** `mustAttack`

[Source](AST/Effects/Combat/MustAttackEffect.cs)

### `MustBeBlockedEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

Combat-block requirement: oracle text states that a creature "must be blocked if able." Rule 509.1c (the defending player's declared blockers must satisfy block requirements when possible).

- **Effect discriminator:** `mustBeBlocked`

[Source](AST/Effects/Combat/MustBeBlockedEffect.cs)

### `MustBlockEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

Combat-block requirement (blocker-side): oracle text states that a creature "blocks each combat if able" or "must block if able." Rule 509.1c (the defending player's declared blockers must satisfy any block requirements that apply to their creatures when possible).

- **Effect discriminator:** `mustBlock`

[Source](AST/Effects/Combat/MustBlockEffect.cs)

### `NoCombatDamageEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Records that a source assigns no combat damage this combat: "[Source] assigns no combat damage this combat."

Rule 510.1: "Each attacking creature and each blocking creature assigns combat damage." This effect records that the named source is explicitly excluded from assigning combat damage for the remainder of the current combat, overriding the default rule. Descriptive only — MAST records what the oracle text says; the zero-damage assignment is engine territory.

Canonical use: Master of Cruelties — after triggering its life-total-setting ability, the clause "This creature assigns no combat damage this combat" prevents the creature from also dealing normal combat damage that turn. This effect accompanies the `SetLifeTotalEffect` in the same triggered ability.

- **Effect discriminator:** `noCombatDamage`

[Source](AST/Effects/Combat/NoCombatDamageEffect.cs)

### `PairEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"pair [this creature] with [another creature]" — the Soulbond pairing action (CR 702.95): Soulbond is two triggered abilities meaning, in part, "you may pair this creature with another unpaired creature you control for as long as both remain creatures on the battlefield under your control."

Records only the pairing action and its partner reference. The shared abilities a paired pair grants are expressed elsewhere over `BothPaired` (the consumer side, already present); maintaining the pairing while control persists is engine territory.

- **Effect discriminator:** `pair`

[Source](AST/Effects/Combat/PairEffect.cs)

### `TargetingCondition` — *sealed record*

A condition for targeting restrictions.

[Source](AST/Effects/Combat/TargetingCondition.cs)

### `TargetingRestrictionEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

A restriction on what can be targeted when casting a spell or activating an ability. e.g., "You can't choose an untapped creature as this spell's target"

- **Effect discriminator:** `targetingRestriction`

[Source](AST/Effects/Combat/TargetingRestrictionEffect.cs)

### `CombatDamageTiming` — *enum*

Combat damage timing options.

[Source](AST/Effects/Combat/CombatDamageTiming.cs)

## `/AST/Effects/Control/`

### `ChangeTargetEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"Change the target of target spell with a single target." Redirects the target of a spell on the stack to a new legal target. Rule 115.7. Covers Divert, Misdirection, and functional equivalents.

- **Effect discriminator:** `changeTarget`

[Source](AST/Effects/Control/ChangeTargetEffect.cs)

### `CounterSpellEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"counter [target spell/ability]"

- **Effect discriminator:** `counterSpell`

[Source](AST/Effects/Control/CounterSpellEffect.cs)

### `DoesntUntapEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

"[Permanent] doesn't untap during your untap step." A specific type of replacement effect that's common enough to warrant its own type.

- **Effect discriminator:** `doesntUntap`

[Source](AST/Effects/Control/DoesntUntapEffect.cs)

### `TapEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"tap [target]"

- **Effect discriminator:** `tap`

[Source](AST/Effects/Control/TapEffect.cs)

### `TapOrUntapEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"tap or untap [target]" — disjunctive tap/untap effect. The controller chooses whether to tap or untap the target. Commonly appears as "You may tap or untap target [filter]." Rule 701.26 (Tap and Untap).

- **Effect discriminator:** `tapOrUntap`

[Source](AST/Effects/Control/TapOrUntapEffect.cs)

### `UntapEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"untap [target]"

- **Effect discriminator:** `untap`

[Source](AST/Effects/Control/UntapEffect.cs)

## `/AST/Effects/Core/`

### `CompositeEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Multiple effects combined.

- **Effect discriminator:** `composite`

[Source](AST/Effects/Core/CompositeEffect.cs)

### `ConditionalEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"If [condition], [effect]." — an in-ability conditional gate where a condition is checked mid-ability-resolution and an effect fires only if it is true.

Distinct from `InterveningIf` (CR 603.4), which gates whether a TRIGGERED ABILITY fires at all: `ConditionalEffect` is an effect-level gate that fires AFTER the preceding effects in the same ability have already resolved, e.g. "If X is greater than or equal to the number of cards in your library, you win the game." after the look on Thassa's Oracle.

MAST describes, does not execute (ADR 0004): the engine evaluates `Condition` and conditionally applies `Then`; MAST simply records that the oracle text has an if-then structure.

- **Effect discriminator:** `conditional`

[Source](AST/Effects/Core/ConditionalEffect.cs)

### `CreateDelayedTriggerEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

An effect that, on resolution, creates a `DelayedTriggeredAbility` (CR 603.7) — the structured home for "…at the beginning of the next end step", "Whenever you cast a creature spell this turn, …", etc. Replaces the former practice of hanging an `AtBeginningOfNext*` duration on a one-shot effect: a delayed trigger is an ability that fires later, not a duration on the effect that set it up (ADR 0002/0004).

- **Effect discriminator:** `createDelayedTrigger`

[Source](AST/Effects/Core/CreateDelayedTriggerEffect.cs)

### `EffectWrap` — *static class*

Conditional clause-modifier wrapping (ADR 0005). Producers that detect a "you may" / "unless [player] pays" clause wrap their effect through these so a wrapper is emitted only when the clause is actually present — keeping the modifier off the effect node itself.

[Source](AST/Effects/Core/EffectWrap.cs)

### `ModalEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

An effect that wraps a modal choice inside another ability's resolution. Used when oracle text inserts a "choose one —" preamble inside a triggered or activated ability's effect stream, e.g.: <code> When [self] dies, choose one — • [option A] • [option B] </code> The ability's `Effects` list carries a single `ModalEffect` whose `Modes` mirror the spell-level `ModalAbility` shape: each option is its own `Ability` (typically a `SpellAbility`) wrapped in a `ModalOption`. Rule 700.2b — modal triggered abilities. The controller chooses the mode(s) as part of putting that ability on the stack. (See also Rule 700.2 for modal mechanics generally.)

- **Effect discriminator:** `modal`

[Source](AST/Effects/Core/ModalEffect.cs)

### `OptionalEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"You may [Inner]. If you do, [IfYouDo] / if you don't, [IfYouDoNot]." (CR 117.7). Wrapper presence is the "you may" — no bool. One-shot action effects only (ADR 0005).

- **Effect discriminator:** `optional`

[Source](AST/Effects/Core/OptionalEffect.cs)

### `PreventableEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"[Inner] unless [player] pays [cost]." (Mana Leak, Rhystic Study). A distinct clause shape from `OptionalEffect`; nests with it in the rare co-occurrence. One-shot action effects only (ADR 0005).

- **Effect discriminator:** `preventable`

[Source](AST/Effects/Core/PreventableEffect.cs)

### `RepeatEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"Repeat this process [N] more times." — a loop-repeat instruction that names a previously stated base process (`Inner`) and the number of <b>additional</b> repetitions (`AdditionalTimes`). The total execution count is `AdditionalTimes + 1` (the initial occurrence plus the stated extra iterations).

Example: Professor Onyx's −8: "Each opponent may discard a card. If they don't, they lose 3 life. Repeat this process six more times." → `AdditionalTimes` = 6; total iterations = 7.

MAST describes, does not execute (ADR 0004): the engine evaluates `Inner` the stated total number of times; MAST records that oracle text requests a fixed-count repetition of the given process.

CR 701 (game actions); CR 608.2 (effects resolve once on resolution, unless the text explicitly directs a repetition — as here). Cluster axis: any card with "repeat this process N more times" maps to this node keyed on the count, making the repeat count queryable without re-parsing raw text.

- **Effect discriminator:** `repeat`

[Source](AST/Effects/Core/RepeatEffect.cs)

### `UnparsedEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect, IUnparsed

An effect that couldn't be parsed.

- **Effect discriminator:** `unparsed`

[Source](AST/Effects/Core/UnparsedEffect.cs)

### `WinTheGameEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"You win the game." — an absolute game-ending effect where the controller immediately wins (CR 104.3a: "A player can win the game by having the highest life total when the game ends, by having their opponent lose the game, or by a card effect that says 'you win the game'.").

MAST models this descriptively — it records that the oracle text says "you win the game", without encoding any turn-state or priority machinery. Distinct from opponent-loss effects (which say "an opponent loses the game").

- **Effect discriminator:** `winTheGame`

[Source](AST/Effects/Core/WinTheGameEffect.cs)

## `/AST/Effects/Counter/`

### `BlightEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Blight keyword action (CR 701.68). "To blight N means to put N -1/-1 counters on a creature you control."

CR 701.68a (verbatim): "To 'blight N' means to put N -1/-1 counters on a creature you control." CR 701.68b: "If a player is given the choice to blight but is unable to put N -1/-1 counters on a creature they control (usually because they control no creatures), they can't choose to blight."

MAST records who blights and how many, matching the oracle phrase "[player(s)] blight(s) N". The choice of which creature receives the counters — and whether the player is able to blight at all (CR 701.68b) — is engine territory.

- **Effect discriminator:** `blight`

[Source](AST/Effects/Counter/BlightEffect.cs)

### `MoveCountersEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"move [count] [counter type] counter(s) from [source] onto [target]" — Graft (CR 702.58): "Whenever another creature enters, if this permanent has a +1/+1 counter on it, you may move a +1/+1 counter from this permanent onto that creature."

A distinct cluster axis from `putCounters` / `removeCounters`: a move relocates an existing counter, conserving the total, rather than creating or destroying one. Modeled with the shared `ObjectReference` / `Quantity` primitives (ADR 0003).

- **Effect discriminator:** `moveCounters`

[Source](AST/Effects/Counter/MoveCountersEffect.cs)

### `ProliferateEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Proliferate (Rule 701.27). "Choose any number of permanents and/or players, then give each another counter of each kind already there."

Proliferate is a parameterless keyword action: it has no subject, target list, or quantity beyond its own definition. MAST records the invocation descriptively; which permanents and players are chosen is a game-state decision handled at the table.

- **Effect discriminator:** `proliferate`

[Source](AST/Effects/Counter/ProliferateEffect.cs)

### `PutCountersEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"put [count] [counter type] counters on [target]"

- **Effect discriminator:** `putCounters`

[Source](AST/Effects/Counter/PutCountersEffect.cs)

### `RemoveCountersEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"remove [count] [counter type] counters from [target]"

- **Effect discriminator:** `removeCounters`

[Source](AST/Effects/Counter/RemoveCountersEffect.cs)

## `/AST/Effects/Damage/`

### `DealDamageEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"deals N damage to [target]"

- **Effect discriminator:** `dealDamage`

[Source](AST/Effects/Damage/DealDamageEffect.cs)

### `LifelinkEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Lifelink effect: damage dealt by this source causes its controller to gain that much life. Applies to all damage (not just combat damage) dealt from any zone. The source's controller (or owner if no controller) gains the life. Rule 702.15

- **Effect discriminator:** `lifelink`

[Source](AST/Effects/Damage/LifelinkEffect.cs)

### `PreventDamageEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

"prevent [damage]"

- **Effect discriminator:** `preventDamage`

[Source](AST/Effects/Damage/PreventDamageEffect.cs)

## `/AST/Effects/Dice/`

### `RollDieEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"roll a dN" — instructs a player to roll an N-sided die (CR 706.1).

CR 706.1: "An effect that instructs a player to roll a die will specify what kind of die to roll and how many of those dice to roll." CR 706.1a: "Such an effect may refer to … one or more 'dN,' where N is a positive integer. … a d20 is a twenty-sided die with possible outcomes from 1 to 20."

CR 706.4: "Some abilities that instruct a player to roll one or more dice do not include a results table. The text of those abilities will indicate how to use the results of the die rolls, if at all." Ancient Copper Dragon rolls a d20 without a results table: the result is used directly by the following CreateTokenEffect via `DieRollResultQuantity`.

The `Sides` field captures N from "dN" (e.g. 20 for a d20). The number of dice rolled is 1 (the common case); multi-die variants are out of scope for this card and would extend this node.

- **Effect discriminator:** `rollDie`

[Source](AST/Effects/Dice/RollDieEffect.cs)

## `/AST/Effects/Format/`

### `AnyNumberInDeckEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"A deck can have any number of cards named [this]." — the self-referential exception to the four-of deck-construction rule (Relentless Rats, Rat Colony, Shadowborn Apostle, Persistent Petitioners, Dragon's Approach).

CR 100.2a: "In constructed play …, each deck has a minimum deck size of 60 cards. A constructed deck may contain any number of basic land cards and no more than four of any card with a particular English name other than basic land cards." This line lifts that four-of limit for cards sharing this card's name.

Field-less: the "named [this]" self-reference is implicit (it always names the card the line appears on, mirroring how self-referential continuous abilities elide their subject). MAST records only that the exception exists; the actual deck-building enforcement and the 60-card minimum are engine/format territory.

- **Effect discriminator:** `anyNumberInDeck`

[Source](AST/Effects/Format/AnyNumberInDeckEffect.cs)

## `/AST/Effects/Keyword/`

### `AdaptEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Adapt N (CR 701.46a: "\"Adapt N\" means \"If this permanent has no +1/+1 counters on it, put N +1/+1 counters on it.\"")

MAST records the keyword-action and its integer value N as a descriptive node. The conditional check (no +1/+1 counters present) and counter placement are engine territory — the node names the action, not the execution.

Integer-parameterized keyword-action; mirrors the `ScryEffect` shape with a `Count` quantity field. Distinct from `MonstrosityEffect` — Adapt is an activated keyword-action, Monstrosity is a different mechanic.

- **Effect discriminator:** `adapt`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/AdaptEffect.cs)

### `AmassEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"Amass [Subtype] N" keyword action (CR 701.47a).

CR 701.47a: "Amass [subtype] N means 'Either put N +1/+1 counters on an Army you control, or create a 0/0 black [Subtype] Army creature token, then put N +1/+1 counters on it.'"

MAST records the keyword-action with its subtype (e.g., "Orcs") and integer value N. The token creation, counter placement, and army selection are engine territory — the node names the action, not the execution.

Modern Amass printings (War of the Spark and later) specify the army subtype explicitly (e.g., "amass Orcs 1"). The `ArmySubtype` field carries that subtype; it is null for legacy Amass printings that do not specify a subtype (those are untyped Army tokens).

- **Effect discriminator:** `amass`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/AmassEffect.cs)

### `AmplifyEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Amplify N (Rule 702.37). "As this creature enters, put a +1/+1 counter on it for each [creature type] card you reveal in your hand." An enters-the-battlefield keyword from Legions. MAST records the keyword's presence and its integer value N; the creature-type-reveal and counter-placement semantics are engine territory (per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine).

The creature type associated with the amplify trigger is defined by the card's type line and is not encoded as a field — MAST captures the N multiplier only, mirroring how `BushidoEffect` omits flavour-coupling details that the rules engine derives from context.

Integer-parameterized keyword; mirrors the `BushidoEffect` shape.

- **Effect discriminator:** `amplify`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/AmplifyEffect.cs)

### `AwakenEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Awaken (Rule 702.113). Printed as "Awaken N—[cost]" on an instant or sorcery. It establishes an alternative cost: if the spell is cast for its awaken cost, the spell does its normal thing and additionally puts N +1/+1 counters on a target land you control and turns that land into a 0/0 Elemental creature that's still a land. MAST records the keyword's presence and the N + cost parameters; the counters-on-land and land-becomes-creature semantics are conventionally inferred from the rules (and echoed in reminder text).

- **Effect discriminator:** `awaken`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/AwakenEffect.cs)

### `BackupEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Backup (Rule 702.165). A triggered keyword ability printed as "Backup N". When this creature enters, put N +1/+1 counters on target creature. If that is another creature, it also gains the non-backup abilities of this creature printed below the backup ability until end of turn. MAST records the keyword and its integer value; the counter placement, ability-grant, and "printed below this one" scoping are engine territory per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine.

Integer-parameterized keyword; mirrors the BushidoEffect shape — `Value` is the backup number lifted from the printed oracle text.

- **Effect discriminator:** `backup`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/BackupEffect.cs)

### `CantBeCounteredEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"This spell can't be countered." Rule 702.64 (for Uncounterable as a keyword)

- **Effect discriminator:** `cantBeCountered`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/CantBeCounteredEffect.cs)

### `ChampionEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Champion (Rule 702.71). A keyword ability that appears as "Champion a [type]" on a creature card. When this creature enters the battlefield, its controller must sacrifice it unless they exile another creature of the named type they control. When this creature leaves the battlefield, the exiled card returns. The `CreatureType` field records the type restriction from the oracle line (e.g., "creature", "Elemental", "Goblin"). "creature" indicates the general form with no subtype restriction. MAST records the keyword's presence and type parameter; the sacrifice-unless and return mechanics are engine territory, not described by the oracle line itself.

- **Effect discriminator:** `champion`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/ChampionEffect.cs)

### `ChooseCardTypeEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"Choose artifact, creature, enchantment, instant, or sorcery." — the card-type-choice declaration. The oracle line records that the controller selects a card type; subsequent abilities that reference "the chosen type" (via `CardType`) are downstream consumers of this choice (CR 607 linked abilities). MAST models only the choice declaration itself, not the producer/consumer link.

Timing is a separate axis: when this choice happens as the permanent enters ("As this artifact enters, choose artifact, creature, enchantment, instant, or sorcery." — Cloud Key, the CR 614.1c shape), the enclosing `StaticAbility` carries `AsThisEnters`; the effect itself stays plain. Timing and effect are composable, never baked into the effect discriminator.

Distinct from `ChooseCreatureTypeEffect` (creature-type choice) and `ChooseColorEffect` (color choice): each carries a different descriptive shape in oracle text and a different downstream-reference vocabulary. This node records a card-type-selection decision (the card types being artifact, creature, enchantment, instant, or sorcery — the permanent card types that can also be spell types on the stack).

- **Effect discriminator:** `chooseCardType`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/ChooseCardTypeEffect.cs)

### `ChooseColorEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"Choose a color." — the color-choice declaration. The oracle line records that the controller makes a color selection; subsequent abilities that reference "the chosen color" are downstream consumers of this choice. MAST models only the choice declaration itself, not the producer/consumer link.

Timing is a separate axis: when this choice happens as the permanent enters ("As this land enters, choose a color." — CR 614.1c), the enclosing `StaticAbility` carries `AsThisEnters`; the effect itself stays plain. The same effect appears in non-entry contexts too (e.g. a spell that grants "protection from the color of your choice"), where no entry timing is present — another reason the timing belongs on the ability, not the effect discriminator.

Design rationale: This is a separate effect type because the surface verb in oracle text is "choose a color" — an explicit player-decision instruction, not a board-state check or cost payment. Keeping the choice declaration as its own node is descriptively faithful to what the oracle line says (per the MAST descriptive-not-engine doctrine) and leaves the consumer connection (how "the chosen color" references resolve) to the rules engine.

Distinct from `PayLifeEffect` (Shockland pattern): that effect records a cost-payment decision; this effect records a color-selection decision.

- **Effect discriminator:** `chooseColor`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/ChooseColorEffect.cs)

### `ChooseCreatureTypeEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"Choose a creature type." — the creature-type-choice declaration. The oracle line records that the controller selects a creature type; subsequent abilities that reference "the chosen type" are downstream consumers of this choice. MAST models only the choice declaration itself, not the producer/consumer link.

Timing is a separate axis: when this choice happens as the permanent enters ("As this land enters, choose a creature type." — the Unclaimed Territory shape, CR 614.1c), the enclosing `StaticAbility` carries `AsThisEnters`; the effect itself stays plain. Timing and effect are composable, never baked into the effect discriminator.

Design rationale: This is a separate effect type rather than a variant of `ChooseColorEffect` because the surface noun chosen differs — a creature type versus a color — and the two carry distinct downstream-reference vocabularies ("the chosen type" versus "the chosen color"). Both are explicit player-decision instructions, but collapsing them into one node would erase the descriptive distinction the oracle text draws (per the MAST descriptive-not-engine doctrine).

Distinct from `ChooseColorEffect` (color choice) and from `PayLifeEffect` (cost-payment decision, Shockland pattern): each carries a different descriptive shape in oracle text. This node records a creature-type-selection decision.

- **Effect discriminator:** `chooseCreatureType`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/ChooseCreatureTypeEffect.cs)

### `CrewEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Crew effect: this Vehicle becomes an artifact creature until end of turn if a total power of N or more of other creatures is tapped to crew it. "Crew N" — Rule 702.122.
