The oracle keyword reads "Crew N" and expands to an activated ability whose cost is tapping any number of other untapped creatures with total power N or more. MAST records only the keyword's presence and its parameter; the cost-and-resolution machinery is engine territory, not described by the oracle line itself.

- **Effect discriminator:** `crew`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/CrewEffect.cs)

### `CumulativeUpkeepEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Cumulative upkeep (Rule 702.24). "At the beginning of your upkeep, put an age counter on this permanent, then sacrifice it unless you pay its upkeep cost for each age counter on it." MAST records the keyword's presence and the cumulative upkeep cost; the upkeep-trigger, age-counter-scaling, and sacrifice-unless-pay semantics are conventionally inferred from the rules (per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine), mirroring the EchoEffect, BestowEffect, and KickerEffect patterns.

`Cost` is the polymorphic base type for parity with the other cost-bearing keyword effects (Echo, Kicker, Bestow, Unearth, Plot). All known printings of cumulative upkeep use a `ManaCost`, but the polymorphic base accommodates future non-mana variants.

- **Effect discriminator:** `cumulativeUpkeep`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/CumulativeUpkeepEffect.cs)

### `DayNightEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Daybound/Nightbound (Rule 702.145). Keyword abilities found on the front and back faces, respectively, of day/night double-faced cards. Daybound is on the front face; nightbound is on the back face. MAST records the keyword's presence and which phase it belongs to; the day/night transformation rules (Rule 731), state-based checks, and transformation triggers are engine territory per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine.

- **Effect discriminator:** `dayNight`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/DayNightEffect.cs)

### `DevourEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Devour (Rule 702.82). A static ability printed as "Devour N" on creature cards. As this creature enters, you may sacrifice any number of creatures; this creature enters with N +1/+1 counters on it for each creature sacrificed this way. MAST records the keyword and its integer devour value; the sacrifice-on-entry, counter-placement, and optional semantics are engine territory.

Integer-parameterized keyword effect; follows the `BushidoEffect` shape.

- **Effect discriminator:** `devour`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/DevourEffect.cs)

### `DisguiseEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Disguise (Rule 702.168). An alternative casting mode that puts the card onto the battlefield face down as a 2/2 creature with ward {2}. MAST records the keyword's presence and the disguise cost; the face-down/ward and turn-face-up semantics are conventionally described in the Reminder parenthetical.

- **Effect discriminator:** `disguise`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/DisguiseEffect.cs)

### `DredgeEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Dredge N (Rule 702.52). A keyword ability that lets a player return a card from their graveyard to their hand. Oracle form: "Dredge N (If you would draw a card, you may mill N cards instead. If you do, return this card from your graveyard to your hand.)".

MAST records the keyword's presence and its integer value (the number of cards milled to return this card); the draw-replacement choice and mill-and-return mechanics are engine territory.

Integer-parameterized keyword; mirrors `BushidoEffect` and the Modular/Backup/Afflict family. `Value` is the dredge number lifted from the printed oracle text.

- **Effect discriminator:** `dredge`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/DredgeEffect.cs)

### `EmergeEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Emerge (Rule 702.119). An alternative cost: sacrifice a creature and pay the emerge cost reduced by that creature's mana value. MAST records the keyword's presence and the printed emerge cost; the sacrifice-a-creature, cost-reduction, and timing semantics are conventionally inferred from the rules.

`Cost` is the polymorphic base type so future variants can plug in without a schema change.

- **Effect discriminator:** `emerge`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/EmergeEffect.cs)

### `EncoreEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Encore (Rule 702.142). An activated ability functioning only from the graveyard: "[Cost], Exile this card from your graveyard: For each opponent, create a token copy of this card that attacks that opponent this turn if able. Those tokens gain haste. Sacrifice those tokens at the beginning of the next end step."

MAST records only the keyword and its associated mana cost. The token-copy-per-opponent structure is conventional from the rules and is carried in reminder text.

- **Effect discriminator:** `encore`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/EncoreEffect.cs)

### `EscapeEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Escape (Rule 702.138). A keyword on instants and sorceries: "Escape—[cost], Exile N other cards from your graveyard." lets the player cast the card from their graveyard for an alternative cost. That escape cost has two parts — a mana cost and the additional cost of exiling a fixed number of other cards from the controller's graveyard. MAST records both components of the printed escape cost; the cast-from-graveyard permission itself is conventionally inferred from the rules and captured as the `Reminder` parenthetical.

`CardsToExile` is the count of cards in the graveyard-exile half of the escape cost — the additional cost, not a separate game action. MAST describes the cost; it does not model the exile as an `ExileEffect` subtree.

- **Effect discriminator:** `escape`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/EscapeEffect.cs)

### `EvasionCondition` — *sealed record*

Condition for evasion (used by landwalk and similar effects).

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/EvasionCondition.cs)

### `EvasionEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Evasion effect: restricts what can block this creature. Covers: Flying, Menace, Shadow, Horsemanship, Fear, Intimidate, Skulk, Landwalk, etc. "This creature can't be blocked except by [filter]" "This creature can't be blocked as long as [condition]"

- **Effect discriminator:** `evasion`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/EvasionEffect.cs)

### `FabricateEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Fabricate (Rule 702.118). A triggered keyword ability printed as "Fabricate N". When this creature enters, put N +1/+1 counters on it or create N 1/1 colorless Servo artifact creature tokens. MAST records the keyword and its integer value; the enters trigger, the choice between counters and tokens, and token creation are engine territory.

Integer-parameterized keyword; mirrors the BushidoEffect and BackupEffect shape — `Value` is the fabricate number lifted from the printed oracle text.

- **Effect discriminator:** `fabricate`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/FabricateEffect.cs)

### `FadingEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Fading N (Rule 702.32). A keyword ability that limits how long a permanent remains on the battlefield. Oracle form: "Fading N (This creature enters with N fade counters on it. At the beginning of your upkeep, remove a fade counter from it. If you can't, sacrifice it.)".

MAST records the keyword's presence and its integer value (the number of fade counters the permanent enters with); the counter-removal upkeep trigger and sacrifice-unless-counter mechanics are engine territory.

Integer-parameterized keyword; mirrors `BushidoEffect` and the Modular/Backup family. `Value` is the fading number lifted from the printed oracle text.

- **Effect discriminator:** `fading`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/FadingEffect.cs)

### `ForetellEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Foretell (Rule 702.143). A keyword ability that lets a player pay {2} during their turn to exile a card from their hand face down, then cast it on a later turn for its foretell cost. Oracle form: "Foretell [cost]". MAST records the keyword's presence and the foretell cost; the exile-and-deferred-cast machinery (along with the {2} foretell activation cost) is engine territory.

- **Effect discriminator:** `foretell`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/ForetellEffect.cs)

### `GraftEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Graft N (Rule 702.58). A keyword ability that has a permanent enter with +1/+1 counters on it and can move those counters to other creatures. Oracle form: "Graft N (This creature enters with N +1/+1 counters on it. Whenever another creature enters, you may move a +1/+1 counter from this creature onto it.)".

MAST records the keyword's presence and its integer value (the number of +1/+1 counters the permanent enters with); the enters-with-counters and optional-counter-move triggered ability are engine territory.

Integer-parameterized keyword; mirrors `BushidoEffect` and the Modular/Backup family. `Value` is the graft number lifted from the printed oracle text.

- **Effect discriminator:** `graft`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/GraftEffect.cs)

### `HarmonizeEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Harmonize (Rule 702.157). "You may cast this card from your graveyard for its harmonize cost. You may tap a creature you control to reduce that cost by {X}, where X is its power. Then exile this spell." MAST records the keyword's presence and the printed harmonize cost; the graveyard-cast mechanics, power-based reduction, and exile-after-cast are conventionally inferred from the rules (reminder text).

`Cost` is the polymorphic base type for parity with other cost-bearing keyword effects (Cycling, Bestow, Dash) — most printings use a `ManaCost`.

- **Effect discriminator:** `harmonize`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/HarmonizeEffect.cs)

### `HideawayEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Hideaway N (Rule 702.74). A triggered keyword ability printed as "Hideaway N (When this [permanent] enters, look at the top N cards of your library, exile one face down, then put the rest on the bottom in a random order.)". MAST records the keyword and its integer lookahead count; the ETB trigger, look/exile/shuffle semantics are engine territory per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine.

Integer-parameterized keyword; the parameter is lifted into a `Quantity` (typically a `LiteralQuantity`) to mirror the Crew and Saddle shapes rather than the raw-int Bushido/Toxic/ Backup shapes, since the printed value governs a count of cards looked at.

- **Effect discriminator:** `hideaway`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/HideawayEffect.cs)

### `IncrementEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Increment (CR 702.191a). A triggered keyword ability printed as a bare keyword token. Per CR 702.191a: "Increment is a triggered ability. 'Increment' means 'Whenever you cast a spell, if this permanent is a creature and the amount of mana spent to cast that spell is greater than this creature's power or this creature's toughness, put a +1/+1 counter on this creature.'"

Although Increment is a triggered ability under the rules, it is printed as a bare keyword token (no cost, no reminder text inline). MAST records the keyword's presence (descriptive-not-engine doctrine); the trigger condition, mana comparison, and counter placement are engine territory.

Parameterless keyword marker; mirrors the `StartYourEnginesEffect` and `TrampleEffect` shape.

- **Effect discriminator:** `increment`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/IncrementEffect.cs)

### `KeywordAbilityEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

A parameterless keyword ability (CR 702) carried by its identity alone — deathtouch, trample, vigilance, cascade, persist, … The single node every such marker collapses into (ADR 0006): the `KeywordAbility` enum value is the whole content, so the 67 former one-per-keyword empty records become one.

The same enum identifies a keyword wherever it appears — this effect, a granted ability (`GainAbilityEffect.GainedAbility`), a characteristic filter (`KeywordCharacteristic`), an ability-class filter — one source of truth. Carries no trait fields: a temporary grant ("gains trample until end of turn") is a `GainAbilityEffect` whose `Duration` holds the span, not this node.

- **Effect discriminator:** `keywordAbility`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/KeywordAbilityEffect.cs)

### `MegamorphEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Megamorph (Rule 702.37b). A variant of Morph: the player may cast this card face down as a 2/2 colorless creature for {3}, and may turn it face up by paying its megamorph cost; when turned face up via the megamorph cost, a +1/+1 counter is placed on the permanent. MAST records the keyword's presence and the megamorph cost; the cast-face-down rules, turn-face-up mechanics, and counter-placement are engine territory (per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine).

Direct structural mirror of `MorphEffect` — same mana-cost-parameterized keyword shape. The counter-placement on face-up flip is the sole mechanical distinction and is engine territory, so no additional AST field is needed beyond `Cost`.

- **Effect discriminator:** `megamorph`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/MegamorphEffect.cs)

### `MiracleEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Miracle {cost} (Rule 702.94). An alternative cost a player may pay when they draw this card as the first card drawn during their draw step. MAST records the keyword's presence and its printed miracle cost; the draw-trigger and cast-timing semantics are conventionally inferred from the rules.

- **Effect discriminator:** `miracle`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/MiracleEffect.cs)

### `MobilizeEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Mobilize N (Rule 702.175, introduced in Tarkir: Dragonstorm). A triggered keyword ability printed as "Mobilize N (Whenever this creature attacks, create N tapped and attacking 1/1 red Warrior creature tokens. Sacrifice them at the beginning of the next end step.)". MAST records the keyword and its integer token-creation count; the attack trigger, token-creation, tapped-and-attacking entry, and delayed-sacrifice semantics are engine territory per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine.

Integer-parameterized keyword; mirrors the HideawayEffect shape — parameter is a `Quantity` (typically a `LiteralQuantity`).

- **Effect discriminator:** `mobilize`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/MobilizeEffect.cs)

### `MonstrosityEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Monstrosity N (CR 701.37a): "Monstrosity N means 'If this permanent isn't monstrous, put N +1/+1 counters on it and it becomes monstrous.'"

MAST records the keyword-action and its integer value N. The monstrous designation (CR 701.37b: "Monstrous is a designation that has no rules meaning other than to act as a marker that the monstrosity action and other spells and abilities can identify."), state tracking, and counter placement are engine territory — this node is purely descriptive.

Do NOT decompose into PutCounters + state-change nodes; the full monstrosity keyword-action is the atomic unit (per MAST doctrine: describes, does not execute). Mirrors the integer-parameterized shape of `RenownEffect` and `TributeEffect` — the `Value` field carries N from the oracle text.

- **Effect discriminator:** `monstrosity`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/MonstrosityEffect.cs)

### `MorphEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Morph (Rule 702.37). A static ability functioning in any zone from which the card could be cast: the player may cast it face down as a 2/2 colorless creature spell for {3}, and may turn it face up later by paying its morph cost. MAST records the keyword's presence and the morph cost; the cast-face-down rules and turn-face-up mechanics are conventionally inferred from the rules (per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine).

`Cost` is the polymorphic base type, mirroring `CyclingEffect` and `EquipEffect`. While Morph costs in printed cards are always mana, the base accommodates future variants (e.g., Megamorph and other morph-family keywords route through separate Effect types) without a schema change.

- **Effect discriminator:** `morph`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/MorphEffect.cs)

### `MutateEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Mutate (Rule 702.140). An alternative casting cost: if you cast this spell for its mutate cost, put it over or under target non-Human creature you own. MAST records the keyword's presence and the mutate cost only; the over/under stacking mechanics and the resulting merged permanent are engine territory (Reminder text describes them).

`Cost` is the polymorphic `Cost` base type, consistent with other keyword-cost effects (e.g. `CyclingEffect`).

- **Effect discriminator:** `mutate`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/MutateEffect.cs)

### `NinjutsuEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Ninjutsu (Rule 702.49). "[Cost], Return an unblocked attacker you control to hand: Put this card onto the battlefield from your hand tapped and attacking." MAST records the keyword's presence and the ninjutsu cost; the return-attacker, enter-tapped-and-attacking semantics are conventionally inferred from the rules (per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine), mirroring the BestowEffect, KickerEffect, and EchoEffect patterns.

`Cost` is the polymorphic base type for parity with the other cost-bearing keyword effects (Bestow, Kicker, Echo, Equip) — all known Ninjutsu printings use a `ManaCost`, but the polymorphic base accommodates future non-mana variants.

- **Effect discriminator:** `ninjutsu`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/NinjutsuEffect.cs)

### `NoMaxHandSizeEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"You have no maximum hand size." — Rule 402.2. A continuous static effect that removes the controller's maximum hand size restriction for as long as the source permanent is on the battlefield. The subject is always "You" (the controller); no target, duration, or condition field is needed because the effect persists by virtue of the permanent's presence (Rule 604.3). Parameterless, mirroring AscendEffect and TakeInitiativeEffect.

- **Effect discriminator:** `noMaxHandSize`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/NoMaxHandSizeEffect.cs)

### `OffspringEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Offspring (Rule 702.175). "You may pay an additional {cost} as you cast this spell. If you do, when this creature enters, create a 1/1 token that's a copy of it." MAST records the keyword's presence and the additional cost; the token-copy creation is conventionally inferred from the rules and captured in the Reminder parenthetical.

`Cost` is the polymorphic base type, mirroring `CyclingEffect` and other cost-bearing keyword effects.

- **Effect discriminator:** `offspring`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/OffspringEffect.cs)

### `OverloadEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Overload (Rule 702.96). A spell modifier printed as "Overload [cost]" on instants and sorceries. When cast for the overload cost the spell's target references change to "each" references (per reminder text); MAST records only the keyword's presence and the alternative cost — the target-to-each rewrite is engine territory, not a descriptive axis of the card.

- **Effect discriminator:** `overload`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/OverloadEffect.cs)

### `PartnerEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Partner effects: Partner, Partner with [name], Choose a Background, Friends forever, Doctor's companion. Rule 702.124

- **Effect discriminator:** `partner`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/PartnerEffect.cs)

### `PayLifeEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"Pay N life." — an optional-life-payment declaration. The oracle line describes a single payment the controller MAY make; the `IfYouDoNot` branch carries the fallback effect when payment is declined.

Timing is a separate axis: in the Shockland pattern ("As this land enters, you may pay N life. If you don't, it enters tapped." — CR 614.1c) the enclosing `StaticAbility` carries `AsThisEnters` and the declined branch holds a plain `TapEffect` targeting self; the payment effect itself stays plain. Modelling the verb directly keeps the AST descriptively faithful (MAST records what oracle text says, not what the rules engine derives at run-time).

Distinct from the enters-tapped + condition shape used for checklands/fastlands: those gate entry on a board-state predicate ("unless you control two or fewer lands") via `Condition`; painlands/shocklands carry a life-payment cost instead. CR 614.1c covers both, but the descriptive shapes differ.

- **Effect discriminator:** `payLife`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/PayLifeEffect.cs)

### `PlayAdditionalLandEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"You may play an additional land on each of your turns." — Rule 305.2 (land-play limit). A continuous static effect that grants the controller permission to play one or more additional lands per turn beyond the normal limit of one (CR 305.2: "A player can normally play one land during their turn; however, continuous effects may increase this number."). The subject is always "You" (the controller); no target, duration, or condition field is needed because the effect persists by virtue of the permanent's presence on the battlefield.

- **Effect discriminator:** `playAdditionalLand`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/PlayAdditionalLandEffect.cs)

### `PlotEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Plot (Rule 702.170). "You may pay [cost] and exile this card from your hand. Cast it as a sorcery on a later turn without paying its mana cost. Plot only as a sorcery." MAST records the keyword's presence and the plot cost; the exile-from-hand, deferred-cast, and sorcery-speed restrictions are conventionally inferred from the rules (per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine), mirroring the KickerEffect, UnearthEffect, and BestowEffect patterns.

`Cost` is the polymorphic base type for parity with the other cost-bearing keyword effects (Equip, Cycling, Bestow, Echo, Kicker, Unearth) — all known Plot printings use a `ManaCost`, but the base accommodates future non-mana variants.

Scope: single-cost plot only (Rule 702.170a). The "cast it as a sorcery on a later turn without paying its mana cost" clause is engine territory — MAST does not model the deferred-cast mechanic.

- **Effect discriminator:** `plot`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/PlotEffect.cs)

### `ProtectionEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Protection effect: comprehensive immunity from a quality. "This permanent can't be blocked, targeted, dealt damage, enchanted, or equipped by [quality]." Rule 702.16

- **Effect discriminator:** `protection`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/ProtectionEffect.cs)

### `ProtectionQuality` — *sealed record*

A quality that protection applies to.

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/ProtectionQuality.cs)

### `PrototypeEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Prototype (Rule 702.160, Rule 718). A keyword ability printed as "Prototype {cost} — P/T" (e.g. "Prototype {1}{B} — 1/1"). It lets a card be cast for an alternative cost, as a smaller creature of a different color and size that keeps its abilities and types.

Per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine, MAST records only what the printed line states: the alternative prototype `Cost` and the prototype `Power`/`Toughness` the card has when cast that way. The alternative-characteristics casting rules ("you may cast … it keeps its abilities and types") are engine territory captured by the reminder text, not modelled as structure here.

`Cost` is the polymorphic base type for parity with the other cost-bearing keyword effects (Cycling, Bestow, Equip) — every printing uses a `ManaCost`, but the base accommodates future variants. `Power` and `Toughness` are kept as raw strings because printed P/T values may be non-numeric (e.g. "*"), mirroring the level-up stanza P/T treatment.

- **Effect discriminator:** `prototype`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/PrototypeEffect.cs)

### `RecoverEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Recover [cost] (Rule 702.59). A triggered ability that functions only while the card with recover is in a player's graveyard: "When a creature is put into your graveyard from the battlefield, you may pay [cost]. If you do, return this card from your graveyard to your hand. Otherwise, exile this card." MAST records the keyword's presence and the recover cost; the trigger, conditional return, and exile clause are all reminder-text territory.

`Cost` is the polymorphic `Cost` base type so future variants with non-mana costs can plug in without a schema change, mirroring the `CyclingEffect` pattern.

- **Effect discriminator:** `recover`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/RecoverEffect.cs)

### `RenownEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Renown (Rule 702.112). A triggered keyword ability printed as "Renown N". When this creature deals combat damage to a player, if it isn't renowned, put N +1/+1 counters on it and it becomes renowned. MAST records the keyword and its integer value; the trigger, renowned designation, and counter-placement are engine territory.

Integer-parameterized keyword; mirrors the BushidoEffect shape — `Value` is the renown number lifted from the printed oracle text.

- **Effect discriminator:** `renown`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/RenownEffect.cs)

### `RingTemptsEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"the Ring tempts you" — the keyword action from Rule 701.54. A one-shot instruction that advances the Ring's temptation track and designates a Ring-bearer. MAST records only the instruction; the Ring-bearer choice, the temptation-track level, and the resulting Ring-bearer designation are resolved by the engine and intentionally not modeled here. CR 701.54a: "Certain spells and abilities have the text 'the Ring tempts you.' Each time the Ring tempts you, choose a creature you control. That creature becomes your Ring-bearer until another creature becomes your Ring-bearer or another player gains control of it."

- **Effect discriminator:** `ringTemptsYou`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/RingTemptsEffect.cs)

### `SaddleEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Saddle (Rule 702.171). An activated keyword ability printed as "Saddle N (Tap any number of other untapped creatures you control with total power N or greater: This permanent becomes saddled until end of turn. Activate only as a sorcery.)". Although Saddle is mechanically an activated ability, MAST records it as a keyword marker — same approach as Crew, Bushido, Soulshift — and treats the canonical activation / saddled designation as engine territory.

- **Effect discriminator:** `saddle`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/SaddleEffect.cs)

### `SneakEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Sneak (Rule 702.173). An alternative-cost keyword found on spells: "Sneak [Cost] (You may cast this spell for [Cost] if you also return an unblocked attacker you control to its owner's hand during the declare blockers step.)"

MAST records the keyword and its alternative mana cost only. The "return an unblocked attacker" condition is part of the reminder text and is inferred from the rules; it is not represented in the AST.

- **Effect discriminator:** `sneak`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/SneakEffect.cs)

### `SpliceEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Splice (Rule 702.47). Printed as "Splice onto [subtype] [cost]" (e.g. "Splice onto Arcane {G}"). While a card with splice is in hand, its controller may reveal it as they cast a spell of the named subtype and pay the splice cost to graft this card's instructions onto that spell.

Per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine, MAST records only the two printed parameters: the spell `Subtype` a spell must share to be a legal splice target, and the `Cost` paid to splice. The text-grafting machinery (revealing, copying instructions onto the target spell) is reminder text and is conventionally inferred from the rules — it is not modeled here.

`Cost` is the polymorphic base type for parity with the other cost-bearing keyword effects (Bestow, Cycling, Flashback) — every printed splice cost is a `ManaCost`, but the base accommodates future variants without a schema change.

- **Effect discriminator:** `splice`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/SpliceEffect.cs)

### `SuspendEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Suspend (Rule 702.62). A keyword that represents three abilities — a static "Suspend N—[cost]" ability that functions in hand, a triggered upkeep ability that removes a time counter, and a triggered ability that plays the card when the last time counter is removed. MAST records the keyword's presence and (when present in oracle text) the N + cost parameters; the three sub-abilities are conventionally inferred from the rules. When granted by another effect ("It gains suspend"), the gained keyword carries no parameters of its own — the parameters are filled in from the granting context (e.g. the time counters already placed by the same resolving effect, and the card's own mana cost). In that case `N` and `Cost` are both null.

- **Effect discriminator:** `suspend`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/SuspendEffect.cs)

### `ToxicEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Toxic (Rule 702.164). A static ability printed as "Toxic N" where N is a positive integer. Whenever a creature with toxic deals combat damage to a player, that player gets N poison counters (in addition to the damage). MAST records the keyword and its integer value; the poison-counter placement and interaction with combat damage are engine territory per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine.

Integer-parameterized keyword; mirrors the BushidoEffect shape — `Value` is the toxic number lifted from the printed oracle text.

Multiple toxic instances on one creature add their N values (702.164b); MAST records each instance separately.

- **Effect discriminator:** `toxic`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/ToxicEffect.cs)

### `TransmuteEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Transmute {cost} (Rule 702.49). An activated ability usable only in hand (as a sorcery): pay the transmute cost and discard this card to search your library for a card with the same mana value, reveal it, put it into your hand, then shuffle. MAST records the keyword's presence and the transmute cost; the discard/search/ reveal/shuffle resolution is described by the rules and left to the engine.

Mirrors `CyclingEffect` — keyword presence + polymorphic cost, no inner effect structure needed.

- **Effect discriminator:** `transmute`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/TransmuteEffect.cs)

### `TributeEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Tribute N (Rule 702.102). "As this creature enters, an opponent of your choice may put N +1/+1 counters on it." An enters-the-battlefield keyword from Born of the Gods. If the opponent does not pay tribute, an ETB triggered ability typically fires.

MAST records the keyword's presence and its integer value N; the opponent-choice, counter-placement, and conditional ETB semantics are engine territory (per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine).

Integer-parameterized keyword; mirrors the `BushidoEffect` shape.

- **Effect discriminator:** `tribute`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/TributeEffect.cs)

### `TypecyclingEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Typecycling (Rule 702.32; variant subrule 702.32f): a parameterized keyword printed as "[Type]cycling [cost]" — e.g., Plainscycling {2}, Forestcycling {1}, Swampcycling {2}. An activated ability functioning only in hand: "[Cost], Discard this card: Search your library for a [Type] card, reveal it, put it into your hand, then shuffle."

Distinct from the plain `CyclingEffect` (Rule 702.32 base): plain Cycling's inner effect is "Draw a card", whereas Typecycling's inner effect is "Search your library for a [Type] card, reveal it, put it into your hand, then shuffle." The Comprehensive Rules treat these as variants of the same keyword, but the inner effects differ enough that MAST models them as separate effect types (per the existing pattern of one effect type per distinct oracle-template shape).

MAST records the keyword's presence, the land type, and the typecycling cost; the inner search/reveal/shuffle structure is conventionally inferred from the rules and not re-modeled per fixture.

- **Effect discriminator:** `typecycling`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/TypecyclingEffect.cs)

### `UnearthEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Unearth (Rule 702.84). "[Cost]: Return this card from your graveyard to the battlefield. It gains haste. Exile it at the beginning of the next end step or if it would leave the battlefield. Unearth only as a sorcery." MAST records the keyword's presence and the unearth cost; the return-to- battlefield, haste-grant, and exile-at-end-step semantics are conventionally inferred from the rules (per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine), mirroring the KickerEffect, BestowEffect, and EchoEffect patterns.

`Cost` is the polymorphic base type for parity with the other cost-bearing keyword effects (Equip, Cycling, Bestow, Echo, Kicker) — all known Unearth printings use a `ManaCost`, but the base accommodates future variants.

- **Effect discriminator:** `unearth`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/UnearthEffect.cs)

### `VanishingEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Vanishing N (Rule 702.63). A keyword ability that limits how long a permanent remains on the battlefield. Oracle form: "Vanishing N (This creature enters with N time counters on it. At the beginning of your upkeep, remove a time counter from it. When the last is removed, sacrifice it.)".

MAST records the keyword's presence and its integer value (the number of time counters the permanent enters with); the counter-removal upkeep trigger and sacrifice-when-empty mechanics are engine territory.

Integer-parameterized keyword; mirrors `BushidoEffect` and `FadingEffect`. Vanishing differs from Fading in using time counters and in triggering sacrifice when the last counter is removed (rather than when you can't remove one). MAST records the value only; the counter-type distinction is rules-territory.

- **Effect discriminator:** `vanishing`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/VanishingEffect.cs)

### `WarpEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Warp [cost] (Rule 702.185). "You may cast this card from your hand for its warp cost. It enters the battlefield tapped..." An alternative-cast keyword that lets a controller cast a permanent for an alternative mana cost, with the permanent entering tapped as a consequence. MAST records the keyword and the warp cost; the alternative-cast and enters-tapped mechanics are engine territory per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine.

Mana-cost-parameterized keyword; mirrors the KickerEffect, PlotEffect, and FlashbackEffect shape.

- **Effect discriminator:** `warp`

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/WarpEffect.cs)

### `WebSlingingEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Web-slinging (Rule 702.188). A static ability that functions while the spell is on the stack: "Web-slinging [cost]" means "You may cast this spell by paying [cost] and returning a tapped creature you control to its owner's hand rather than paying its mana cost." MAST records the keyword's presence and the web-slinging cost; the return-a-tapped-creature component of the alt cost is expressed in reminder text and is engine territory per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine.

`Cost` is the polymorphic base type for parity with other alt-cost keyword effects (Cycling, Evoke, Flashback, Dash). All known printings use a `ManaCost`.

The day/night phase for the Daybound and Nightbound keyword abilities. Rule 702.145.

### `EvasionConditionType` — *enum*

Types of evasion conditions.

### `PartnerType` — *enum*

Types of partner abilities.

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/PartnerType.cs)

### `ProtectionQualityKind` — *enum*

The kind of quality for protection.

[Source](AST/Effects/Keyword/ProtectionQualityKind.cs)

## `/AST/Effects/Modification/`

### `AddTypeEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

"[Subject] are [card-type(s)] [subtype(s)] in addition to their other types." — a layer-4 (CR 613.1d) continuous effect that ADDITIVELY grants one or more card types and/or subtypes to a group of permanents. Unlike `SetCardTypesEffect` (which replaces) and `ChangeSubtypeEffect` (which replaces subtypes), this node models the "in addition to their other types" clause: existing card types and subtypes are retained and the new type(s) are appended.

CR 205.1a (verbatim): "Some effects change an object's card type, subtype, and/or supertype but specify that the object retains a prior card type, subtype, and/or supertype. In such cases, all the object's prior card types, subtypes, and supertypes are retained, and the effect causes the object to gain or lose other card types, subtypes, and/or supertypes."

The canonical example is Ashaya, Soul of the Wild: "Nontoken creatures you control are Forest lands in addition to their other types." → the creatures gain both the 'land' card type and the 'Forest' land subtype while retaining their existing types.

MAST is descriptive: this node records the oracle declaration. The rules engine applies it in layer 4 and resolves downstream consequences (e.g. the implicit basic land mana ability from CR 305.6 for Forest lands).

- **Effect discriminator:** `addType`

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/AddTypeEffect.cs)

### `AttachEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"Attach [this permanent] to target [filter]." — the oracle-text instruction to move an Equipment, Aura, or Fortification onto a qualifying object or player. Rule 701.3: "To take an Aura, Equipment, or Fortification from where it currently is and put it onto a specified object or player."

MAST records what oracle text says (the attachment instruction and its target); the rules-engine mechanics of legality checks, zone-change effects, and continuous effects from the attachment are engine territory (per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine).

Distinct from `EquipEffect`, which models the Equip keyword ability (an activated ability defined by Rule 702.6). This effect models explicit oracle-text "attach" instructions, typically inside triggered abilities on Equipment that auto-attach on entry.

- **Effect discriminator:** `attach`

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/AttachEffect.cs)

### `BecomesCopyEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

"[This permanent] becomes a copy of [target] [until end of turn]." — a layer-1 copy effect (CR 707) that makes the source object become a copy of the named object for the stated duration.

Distinct from `CopyEffect`, which <em>creates a new token or spell copy</em> (CR 707.1: "create a token that's a copy of"). `BecomesCopyEffect` modifies the existing permanent in place — no new object is created (CR 707.6: "Some effects cause a permanent that's copying a permanent to copy a different object while remaining on the battlefield."). The subject is the source of the ability (typically Self); the `CopyTarget` is the object it becomes a copy of.

CR 707.2 governs which characteristics are copied; CR 707.6 governs the remain-in-place semantics. Both are engine territory and are not modelled here (descriptive-not-executive doctrine).

<b>Examples:</b> Shifting Woodland — "Delirium — {2}{G}{G}: This land becomes a copy of target permanent card in your graveyard until end of turn." → Subject: Self, CopyTarget: Target(CardTypes:["permanent"], Zone:Graveyard, Controller:You), Duration: untilEndOfTurn.

- **Effect discriminator:** `becomesCopy`

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/BecomesCopyEffect.cs)

### `BecomesCreatureEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

"[This permanent] becomes a [P/T] [colors] [subtypes] [card types] creature with [keywords] [until end of turn]." — the Keyrune/Monument "animate" template: a single continuous effect that, for a duration, makes the source permanent into a creature with a fully-specified characteristic set.

There is no single keyword <em>action</em> for "becomes a creature" — the line is one continuous effect (CR 611) that SETS several characteristics of one object at once. The relevant characteristic rules:

<b>CR 205</b> — card types (the added "creature" type) and subtypes (the added "Bird"/"Beast" creature type). <b>CR 208</b> — power and toughness (the stated 2/2, 3/2 box). <b>CR 105</b> — colors (the stated "white and blue" / "red and green"). <b>CR 113.6/702</b> — the granted keyword ability (flying, trample).

<b>Why one composite node rather than a `CompositeEffect` of primitives.</b> MAST is descriptive: it models what the oracle line SAYS, not how a rules engine applies it. The line is grammatically and conceptually ONE thing — "this permanent becomes a [full spec]" — so a single faithful node is truer than a bag of five independent effects (`definePT` + `changeColor` + `changeSubtype` + add-type + `gainAbility`) that would imply five separately-stated effects. The single `Duration` scopes the whole transformation as a unit (it would otherwise have to be replicated onto each child). Reusing `ChangeColorEffect` / `ChangeSubtypeEffect` would also import their replace / Aura-attached semantics, which do not fit an additive animate, and there is no existing "add a card type" primitive at all.

Layer/timestamp ordering (CR 613) — how this continuous effect interacts with others, which layer each characteristic change lands in — is ENGINE territory and is deliberately not modeled here (descriptive-not-executive doctrine).

Examples:

Azorius Keyrune — "This artifact becomes a 2/2 white and blue Bird artifact creature with flying until end of turn." → Subject: Self, Power/ Toughness: 2/2, Colors: ["W","U"], AddedSubtypes: ["Bird"], CardTypes: ["artifact","creature"], GainedAbilities: [flying], Duration: untilEndOfTurn. Gruul Keyrune — "This artifact becomes a 3/2 red and green Beast artifact creature with trample until end of turn." → 3/2, ["R","G"], ["Beast"], ["artifact","creature"], [trample], untilEndOfTurn.

- **Effect discriminator:** `becomesCreature`

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/BecomesCreatureEffect.cs)

### `ChangeColorEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

"Target [object] becomes [color(s)] until end of turn." — layer-5 (CR 613.1e) color-changing continuous effect. Describes the oracle-text declaration that the named permanent's colors are replaced by the specified set for the duration of the effect.

CR 105.3 (verbatim): "Effects may change an object's color or give a color to a colorless object. If an effect gives an object a new color, the new color replaces all previous colors the object had (unless the effect said the object became that color 'in addition' to its other colors)."

Examples:

Metathran Transport — "{U}: Target creature becomes blue until end of turn." → Colors: ["U"], Duration: untilEndOfTurn

Colors are encoded as one-character color codes matching the oracle-text color name: white = "W", blue = "U", black = "B", red = "R", green = "G", colorless = "C". The "colorless" case is included per CR 105.3 ("give a color to a colorless object") and its inverse (making a colored object colorless), encoded as Colors: [] (empty list) — but for "becomes colorless" the single entry "C" is used for clarity.

MAST is descriptive: this node records what the oracle line says. The rules engine is responsible for how layer-5 color changes interact with other continuous effects (CR 613.7).

- **Effect discriminator:** `changeColor`

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/ChangeColorEffect.cs)

### `ChangeSubtypeEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

"Enchanted [type] is a(n) [Subtype]." / "This creature becomes the creature type of your choice [until end of turn]." — a layer-4 (CR 613.1d) subtype-changing continuous effect. Describes the oracle-text declaration that the target's subtypes from the appropriate set are <em>set</em> to a value (CR 205.1a: "when an effect sets one or more of an object's subtypes, the new subtype(s) replaces any existing subtypes from the appropriate set"). The new value is either a literal list (`Subtypes`) or a value the controller picks on resolution (`ChosenSubtype`) — exactly one of the two is populated.

The static Aura form ("Enchanted land is an Island") carries no `Duration`: it persists while the Aura remains attached (CR 702.5 / 613.1d). The Mistform activated form is bounded — "until end of turn" lands in the inherited `Duration`.

Examples:

Spreading Seas — "Enchanted land is an Island." → Subtypes: ["Island"] Convincing Mirage — "Enchanted land is a Plains." → Subtypes: ["Plains"] Phantasmal Terrain — various basic land type shapes. Mistform Stalker / Mistform Dreamer — "This creature becomes the creature type of your choice until end of turn." → ChosenSubtype: CreatureType, Duration: until end of turn.

MAST is descriptive: this node records what the oracle line says. The rules engine is responsible for how layer-4 subtype changes interact with other continuous effects (CR 613.7), the implicit basic land mana ability grant (CR 305.6), and landwalk evasion (CR 702.14). This stays a subtype-set effect, not a `BecomesCreatureEffect`: the Mistform card remains a creature and only its creature type is replaced (CR 205.1a) — no card type is added.

- **Effect discriminator:** `changeSubtype`

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/ChangeSubtypeEffect.cs)

### `DefinePTEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

A characteristic-defining ability that sets a creature's power and/or toughness to a derived value. Rule 604.3 — the value is equal to some game-state quantity. e.g., "[CardName]'s power is equal to the number of lands you control." e.g., "[CardName]'s power and toughness are each equal to the number of creatures in your graveyard." Distinct from `ModifyPTEffect` which adds to or subtracts from P/T (layer 7c). This effect defines the base value (layer 7a) — it answers "what is the * in the P/T box?" rather than "what modifier is applied?".

- **Effect discriminator:** `definePT`

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/DefinePTEffect.cs)

### `ExchangeCharacteristicEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

Exchange a characteristic between two objects. e.g., "exchange text boxes", "exchange power and toughness", "exchange control"

- **Effect discriminator:** `exchangeCharacteristic`

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/ExchangeCharacteristicEffect.cs)

### `GainAbilitiesFromExiledCardsEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

"[Permanents] have all [abilityKind] abilities of all [card filter] exiled with [source]."

Models the Hazel's Brewmaster static ability pattern: "Foods you control have all activated abilities of all creature cards exiled with this creature." The target permanents (`Target`) continuously gain every ability of the stated kind from the full set of cards currently in exile linked to this object.

<b>Reference, not resolution (ADR 0004):</b> `SourceFilter` names the class of exiled cards declaratively ("creature cards exiled with this creature") via `ExiledWith` pointing at `Self`; it does not pre-resolve which cards are currently in that set. The engine evaluates the reference at each relevant game moment.

CR 613.1f (layer 6 — ability-granting continuous effects): the continuous static ability grants the listed ability kinds to the target permanents for as long as the source remains on the battlefield. CR 602.1c: "An activated ability is the only kind of ability that can be activated."

- **Effect discriminator:** `gainAbilitiesFromExiledCards`

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/GainAbilitiesFromExiledCardsEffect.cs)

### `GainAbilityEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

"[target] gains [ability]"

- **Effect discriminator:** `gainAbility`

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/GainAbilityEffect.cs)

### `GainControlEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

"gain control of [target]"

- **Effect discriminator:** `gainControl`

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/GainControlEffect.cs)

### `HasAllAbilitiesOfControlledCreaturesEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

"[Subject] has all [abilityKind] abilities of [controlled creatures]."

Models the Marvin, Murderous Mimic static ability pattern: "Marvin has all activated abilities of creatures you control that don't have the same name as this creature." The subject (`Subject`) continuously gains every ability of the stated kind from all permanents matching `SourceFilter` while this permanent is on the battlefield.

<b>Reference, not resolution (ADR 0004):</b> `SourceFilter` encodes the oracle-stated selection criterion ("creatures you control that don't have the same name as this creature") declaratively; the engine evaluates the reference at each relevant game moment to determine which permanents currently satisfy it.

<b>CR 613.1f</b> (layer 6 — ability-adding continuous effects): this static ability continuously grants the listed ability kind to the subject permanent while the source permanent remains on the battlefield. <br/> <b>CR 602.1</b>: "An activated ability is the only kind of ability that can be activated." (Defines what "activated abilities" means as a class.)

- **Effect discriminator:** `hasAllAbilitiesOfControlledCreatures`

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/HasAllAbilitiesOfControlledCreaturesEffect.cs)

### `LoseAbilityEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

"[target] loses [ability]"

- **Effect discriminator:** `loseAbility`

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/LoseAbilityEffect.cs)

### `LoseTypeEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

"[This permanent] isn't a [card type]" / "[target] loses [card type] type" — a layer-4 continuous effect (CR 613.1d) that removes a named card type from the subject. The canonical Theros God template: "As long as your devotion to [color] is less than [N], [this card name] isn't a creature." (CR 205.1a: when an effect says a permanent "isn't" of a given type, it loses that type for the duration of the effect.)

This is the structural inverse of `BecomesCreatureEffect`: where that node ADDS a type (often with a full characteristic bundle), this node REMOVES a single named type from the permanent's type line. The removed type is a card-type keyword (creature, artifact, enchantment, land, planeswalker, battle — CR 205.2), not a subtype (CR 205.3) or supertype (CR 205.4); those are handled by `ChangeSubtypeEffect` and future nodes respectively.

Distinct from `LoseAbilityEffect`: abilities and card types are separate axes (CR 113 vs CR 205); losing the creature type does not automatically lose any keyword abilities.

CR 205.1a (verbatim): "Some effects change an object's card type, subtype, and/or supertype but specify that the object retains a prior card type, subtype, and/or supertype. In such cases, all the object's prior card types, subtypes, and supertypes are retained, and the effect causes the object to gain or lose other card types, subtypes, and/or supertypes."

- **Effect discriminator:** `loseType`

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/LoseTypeEffect.cs)

### `ModifyPTEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

"[target] gets +X/+Y" or "gets -X/-Y"

- **Effect discriminator:** `modifyPT`

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/ModifyPTEffect.cs)

### `SetCardTypesEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

"It's an [type]." / "It's a [type]." — a layer-4 (CR 613.1d) card-type–setting continuous effect that declares the subject's card types, overriding its existing types. Distinct from `BecomesCreatureEffect` (which <em>adds</em> a creature type alongside existing types and pins P/T and abilities) and from `ChangeSubtypeEffect` (which operates on subtypes, not card types).

CR 613.1d: "Layer 4: Type-changing effects are applied. These include effects that change an object's card type, subtype, and/or supertype." An object whose card types are set to `["enchantment"]` ceases to be a creature; it is only an enchantment. This is a descriptive record of the oracle instruction; the engine applies the effect in layer 4 and resolves all downstream consequences (loss of creature status, P/T, creature-type subtypes per CR 205.1a, etc.) without MAST encoding them (descriptive-not-executive doctrine, ADR 0003).

Examples:

Enduring Tenacity — dies trigger: "… return it to the battlefield under its owner's control. It's an enchantment." → Subject: It, CardTypes: ["enchantment"]. The permanent re-enters as a pure enchantment; the "it's not a creature" parenthetical is a clarifying note, not a separate effect.

- **Effect discriminator:** `setCardTypes`

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/SetCardTypesEffect.cs)

### `UnattachEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"Unattach this permanent." — the oracle-text instruction to remove an Equipment, Aura, or Fortification from the object it is attached to, returning it to the battlefield as an unattached permanent.

CR 702.151a (verbatim): "Reconfigure represents two activated abilities. Reconfigure [cost] means "[Cost]: Attach this permanent to another target creature you control. Activate only as a sorcery" and "[Cost]: Unattach this permanent. Activate only if this permanent is attached to a creature and only as a sorcery."

MAST records what oracle text says (the unattach instruction). The activation condition "only if this permanent is attached to a creature" is a runtime-state guard — no structured node for conditional activation guards exists yet; it is omitted per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine (engine territory, not present as a free-text field per the no-free-text rule).

Distinct from `AttachEffect`, which models explicit "attach" instructions. This effect models the inverse action: detaching a previously attached permanent from its host.

- **Effect discriminator:** `unattach`

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/UnattachEffect.cs)

Characteristics that can be exchanged.

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/ExchangeableCharacteristic.cs)

Which power/toughness characteristic a `DefinePTEffect` defines.

## `/AST/Effects/Replacement/`

### `ReplacementEvent` — *abstract record*

Base type for events that can be replaced.

- **Polymorphic base, discriminator property:** `EventType`

[Source](AST/Effects/Replacement/ReplacementEvent.cs)

### `AbilityTriggerEvent` — *sealed record* : ReplacementEvent

Replacement event for the triggering of a triggered ability — "if [a triggered ability of X] triggers, it triggers an additional time."

This models the trigger-multiplication static ability found on cards like Echoes of Eternity and Panharmonicon. Per CR 603: triggered abilities trigger whenever the event they watch for occurs. When a trigger-multiplying effect is in play, the matching triggering event produces an additional trigger occurrence — i.e. the ability goes on the stack twice (or more). MAST records the <em>source</em> of the watched ability via `AffectedObjects` (the permanent or spell whose triggered ability fires) and the multiplying modifier via `Modifier` (`Type:"additionalTime"`), while `OriginalEventOccurs` is `true` (the original trigger still fires once; the additional occurrence is on top of it).

When the trigger-multiplying effect is scoped to triggers <em>caused by</em> a particular entering object (Panharmonicon: "if an artifact or creature entering causes … to trigger"), `CausedByEntering` carries an `ObjectFilter` for that entering object. Null when the scope is unconditional (Echoes of Eternity) — omitted in serialization so existing cards round-trip unchanged.

CR 603.2d (verbatim from rules-structure.json): "An ability may state that a triggered ability triggers additional times. In this case, rather than simply determining that such an ability has triggered, determine how many times it should trigger, then that ability triggers that many times."

CR 603.9 (descriptive reference, verbatim from rules-structure.json): "If a triggered ability is linked to a static ability that prevents it from triggering unless some condition has been met, it won't trigger if that condition hasn't been met." The doubling/additional-time interaction is engine territory; MAST describes the <em>scope</em> of which triggered abilities are multiplied (via the AffectedObjects filter) and the multiplying factor (Modifier.Type).

- **ReplacementEvent discriminator:** `abilityTrigger`

[Source](AST/Effects/Replacement/AbilityTriggerEvent.cs)

Counter placement event: "counters would be put on"

- **ReplacementEvent discriminator:** `counterPlacement`

[Source](AST/Effects/Replacement/CounterPlacementEvent.cs)

Draw-a-card event: "would draw [N] card(s)". Rule 121 (drawing a card), Rule 614 (replacement effects).

### `LegendRuleSuppressionEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"The 'legend rule' doesn't apply." A meta-rule suppression that disables the state-based action described by Rule 704.5j. Rule 704.5j: "If a player controls two or more legendary permanents with the same name, that player chooses one of them, and the rest are put into their owners' graveyards. This is called the 'legend rule.'" Mirror Gallery (MRD) is the canonical card. The effect carries no parameters; its mere presence on a `StaticAbility` records that the legend rule's state-based action is suppressed while the source permanent is on the battlefield.

- **Effect discriminator:** `legendRuleSuppression`

[Source](AST/Effects/Replacement/LegendRuleSuppressionEffect.cs)

### `LifeChangeEvent` — *sealed record* : ReplacementEvent

Life change event: "would gain/lose life"

- **ReplacementEvent discriminator:** `lifeChange`

[Source](AST/Effects/Replacement/LifeChangeEvent.cs)

### `MillEvent` — *sealed record* : ReplacementEvent

Mill event: "[a player] would mill one or more cards" (CR 701.17a — to mill a number of cards is to put that many cards from the top of a library into a graveyard). The replaceable event watched by effects such as Bruvac the Grandiloquent. Mirrors `TokenCreationEvent`/`CounterPlacementEvent`: the milling player is carried on the inherited `Controller` (e.g. `Opponent` for "an opponent would mill").

- **ReplacementEvent discriminator:** `mill`

[Source](AST/Effects/Replacement/MillEvent.cs)

### `ReplacementEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"If [event] would [happen], [alternative] instead" Rule 614

- **Effect discriminator:** `replacement`

[Source](AST/Effects/Replacement/ReplacementEffect.cs)

### `ReplacementModifier` — *sealed record*

Modifier for replacement effects that scale the original event.

[Source](AST/Effects/Replacement/ReplacementModifier.cs)

### `SpellCopyEvent` — *sealed record* : ReplacementEvent

Replacement event for when a player would copy a spell one or more times — "If you would copy a spell one or more times, instead …"

CR 707.10: "To copy a spell, activated ability, or triggered ability means to put a copy of it onto the stack; a copy of a spell isn't cast…" Twinning Staff's static ability is a replacement effect that intercepts the act of copying a spell and adds one additional copy on top of however many copies would otherwise be made. The `MinimumQuantity` encodes the "one or more" threshold.

- **ReplacementEvent discriminator:** `spellCopy`

[Source](AST/Effects/Replacement/SpellCopyEvent.cs)

Token creation event: "one or more tokens would be created"

- **ReplacementEvent discriminator:** `tokenCreation`

Untap event: "would become untapped"
