(CR 702.128a verbatim): "Embalm is an activated ability that functions while the card with embalm is in a graveyard. 'Embalm [cost]' means '[Cost], Exile this card from your graveyard: Create a token that's a copy of this card, except it's white, it has no mana cost, and it's a Zombie in addition to its other types. Activate only as a sorcery.'"

MAST models this as a single `ActivatedAbility` with two costs: the mana cost parameter and an `ExileCost` (this card from graveyard). The effect is a `CreateTokenEffect` with a copy-override `TokenDefinition`: `IsCopy = true`, overrides white (`Colors = ["W"]`) and Zombie subtype added (`Subtypes = ["Zombie"]`). "No mana cost" and "in addition to its other types" are copy-rules engine concerns — only the structured overrides are modelled.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/EmbalmKeyword.cs)

### `EmergeKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Emerge {cost}: You may cast this spell by sacrificing a creature and paying the emerge cost reduced by that creature's mana value. Rule 702.119. MAST records the keyword and its associated mana cost; the sacrifice mechanic, cost-reduction, and timing are inferred from the rules. Combinator-only: no matching `KeywordDefinitions` entry exists in the legacy registry.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/EmergeKeyword.cs)

### `EncoreKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Encore {cost}: [Cost], Exile this card from your graveyard: For each opponent, create a token copy of this card that attacks that opponent this turn if able. Those tokens gain haste. Sacrifice those tokens at the beginning of the next end step. Rule 702.142. An activated ability playable only from the graveyard. MAST records the keyword and its associated mana cost; the token-copy-per-opponent structure is inferred from the rules. Combinator-only: no matching `KeywordDefinitions` entry exists in the legacy registry.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/EncoreKeyword.cs)

### `EnlistKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Enlist: As this creature attacks, you may tap an untapped creature you control that could have attacked. If you do, add its power to this creature's until end of turn. Rule 702.154. Although mechanically a static + triggered ability pair, MAST records it as a keyword marker — same approach as Evolve and Flanking. The tapping cost and power-addition are engine territory.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/EnlistKeyword.cs)

### `EntwineKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Entwine [cost]: You may choose all modes of this spell instead of just the number specified. If you do, you pay an additional [the entwine cost].

CR 702.42: "Entwine is a static ability of modal spells (see rule 700.2) that functions while the spell with entwine is on the stack. 'Entwine [cost]' means 'You may choose all modes of this spell instead of just the number specified. If you do, you pay an additional [the entwine cost].'"

Entwine is optional (IsOptional: true) and paid at most once (Repeatable: false) — you either pay it to unlock all modes or you don't. The mode-selection override is engine territory; MAST models only the additional cost.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/EntwineKeyword.cs)

### `EquipKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Equip [cost]: Attach to target creature you control. Activate only as a sorcery. CR 702.6a (verbatim): "Equip is an activated ability of Equipment cards. 'Equip [cost]' means '[Cost]: Attach this permanent to target creature you control. Activate only as a sorcery.'" CR 702.6c: "Equip [quality]" restricts legal targets to a creature with the chosen quality controlled by the activating player. CR 702.6e: "Equip planeswalker [cost]" attaches to target planeswalker you control. Combinator-only: no KeywordDefinition entry in the legacy registry.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/EquipKeyword.cs)

### `EscalateKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Escalate [cost]: pay this cost for each mode chosen beyond the first. CR 702.120: "Escalate is a static ability of modal spells (see rule 700.2) that functions while the spell with escalate is on the stack. 'Escalate [cost]' means 'For each mode you choose beyond the first as you cast this spell, you pay an additional [cost].'" IsOptional:true — the controller may choose only one mode (paying zero escalate costs). Repeatable:true — the cost is paid once per extra mode chosen (zero, one, or more times). Combinator-only: no KeywordDefinition entry in the legacy registry.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/EscalateKeyword.cs)

### `EscapeKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Escape (Rule 702.138): "Escape—[cost], Exile N other cards from your graveyard." An alternative cost on instants and sorceries that lets the card be cast from the graveyard. The escape cost has two printed halves — a mana cost and the additional cost of exiling a fixed number of other cards from the controller's graveyard. MAST records both halves; the cast-from-graveyard permission is inferred from the rules and captured as the reminder parenthetical. Combinator-only: no `KeywordDefinition` entry exists.

The exile count is part of the COST. The graveyard exile is deliberately recorded as a count (`CardsToExile`), not modelled as a separate `ExileEffect` subtree — MAST describes the cost, it does not execute it.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/EscapeKeyword.cs)

### `EternalizeKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Eternalize [cost]: activated ability from the graveyard — single ActivatedAbility node.

CR 702.129a (verbatim): "Eternalize is an activated ability that functions while the card with eternalize is in a graveyard. 'Eternalize [cost]' means '[Cost], Exile this card from your graveyard: Create a token that's a copy of this card, except it's black, it's 4/4, it has no mana cost, and it's a Zombie in addition to its other types. Activate only as a sorcery.'"

Costs: mana cost + ExileCost (this card from graveyard). Effects: CreateTokenEffect with IsCopy=true and black/4/4/Zombie overrides. Restriction: OnlyAsSorcery. "No mana cost" and "in addition to its other types" are copy-rules handled by the engine.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/EternalizeKeyword.cs)

### `EvokeKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Evoke [cost]: two abilities per CR 702.74a.

CR 702.74a (verbatim): "Evoke represents two abilities: a static ability that functions in any zone from which the card with evoke can be cast and a triggered ability that functions on the battlefield. 'Evoke [cost]' means 'You may cast this card by paying [cost] rather than paying its mana cost' and 'When this permanent enters, if its evoke cost was paid, its controller sacrifices it.' Casting a spell for its evoke cost follows the rules for paying alternative costs in rules 601.2b and 601.2f-h."

Oracle-text parsing is handled by `EvokeStaticRule` (priority 1001), which returns both abilities as a list. This keyword file keeps the combinator live as a fallback, emitting the PRIMARY alternative-cast `StaticAbility` (no longer the deleted opaque `EvokeEffect` marker). The `Definition` is null because `Expand` can only return a single `Ability` and Evoke decomposes into two.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/EvokeKeyword.cs)

### `EvolveKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Evolve: Whenever a creature you control enters, if that creature has greater power or toughness than this creature, put a +1/+1 counter on this creature. Rule 702.100. Although mechanically a triggered ability, MAST records it as a keyword marker — same approach as Prowess, Exalted, Cascade — and treats the canonical trigger / power-comparison / counter-placement as engine territory.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/EvolveKeyword.cs)

### `ExaltedKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Exalted: Whenever a creature you control attacks alone, that creature gets +1/+1 until end of turn. CR 702.83a (verbatim): "Exalted is a triggered ability. 'Exalted' means 'Whenever a creature you control attacks alone, that creature gets +1/+1 until end of turn.'" MAST shape (ADR 0003 decomposition): TriggeredAbility{ KeywordSource:"Exalted", Trigger:{ Timing:Whenever, Event:Attacks, Filter:{CardTypes:["creature"], Controller:You, Characteristics:[CombatStateCharacteristic{AttackingAlone}]} }, Effects:[ ModifyPTEffect{ Target:{Kind:ThatCreature}, PowerModifier:+1, ToughnessModifier:+1, Duration:untilEndOfTurn } ] }. "attacks alone" is the combat-state predicate CombatStateCharacteristic{AttackingAlone} (CR 508, the attacking-alone role). The trigger subject is "a creature you control" (not this creature): Exalted triggers off ANY of the controller's creatures attacking alone. "that creature" maps to ObjectReferenceKind.ThatCreature — the creature named by the trigger's Filter.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/ExaltedKeyword.cs)

### `ExploitKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Exploit: When this creature enters, you may sacrifice a creature. Rule 702.110. MAST records the keyword's presence; the ETB trigger, optional sacrifice, and downstream sacrifice-payoff abilities are engine territory.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/ExploitKeyword.cs)

### `ExtortKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Extort: Whenever you cast a spell, you may pay {W/B}. If you do, each opponent loses 1 life and you gain that much life. Rule 702.101. MAST records the keyword's presence; the spell-cast trigger and life-drain/gain are engine territory. Parameterless keyword marker — mirrors AscendEffect, ConvokeEffect, EvolveEffect, and PersistEffect.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/ExtortKeyword.cs)

### `FabricateKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Fabricate N: When this creature enters, put N +1/+1 counters on it or create N 1/1 colorless Servo artifact creature tokens. Rule 702.118. MAST records the keyword and its integer value; the enters trigger, choice between counters and tokens, and token creation are engine territory.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/FabricateKeyword.cs)

### `FadingKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Fading N: This permanent enters with N fade counters on it. At the beginning of your upkeep, remove a fade counter from it. If you can't, sacrifice it. Rule 702.32. MAST records the keyword and its integer value; the counter-removal upkeep trigger and sacrifice-unless-counter mechanics are engine territory.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/FadingKeyword.cs)

### `FearKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Fear: This creature can't be blocked except by artifact creatures and/or black creatures. Rule 702.36. MAST records keyword presence; the evasion semantics are expressed via EvasionEffect with an ObjectFilter structuring the artifact type (CardTypes) and the black color (Colors). The "artifact creatures and/or black creatures" disjunction is modelled as one filter carrying both axes (the same simplification the prior free-text list used), per the slice spec's artifact→CardTypes + black→Colors mapping.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/FearKeyword.cs)

### `FirebendingKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Firebending N (Avatar: The Last Airbender). CR 702.189a (verbatim): "Firebending is a triggered ability. 'Firebending N' means 'Whenever this creature attacks, add N {R}. Until end of combat, you don't lose this mana as steps and phases end.'" MAST shape (ADR 0003 decomposition): TriggeredAbility{ KeywordSource:"Firebending", Trigger:{ Timing:"Whenever", Event:"Attacks", Filter:{CardTypes:["creature"]} }, Effects:[ AddManaEffect{ Mana: N×"{R}" } ] }. Mirrors Radha, Heir to Keld ("Whenever Radha attacks, add {R}{R}") — the self-attack trigger uses the creature filter, matching the corpus convention. The end-of-combat mana persistence ("you don't lose this mana as steps and phases end") is an exception to the CR 500.4 mana-emptying turn-based action — engine state bookkeeping, not described content; the verbatim clause survives in the reminder text. The added {R} is the keyword's DEFINITIONAL mana, printed only in reminder text, so it does NOT enter color identity (CR 903.4c) — color identity is derived from printed text by `ColorIdentityDeriver`, which never sees it. Variable-value printings ("Firebending X, …") are out of scope.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/FirebendingKeyword.cs)

### `FirstStrikeKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

First strike: This creature deals combat damage before creatures without first strike. Rule 702.7.

Exemplar of the <b>multi-word simple</b> keyword shape (Stage A template). The combinator matches two sequential word tokens ("First" then "Strike"/"strike") via chained `Keyword(string)` calls. The `Definition` is the verbatim former `KeywordDefinitions.FirstStrike`; the `Combinator` is the verbatim former `OracleParsers.FirstStrike`.

<b>Ordering note:</b> multi-word keywords whose first token is a prefix shared with a shorter keyword must sort earlier in the Or-chain (raise `Priority`). "First" has no single-word collision today, so default priority 50 preserves the legacy ordering. The legacy chain placed First strike / Double strike late; because their leading tokens ("First"/"Double") are unique, order among unrelated keywords is immaterial to first-success-wins.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/FirstStrikeKeyword.cs)

### `FlankingKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Flanking: Whenever this creature becomes blocked by a creature without flanking, the blocking creature gets -1/-1 until end of turn. CR 702.25 (verbatim): "Flanking is a triggered ability that triggers during the declare blockers step. 'Flanking' means 'Whenever this creature becomes blocked by a creature without flanking, the blocking creature gets -1/-1 until end of turn.'" MAST shape (ADR 0003 decomposition): TriggeredAbility{ KeywordSource:"Flanking", Trigger:{ Timing:"Whenever", Event:"BecomesBlocked", Filter:{CardTypes:["creature"], Characteristics:[OtherCharacteristic{Description:"withoutFlanking"}]} }, Effects:[ ModifyPTEffect{ Target:{Kind:ThatCreature}, PowerModifier:-1, ToughnessModifier:-1, Duration:untilEndOfTurn } ] }.

The trigger's Filter encodes the blocker qualification ("by a creature without flanking") — TriggerCondition has a single Filter field and no dedicated By/blocker axis; the blocking-creature qualifier is placed there and noted. The effect target is ObjectReferenceKind.ThatCreature (the creature named by the trigger condition — the blocking creature), not Self/It (those refer to the Flanking creature itself).

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/FlankingKeyword.cs)

### `FlashKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Flash: You may cast this spell any time you could cast an instant. Rule 702.8. Combinator-only keyword — no `KeywordDefinition` exists in the legacy `KeywordDefinitions` registry.

### `FreerunningKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Freerunning [cost] — CR 702.173a: "Freerunning is a static ability that functions on the stack. 'Freerunning [cost]' means 'You may pay [cost] rather than pay this spell's mana cost if a player was dealt combat damage this turn by a creature that, at the time it dealt that damage, was an Assassin creature or a commander under your control.' Casting a spell for its freerunning cost follows the rules for paying alternative costs in rules 601.2b and 601.2f-h."

Emits a `StaticAbility` carrying an `AlternativeCastEffect` with `FromZone = Zone.Hand` and a hard-coded `OtherCondition` residual carrying the verbatim CR 702.173a gate text — the established pattern for Surge/Spectacle conditional cast permissions.

Fuse: You may cast one or both halves of this card from your hand. Rule 702.102. Found on split cards from Dragon's Maze. MAST records the keyword's presence; the split-card casting modes and cost-combination mechanics are engine territory.

Graft N: This permanent enters with N +1/+1 counters on it. Whenever another creature enters, you may move a +1/+1 counter from this creature onto it. Rule 702.58. Harmonize {cost}: You may cast this card from your graveyard for its harmonize cost. You may tap a creature you control to reduce that cost by {X}, where X is its power. Then exile this spell. Rule 702.157. MAST records the keyword and its associated mana cost; the graveyard-cast, power-based cost reduction, and exile-after-cast mechanics are conventionally inferred from the rules (reminder text).

Haste: This creature can attack and tap as soon as it comes under your control. Rule 702.10. MAST records keyword presence; the timing-restriction removal is engine territory.

Hexproof: This permanent can't be the target of spells or abilities your opponents control. Rule 702.11. MAST records keyword presence.

Hideaway N: When this permanent enters, look at the top N cards of your library, exile one face down, then put the rest on the bottom in a random order. Rule 702.74. Horsemanship: This creature can't be blocked except by creatures with horsemanship. Rule 702.32. An evasion keyword — MAST records the keyword's presence; the mutual-horsemanship blocking restriction is engine territory.

### `ImproviseKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Improvise: for each generic mana in this spell's total cost, you may tap an untapped artifact you control rather than pay that mana.

CR 702.126a: "Improvise is a static ability that functions while the spell with improvise is on the stack. 'Improvise' means 'For each generic mana in this spell's total cost, you may tap an untapped artifact you control rather than pay that mana.'"

CR 702.126b: "The improvise ability isn't an additional or alternative cost and applies only after the total cost of the spell with improvise is determined."

MAST models the payment substitution as an `AlternativePaymentEffect` on the static ability (per ADR 0003 keyword-decomposition shape): tap artifacts you control to pay generic mana. "Untapped" is a game-state precondition (engine territory), not a filter axis.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/ImproviseKeyword.cs)

Increment (CR 702.191a). A triggered keyword ability printed as a bare keyword token: "Whenever you cast a spell, if this permanent is a creature and the amount of mana spent to cast that spell is greater than this creature's power or this creature's toughness, put a +1/+1 counter on this creature." MAST records the keyword's presence; the trigger condition, mana comparison, and counter placement are engine territory per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine.

Simple parameterless keyword — combinator-only (no KeywordDefinition in the legacy registry). Tier: Simple. Mirrors `TrampleKeyword` shape.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/IncrementKeyword.cs)

Indestructible: This permanent can't be destroyed. Damage and "destroy" effects that would destroy it have no effect. Rule 702.12. MAST records keyword presence; engine-territory semantics are omitted.

Infect: This creature deals damage to creatures in the form of -1/-1 counters and to players in the form of poison counters. Rule 702.91. MAST records keyword presence; the damage-redirection semantics are engine territory.

### `IngestKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Ingest: Whenever this creature deals combat damage to a player, that player exiles the top card of their library. CR 702.115a (verbatim): "Ingest is a triggered ability. 'Ingest' means 'Whenever this creature deals combat damage to a player, that player exiles the top card of their library.'" MAST shape (ADR 0003 decomposition): TriggeredAbility{ KeywordSource:"Ingest", Trigger:{ Timing:Whenever, Event:DealsCombatDamageToPlayer, Filter:{CardTypes:["creature"]} }, Effects:[ ExileEffect{ Target:{Kind:Designated, Filter:{CardTypes:["card"], Zone:Library, Characteristics:[Other("top"), Other("that player's")]}} } ] }. "Top" (positional) and "that player's" (ownership back-reference to the damaged player established by the trigger) are predicates that do not yet have first-class ObjectFilter fields; they are carried as OtherCharacteristic residuals per the ADR 0001 free-text doctrine and the Mentor/Flanking convention. The card is Designated (deterministic by position, no choice) rather than Target (no targeting rule keyword in CR 702.115a).

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/IngestKeyword.cs)

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/IntimidateKeyword.cs)

Jump-start: You may cast this card from your graveyard by discarding a card in addition to paying its other costs. If you do, this card is exiled as it resolves. Rule 702.133. ### `KeywordCombinators` — *static class*

Shared combinator primitives for one-file-per-keyword `IKeyword` implementations under `Keywords/Definitions/`. These are lifted from the private helpers in `OracleParsers` so each keyword file can build its combinator without depending on `OracleParsers` internals.

Behaviour-identical to the legacy private helpers: a keyword migrated to a file produces the exact same `StaticAbility` as the combinator it shadows.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/KeywordCombinators.cs)

### `KickerKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Kicker {cost} (CR 702.33): "Kicker is a static ability that functions while the spell with kicker is on the stack. 'Kicker [cost]' means 'You may pay an additional [cost] as you cast this spell.'" It is a static ability, so the combinator emits a `StaticAbility` carrying the shared `AdditionalCastCostEffect` primitive (`IsOptional = true` — "you may pay"; `Repeatable = false` — at most once, distinguishing it from Multikicker's `Repeatable = true`). The linked "if this spell was kicked, ..." resolution (CR 702.33e / 607) is a separate ability on the card and is not modeled here (ADR 0003/0004 describe-not-execute).

Combinator-only keyword — no `KeywordDefinition` exists in the legacy `KeywordDefinitions` registry. The `Keyword("Kicker")` word-matcher matches the whole word "Kicker", so the longer word "Multikicker" (CR 702.33c, handled by `MultikickerKeyword`) does not match this combinator.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/KickerKeyword.cs)

### `LearnKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Learn: You may reveal a Lesson card you own from outside the game and put it into your hand, or discard a card to draw a card. Rule 702.148. A keyword action: parameterless keyword marker. MAST records keyword presence; the choice semantics are engine territory. Note: oracle text uses "Learn." (with a trailing period absorbed by the sentence tokenizer) followed by the reminder in parentheses.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/LearnKeyword.cs)

### `LifelinkKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Lifelink: Damage dealt by this creature also causes you to gain that much life. Rule 702.15.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/LifelinkKeyword.cs)

### `LivingWeaponKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Living weapon: When this Equipment enters, create a 0/0 black Phyrexian Germ creature token, then attach this to it. Rule 702.77. Although mechanically a triggered ability, MAST records it as a keyword marker — same approach as Evolve and Flanking; the ETB trigger, token-creation, and auto-attach semantics are engine territory. Multi-word keyword matched by chaining two Keyword() calls ("Living" then "weapon").

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/LivingWeaponKeyword.cs)

### `MadnessKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Madness {cost} (CR 702.35a/702.35b): "Madness [cost]" represents two abilities. The first is a static ability: "If a player would discard this card, that player discards it, but exiles it instead of putting it into their graveyard." The second is a triggered ability: "When this card is exiled this way, its owner may cast it by paying [cost] rather than paying its mana cost." (CR 702.35a verbatim.) Casting uses rules for alternative costs (CR 702.35b → 601.2b, 601.2f-h).

MAST describes, does not execute. The describe axis MAST keeps is the permission to cast from exile at an alternative cost, modeled by the shared `AlternativeCastEffect` primitive (`FromZone = Exile`). The discard-into-exile replacement event and the "when exiled this way" triggered wrapper are engine territory (ADR-0003 describe-not-execute). No `Condition` — Madness is unconditional once the discard occurs.

Combinator-only keyword: Madness has no entry in `KeywordDefinitions.All` (it is not registered as a `KeywordDefinition`), so `Definition` is <see langword="null"/>.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/MadnessKeyword.cs)

### `MayhemKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Mayhem {cost}: A static ability that functions while the card is in the graveyard, allowing it to be cast from there by paying the mayhem cost rather than its mana cost, gated on having discarded the card this turn.

CR 702.187a: "Mayhem is a static ability that functions while the card with mayhem is in a player's graveyard."

CR 702.187b: "'Mayhem [cost]' means 'As long as you discarded this card this turn, you may cast it from your graveyard by paying [cost] rather than paying its mana cost.' Casting a spell using its mayhem ability follows the rules for paying alternative costs in rules 601.2b and 601.2f-h."

Combinator-only keyword: Mayhem has no entry in `KeywordDefinitions.All` (it is not registered as a `KeywordDefinition`), so `Definition` is <see langword="null"/>.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/MayhemKeyword.cs)

### `MegamorphKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Megamorph [cost]: A variant of Morph (Rule 702.37b). The player may cast this card face down as a 2/2 colorless creature for {3}, and may turn it face up by paying its megamorph cost; when turned face up via the megamorph cost, a +1/+1 counter is placed on the permanent. MAST records the keyword and the megamorph cost; the cast-face-down rules, turn-face-up mechanics, and counter-placement are engine territory (descriptive-not-engine doctrine). Rule 702.37b.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/MegamorphKeyword.cs)

### `MeleeKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Melee: Whenever this creature attacks, it gets +1/+1 until end of turn for each opponent you attacked with a creature this combat. CR 702.121a (verbatim): "Melee is a triggered ability. 'Melee' means 'Whenever this creature attacks, it gets +1/+1 until end of turn for each opponent you attacked with a creature this combat.'" MAST shape (ADR 0003 decomposition): TriggeredAbility{ KeywordSource:"Melee", Trigger:{ Timing:"Whenever", Event:"Attacks", Filter:{CardTypes:["creature"]} }, Effects:[ ModifyPTEffect{ Target:{Kind:"It"}, PowerModifier:CalculatedQuantity{Expression:"for each opponent you attacked with a creature this combat"}, ToughnessModifier:CalculatedQuantity{Expression:"for each opponent you attacked with a creature this combat"}, Duration:untilEndOfTurn } ] }. The per-opponent amount is not a simple integer or a CountQuantity (the "opponents attacked" set is a game-state query outside MAST's ObjectFilter scope); it is carried as a CalculatedQuantity{Expression} per the ADR free-text residual doctrine for quantities not yet expressible structurally.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/MeleeKeyword.cs)

### `MenaceKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Menace: This creature can't be blocked except by two or more creatures. Rule 702.111. Evasion keyword whose distinguishing feature is a minimum blocker count rather than a characteristic filter on the blockers.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/MenaceKeyword.cs)

### `MentorKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Mentor: Whenever this creature attacks, put a +1/+1 counter on target attacking creature with power less than this creature's power. CR 702.134 (verbatim): "Mentor is a triggered ability. 'Mentor' means 'Whenever this creature attacks, put a +1/+1 counter on target attacking creature with power less than this creature's power.'" MAST shape (ADR 0003 decomposition): TriggeredAbility{ KeywordSource:"Mentor", Trigger:{ Timing:Whenever, Event:Attacks, Filter:{CardTypes:["creature"]} }, Effects:[ PutCountersEffect{ Target:{Kind:Target, Filter:{CardTypes:["creature"], Characteristics:[CombatStateCharacteristic{Attacking}], PowerComparison:{LessThan, RelativeTo:Self, RelativeCharacteristic:Power}}}, CounterType:"+1/+1", Count:1 } ] }. "Attacking" is the combat-state predicate (CombatStateCharacteristic, CR 508). "With power less than this creature's power" is a relative power comparison against the source object (PowerComparison with RelativeTo=Self), matching the CantBeBlockedRule relative-power convention.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/MentorKeyword.cs)

### `MiracleKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Miracle {cost}: You may cast this card for its miracle cost when you draw it, if it's the first card you drew this turn. Rule 702.94. MAST records the keyword and the printed alternative cost; the draw-trigger and cast-timing mechanics are conventionally inferred from the rules and captured in the reminder parenthetical.

Combinator-only: no `KeywordDefinition` exists in the legacy `KeywordDefinitions.cs`. `Definition` returns `null`.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/MiracleKeyword.cs)

Mobilize N: Whenever this creature attacks, create N tapped and attacking 1/1 red Warrior creature tokens. Sacrifice them at the beginning of the next end step. Rule 702.175 (Tarkir: Dragonstorm). Category is Triggered because the comp-rules expansion is a triggered ability. MAST records the keyword and its integer token-creation count.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/MobilizeKeyword.cs)

### `ModularKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Modular N: two abilities per CR 702.43a.

CR 702.43a (verbatim): "Modular represents both a static ability and a triggered ability. 'Modular N' means 'This permanent enters with N +1/+1 counters on it' and 'When this permanent is put into a graveyard from the battlefield, you may put a +1/+1 counter on target artifact creature for each +1/+1 counter on this permanent.'"

Oracle-text parsing is handled by `ModularStaticRule` (priority 1001), which returns both abilities as a list. This keyword file keeps the combinator live as a fallback but no longer uses the deleted `ModularEffect` opaque marker: it emits only the PRIMARY enters-with-counters static ability ("This permanent enters with N +1/+1 counters on it"). The `Definition` is null because `Expand` can only return a single `Ability` and Modular decomposes into two.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/ModularKeyword.cs)

### `PartnerKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Partner (parameterless): You can have two commanders if both have partner. Rule 702.124. The bare form allows any two Partner commanders to pair up, as opposed to "Partner with [Name]" which binds two specific cards.

Default priority 50 — must be tried <b>after</b> `PartnerWithKeyword` (priority 60): both lead with the "Partner" token; `PartnerWith` backtracks when "with" is absent, leaving this parser to match the bare form.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/PartnerKeyword.cs)

### `PartnerWithKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Partner with [Name]: A pair-binding variant of the Partner keyword. The parameter is the literal name of the paired card (e.g., "Amy Pond"). Rule 702.124. MAST records the keyword and the partner name; the commander-pairing rules are engine territory.

<b>Priority 60</b> — must be tried before `PartnerKeyword` (priority 50): both lead with the "Partner" token; giving this a higher priority ensures the registry tries `Partner with [Name]` first and backtracks to bare `Partner` only when the "with" token is absent.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/PartnerWithKeyword.cs)

### `ScavengeKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

'Scavenge [cost]' means '[Cost], Exile this card from your graveyard: Put a number of +1/+1 counters equal to the power of the card you exiled on target creature. Activate only as a sorcery.'"

MAST models this as a fully decomposed `ActivatedAbility`:

Costs: the printed mana cost + an `ExileCost` (this card, from Graveyard, quantity 1). Effects: a `PutCountersEffect` placing +1/+1 counters on a target creature; the count is a `DerivedQuantity` keyed on `Power` of "the card you exiled". Restrictions: `OnlyAsSorcery`.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/ScavengeKeyword.cs)

Casting a spell for its spectacle cost follows the rules for paying alternative costs in rules 601.2b and 601.2f-h."

Decomposed to the shared `AlternativeCastEffect` primitive (`FromZone = Hand`): Spectacle is a cast-from-hand conditional alternative cost, matching the Surge/Freerunning family. The opponent-lost-life precondition is held in an `OtherCondition` residual (per the established Echo/Persist pattern).

The text-grafting machinery (reveal, copy instructions onto the target spell) is reminder text and is conventionally inferred from the rules — not modeled. [Source](Keywords/Definitions/SpreeKeyword.cs)

Two linked abilities. This combinator is the single-ability fallback path: it emits only the PRIMARY additional-cost static ability (the cost half). ### `SurgeKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Surge {cost} (CR 702.117a): "Surge [cost] means 'You may pay [cost] rather than pay this spell's mana cost as you cast this spell if you or one of your teammates has cast another spell this turn.'" It is a static ability, so the combinator emits a `StaticAbility` carrying the shared `AlternativeCastEffect` primitive (`FromZone = Hand`, `Cost =` the surge mana cost, `Condition =` the cast-history requirement as an `OtherCondition` residual). MAST records the keyword plus the printed N (time counters) and cost; the three sub-abilities are inferred from the rules and carried in the `Reminder`. Reuses the existing `SuspendEffect`. [Source](Keywords/Definitions/SwampwalkKeyword.cs)

### `TotemArmorKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Totem armor (oracle text: "Umbra armor" on some Auras): If enchanted creature would be destroyed, instead remove all damage from it and destroy this Aura. Rule 702.102. The comp-rules name is "totem armor"; MAST stores the keyword using that discriminator. The replacement-effect semantics are engine territory.

Multi-word simple keyword ("Totem" "armor"). The `Definition` is the verbatim former `KeywordDefinitions.TotemArmor`; the `Combinator` is the verbatim former `OracleParsers.TotemArmor`.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/TotemArmorKeyword.cs)

### `ToxicKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Toxic N: Whenever this creature deals combat damage to a player, that player gets N poison counters in addition to the damage. Rule 702.164. MAST records the keyword and its integer value; the poison-counter placement is engine territory.

Exemplar of the <b>integer-parameterized</b> keyword shape (Stage A template). The `Definition` is the verbatim former `KeywordDefinitions.Toxic` (including its inlined `ParseIntValue` guard); the `Combinator` is the verbatim former `OracleParsers.Toxic`.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/ToxicKeyword.cs)

### `TrainingKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Training: Whenever this creature and at least one other creature with power greater than this creature's power attack, put a +1/+1 counter on this creature. CR 702.149a (verbatim): "Training is a triggered ability. 'Training' means 'Whenever this creature and at least one other creature with power greater than this creature's power attack, put a +1/+1 counter on this creature.'" MAST shape (ADR 0003 decomposition): TriggeredAbility{ KeywordSource:"Training", Trigger:{ Timing:Whenever, Event:Attacks, Filter:{CardTypes:["creature"]} }, InterveningIf:OtherCondition{ Text:"this creature attacks with at least one other creature with greater power" }, Effects:[ PutCountersEffect{ Target:{Kind:Self}, CounterType:"+1/+1", Count:1 } ] }. "At least one other creature with greater power" is a relative/state predicate with no first-class ObjectFilter field; carried as OtherCondition residual per the ADR 0001 free-text doctrine.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/TrainingKeyword.cs)

### `TrampleKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Trample: If this creature would assign enough damage to its blockers to destroy them, you may have it assign the rest of its damage to the defending player or planeswalker. Rule 702.19. MAST records keyword presence; the excess-damage assignment is engine territory.

Simple parameterless keyword — combinator-only (no KeywordDefinition in the legacy registry). Tier: Simple.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/TrampleKeyword.cs)

### `TransmuteKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Transmute {cost}: {cost}, Discard this card: Search your library for a card with the same mana value as this card, reveal it, put it into your hand, then shuffle. Activate only as a sorcery. Rule 702.49. MAST records the keyword and its associated mana cost; the inner discard/search/reveal/shuffle structure is described by the rules and left to the engine. Mirrors `CyclingKeyword` — combinator-only, no legacy registry entry needed.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/TransmuteKeyword.cs)

### `TributeKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Tribute N: As this creature enters, an opponent of your choice may put N +1/+1 counters on it. If they don't, a triggered ability fires. Rule 702.102. MAST records the keyword and its integer value; the opponent-choice, counter-placement, and conditional ETB semantics are engine territory.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/TributeKeyword.cs)

### `TypecyclingKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

[Type]cycling [cost]: Discard this card: Search your library for a [Type] card, reveal it, put it into your hand, then shuffle. Rule 702.28 (and the 702.29 landcycling variant). Catch-all parser for the type-cycling family. Recognizes three printed shapes:

single-word land-type variants — Forestcycling, Swampcycling, Plainscycling, etc. (the land type is the prefix before "cycling"); two-word "Basic landcycling {cost}" — searches for a basic land card (Type = "Basic land"); bare "Landcycling {cost}" — searches for any land card (Type = "Land").

MAST records the keyword, the land type, and the cost; the inner search/reveal/shuffle structure is inferred from the rules. Lower priority (40) so specific cycling variants can outrank this catch-all in the registry Or-chain.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/TypecyclingKeyword.cs)

### `UmbraArmorKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Umbra armor (oracle text: "Umbra armor"): If enchanted creature would be destroyed, instead remove all damage from it and destroy this Aura. Rule 702.89. "Umbra armor" is the current Oracle name; "totem armor" is the obsolete prior name (CR 702.89: the older text was renamed). MAST records keyword presence using the discriminator that matches the card's current Oracle wording — `UmbraArmor` — distinct from the obsolete `TotemArmor` the literal "totem armor" combinator emits. The replacement-effect semantics are engine territory. Multi-word keyword; mirrors LivingWeapon.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/UmbraArmorKeyword.cs)

### `UndyingKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Undying (CR 702.93, verbatim): "Undying is a triggered ability. 'Undying' means 'When this permanent is put into a graveyard from the battlefield, if it had no +1/+1 counters on it, return it to the battlefield under its owner's control with a +1/+1 counter on it.'" MAST shape (ADR 0003 decomposition): TriggeredAbility{ KeywordSource:"Undying", Trigger:{ Timing:"When", Event:"Dies", Filter:{CardTypes:["creature"]} }, InterveningIf:{ ConditionType:"other", Text:"it had no +1/+1 counters on it" }, Effects:[ ReturnToBattlefieldEffect{ Target:{Kind:"It"}, UnderControl:{Kind:"Owner"}, WithCounters:{ CounterType:"+1/+1", Count:{QuantityType:"literal", Value:1} } }] }. Mirror of Persist (CR 702.78) with opposite counter polarity: Persist checks for no -1/-1 counters; Undying checks for no +1/+1 counters. The intervening-if "if it had no +1/+1 counters on it" is a CR 603.4 condition on the triggered ability, not an effect guard.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/UndyingKeyword.cs)

### `UnearthKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Unearth [cost]: Return this card from your graveyard to the battlefield. It gains haste. Exile it at the beginning of the next end step or if it would leave the battlefield. Unearth only as a sorcery. Rule 702.84. Scope: mana-cost parameter (all known printings). MAST records the keyword and its activation cost; the return, haste-grant, and exile-at-end-step semantics are engine territory.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/UnearthKeyword.cs)

### `UnleashKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Unleash: two static abilities per CR 702.98a.

CR 702.98a (verbatim): "Unleash is a keyword that represents two static abilities. 'Unleash' means 'You may have this permanent enter with an additional +1/+1 counter on it' and 'This permanent can't block as long as it has a +1/+1 counter on it.'"

Oracle-text parsing is handled by `UnleashStaticRule` (priority 1001), which returns both abilities as a list. This keyword file keeps the combinator live as a fallback but no longer emits the opaque `KeywordAbilityEffect{Unleash}` marker: it emits only the PRIMARY enters-with-counter static replacement ability ("You may have this permanent enter with an additional +1/+1 counter on it"). The `Definition` is null because `Expand` can only return a single `Ability` and Unleash decomposes into two.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/UnleashKeyword.cs)

### `VanishingKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Vanishing N: This permanent enters with N time counters on it. At the beginning of your upkeep, remove a time counter from it. When the last is removed, sacrifice it. Rule 702.63. MAST records the keyword and its integer value; the counter-removal upkeep trigger and last-counter sacrifice mechanics are engine territory. Mirrors Fading (702.32) but uses time counters and triggers on the last counter rather than inability to remove.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/VanishingKeyword.cs)

### `VigilanceKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Vigilance: Attacking doesn't cause this creature to tap. Rule 702.20.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/VigilanceKeyword.cs)

### `WardKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Ward {cost}: A triggered ability that counters spells or abilities targeting this permanent unless the opponent pays the stated cost. CR 702.21a (verbatim): "Ward is a triggered ability. Ward [cost] means 'Whenever this permanent becomes the target of a spell or ability an opponent controls, counter that spell or ability unless that player pays [cost].'" MAST shape (ADR 0003 decomposition): TriggeredAbility{ KeywordSource:"Ward", Trigger:{ Timing:"Whenever", Event:"BecomesTarget", Filter:{Controller:"Opponent"} }, Effects:[ PreventableEffect{ Inner:CounterSpellEffect{ Target:{Kind:"It"} }, Unless:{ Player:{Kind:"ThatPlayer"}, Cost:ManaCost } } ] }.

This combinator handles only the mana-cost form of Ward (e.g. "Ward {2}", "Ward {1}{G}"). The life-cost and sacrifice-cost forms are handled by the existing `WardKeywordRule` static rule (Priority 989), which is reached for standalone clauses when the KeywordListRule does not consume the full clause. Both produce the same TriggeredAbility shape.

MAST Priority: `Parameterized` — must be tried after all Simple keywords in the Or-chain so a bare keyword word "ward" doesn't mask the parameterized "ward {cost}" form (KeywordRegistry builds Simple.Try().Or(Parameterized)).

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/WardKeyword.cs)

### `WarpKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Warp [cost]: You may cast this card from your hand for its warp cost. It enters the battlefield tapped. Rule 702.185. An alternative-cast keyword. MAST records the keyword and the warp cost; the alternative-cast and enters-tapped mechanics are engine territory — same approach as Plot (702.170) and Dash (702.109).

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/WarpKeyword.cs)

### `WebSlingingKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Web-slinging [cost]: You may cast this spell by paying [cost] and returning a tapped creature you control to its owner's hand rather than paying its mana cost. Rule 702.188. Scope: mana-cost parameter. The return-a-tapped-creature component of the alt cost is engine territory — MAST records the keyword's presence and cost only, mirroring the Cycling/Evoke pattern. Combinator-only: no matching `KeywordDefinitions` entry exists in the legacy registry.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/WebSlingingKeyword.cs)

### `WitherKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Wither: This creature deals damage to creatures in the form of -1/-1 counters. Rule 702.80. MAST records keyword presence; the damage-redirection semantics are engine territory.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/WitherKeyword.cs)

## `/Parsing/`

### `IAbilityParser` — *interface*

Contract for an ability-kind-specific parser. Each implementation handles one `AbilityKind` and is discovered at startup by `AbilityParserRegistry` via an `OracleAbilityParserAttribute` decoration. Implementations own their own fallback behavior — `Parse` must always return at least one ability node (typically an `UnparsedAbility` on failure) so the orchestrator never needs to special-case missing output.

[Source](Parsing/IAbilityParser.cs)

### `AbilityClassifier` — *sealed class*

Classifies oracle text clauses into ability types based on structural patterns. This pre-classification enables routing to specialized parsers.

[Source](Parsing/AbilityClassifier.cs)

### `AbilityParserRegistry` — *sealed class*

Discovers `IAbilityParser` implementations decorated with `OracleAbilityParserAttribute` and maps each `AbilityKind` to its parser. Discovery runs once on first access and is cached for the process lifetime. Adding a new ability-kind parser means creating a new file with the attribute and the interface implementation; no edits to this registry, to `OracleParser`, or to any other existing file are required. Kinds with no registered parser (today: `Modal`, `Spell`, and the fallback path) resolve to a built-in fallback that produces a generic `UnparsedAbility`.

[Source](Parsing/AbilityParserRegistry.cs)

### `AttributeExtractor` — *sealed partial class*

Extracts card attributes from a CardInputDTO. Handles mana costs, colors, color identity, creature stats, and loyalty.

[Source](Parsing/AttributeExtractor.cs)

### `CardParseMetrics` — *sealed record*

Metrics for card parsing.

[Source](Parsing/CardParser.cs)

### `CardParseResult` — *sealed record*

Result of parsing a complete card.

[Source](Parsing/CardParser.cs)

### `CardParser` — *sealed class*

Main entry point for parsing Magic: The Gathering cards. Orchestrates type line parsing, oracle text parsing, and attribute extraction.

[Source](Parsing/CardParser.cs)

### `ClauseClassification` — *sealed record*

Classification result for an oracle clause.

[Source](Parsing/AbilityClassifier.cs)

### `ClauseSplitter` — *sealed class*

Splits oracle text into individual ability clauses. Handles paragraph breaks, modal structures, level/chapter markers, and loyalty abilities.

[Source](Parsing/ClauseSplitter.cs)

### `LevelStanzaClause` — *sealed record*

One pre-grouped LEVEL N-M stanza from a level-up cluster. Carries the raw P/T strings and the inner-ability sub-clauses; the parser converts these into `LevelStanza` AST nodes.

[Source](Parsing/ClauseSplitter.cs)

### `ManaCostParser` — *sealed partial class*

Parses mana cost strings into a list of ManaSymbol objects. Handles all standard MTG mana symbol formats: - Basic: {W}, {U}, {B}, {R}, {G} - Colorless: {C} - Generic: {0}, {1}, {2}, ..., {16}, etc. - Variable: {X}, {Y}, {Z} - Hybrid: {W/U}, {B/G}, etc. - Hybrid generic: {2/W}, {2/U}, etc. - Phyrexian: {W/P}, {U/P}, {B/P}, {R/P}, {G/P} - Phyrexian hybrid: {W/U/P}, {G/W/P}, etc. - Snow: {S}

[Source](Parsing/ManaCostParser.cs)

### `OracleAbilityParserAttribute` — *sealed class* : Attribute

Marks a class as the parser for a specific `AbilityKind`. Discovered at startup by `AbilityParserRegistry`. The tagged class must implement `IAbilityParser` and have a public parameterless constructor.

[Source](Parsing/OracleAbilityParserAttribute.cs)

### `OracleClause` — *sealed record*

Represents a clause (ability segment) extracted from oracle text. Each clause typically represents a single ability.

[Source](Parsing/ClauseSplitter.cs)

### `OracleParser` — *sealed class*

Main orchestrator for parsing Magic: The Gathering oracle text. Coordinates the tokenizer, clause splitter, classifier, and the reflection-discovered registry of `IAbilityParser` implementations.

[Source](Parsing/OracleParser.cs)

### `TypeLineParser` — *sealed class*

Parses a card's type line into its structural components. Type lines follow the format: "[Supertypes] [Types] — [Subtypes]" Examples: - "Creature — Elf Druid" → types: [Creature], subtypes: [Elf, Druid] - "Legendary Creature — Squirrel Warrior" → supertypes: [Legendary], types: [Creature], subtypes: [Squirrel, Warrior] - "Artifact" → types: [Artifact] - "Basic Land — Forest" → supertypes: [Basic], types: [Land], subtypes: [Forest] - "Legendary Planeswalker — Daretti" → supertypes: [Legendary], types: [Planeswalker], subtypes: [Daretti]

[Source](Parsing/TypeLineParser.cs)

## `/Parsing/Combinators/`

### `OracleParsers` — *static class*

Keyword-ability parser combinators for Magic: The Gathering oracle text.

As of Phase-2 Stage C this type is a thin facade over `KeywordRegistry`: the per-keyword combinators and the `SimpleKeyword` / `ParameterizedKeyword` Or-chains that used to live here have moved to one-file-per-keyword `IKeyword` implementations under `Keywords/Definitions/`, discovered by the registry. The two members below preserve the public call shape (`OracleParsers.KeywordList(tokens)` from `StaticAbilityParser`) while delegating the actual recognition to the registry.

[Source](Parsing/Combinators/OracleParsers.cs)

## `/Parsing/Parsers/`

### `IPrioritizedRuleAttribute` — *interface*

Contract for an attribute that marks a reflection-discovered rule and carries its intra-tier dispatch priority. Shared by every registry-style parser family (spell rules, triggered rules, keywords, …) so a single generic discovery helper (`RuleRegistry`) can rank decorated types without knowing the concrete attribute.

<b>Priority convention (range 0–100, default 50, higher = tried first):</b> higher-priority rules are dispatched before lower-priority siblings. Each rule family documents its own band convention on its concrete attribute; the shared invariant is only that a larger number wins ties by sorting earlier.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/IPrioritizedRuleAttribute.cs)

### `ActivatedAbilityParser` — *sealed partial class* : IAbilityParser

Parses activated abilities of the form "[Cost]: [Effect.]" Handles: - Mana abilities: {T}: Add {G}. - Complex activated abilities: {3}{B}{B}: Creatures you control gain lifelink until end of turn. - Loyalty abilities: +2: Discard up to two cards, then draw that many cards.

- **Ability-parser registration, kind:** `AbilityKind.Activated`

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/ActivatedAbilityParser.cs)

### `FallbackParser` — *sealed class* : IAbilityParser

Fallback parser that always succeeds by producing an UnparsedAbility. This ensures the parsing pipeline never fails completely. Registered for `Unparsed` in the `AbilityParserRegistry`. Also invoked internally by the other concrete parsers when their `TryParse` step fails.

- **Ability-parser registration, kind:** `AbilityKind.Unparsed`

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/FallbackParser.cs)

### `LevelUpAbilityParser` — *sealed class* : IAbilityParser

Parses Level Up clusters (Rule 711). Consumes a level-up cost clause (one that `ClauseSplitter` has pre-grouped with its LEVEL stanzas on `LevelUpStanzas`), then constructs the cost activated ability + the stanza records.

- **Ability-parser registration, kind:** `AbilityKind.LevelUp`

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/LevelUpAbilityParser.cs)

### `ModalAbilityParser` — *sealed class* : IAbilityParser

Parser for modal abilities ("Choose one —", "Choose two —", "Choose one or both —"). The clause splitter attaches each option as a child `OracleClause` on `ModalOptions`; this parser interprets the header text to derive a `ModeSelection` and dispatches each option clause back through the classifier+registry to parse the option body.

Also handles the commander-conditional mode-expansion pattern: "Choose one. If you control a commander as you cast this spell, you may choose both instead." — a `ModeSelection` with base `ChooseOne` and a `ModeSelectionOverride` whose condition is a `CountCondition` filtering Commander-supertype creatures you control. CR 903 (Commander format), MAST describes the condition as written.

- **Ability-parser registration, kind:** `AbilityKind.Modal`

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/ModalAbilityParser.cs)

### `RuleRegistry` — *static class*

Generic reflection-based rule discovery, shared by every ability parser that dispatches over a priority-ordered set of one-file-per-rule implementations. Each parser is a thin dispatcher over `Discover{TRule, TAttr}`; adding a new shape is dropping a new `[TAttr]`-decorated `TRule` file with no edits to any shared file — which is what keeps the rule directories free of merge conflicts under parallel batch dispatch.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/RuleRegistry.cs)

### `SagaAbilityParser` — *sealed class* : IAbilityParser

Parses Saga chapter structures (Rule 715). Consumes a saga-preamble clause (one that `ClauseSplitter` has pre-grouped with its chapter bodies on `SagaChapters`), then dispatches each chapter body through the registry so individual chapters land as whatever ability shape best matches their text.

- **Ability-parser registration, kind:** `AbilityKind.Saga`

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/SagaAbilityParser.cs)

### `SpellAbilityParser` — *sealed class* : IAbilityParser

Dispatches spell-ability oracle-text clauses to the priority-ordered set of `ISpellRule` implementations discovered by reflection at construction time. Each rule lives in its own file under `Parsers/Spell/Rules/` and is decorated with `SpellRuleAttribute` (see attribute docs for priority convention). Adding a new shape means dropping a new file in `Rules/` with no edits to any shared file.

- **Ability-parser registration, kind:** `AbilityKind.Spell`

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/SpellAbilityParser.cs)

### `StaticAbilityParser` — *sealed class* : IAbilityParser

Dispatches static-ability oracle-text clauses to the priority-ordered set of `IStaticRule` implementations discovered by reflection at construction time. Each rule lives in its own file under `Parsers/Static/Rules/` and is decorated with `StaticRuleAttribute` (see attribute docs for the order-preserving priority convention). Adding a new shape means dropping a new file in `Rules/` with no edits to any shared file.

- **Ability-parser registration, kind:** `AbilityKind.Static`

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/StaticAbilityParser.cs)

### `TriggeredAbilityParser` — *sealed class* : IAbilityParser

Parser for triggered abilities: "When/Whenever/At [trigger], [effect]" Supports multiple trigger events and effect types using compositional parsing.

- **Ability-parser registration, kind:** `AbilityKind.Triggered`

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/TriggeredAbilityParser.cs)

## `/Parsing/Parsers/Activated/`

### `IActivatedCostRule` — *interface*

One activated-ability cost-component recognizer. Dispatched by `ActivatedAbilityParser` in descending `Priority` order over each comma-separated cost component; the first non-null result wins. Each rule self-guards and returns `null` when it doesn't recognise the component.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/IActivatedCostRule.cs)

### `IActivatedEffectRule` — *interface*

One activated-ability effect recognizer. Dispatched by `ActivatedAbilityParser` in descending `Priority` order, then ordinal name for tie-breaking; the first non-null result wins. `effectText` is the post-colon effect fragment (a single sentence when reached via the multi-sentence pre-pass). Each rule self-guards and returns `null` when it doesn't recognise the text.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/IActivatedEffectRule.cs)

### `IMultiActivatedEffectRule` — *interface*

Extension of `IActivatedEffectRule` for activated-ability effect shapes that expand to a flat list of sibling effects on `Effects` rather than a single `Effect`. Implement this alongside `IActivatedEffectRule` (returning `null` from `TryMatch` so the single-effect path never fires) when a single oracle sentence joins two effects with ", then" and should be represented as a flat array — e.g. Sensei's Divining Top's "Draw a card, then put this artifact on top of its owner's library."

This mirrors `IMultiSpellRule`: not every ", then" is a join (some are a single effect's disposition, such as "look at the top three cards, then put them back in any order"), so the decision belongs to the rule that recognizes the whole sentence, not a generic dispatcher split.

Discovery: `ActivatedAbilityParser` picks this up automatically for any `ActivatedEffectRuleAttribute`-decorated class that also implements this interface, trying the multi-effect path before the single-effect path.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/IMultiActivatedEffectRule.cs)

### `ActivatedCostRuleAttribute` — *sealed class* : Attribute, MagicAST.Parsing.Parsers.IPrioritizedRuleAttribute

Marks an `IActivatedCostRule` implementation for reflection-based discovery by `ActivatedAbilityParser`. Priorities are migrated order-preserving from the legacy `ParseCosts` chain as `Priority = 1000 - (chain index)`. See `IPrioritizedRuleAttribute` for the shared priority contract (higher = more specific = tried first).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/ActivatedCostRuleAttribute.cs)

### `ActivatedEffectRuleAttribute` — *sealed class* : Attribute, MagicAST.Parsing.Parsers.IPrioritizedRuleAttribute

Marks an `IActivatedEffectRule` implementation for reflection-based discovery by `ActivatedAbilityParser`. Priorities are migrated order-preserving from the legacy `ParseEffects` chain as `Priority = 1000 - (chain index)`, so an extracted rule keeps the exact relative dispatch order it had as an inline branch. See `IPrioritizedRuleAttribute` for the shared priority contract (higher = more specific = tried first).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/ActivatedEffectRuleAttribute.cs)

### `ActivatedRuleHelpers` — *static class*

Shared utilities used across `IActivatedEffectRule` and `IActivatedCostRule` implementations.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/ActivatedRuleHelpers.cs)

## `/Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/`

### `ActivateLoyaltyAbilityAsThoughEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"For each planeswalker you control, you may activate one of its loyalty abilities once this turn as though none of its loyalty abilities have been activated this turn."

CR 606.3: the once-per-turn restriction on loyalty-ability activation. This activated-effect rule recognises the Chain Veil family: a per-planeswalker permission to activate a loyalty ability once this turn, ignoring the once-per-turn restriction (the "as though none … have been activated" clause).

ANCHORED (`^…$`): the surface phrase "loyalty abilities" could appear inside other ability text; anchoring prevents this rule from matching a substring of a broader effect. Priority 985 — specific enough to precede generic effects.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ActivateLoyaltyAbilityAsThoughEffectRule.cs)

### `AdaptEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Adapt N" — e.g. "Adapt 2" (CR 701.46a: "\"Adapt N\" means \"If this permanent has no +1/+1 counters on it, put N +1/+1 counters on it.\"") The trailing parenthetical reminder is stripped by the caller before this rule is invoked.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/AdaptEffectRule.cs)

### `AddManaAnyColorAmongEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Add one mana of any color among [filter] you control." — the Mox Amber shape.

This rule handles the "one mana of any color among [filter]" family where the controller may add one mana of freely-chosen color, but that color must be present among a specific set of permanents they control. The canonical example is Mox Amber: "Add one mana of any color among legendary creatures and planeswalkers you control."

Distinct from `AddManaForEachColorAmongControlledPermanentsEffectRule` (Bloom Tender — adds one mana per EACH distinct color present) and from `AddManaEffectRule`'s "one mana of any color" branch (unconstrained — any of the five colors). Here the color is freely chosen but constrained to those present among the filter'd permanents.

CR 605.1a: "An activated ability is a mana ability if it meets all of the following criteria: it doesn't require a target (see rule 115.6), it could add mana to a player's mana pool when it resolves, and it's not a loyalty ability." The "legendary creatures and planeswalkers you control" filter is not a target (CR 115.6); the ability satisfies all three criteria.

Runs at Priority = 1040 — above `AddManaForEachColorAmongControlledPermanentsEffectRule` (1030) and `AddManaEffectRule` (1000) so the "any color among" shape wins before the per-color loop or the unconstrained "any color" match. The regex is anchored at both ends (^…$) so it cannot match as a substring of a more-specific sibling.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/AddManaAnyColorAmongEffectRule.cs)

### `AddManaEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Add {mana}" — e.g. "Add {G}", "Add {C}{C}{C}", "Add {W}{U}{B}{R}{G}". Also handles "Add one mana of any color" (Crystal Grotto / Chromatic Lantern shape) where the produced mana is a single choice across all five colors, and "Add one mana of the chosen color" (Coldsteel Heart / Shimmerdrift Vale / Thriving lands) where the produced mana's color is the color chosen as this permanent entered — the consumer side of a CR 607 linked "choose a color".

Per CR 605.1a — "An activated ability is a mana ability if it meets all of the following criteria: it doesn't require a target (see rule 115.6), it could add mana to a player's mana pool when it resolves, and it's not a loyalty ability." — the enclosing "{T}: Add ..." ability is a mana ability; the chosen-color back- reference does not introduce a target.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/AddManaEffectRule.cs)

### `AddManaForEachColorAmongControlledPermanentsEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

S10 — "For each color among permanents you control, add one mana of that color." (Bloom Tender / Vivid ability-word shape, Eventide).

The effect produces one mana of each distinct color present among the controller's permanents on the battlefield — a per-color loop rather than a fixed symbol or a "choose one" free pick. This is modelled with `ForEachColorAmong` carrying an `ObjectFilter` for controller-scoped permanents, and `Mana` left as `""` because the colors are determined at resolution. The outer ability carries `IsManaAbility = true` (CR 605.1a: no target, could add mana, not loyalty).

CR 207.2c: "Vivid" is an ability word — it appears in italics before the em-dash and has no special rules meaning. The classifier strips the "Vivid — " prefix so this rule receives only the post-colon effect text.

CR 605.1a: "An activated ability is a mana ability if it meets all of the following criteria: it doesn't require a target (see rule 115.6), it could add mana to a player's mana pool when it resolves, and it's not a loyalty ability." The "permanents you control" filter is not a target (CR 115.6); the ability satisfies all three criteria.

Runs at Priority = 1030 — above `AddManaForEachControlledCreatureEffectRule` (1020) and `AddManaForEachControlledSubtypeEffectRule` (1010) so the color-loop shape wins before any "Add {mana} for each …" shape in `AddManaEffectRule`. The regex is anchored at both ends (^…$) so it cannot match as a substring of a more-specific sibling.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/AddManaForEachColorAmongControlledPermanentsEffectRule.cs)

### `AddManaForEachControlledCreatureEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

S8 — "Add {mana} for each creature you control." (Circle of Dreams Druid and similar). Counts every creature the ability's controller controls on the battlefield — no subtype restriction, card-type filter only.

CR 605.1a: "An activated ability is a mana ability if it meets all of the following criteria: it doesn't require a target (see rule 115.6), it could add mana to a player's mana pool when it resolves, and it's not a loyalty ability." — the enclosing "{T}: Add … you control" ability is a mana ability; the controller filter does not introduce a target.

CR 122/quantity 'for each': the count is a `CountQuantity` over an `ObjectFilter` with `CardTypes=["creature"]`, `Controller=`You``, `Zone=`Battlefield``.

Runs at Priority = 1020 (higher than `AddManaForEachControlledSubtypeEffectRule`'s 1010) so the card-type "creature" shape is tried before the subtype match — the regex is anchored at both ends so there is no substring-overlap risk with the subtype rule.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/AddManaForEachControlledCreatureEffectRule.cs)

### `AddManaForEachControlledSubtypeEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

S7 — "{mana} for each [Subtype] you control" (Elvish Archdruid and similar). Counts only permanents of the named creature subtype controlled by the ability's controller — a controller-scoped variant of S6 (Priest of Titania's "on the battlefield", which counts ALL permanents of the subtype regardless of controller).

CR 605.1a: "An activated ability is a mana ability if it meets all of the following criteria: it doesn't require a target (see rule 115.6), it could add mana to a player's mana pool when it resolves, and it's not a loyalty ability." — the enclosing "{T}: Add … you control" ability is a mana ability; the controller filter does not introduce a target.

Runs at Priority = 1010 (higher than `AddManaEffectRule`'s 1000) so the more-specific "you control" shape wins before the general bail on "for each" in `AddManaEffectRule` is reached.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/AddManaForEachControlledSubtypeEffectRule.cs)

### `AddManaXCountDefenderCreaturesEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Add X mana in any combination of colors, where X is the number of creatures you control with defender." — Axebane Guardian's mana ability.

The X is NOT a cost variable (CR 107.3): it is defined inline by the "where X is …" clause as the count of defender creatures the controller controls. MAST records this as a `CountQuantity` over an `ObjectFilter` with `CardTypes=["creature"]`, `Controller=`You``, `Zone=`Battlefield``, and `Characteristics=[KeywordCharacteristic{Defender}]`. Reference-not-resolution (ADR 0004): MAST names the quantity; the engine evaluates it at resolution time.

The mana produced is X units, freely distributed across any combination of the five colors (`AnyCombinationOf` = `["W","U","B","R","G"]`). Per CR 106.4: "When an effect instructs a player to add mana, that mana goes into a player's mana pool."

Defender is a static ability (CR 702.3a: "Defender is a static ability."; CR 702.3b: "A creature with defender can't attack."). A creature with defender in the filter is expressed as `Characteristics=[KeywordCharacteristic{Defender}]` — the structured `KeywordCharacteristic` rather than an `OtherCharacteristic` residual, because Defender is a first-class `KeywordAbility` enum value.

Per CR 605.1a — "An activated ability is a mana ability if it meets all of the following criteria: it doesn't require a target (see rule 115.6), it could add mana to a player's mana pool when it resolves, and it's not a loyalty ability." — the enclosing `{T}:` ability IS a mana ability; the controller filter does not introduce a target.

Runs at Priority = 1003 (above `AddManaXGreatestPowerEffectRule`'s 1002 and `AddManaEffectRule`'s 1000) so the Axebane Guardian "where X is the number of creatures … with defender" shape is claimed before any more-general rule in the family.

The rule is anchored (`^…$`) to prevent a substring match against a more-specific sibling.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/AddManaXCountDefenderCreaturesEffectRule.cs)

### `AddManaXGreatestPowerEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Add X mana in any combination of colors, where X is the greatest power among creatures you control." — Selvala, Heart of the Wilds mana ability.

The X here is NOT a cost variable (CR 107.3): it is defined inline by the "where X is …" clause as the current greatest power among creatures the controller controls. MAST records this as a `CalculatedQuantity` with `Expression` = `"greatest power among creatures you control"` — a game-state query (the maximum over a set of power values) with no structured analog in the existing quantity hierarchy. Reference-not-resolution (ADR 0004): MAST names the quantity; the engine evaluates it.

The mana produced is X units, freely distributed across any combination of the five colors (`AnyCombinationOf` = `["W","U","B","R","G"]`). Per CR 106.4: "When an effect instructs a player to add mana, that mana goes into a player's mana pool."

Per CR 605.1a — "An activated ability is a mana ability if it meets all of the following criteria: it doesn't require a target, it could add mana to a player's mana pool when it resolves, and it's not a loyalty ability." — the enclosing `{G}, {T}:` ability IS a mana ability; the enclosing parser derives `IsManaAbility = true`.

Runs at Priority = 1002 (above `AddManaXOnePlusManaValueEffectRule`'s 1001 and `AddManaEffectRule`'s 1000) so the Selvala "where X is …" shape is claimed before the base rule's `UnmodeledManaClause` bail (or the S3 `AnyCombination` regex's partial match of the "X mana in any combination of colors" prefix).

The rule is anchored (`^…$`) to prevent a substring match against a more-specific sibling.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/AddManaXGreatestPowerEffectRule.cs)

### `AddManaXOnePlusManaValueEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Add X mana of any one color, where X is 1 plus the exiled creature's mana value. Spend this mana only to cast creature spells." — Food Chain's exile-creature mana production with a spend restriction.

The X quantity is a derived calculation: 1 plus the exiled creature's mana value. MAST models this as a `CalculatedQuantity` whose `BaseQuantity` is a `DerivedQuantity` keyed on `ManaValue` with `Source = "exiled creature"` (reference-not-resolution per ADR 0004 — MAST names the source object, it does not thread the runtime value). `Operation = "add"`, `Operand = 1`.

The spend restriction ("Spend this mana only to cast creature spells") is captured verbatim in `SpendRestriction` per the existing MAST doctrine: MAST describes, does not execute; the restriction text is the engine's domain, not the AST's.

Per CR 605.1a — "An activated ability is a mana ability if it meets all of the following criteria: it doesn't require a target, it could add mana to a player's mana pool when it resolves, and it's not a loyalty ability." — this ability is a mana ability despite its spend restriction (a spend restriction does not make an ability non-mana; cf. Unclaimed Territory, Secluded Courtyard).

This rule fires at Priority 1001 (above the existing `AddManaEffectRule`'s 1000) so it claims the Food Chain combined string before the base rule's `UnmodeledManaClause` bail would swallow it.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/AddManaXOnePlusManaValueEffectRule.cs)

### `AdditionalCombatAndMainPhaseEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"After this main phase, there is an additional combat phase followed by an additional main phase." — inserts a combat phase and main phase into the current turn's timeline.

CR 500.7 (verbatim): "Some spells and abilities can add phases to a player's turn. They do this by adding the phases directly after the specified phase. If two effects add phases to a player's turn, the most recently created one is added first."

Emits an `AdditionalCombatAndMainPhaseEffect` (a parameterless marker — the insertion point "after this main phase" and the specific phases inserted are fixed by the oracle text and need no parameters, just as SkipUntapEffect is parameterless).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/AdditionalCombatAndMainPhaseEffectRule.cs)

### `AdditionalCombatPhaseEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"After this phase, there is an additional combat phase." — inserts a single additional combat phase into the current turn without an accompanying main phase.

Distinct from `AdditionalCombatAndMainPhaseEffectRule`, which handles "After this main phase, there is an additional combat phase followed by an additional main phase." (Aggravated Assault). Godo, Bandit Warlord's trigger says only "After this phase, there is an additional combat phase." — no additional main phase, and "this phase" rather than "this main phase".

CR 506: The combat phase. CR 500.1: turn structure. The insertion point ("after this phase") and the single inserted phase (combat) are the full printed content; engine phase-order bookkeeping (CR 505.1a) is not encoded here.

Priority 990 — above `AdditionalCombatAndMainPhaseEffectRule` (Priority 991) so the more-specific single-phase form is tried first. Both are anchored (^...$), so they cannot cross-match.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/AdditionalCombatPhaseEffectRule.cs)

### `AnimateTargetNoncreatureArtifactByManaValueRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Until your next turn, up to one target noncreature artifact becomes an artifact creature with power and toughness each equal to its mana value." — Karn, the Great Creator's +1 loyalty ability. A duration-bounded continuous effect (CR 611.1) that makes the chosen artifact into an artifact creature whose P/T box equals its own mana value (CR 202.3 / CR 208).

Modelled as a `BecomesCreatureEffect` with:

`Subject` — `Target` reference to "up to one … noncreature artifact" (quantity `UpTo 1`, CardTypes ["artifact"], ExcludedCardTypes ["creature"]). `Power` / `Toughness` — `DerivedQuantity` (`ManaValue`) for "each equal to its mana value". `Colors` — empty (no color change stated). `CardTypes` — `["artifact","creature"]` (additive, per CR 205.1b). `AddedSubtypes` — empty (no creature subtype stated). `GainedAbilities` — empty (no keyword granted). `Duration` — `YourNextTurn`.

"Up to one" is modelled as `Quantity = UpToQuantity { Maximum = 1 }` on the `ObjectReference`, not an `OptionalEffect` wrapper (CR 117.3a: "up to X" targets means 0–X are chosen at targeting time, which is a cardinality constraint, not an optionality on the one-shot action).

The pattern is anchored (^…$) to prevent substring collision with other "becomes … creature" animate rules.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/AnimateTargetNoncreatureArtifactByManaValueRule.cs)

### `AnyNumberOfTargetPlayersMillEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

Activated-ability mill keyword action targeting any number of players. CR 701.17a: "For a player to mill a number of cards, that player puts that many cards from the top of their library into their graveyard." Handles two oracle forms: "Any number of target players each mill N cards." → Count: LiteralQuantity(N), Player: Target+player+AnyAmount "Any number of target players each mill cards equal to the number of cards in their graveyard." → Count: CountQuantity(cards in target player's graveyard), Player: Target+player+AnyAmount ANCHOR: Both patterns are anchored (^...$) to prevent matching substrings of more-specific sibling effects. For the singular "Target player mills N cards" form see `MillTargetPlayerEffectRule`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/AnyNumberOfTargetPlayersMillEffectRule.cs)

### `BecomeMonarchEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Target player becomes the monarch." — Rule 716 monarch designation granted to a chosen player.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/BecomeMonarchEffectRule.cs)

### `BecomesChosenCreatureTypeEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"This [permanent] becomes the creature type of your choice [until end of turn]." — the Mistform "shapeshift" template. Emits a single `ChangeSubtypeEffect` whose new subtype is player-chosen (`CreatureType`) rather than literal.

This is one layer-4 type-changing continuous effect (CR 613.1d: "Layer 4: Type-changing effects are applied. These include effects that change an object's card type, subtype, and/or supertype."). It <em>sets</em> the creature's creature type, replacing its existing creature types (CR 205.1a: "when an effect sets one or more of an object's subtypes, the new subtype(s) replaces any existing subtypes from the appropriate set (creature types, land types, …)"). The chosen value must be a single creature type per CR 205.3 ("'Merfolk' or 'Wizard' is acceptable, but 'Merfolk Wizard' is not. Words like 'artifact,' 'opponent,' 'Swamp,' or 'truck' can't be chosen because they aren't creature types.").

The permanent stays a creature and only its creature type changes, so this is a subtype-set effect and not a `BecomesCreatureEffect` (which adds the creature card type). The "of your choice" is a fresh on-resolution choice, so it is modeled on the effect itself, not via the back-referencing `ChosenCharacteristic` filter.

Examples:

Mistform Stalker — "{1}: This creature becomes the creature type of your choice until end of turn." Mistform Dreamer — same activated line.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/BecomesChosenCreatureTypeEffectRule.cs)

### `BecomesCopyEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"This [permanent] becomes a copy of target [filter] until end of turn." — the Shifting Woodland delirium template. Emits a single `BecomesCopyEffect` describing the source permanent becoming a copy of the targeted object.

This is a layer-1 copy effect (CR 707.6: "Some effects cause a permanent that's copying a permanent to copy a different object while remaining on the battlefield") — the source stays on the battlefield and takes on the copiable values of the target. Distinct from `CreateCopyTokenWithModificationsEffectRule`, which <em>creates a new token</em> that is a copy.

CR 207.2c (verbatim): "Delirium" is listed as an ability word — the em-dash prefix is mechanically inert and is stripped by `ActivatedAbilityParser` before this rule fires. The `AbilityWord` field on the emitted `ActivatedAbility` is populated by the classifier, not here.

Anchored (^…$) to prevent matching inside a more-specific sibling such as `BecomesCreatureEffectRule` — the surface phrase "becomes a copy of" cannot appear inside a "becomes a [P/T] … creature" animate spec. The guard is belt-and-suspenders: the two patterns are structurally disjoint ("copy of target" vs. a P/T box), but explicit anchoring is the project standard.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/BecomesCopyEffectRule.cs)

### `BecomesCreatureEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"This [permanent] becomes a [P/T] [colors] [subtype] [card types] creature with [keyword] until end of turn." — the Keyrune/Monument "animate" template. Emits a single `BecomesCreatureEffect` describing the full set of characteristics the source permanent takes on for the duration.

There is no keyword <em>action</em> for "becomes a creature"; this is one continuous effect (CR 611.1 — "A continuous effect modifies characteristics of objects ... for a fixed or indefinite period.") that sets card types/subtypes (CR 205), power/toughness (CR 208), colors (CR 105), and grants a keyword ability (CR 113.6). Layer/timestamp ordering (CR 613) is engine territory and is not modeled.

<b>Word order.</b> The Keyrune/Monument form puts the duration LAST ("...with flying until end of turn"). The "manland" animate (Stirring Wildwood, Restless Vents) puts it FIRST ("Until end of turn, this land becomes a 3/4 ... creature with reach"). Both are the same continuous effect; the rule accepts either word order and normalizes to `Duration: untilEndOfTurn`.
