<b>"It's still a [type]" retention.</b> Per CR 205.1b — "Some effects change an object's card type, supertype, or subtype but specify that the object retains a prior card type ... This rule applies to effects that ... state that something is 'still a [type...]'" — the animate is ADDITIVE: the source keeps its prior card type and gains `creature`. CR 305.7 says setting/animating a land "doesn't add or remove any card types ... it keeps its land types". So for the manlands the retained `land` card type sits ahead of the added `creature` in `CardTypes`. The retained type is derived from the subject noun ("this <b>land</b> becomes..."); the trailing reminder sentence "It's still a land." is consumed as confirmation, NOT emitted as a separate effect.

Examples:

Azorius Keyrune — "This artifact becomes a 2/2 white and blue Bird artifact creature with flying until end of turn." Gruul Keyrune — "This artifact becomes a 3/2 red and green Beast artifact creature with trample until end of turn." Stirring Wildwood — "Until end of turn, this land becomes a 3/4 green and white Elemental creature with reach. It's still a land." → CardTypes ["land","creature"]. Restless Vents — "Until end of turn, this land becomes a 2/3 black and red Insect creature with menace. It's still a land."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/BecomesCreatureEffectRule.cs)

### `BecomesTreasureArtifactEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule, IMultiActivatedEffectRule

"Target creature becomes a Treasure artifact with &quot;{T}, Sacrifice this artifact: Add one mana of any color&quot; and loses all other card types and abilities." — Vraska, Betrayal's Sting's −2 loyalty ability.

A single continuous-effect sentence that applies four simultaneous layer-4 changes (CR 613.1d) and a layer-6 ability grant (CR 613.1f) to the target creature:

`SetCardTypesEffect` — sets the target's card types to `["artifact"]`, implementing "becomes … artifact … and loses all other card types". `ChangeSubtypeEffect` — sets the target's artifact subtypes to `["Treasure"]`, implementing "becomes a Treasure …". `GainAbilityEffect` — grants the Treasure mana ability `{T}, Sacrifice this artifact: Add one mana of any color` as a nested `ActivatedAbility`. `LoseAbilityEffect` — the "loses all other … abilities" clause, carrying the AbilityText residual `"all other abilities"`.

ANCHORED (`^…$`): the phrase "becomes a Treasure artifact" does not recur as a substring of any other sibling rule, but anchoring prevents future broad patterns from consuming it silently.

Implemented as `IMultiActivatedEffectRule` so the four effects sit as flat siblings on `Effects`. `TryMatch` always returns null.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/BecomesTreasureArtifactEffectRule.cs)

### `CantBeBlockedThisTurnEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"This creature can't be blocked this turn." / "Target creature can't be blocked this turn." — activated ability effect that grants full unblockability until end of turn. CR 509.1b: "The defending player checks each creature they control to see whether it's affected by any restrictions (effects that say a creature can't block, or that it can't block unless some condition is met)." A "can't be blocked" effect is a blocking restriction on the attacker. Maps to `CantBeBlockedEffect` with:

`Target` = `Self` for the "This creature" form — the creature that pays the cost is the one that becomes unblockable; or a `Target` creature reference for the "Target creature" form (Whirler Rogue, CR 115.1). `Duration` = `untilEndOfTurn` — oracle phrase "this turn" maps to end-of-turn expiry. No `BlockedByFilter` — the restriction is unconditional.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/CantBeBlockedThisTurnEffectRule.cs)

### `ChangeColorEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Target creature becomes [color] until end of turn." — layer-5 color-changing continuous effect (CR 105.3). Recognizes the standard activated-ability pattern on a target creature.

CR 105.3 (verbatim): "Effects may change an object's color or give a color to a colorless object. If an effect gives an object a new color, the new color replaces all previous colors the object had (unless the effect said the object became that color 'in addition' to its other colors)."

Supported colors: white, blue, black, red, green, colorless (CR 105.1). Color codes follow the WUBRG convention used throughout the MAST schema.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ChangeColorEffectRule.cs)

### `ConduitOfWorldsActivatedEffectRule` — *sealed class*

"Choose target nonland permanent card in your graveyard. If you haven't cast a spell this turn, you may cast that card. If you do, you can't cast additional spells this turn." — Conduit of Worlds' activated-ability effect.

This is a three-sentence activated-ability body that forms one semantically coupled unit — the "Choose target" targeting declaration, a history-predicate gate, and a conditional cast permission with an IfYouDo restriction — and must be recognized as a whole rather than dispatched sentence-by-sentence.

AST shape:

`ConditionalEffect` whose `Condition` is a `CountCondition` checking that you have cast zero spells this turn (`CastThisTurnPredicate` with Caster=You, Count=Equal:0). The `Then` branch is an `OptionalEffect` ("you may") wrapping a `MayCastTargetFromGraveyardEffect` targeting the chosen nonland permanent card in the controller's graveyard. The `IfYouDo` consequence is a `CantCastAdditionalSpellsThisTurnEffect` — the controller may cast no further spells this turn.

CR 602.5d (verbatim): "Activated abilities that read 'Activate only as a sorcery' mean the player must follow the timing rules for casting a sorcery spell, though the ability isn't actually a sorcery." (The `OnlyAsSorcery` restriction is stripped by `ActivatedAbilityParser` before this rule is invoked.)

ANCHORED (^…$) on the full three-sentence pattern so it cannot fire as a substring of a more-specific sibling or be claimed by a looser dispatcher.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ConduitOfWorldsActivatedEffectRule.cs)

### `CopyExiledCardAndCastWithoutPayingEffectRule` — *sealed class*

"You may copy the exiled card. If you do, you may cast the copy without paying its mana cost." — Isochron Scepter's activated-ability effect (MRD:200).

CR 707.10: "To copy a spell, activated ability, or triggered ability means to put a copy of it onto the stack … a copy of a spell isn't cast." Here the copy is made from a card in exile (the imprinted card), not from the stack; it is a copy of the card, and then the controller MAY cast that copy — two separate optional actions. The first "you may" (copy) gates the second "you may" (cast without paying) via an `IfYouDo` consequence (ADR 0005 / CR 117.7).

"The exiled card" refers to the card exiled by this permanent's Imprint trigger (CR 406.6 linked ability). It is modelled as an `Any` reference with Zone.Exile + ExiledWith: Self — a linked-ability reference (ADR 0004 "reference not resolution") that names the linking object, not a threaded binding. The copy target is then referenced as `It`.

Implemented as `IMultiActivatedEffectRule` because the two-sentence body forms ONE `OptionalEffect` (not two sibling effects): the "If you do" clause is the consequence of choosing to copy, not an independent effect. Returning null from `TryMatch` ensures the single-effect path never claims the text.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/CopyExiledCardAndCastWithoutPayingEffectRule.cs)

### `CopyTargetSpellYouControlEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Copy target instant or sorcery spell you control. You may choose new targets for the copy." — Twinning Staff's activated-ability copy-a-spell-you-control effect (Commander 2021, C21:219).

CR 707.10: "To copy a spell, activated ability, or triggered ability means to put a copy of it onto the stack; a copy of a spell isn't cast…" The copy source is a targeted instant or sorcery spell on the stack controlled by you — modelled as an `Target` reference with `Stack`, the instant/sorcery card types, and `You` on the filter.

"You may choose new targets for the copy" is the structured retarget permission carried on `MayChooseNewTargets`.

Distinguished from `CopyTargetSpellTriggeredRule` (triggered ability) and `CopyTargetSpellRule` (spell ability) by the "you control" controller restriction, which appears uniquely in activated-ability spell-copy contexts and must not be silently dropped. The higher priority (74, below the triggered rule's 75) ensures this rule fires first when the text includes "you control", preventing the more-general triggered variant from claiming it.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/CopyTargetSpellYouControlEffectRule.cs)

### `CreateColorlessArtifactCreatureTokenEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Create a N/N colorless [Subtype] artifact creature token [with keyword]." — creates a colorless artifact creature token with fixed power/toughness, a subtype, and an optional single keyword ability (Rule 111.3).

"Colorless" is not a color (CR 105.1: "Colorless is not a color"); the token's `Colors` list is `["C"]`, matching the colorless encoding convention for artifact creature tokens throughout MAST (e.g. the 1/1 colorless Thopter artifact creature token from Whirler Rogue, Whirler Virtuoso, Thopter Foundry, etc.).

Distinct from `CreateColorlessCreatureTokenEffectRule` which handles pure creature tokens only (and explicitly rejects the word "artifact" in the subtype slot via negative lookahead). This rule matches the more specific "artifact creature" multi-type form.

Fully anchored (^…$). Priority 984 — one below `CreateColorlessCreatureTokenEffectRule` (985) so the plain-creature rule is tried first; only artifact-creature forms fall through to this rule.

CR 111.3 (token creation); CR 105.1 (colorless); CR 701.7 (Create).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/CreateColorlessArtifactCreatureTokenEffectRule.cs)

### `CreateColorlessCreatureTokenEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Create a N/N colorless [Subtype] creature token." — creates a colorless (no color) creature token with fixed power/toughness, a creature subtype, and no abilities (Rule 111.3).

"Colorless" is not a color (CR 105.1: "Colorless is not a color"); the token's `Colors` list is empty (`[]`), matching the colorless encoding convention throughout MAST. Distinct from `CreateTokenEffectRule`, which handles colored tokens only (the pattern requires one of white/blue/black/red/green).

Fully anchored (^…$). Priority 985 — one below `CreateTokenEffectRule` (986) so the colored-token rule is tried first; only colorless-specific text falls through to this rule.

CR 111.3 (token creation); CR 105.1 (colorless is not a color).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/CreateColorlessCreatureTokenEffectRule.cs)

### `CreateCopyTokenWithModificationsEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Create a token that's a copy of target nonlegendary creature you control, except it has haste." — the copy-token line of an activated ability (Kiki-Jiki, Mirror Breaker). Produces a single `CopyEffect` whose `Target` is the targeted creature and whose `Modifications` carry the "except" clause(s).

CR 111.1: "A token is a marker used to represent any permanent that isn't represented by a card." CR 707.2: "When copying an object, the copy acquires the copiable values of the original object's characteristics…" — the "except" clause overrides one of those copiable values; here "it has haste" is an `AbilityAdder` modification. "nonlegendary creature you control" is encoded as a targeted reference with the Legendary supertype negated via `ExcludedSupertypes`.

Reached through `ActivatedAbilityParser`'s multi-sentence pre-pass, which splits Kiki's two-sentence effect body ("Create … except it has haste. Sacrifice it …") and dispatches each sentence independently. The companion delayed-sacrifice sentence is handled by `SacrificeAtEndStepEffectRule`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/CreateCopyTokenWithModificationsEffectRule.cs)

### `CreateEmblemLoyaltyTimingGrantActivatedEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"You get an emblem with [LDQUO]You may activate loyalty abilities of planeswalkers you control on any player's turn any time you could cast an instant.[RDQUO]" -- Teferi, Temporal Archmage -10 loyalty ability.

Rule 114.2: "An effect that creates an emblem is written '[Player] gets an emblem with [ability].' This means that [player] puts an emblem with [ability] into the command zone."

The emblem grants a `TimingModificationEffect` that relaxes the loyalty-ability activation restriction (CR 606.3: normally a player may activate a loyalty ability only during a main phase on their own turn while the stack is empty). With this emblem the controller may activate loyalty abilities of planeswalkers they control on any player's turn any time they could cast an instant.

The timing grant applies to activated abilities of planeswalkers the controller controls -- modelled as `ObjectActivatedAbilityReference` with `CardTypes` = ["planeswalker"] and `You`. "On any player's turn" is recorded on `WhoseTurn` = "AnyTurn" -- CR 606.3 establishes the default turn restriction; this field documents its removal.

ANCHORED: the opening "You get an emblem with" is shared with other emblem-grant shapes. The inner ability text is quoted verbatim from Teferi, Temporal Archmage (C14/C16/CMR) and anchoring is the defensive convention.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/CreateEmblemLoyaltyTimingGrantActivatedEffectRule.cs)

### `CreateTokenEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Create a [P]/[T] [color] [subtype] creature token." and the predefined artifact-token form "Create [count] [Treasure|Food|Clue|Blood] token(s)." Token creation as an activated-ability effect (Rule 111).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/CreateTokenEffectRule.cs)

### `CreateTokenWhereXCountEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Create X P/T color subtype creature tokens, where X is the number of [subtype] you control." Handles the Krenko, Mob Boss pattern (CR 111.2: token creation) where X is defined inline as a count of permanents the controller has. Distinct from the plain X-count variant in `CreateTokenEffectRule`: here X is not a free variable — it is immediately resolved to a `CountQuantity` over an `ObjectFilter`. Rule priority is higher than `CreateTokenEffectRule` (986) so this more-specific pattern is tried first.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/CreateTokenWhereXCountEffectRule.cs)

### `CreateTokenWithChangelingEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Create a [P/T] [color] [Subtype] creature token with changeling." — activated creature-token creation where the token bears the Changeling keyword.

Maskwood Nexus pattern (CR 111 — tokens; CR 702.73 — changeling): "{3}, {T}: Create a 2/2 blue Shapeshifter creature token with changeling." The token's Changeling ability is modelled as a `StaticAbility` with `KeywordAbilityEffect`{Changeling}, mirroring the `ChangelingKeyword` combinator shape.

ANCHORED (^…$): the "with changeling" tail is distinctive but substring-safe only when anchored. The generic `CreateTokenEffectRule` (priority 986) does not match "with changeling" because its regex is anchored and ends at "creature token[s]$"; this rule fires first (priority 987) for the changeling variant, ensuring the generic rule never sees it.

Priority 987 — one above the generic `CreateTokenEffectRule` (986) so this more-specific shape is tried first.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/CreateTokenWithChangelingEffectRule.cs)

### `DestroyColoredPermanentEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Destroy target permanent that's one or more colors." — destroys a colored (non-colorless) permanent of any type.

"One or more colors" means the permanent has at least one of the five Magic colors (CR 105.1: "There are five colors in the Magic game: white, blue, black, red, and green. Colorless is not a color."). This is the negation of colorless — any permanent that has a color satisfies this filter. The characteristic "one or more colors" is encoded as an `OtherCharacteristic` residual since `ObjectFilter` does not yet have a structured `IsColored` axis (the complement of `IsColorless`). It is a meaningful, rules-distinct filter — not arbitrary free text — and is a candidate for a future first-class boolean axis on `ObjectFilter`.

Fully anchored (^…$). GUARD: handles only "Destroy target permanent that's one or more colors." Does NOT handle "Destroy target [type]" (sibling `DestroyTargetEffectRule`), "Destroy target multicolored permanent" (spell-parser family), or "Destroy target nonland permanent" (different filter axis). Priority 599 — one below `DestroyTargetEffectRule` (600) so the bare-type destroy rule is tried first.

CR 701.8a (destroy); CR 105.1 (colors).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/DestroyColoredPermanentEffectRule.cs)

### `DestroyTargetEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Destroy target &lt;type&gt;." — single-target destroy as an activated-ability effect. Handles bare card-type targets: creature, artifact, enchantment, land, planeswalker, permanent; and two-type disjunctions: "artifact or enchantment", "creature or planeswalker", etc. CR 701.8a: "To destroy a permanent, move it from the battlefield to its owner's graveyard." GUARD: handles only "Destroy target &lt;type&gt;" shapes. Does NOT handle Exile (sibling family F14 owns "Exile target creature").

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/DestroyTargetEffectRule.cs)

### `DiscardCardsEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Discard N cards" — "Discard up to two cards", "Discard a legendary card".

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/DiscardCardsEffectRule.cs)

### `DiscardCostRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedCostRule

Discard cost: "Discard a card", "Discard a legendary card", "Discard your hand", etc. Reuses the shared `ParseDiscardPattern` for the card-count shapes. "Discard your hand" (or "discard their hand") is the whole-hand shape: the count is a `DerivedQuantity` with `CardsInHand` — descriptively, "hand" means "all cards in your hand at the time the cost is paid" (CR 701.9a).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/DiscardCostRule.cs)

### `DrawCardsEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Draw N cards" — "Draw two cards", "Draw a card", "Each other player draws a card". "Each other player" maps to EachOtherPlayer (broader than EachOpponent in multiplayer, Rule 109.1 / 102.1).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/DrawCardsEffectRule.cs)

### `DrawThenSelfToTopOfLibraryRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule, IMultiActivatedEffectRule

"Draw a card, then put this [artifact/permanent] on top of its owner's library." — Sensei's Divining Top. A single ", then"-joined sentence that is two sibling effects: a `DrawCardsEffect` (you draw 1) followed by a `PutOnTopOfLibraryEffect` moving this permanent (`Self`) to the top of its owner's library.

CR 121.1: "A player draws a card by putting the top card of their library into their hand. … It may also be done as part of a cost or effect of a spell or ability." The draw is the structured action; "on top of its owner's library" is the structured destination carried by `PutOnTopOfLibraryEffect`'s discriminator, not free text.

Implemented as `IMultiActivatedEffectRule` so the two effects sit as a flat sibling pair on `Effects` (the SurveilThenDraw / multi-sentence convention), not nested under a CompositeEffect. `TryMatch` always returns null so the single-effect path never claims the sentence.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/DrawThenSelfToTopOfLibraryRule.cs)

### `EachOpponentLoseLifeEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Each opponent loses N life." — broadcast life-loss as an activated-ability effect (Rule 119), covering drain abilities like Bolas's Citadel's sacrifice-ten activation.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/EachOpponentLoseLifeEffectRule.cs)

### `EachOpponentMayDiscardOtherwiseLoseLifeRepeatEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Each opponent may discard a card. If they don't, they lose N life. Repeat this process M more times." — the Professor Onyx −8 loyalty ability shape.

Three-sentence structure:

The base action: each opponent may discard a card ("each opponent may" means each opponent individually decides whether to discard). The consequence: any opponent who chose not to discard loses N life. A repeat instruction: the entire base action + consequence sequence is performed M additional times (total = M + 1).

Modelled as a `RepeatEffect` wrapping an `OptionalEffect`:

`Inner` — the `OptionalEffect` for one iteration (discard or lose life). `AdditionalTimes` — how many times to repeat after the initial pass (the oracle phrase "six more times" → 6). `Inner` — `DiscardCardsEffect` targeting each opponent. `IfYouDoNot` — `LoseLifeEffect` targeting each opponent who declined to discard.

CR 701.9a (discard); CR 119.3 (lose life); CR 608.2 (effects resolve, then the repeat instruction directs additional resolutions).

ANCHORED (`^…$`): this multi-sentence pattern is highly specific; anchoring is the defensive convention. Priority 999 — above `DiscardCardsEffectRule` (Priority 997) because that rule greedily matches any text containing "discard" with no anchoring, so this more-specific three-sentence shape must be tried first.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/EachOpponentMayDiscardOtherwiseLoseLifeRepeatEffectRule.cs)

### `EachOpponentSacrificesGreatestPowerCreatureEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Each opponent sacrifices a creature with the greatest power among creatures that player controls." — the Professor Onyx −3 loyalty ability shape.

The "with the greatest power among creatures that player controls" qualifier is a comparative game-state predicate (no dedicated CR — comparative relative power among a player's creatures) with no first-class ObjectFilter axis. It is carried as an `OtherCharacteristic` residual on the sacrificed-creature filter per the ADR 0001 free-text doctrine: the type-honest home for "not yet structured".

The sacrifice target is each opponent (`EachOpponent`); each opponent independently chooses which of their greatest-power creatures to sacrifice (in the case of a tie, they choose among the tied creatures). CR 701.21a (Sacrifice); CR 102.2 (opponents).

ANCHORED (`^…$`): prevents false positives against other "each opponent sacrifices" patterns. Priority 998 — just below `EachOpponentMayDiscardOtherwiseLoseLifeRepeatEffectRule` (999) and above the generic effect rules that could partially match.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/EachOpponentSacrificesGreatestPowerCreatureEffectRule.cs)

### `EachPlayerMillsNCardsEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Each player mills N cards." — broadcast mill keyword action applied to every player simultaneously (CR 701.17a: "For a player to mill a number of cards, that player puts that many cards from the top of their library into their graveyard.").

Used by Syr Konrad, the Grim's activated ability: "{1}{B}: Each player mills a card." The symmetrical form applies the same mill count to the controller AND each opponent, modelled as a single `MillEffect` with `EachPlayer` as the recipient — matching the "each player" subject in oracle text.

Anchored (^ … $) to prevent substring collision with the more-targeted `MillTargetPlayerEffectRule` ("target player mills N cards") and the self-mill form handled elsewhere. Distinct from "target player mills" (targeted activation) and "each opponent mills" (opponent-only spread).

CR 701.17a (verbatim): "For a player to mill a number of cards, that player puts that many cards from the top of their library into their graveyard." CR 603.2: triggered abilities fire on each matching mill event.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/EachPlayerMillsNCardsEffectRule.cs)

### `EnchantedEquippedModifyPTEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Enchanted creature gets &lt;±P/±T&gt; until end of turn" and "Equipped creature gets &lt;±P/±T&gt; until end of turn" — an activated pump whose subject is the permanent this card is attached to.

<b>CR 611.1:</b> "A continuous effect modifies characteristics of objects, modifies control of objects, or affects players or the rules of the game, for a fixed or indefinite period."

<b>CR 613.4c:</b> "Layer 7c: Effects and counters that modify power and/or toughness (but don't set power and/or toughness to a specific number or value) are applied."

<b>CR 208.1:</b> "...Power and toughness can be modified or set to particular values by effects."

Subject is modelled as `EnchantedOrEquipped` — the permanent this Aura or Equipment is currently attached to — rather than free text.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/EnchantedEquippedModifyPTEffectRule.cs)

### `ExileAnotherCreatureThenReturnRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule, IMultiActivatedEffectRule

Activated flicker (blink) effect: "Exile another target creature you control, then return it to the battlefield under its owner's control." — moves a targeted other creature the controller controls to exile and then immediately returns it, preserving owner control on re-entry (e.g. Emiel the Blessed's {3} ability).

"another target creature" is modelled with `ExcludeSelf = true` (CR 109.5 — "another" means other than the source object). "then return it" is the linked exiled reference (ADR 0004 reference-not-resolution): a `Designated` card in `Exile` whose `ExiledWith` points to `Self` (the same ability instance that exiled it — the Petravark shape). "under its owner's control" rides on `UnderControl` as an `Owner` reference (CR 400.6).

Implemented as `IMultiActivatedEffectRule` so the two effects sit as a flat sibling pair on `Effects`, consistent with `ExileSelfThenReturnToBattlefieldRule`. `TryMatch` always returns null so the single-effect path never claims this sentence.

CR 701.13a (verbatim): "To exile an object, move it to the exile zone from wherever it is." CR 400.6: "If an effect gives a player control of an object … that player controls that object until the effect ends." CR 109.5: "The words 'you' and 'your' on an object refer to the object's controller, its would-be controller (if a player is casting it), or its owner (if it has no controller)."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ExileAnotherCreatureThenReturnRule.cs)

### `ExileCreatureCostRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedCostRule

"Exile a creature you control" — exile-a-permanent-you-control as an activation cost. Used by Food Chain (Exodus, `EXO:193`): CR 118.8a classifies exile as a cost action when it precedes the colon separator of an activated ability.

The encoded cost uses `ExileCost` with: `Filter.CardTypes = ["creature"]` `Filter.Controller = You` — "you control" restricts to the activating player's own permanents (CR 109.4). `FromZone = Battlefield` — the creature is on the battlefield when the ability is activated (CR 701.13a). `Quantity = 1` — "a creature" is a single object.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ExileCreatureCostRule.cs)

### `ExileFromGraveyardEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Exile target card from a graveyard." — single-card graveyard-exile as an activated-ability effect (Rule 701.7), the common graveyard-hoser pattern.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ExileFromGraveyardEffectRule.cs)

### `ExileSelfByNameCostRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedCostRule

"Exile [CardName]" — exile the source permanent by its own card name as an activation cost. Rule 201.5: if an object's oracle text refers to the object it is applying to by name, that name refers to that object.

Encodes the cost as `ExileCost` with: `Filter.IsSelf = true` — the oracle name refers to the source permanent itself (CR 201.5 self-reference by card name). `Filter.CardTypes = ["permanent"]` — we don't have the type line here; "permanent" is the conservative common type, which the SelfReferenceTypeCorrector in CardParser will narrow at the card level. `FromZone = Battlefield` — the permanent is on the battlefield when the ability is activated (CR 701.13a moves it from there to exile). `Quantity = 1` — a single object.

Distinct from `ExileSelfCostRule` which matches the "Exile this [type]" form. This rule matches the self-by-name form used when a card names itself explicitly (Gonti's Aether Heart: "Exile Gonti's Aether Heart").

ANCHOR: pattern is anchored (^...$) to prevent matching substrings inside a longer exile-as-effect clause (e.g. "Exile target creature." in an effect body). Only fires when the entire cost component is "Exile [Name]".

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ExileSelfByNameCostRule.cs)

### `ExileSelfCostRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedCostRule

Exile-self cost: "Exile this artifact", "Exile this creature", etc. The activated permanent is moved to the exile zone as part of paying the activation cost. CR 701.13a: "To exile an object, move it to the exile zone from wherever it is." The encoded cost uses `ExileCost` with: - `Filter.CardTypes` = the permanent type named in oracle text - `Filter.Characteristics` = `[OtherCharacteristic("this permanent")]` to record the self-referential "this" phrasing (parallel to the `SacrificeCostRule` convention for "Sacrifice this artifact" costs) - `FromZone` = `Battlefield` (the permanent is on the battlefield when the ability is activated; CR 701.13a moves it from there to exile) - `Quantity` = 1 (oracle text always names a single permanent)

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ExileSelfCostRule.cs)

### `ExileSelfThenReturnToBattlefieldRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule, IMultiActivatedEffectRule

"Exile this creature, then return it to the battlefield under your control." — the Soulbond blink pattern (Deadeye Navigator, CR 702.95). A single ", then"-joined sentence that is two sibling effects: an `ExileEffect` moving self to exile, followed by a `ReturnToBattlefieldEffect` returning the designated just-exiled card to the battlefield under the ability's controller.

CR 701.13a: "To exile an object, move it to the exile zone from wherever it is." CR 400.7: the exiled object is the same object returned. The return target is modelled as `Designated` with `ExiledWith` pointing to `Self`, mirroring the Felidar Guardian blink fixture's consumer side (ADR 0004: reference-not-resolution).

Implemented as `IMultiActivatedEffectRule` so the two effects sit as a flat sibling pair on `Effects`. `TryMatch` always returns null so the single-effect path never claims this sentence.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ExileSelfThenReturnToBattlefieldRule.cs)

### `ExileTargetCreatureEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Exile target creature." — moves a targeted creature from whatever zone it occupies to the exile zone. Also handles the Aura/Equipment form: "Exile enchanted creature." — exiles the permanent this card is attached to (CR 303.4m). Not a targeted ability; no "target" keyword in oracle text. CR 701.13a: "To exile an object, move it to the exile zone from wherever it is." CR 303.4m: "An ability of a permanent that refers to the 'enchanted [object or player]' refers to whatever object or player that permanent is attached to…"

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ExileTargetCreatureEffectRule.cs)

### `ExileTopCardFaceDownAndLookRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule, IMultiActivatedEffectRule

"Exile the top card of your library face down and look at it." — moves the top card of the controller's library to the exile zone face down, then allows the controller to look at it (CR 406.3: a player instructed to exile a card face down may look at it).

This is a combined effect: the exile is face-down (`IsFaceDown` = true) and is followed by a `LookAtCardsEffect` targeting the just-exiled card in exile. CR 406.3 (verbatim): "Cards 'exiled face down' can't be examined by any player except when instructions allow it. However, if a player is instructed to look at a card and then exile it face down, or once a player is allowed to look at a card exiled face down, that player may continue to look at that card until it leaves the exile zone."

Fully anchored (^…$): the surface phrase "face down and look at it" must not match as a substring of a more-specific sibling (no other activated-effect sentence contains this exact phrasing). Priority 981 — one below `ExileTopCardOfLibraryEffectRule` (982) so the simpler plain-exile rule is tried first and falls through naturally.

CR 406.3: exiled-face-down visibility rules.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ExileTopCardFaceDownAndLookRule.cs)

### `ExileTopCardOfLibraryEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Exile the top card of your library." — moves the top card of the controller's library to the exile zone, as an activated-ability effect (Mystic Forge: "{T}, Pay 1 life: Exile the top card of your library.").

The "top card" is a positionally-designated card — it is deterministic (no player choice) and identified by position in the library, not by targeting (no "target" keyword in oracle text). Therefore the reference is `Designated` rather than `Target`. The positional qualifier "top" is carried as an `OtherCharacteristic` residual (the same convention used by the Ingest keyword expansion: GLOSSARY §Ingest). Zone=Library and Controller=You establish which library the top card comes from.

CR 701.13a: "To exile an object, move it to the exile zone from wherever it is."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ExileTopCardOfLibraryEffectRule.cs)

### `ExileTopCardsPlayThisTurnActivatedRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Exile the top N cards of your library. You may play those cards this turn." — the current-turn-bounded impulse in an activated-ability context (Harnfel, Horn of Bounty).

The two oracle sentences are one semantic unit — "those cards" back-references the exiled pile from the first sentence — so they collapse to a single `ImpulseEffect` with `RemainExiled` and `EndOfTurn`, matching the spell-context sibling `ExileTopCardsPlayThisTurnRule`. ADR 0004 (reference, not resolution): MAST records the exile and the play window; the engine tracks which specific cards were exiled and enforces the deadline.

CR 406 (exile zone); CR 701.13 (exile); CR 701.18 ("play").

ANCHORED (^…$): "Exile the top … cards of your library. You may play those cards this turn." is the exact Harnfel shape. Non-anchored matching risks absorbing "Exile the top N … put them into …" shapes that should be plain exile effects. Priority 975 — above mid-range, below ultra-specific multi-sentence composite rules, so the two-sentence split path (TryParseMultiEffectSentences) can't claim either sentence independently.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ExileTopCardsPlayThisTurnActivatedRule.cs)

### `ForageCostRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedCostRule

Forage keyword-action cost (CR 701.61a): "Exile three cards from your graveyard or sacrifice a Food." Printed as the bare word "Forage" on the cost side of an activated ability, e.g. "{2}, Forage: [effect]".

CR 701.61a (verbatim): "To forage means 'Exile three cards from your graveyard or sacrifice a Food.'" The keyword action appears as a single cost component: comma-separated from any mana cost in the standard "[cost]: [effect]" pattern (CR 602.1). MAST records the invocation as a `ForageCost` descriptor; the two alternative payment modes are engine territory per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine (the node names the action, not the execution). Rule reference: 701.61.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ForageCostRule.cs)

### `GainAbilityEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"gains [keyword]" grants on a target. Supported shapes: - "This [type] gains [keyword] [until end of turn]" (Self) - "Target creature gains [keyword] until end of turn" - "Target [Subtype] gains [keyword] until end of turn" (subtype-filtered target, e.g. Olivia's Bloodsworn "Target Vampire gains haste until end of turn") - "Creatures you control gain [keyword]" (Each, creatures you control) The grant is a continuous effect that modifies the target's characteristics for the stated duration (CR 611.1: "A continuous effect modifies characteristics of objects … for a fixed or indefinite period."). The granted keyword is itself a static ability (e.g. CR 702.10a: "Haste is a static ability.").

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/GainAbilityEffectRule.cs)

### `GainLifeAndDrawCardsEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule, IMultiActivatedEffectRule

"You gain N life and draw M cards." — Candy Trail's "{2}, {T}, Sacrifice this artifact: You gain 3 life and draw a card." A single "... and ..."-joined sentence that is two sibling effects: a `GainLifeEffect` (you gain N) followed by a `DrawCardsEffect` (you draw M).

CR 119.3 (gain life), CR 121.1 (draw). The two clauses are independent actions resolved in order; representing them as a flat sibling pair (not one residual) preserves both — the naive re-point otherwise drops the gain-life conjunct.

Implemented as `IMultiActivatedEffectRule` so the two effects sit as a flat sibling pair on `Effects` (the DrawThenSelfToTopOfLibrary / SurveilThenDraw convention), not nested under a CompositeEffect. `TryMatch` always returns null so the single-effect path never claims the sentence.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/GainLifeAndDrawCardsEffectRule.cs)

### `GainLifeEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"You gain N life" — N is a literal number, number word, or variable (X/Y/Z). "You gain X life.", "You gain 2 life.", "You gain three life."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/GainLifeEffectRule.cs)

### `GetsPTAndGainsKeywordEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"This creature gets &lt;±P/±T&gt; and gains &lt;kw&gt; until end of turn" — a single-sentence TWO-clause activated effect that neither `ModifyPTEffectRule` nor `GainAbilityEffectRule` handles alone.

<b>CR 613.4c</b> (P/T modifier clause): "Layer 7c: Effects and counters that modify power and/or toughness (but don't set power and/or toughness to a specific number or value) are applied." The granted keyword is a continuous ability grant that applies in layer 6 (ability-adding effects). Both clauses are present in oracle text; this parser describes them without encoding layer machinery.

Returns a `CompositeEffect` whose child list carries, in order:

A `ModifyPTEffect` targeting Self with EOT duration. A `GainAbilityEffect` targeting Self, the keyword ability, and EOT duration.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/GetsPTAndGainsKeywordEffectRule.cs)

### `InvestigateEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Investigate." — CR 701.16a: "Investigate" means "Create a Clue token." See rule 111.10f. Maps the bare keyword action to `InvestigateEffect` with no count (defaults to one Clue token). Reminder text has already been stripped by the activated-ability parser before this rule runs (Rule 207.2).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/InvestigateEffectRule.cs)

### `KarnWishEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule, IMultiActivatedEffectRule

"You may reveal an artifact card you own from outside the game or choose a face-up artifact card you own in exile. Put that card into your hand." — Karn, the Great Creator's −2 loyalty ability. The controller optionally retrieves an artifact card they own from outside the game (sideboard, CR 400.11) or from face-up exile (CR 406) and puts it into their hand.

Modelled as an `OptionalEffect` whose `Inner` is a `WishEffect` (ADR 0005: "You may" is the wrapper's presence, not a bool). The `WishEffect` records the two retrieval sources (`OutsideGame` and `Exile`), the `ObjectFilter` scoping the card to "artifact" owned by you, and `FaceUpOnly=true` for the exile branch (only face-up exiled cards qualify). Putting the card into hand is the inherent outcome of the Wish action and is recorded in the `WishEffect` node itself — the separate "Put that card into your hand." sentence is consumed as part of the same semantic unit (CR 400.11).

Implemented as `IMultiActivatedEffectRule` because the two oracle sentences ("You may … or choose …" and "Put that card into your hand.") form a single semantic action. `TryMatch` always returns null so the single-sentence path never fires. `TryMatchMulti` matches the full two-sentence form anchored at both ends.

CR 400.11: cards brought into the game from outside the game remain until the game ends, the owner leaves, or a rule removes them.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/KarnWishEffectRule.cs)

### `KinnanTopLookEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

Recognises the Kinnan, Bonder Prodigy activated ability effect: "Look at the top five cards of your library. You may put a non-Human creature card from among them onto the battlefield. Put the rest on the bottom of your library in a random order."

The three-sentence fragment is one atomic action — the "from among them" and "the rest" back-reference the same looked-at pile — and is emitted as a single `TopLookPutOntoBattlefieldEffect`. Splitting sentences would break the pile-binding. CR 701.12 (look); CR 400.7 (onto the battlefield); CR 400.4 (random order).

The non-Human creature filter is encoded as `CardTypes=["creature"], ExcludedSubtypes=["Human"]` per `ExcludedSubtypes` semantics (parallel to the Mutate "target non-Human creature" shape). CR 205.3m (creature subtypes).

Priority 95: must beat generic look-at-top sentence dispatch.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/KinnanTopLookEffectRule.cs)

### `LookAtTopCardsPutBackEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Look at the top N cards of your library, then put them back in any order." — Sensei's Divining Top's "{1}:" ability. The activated-ability sibling of `LookAtTopCardsTriggeredRule`.

This is a SINGLE `LookAtCardsEffect`: the ", then put them back in any order" clause is the look's disposition (carried by `PutBackInAnyOrder`), not a second sibling effect. CR 701.12 (look) does not define a default disposition, so the reorder is explicit.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/LookAtTopCardsPutBackEffectRule.cs)

### `LookAtTopNPutOneInHandRestBottomActivatedRule` — *sealed class*

"Look at the top N cards of your library. Put one of them into your hand and the other on the bottom of your library." — or "the rest on the bottom of your library [in any order]." The activated-ability Impulse family for both two-card and multi-card variants.

Two surface variants share the same `ImpulseEffect` representation:

"the other on the bottom of your library" — singular "other" (Teferi, Temporal Archmage +1; only 2 cards so there is exactly one unchosen card). "the rest on the bottom of your library in any order" — plural "rest" (Anticipate, Impulse). Semantically identical for the AST.

Implemented as `IMultiActivatedEffectRule` — the two-sentence oracle text cannot be split independently ("them" in the second sentence back-references the look set of the first). `TryMatch` always returns null; only the multi-rule path is served.

CR 701.12 (look at cards); CR 400.4 (order at bottom). Rule 606.3 (loyalty ability activation timing) — this rule is used in both loyalty and non-loyalty activated-ability contexts.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/LookAtTopNPutOneInHandRestBottomActivatedRule.cs)

### `LoseLifeEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Target player loses N life." — targeted life-loss as an activated-ability effect (Rule 119), covering drain artifacts like Onyx Goblet.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/LoseLifeEffectRule.cs)

### `ManaCostComponentRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedCostRule

Mana / tap / untap cost component: "{1}", "{2}{G}", "{T}", "{Q}". Returns a `TapCost` for {T}, an `UntapCost` for {Q}, otherwise a `ManaCost`. Only fires for components containing a mana brace.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ManaCostComponentRule.cs)

### `MillTargetPlayerEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

Activated-ability mill keyword action. CR 701.17a: "For a player to mill a number of cards, that player puts that many cards from the top of their library into their graveyard." Handles the targeted-player form used by activated abilities: "Target player mills N cards." "Target opponent mills N cards." For the spell-resolution side see `MillSpellRule`. For the triggered-ability side see `MillTriggeredRule`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/MillTargetPlayerEffectRule.cs)

### `ModifyPTEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Target creature gets [+-](N|X)/[+-](M|X) (until end of turn | for as long as [condition])." and the self-referential "This creature gets …" variant. Handles literal and variable modifiers; variable negation is a CalculatedQuantity with Operation = "negate" (see `ParseSignedModifier`).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ModifyPTEffectRule.cs)

### `ModifyPTTargetAttackingCreatureEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Target attacking creature gets [+-](N|X)/[+-](M|X) until end of turn." — the from-hand bloodrush pump (e.g. Rubblebelt Maaka, "Bloodrush — {R}, Discard this card: Target attacking creature gets +3/+3 until end of turn.").

Distinct from `ModifyPTEffectRule`'s "Target creature gets …" shape: the target is narrowed to an <em>attacking</em> creature, carried as the state-predicate residual `Characteristic.InCombat(CombatState.Attacking)` per ADR 0001 (no first-class ObjectFilter field for combat state) — mirroring how "Attacking creatures get …" (Goblin Oriflamme) and "Destroy target attacking creature" (Immolating Glare) already encode the attacking filter.

"Bloodrush" itself is an ability word with no rules meaning (CR 207.2c — bloodrush is explicitly listed among the ability words); the classifier captures it onto the activated ability's `AbilityWord` label. The P/T modifier is a layer-7c effect (CR 613.4c) — it adds to power/toughness without setting them.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ModifyPTTargetAttackingCreatureEffectRule.cs)

### `MonstrosityEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Monstrosity N" — maps the keyword-action to a `MonstrosityEffect`.

CR 701.37a: "Monstrosity N means 'If this permanent isn't monstrous, put N +1/+1 counters on it and it becomes monstrous.'" CR 701.37b: "Monstrous is a designation that has no rules meaning other than to act as a marker that the monstrosity action and other spells and abilities can identify."

The parenthetical reminder text "(If this creature isn't monstrous, put N +1/+1 counters on it and it becomes monstrous.)" is stripped upstream by `ActivatedAbilityParser.StripTrailingReminder` before this rule fires.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/MonstrosityEffectRule.cs)

### `PayEnergyCostRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedCostRule

"Pay N {E}" — energy-counter payment as an activation cost. Rule 107.14: The energy symbol is {E}; each represents one energy counter removed from the player.

Oracle text may use three forms:

Word-number form: "Pay eight {E}" — word encodes count, symbol names currency. Pay-symbols form: "Pay {E}{E}{E}" — "Pay" prefix + pure symbol repetition; count derived from symbol occurrences (e.g., Whirler Virtuoso, Aetherworks Marvel). Symbol-only form: "{E}{E}{E}" — no "Pay" prefix; count from symbol occurrences.

MAST always emits the declared amount as a LiteralQuantity.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/PayEnergyCostRule.cs)

### `PayLifeCostRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedCostRule

Pay-life cost: "Pay 1 life", "Pay 3 life" (Rule 118). The life payment is a cost that reduces the player's life total.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/PayLifeCostRule.cs)

### `PoisonCounterDifferenceEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"If target player has fewer than nine poison counters, they get a number of poison counters equal to the difference." — Vraska, Betrayal's Sting's −9 loyalty ability.

A `ConditionalEffect` whose condition is an `OtherCondition` ("target player has fewer than nine poison counters") and whose `Then` branch is a `PutCountersEffect` giving that player a `CalculatedQuantity` equal to `"the difference"` (i.e. 9 minus the current poison counter count — engine territory per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine; MAST records the reference, not the resolved value).

CR 122 (counters); CR 704.5c: "If a player has ten or more poison counters, that player loses the game." The ability targets the player (CR 115.6); the target reference is `Target` with a `CardTypes = ["player"]` filter.

ANCHORED (`^…$`): the phrase "fewer than nine poison counters" does not appear in any other sibling rule, but anchoring prevents accidental substring matches.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/PoisonCounterDifferenceEffectRule.cs)

### `PreventNextDamageFromColorSourceEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"The next time a [color] source of your choice would deal damage to you this turn, prevent that damage." — the Circle/Rune of Protection one-shot prevention shield (e.g. Circle of Protection: Red, Rune of Protection: Red). Maps to `PreventDamageEffect` with `NextInstance` set (the "next instance, regardless of amount" semantics — distinct from a bounded `Amount` or the all-instances `All`), the damage `Source` as a `Designated` reference filtered by the named color ("a red source of your choice" — the controller designates which qualifying source at resolution; not a target, no targeting keyword in the oracle, and not controller-bound), the `Target` (recipient) as `You`, and an `untilTime`/end-of-turn `Duration` for "this turn". "of your choice" is recorded as the player-designated reference itself, not its resolution (ADR 0004 reference-not-resolution). This is CR-615 prevention, NOT a replacement effect — no replacement ordering/layering is modelled. CR 615.8 (cited verbatim): "Some prevention effects generated by the resolution of a spell or ability refer to the next time a specific source would deal damage. These effects prevent the next instance of damage from that source, regardless of how much damage that is. Once an instance of damage from that source has been prevented, any subsequent instances of damage that would be dealt by that source are dealt normally." CR 615.2 (cited verbatim): "Many prevention effects apply to damage from a source. See rule 609.7."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/PreventNextDamageFromColorSourceEffectRule.cs)

### `PreventNextNDamageToAnyTargetEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Prevent the next N damage that would be dealt to any target this turn." Maps to `PreventDamageEffect` with a literal `Amount`, `AnyTarget` as the `Target`, and an `untilEndOfTurn` `Duration`. CR 615.8 (cited verbatim): "Some prevention effects generated by the resolution of a spell or ability refer to the next time a specific source would deal damage. These effects prevent the next instance of damage from that source, regardless of how much damage that is."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/PreventNextNDamageToAnyTargetEffectRule.cs)

### `ProliferateActivatedEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Proliferate." as a standalone activated-ability effect sentence (CR 701.27, CR 701.34).

This mirrors `ProliferateSpellRule` for the spell-parser registry but lives in the activated-effect registry so that `TryParseMultiEffectSentences` can resolve the "Proliferate." fragment that appears as the second sentence of Vraska, Betrayal's Sting's 0-ability ("You draw a card and lose 1 life. Proliferate.").

Priority is set high (999) so this fires before any broader pattern can claim a sentence that starts with "Proliferate".

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ProliferateActivatedEffectRule.cs)

### `PutCountersCostRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedCostRule

"Put a -1/-1 counter on this creature" / "Put a +1/+1 counter on this permanent" — a cost that places one or more counters on this permanent as part of activating an ability (Devoted Druid: "Put a -1/-1 counter on this creature: Untap this creature.").

CR 122.1: "A counter is a marker placed on an object or player that modifies its characteristics and/or interacts with a rule, ability, or effect." CR 602.1a: "The activation cost is everything before the colon (:)." Placing a counter is a valid activation cost; this rule is anchored (^…$) to prevent matching an effect sentence that also contains "put" and "counter".

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/PutCountersCostRule.cs)

### `PutCountersEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Put N +1/+1 (or -1/-1) counters on [target]" — "Put a +1/+1 counter on this creature", "Put a +1/+1 counter on target creature you control".

Also handles named counters in the activated path — "Put a storage counter on this land", "Put a charge counter on this artifact" (the storage- land / charge-artifact "load" abilities). CR 122.1: a counter is a marker placed on an object; storage/charge are named counters, the same placement action as +1/+1. Mirrors the named-counter branch already in the triggered PutCounters rule.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/PutCountersEffectRule.cs)

### `PutNamedCounterThenDrawPerCounterRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Put a [counter] counter on [self], then draw a card for each [counter] counter on [self]" — The One Ring's {T} ability ("Put a burden counter on The One Ring, then draw a card for each burden counter on The One Ring").

A two-step `CompositeEffect`: (1) `PutCountersEffect` adds one named counter to the source (`Self`); (2) `DrawCardsEffect` draws a card for each such counter — `Count` is a `CounterCountQuantity` over the same named counter on the source, read AFTER the put (reference-not-resolution, ADR 0004; the engine evaluates the live count). CR 122 (counters); CR 121 (draw). The same counter name must appear in both clauses.

The self target is "it" or the card naming itself (capitalized → self-reference, CR 201.5). ANCHORED on the full clause.

Priority 999: must run BEFORE the generic `DrawCardsEffectRule` (Priority 998), which otherwise greedily claims the sentence's "draw a card" clause as a flat `drawCards` and drops the put-counter clause + the per-counter scaling. This rule is strictly more specific (it owns the full anchored put-then-draw-per-counter shape).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/PutNamedCounterThenDrawPerCounterRule.cs)

### `PutSelfFromCommandZoneOntoBattlefieldEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Put [SelfName] onto the battlefield from the command zone." — the Commander-zone recast activated ability (Derevi, Empyrial Tactician, C13).

This ability functions from the command zone: the card puts itself onto the battlefield without being cast, bypassing the normal casting rules. The effect is modelled as `ReturnToBattlefieldEffect` with `Self` source zone `CommandZone`. The use of `ReturnToBattlefieldEffect` (rather than a dedicated node) is intentional — both graveryard-reanimate and command-zone activate share the "move from [zone] to battlefield" action (CR 400.7); the `Filter.Zone` discriminates the source zone for clustering (command-zone put vs. graveyard reanimate vs. hand put).

CR 903.9a (Commander zone ability): "Each time a commander would be put into its owner's library, hand, graveyard or exile from anywhere, its owner may put it into the command zone instead." Derevi's ability is an activated out-of-band re-entry that avoids the normal casting-tax accumulation by bypassing the cast.

The self-name is one or more title-case name words (capitalised content words and optional lowercase function words) followed by "onto the battlefield from the command zone". The pattern is anchored (^…$) to prevent substring-matching a broader effect sentence. Rule 201.5: "A card's text may refer to itself by its own name as a shorthand for 'this object'" — the name is a self-reference (IsSelf=true on the filter), not a generic card selector.

Rule 701.26 (Tap and Untap) is not involved here; CR 400.7 governs zone changes.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/PutSelfFromCommandZoneOntoBattlefieldEffectRule.cs)

### `RegenerateEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Regenerate this creature." / "Regenerate target [type]." (Rule 701.19). MAST records the effect and target only; shield / destruction-replacement semantics are engine territory.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/RegenerateEffectRule.cs)

### `RemoveCountersCostRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedCostRule

Remove-counters cost: "Remove three spore counters from this creature" / "Remove two charge counters from this permanent" (Rule 122). The counter type is a named counter; the source is `Self()` since oracle text always reads "from this creature / permanent".

Beyond the literal counts, scaling-mana costs (ADR 0009) also match "Remove X [type] counters from this [noun]" → `VariableQuantity.X` (CR 107.3) and "Remove any number of [type] counters from this [noun]" → `AnyAmountQuantity`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/RemoveCountersCostRule.cs)

### `ReturnAllFromGraveyardToBattlefieldRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Return all [type] cards from your graveyard to the battlefield [tapped]." Mass-recursion pattern — e.g. Aftermath Analyst's activated ability. CR 701 (zone change); CR 400.1 (zones); the optional "tapped" entering status is CR 110.5b. Anchored on "Return all" prefix so it cannot match sibling single-target rules.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ReturnAllFromGraveyardToBattlefieldRule.cs)

### `ReturnFromGraveyardToHandEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Return this card from your graveyard to your hand." — the self-retrieval pattern on activated abilities (e.g. Abzan Devotee). Models a graveyard-to-hand zone change where the source zone is the controller's graveyard (CR 404) and the destination is the controller's hand (CR 402). The permanent is in the graveyard at the time the cost is paid and ability resolves; Target = Self with a Graveyard zone qualifier captures both the identity of the object and its source zone. Anchored on "from your graveyard" to avoid matching battlefield-self bounce (F6 sibling: "to its owner's hand"), which has no graveyard source qualifier.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ReturnFromGraveyardToHandEffectRule.cs)

### `ReturnLandSubtypeToHandCostRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedCostRule

Return-a-land-you-control-to-hand cost: "Return a Forest you control to its owner's hand" — Quirion Ranger (CR 602); "Return a land you control to its owner's hand" — Trench Behemoth. Distinct from `ReturnSelfToHandCostRule` (self-bounce) and the effect-position `ReturnSelfToHandEffectRule`: this is a return of a controller-chosen permanent as the activation cost. The returned permanent is a land — identified EITHER by a land subtype (Forest/Island/Swamp/Mountain/Plains and the nonbasic land types, CR 205.3i) OR by the bare card type "land" (CR 300.1 / 205.2a). CR 118.3: any game action may be a cost; CR 602: activated abilities have a cost before the colon. The cost here moves the chosen land to its owner's hand as part of paying. ObjectReferenceKind.Any (not Target): the controller selects any qualifying permanent at activation time — not a legal target (Rule 115.1 — "target" requires the word "target").

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ReturnLandSubtypeToHandCostRule.cs)

### `ReturnNamedToHandEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Return [CardName] to its owner's hand." — a named-card self-bounce effect where the card refers to itself by its printed name (CR 201.4). Paradigm card: KHM Toralf's Hammer: "… Return Toralf's Hammer to its owner's hand." MAST resolves the self-reference to `Self`.

Distinct from `ReturnSelfToHandEffectRule`, which handles the self-referential pronoun form "Return this [noun] to its owner's hand." This rule handles the card-name form, where the card refers to itself by name rather than by a generic self-referential noun. Both map to the same `Self` reference.

Anchored (^…$) to prevent matching a substring of a longer clause. The card name is captured but not stored — all that matters for structuring is that this is a self-bounce effect; the name is engine-resolved at CR 201.4 and not threaded into the AST as a binding variable (ADR 0004: reference-not-resolution).

Priority 988 — just below `ReturnSelfToHandEffectRule` (989) so the self-referential noun form is tried first; this rule catches the card-name form that falls through.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ReturnNamedToHandEffectRule.cs)

### `ReturnSelfFromGraveyardToBattlefieldEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Return this card from your graveyard to the battlefield tapped." — a graveyard-self reanimation activated ability (Drownyard Temple). The ability functions while the card is in its owner's graveyard; activating it returns the card itself (Self) from the graveyard to the battlefield, and the card enters tapped. Distinct from `ReturnToBattlefieldEffectRule` ("Return TARGET X from [zone] to the battlefield"): the subject here is the source card itself (CR 109.2 — "this card" refers to the object with the ability), expressed as a `Self` reference. The source zone "your graveyard" is the structured `Zone` Graveyard + `You`, not free text. CR 611.1 (verbatim): "A continuous effect modifies characteristics of objects, modifies control of objects, or affects players or the rules of the game, for a fixed or indefinite period." — the resolving ability changes the card's zone and returns it under its owner's control; the "tapped" entering state is a structured `Tapped` flag, not free text.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ReturnSelfFromGraveyardToBattlefieldEffectRule.cs)

### `ReturnSelfToHandCostRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedCostRule

Return-self-to-hand cost: "Return this creature to its owner's hand" (Grinning Ignus), "Return this enchantment to its owner's hand" (Recurring Nightmare), etc. — the activated permanent is moved to the Hand zone as part of paying the cost (CR 701.x), so it must be recast to use the ability again. Previously this clause, when in the <em>cost</em> position (before the colon), matched no cost rule and was silently dropped — leaving the ability's `Costs` incomplete (or empty). The companion `ReturnSelfToHandEffectRule` handles the same phrase in the <em>effect</em> position; the two run in different parsing phases, so they don't conflict. Mirrors `ExileSelfCostRule`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ReturnSelfToHandCostRule.cs)

### `ReturnSelfToHandEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Return this {creature|permanent|Aura} to its owner's hand." — battlefield-self bounce (CR 402, hand zone). Matches the self-referential phrasing found on creatures like Darting Merfolk and Auras like Mourning/Conviction. Distinct from `ReturnToBattlefieldEffectRule` (which handles graveyard-source reanimation) and the sibling F5 rule (which handles "from your graveyard" sourced returns). CR 402: Return to hand is a plain zone change to the Hand zone; no dedicated keyword action exists for it. CR 303.4(m): "An ability of a permanent that refers to the 'enchanted [object or player]' refers to whatever object or player that permanent is attached to, even if the permanent with the ability isn't an Aura." — the self-subject here is the Aura itself ("this Aura"), not the enchanted object, so Self is correct. Anchor: "return this &lt;noun&gt; to its owner's hand" where &lt;noun&gt; is a self-referential permanent-type word (creature, permanent, Aura, enchantment, artifact, land).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ReturnSelfToHandEffectRule.cs)

### `ReturnTargetToHandEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Return target [type] [you control|an opponent controls] to its owner's hand." — the single-target battlefield-bounce as an activated-ability effect. Handles the most common permanent types (creature, artifact, enchantment, land, permanent, planeswalker) with an optional controller qualifier.

CR 402: returning an object to its owner's hand is a zone change from any zone to the Hand zone. No dedicated keyword action; the text is stated directly.

ANCHOR: pattern is anchored (^…$) on the full clause so it does not collide with the self-bounce rules (`ReturnSelfToHandEffectRule` at 989, `ReturnNamedToHandEffectRule` at 988) or the "up to one target" disjunction rule (`ReturnUpToOneTargetTypeDisjunctionToHandEffectRule` at 985). This rule runs at Priority 984 — below all of those — so they get first refusal on their tighter shapes.

Controller qualifier "you control" maps to `You`; "an opponent controls" maps to `Opponent`; absence of any qualifier leaves `Controller` null.

Rule 107.14 is not directly relevant here; this rule fires on the effect side of activated abilities. The zone-change semantics are CR 402 (hand zone). The owner's hand destination is the canonical phrasing for bounce (CR 109.5: a permanent's "owner" is the player who started with it in their library or starting hand).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ReturnTargetToHandEffectRule.cs)

### `ReturnToBattlefieldEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Return target [X] from [zone] to the battlefield." — e.g. "Return target creature or Vehicle card from your graveyard to the battlefield." The full type-phrase is captured as a Characteristics entry and the source zone on Zone.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ReturnToBattlefieldEffectRule.cs)

### `ReturnUpToOneTargetTypeDisjunctionToHandEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Return up to one target [type1], [type2], or [type3] to its owner's hand." — the "up to one target" bounce with a card-type disjunction (e.g. "artifact, creature, or enchantment"). Models the multi-type target as a single `ReturnToHandEffect` whose `Target.Filter.CardTypes` is the disjunction of all named types — the filter is satisfied when the object has ANY of the listed types (OR semantics, CR 115.3).

CR 402: returning an object to its owner's hand is a zone change from any zone to the hand. No dedicated keyword action; the text is stated directly.

"Up to one" is modelled as `Quantity = UpToQuantity { Maximum = 1 }` on the `ObjectReference` — parallel to "up to two target creatures" (Soul Salvage) — rather than an `OptionalEffect` wrapper, because the constraint is on the NUMBER of targets chosen (0–1), not on whether a one-shot action is performed at all (CR 117.3a: "up to X" targets means 0 to X are chosen at time of targeting).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ReturnUpToOneTargetTypeDisjunctionToHandEffectRule.cs)

### `SacrificeAtEndStepEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Sacrifice it at the beginning of the next end step." — the delayed-sacrifice sentence that trails a token-creation effect in an activated ability (Kiki-Jiki, Mirror Breaker). The pronoun "it" back-references the token created by the preceding sentence (CR 109.2 game objects).

CR 603.7: a triggered ability created by a resolving effect is a delayed triggered ability. MAST records this as a `CreateDelayedTriggerEffect` whose `DelayedTriggeredAbility` fires "at the beginning of the next end step" (a `GameTime` clock point) and whose resolution sacrifices the referenced object. Mirrors the triggered-ability sibling `SacrificeAtEndStepTriggeredRule` so an activated ability with the same trailing sentence (reached via the multi-sentence pre-pass) lands the same shape. CR 701.21 (Sacrifice); CR 513 (End Step).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/SacrificeAtEndStepEffectRule.cs)

### `SacrificeCostRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedCostRule

Sacrifice cost: "Sacrifice another creature", "Sacrifice X Squirrels", etc. Reuses the shared `ParseSacrificePattern` for most patterns; handles "nonland permanent(s)" directly before delegating.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/SacrificeCostRule.cs)

### `ScryEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Scry N" — e.g. "Scry 2", "Scry 1" (Rule 701.18).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ScryEffectRule.cs)

### `SearchLibraryAnyNumberToBattlefieldEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Search your library for any number of [filter] cards, put them onto the battlefield[tapped], then shuffle."

The "any number" variant of the search-to-battlefield tutor pattern, distinct from `SearchLibraryToBattlefieldEffectRule` which handles the singular "search for a[n] … card, put it onto the battlefield" shape. Here the oracle text uses "any number" (an open-ended count) and "them" (plural pronoun) rather than "a" (singular) and "it".

The World Tree's fourth ability is the canonical example: "Search your library for any number of God cards, put them onto the battlefield, then shuffle."

Maps to a `CompositeEffect` containing:

`SearchLibraryEffect` with `Count = AnyAmountQuantity` and `Destination = Battlefield` (or `BattlefieldTapped` when the oracle says "tapped"). `ShuffleEffect` targeting the controller (You).

CR 701.23a: "To search for a card in a zone, look at all cards in that zone (even if it's a hidden zone) and find a card that matches the given description." CR 701.23b: "If a player is searching a hidden zone for cards with a stated quality … that player isn't required to find some or all of those cards even if they're present in that zone." — "any number" is the oracle encoding of this optionality.

Priority 66 — one above `SearchLibraryToBattlefieldEffectRule` (priority 65) so this rule's "any number of … cards, put them" pattern is tried before that rule's "a[n] … card, put it" pattern. Both patterns are anchored (^…$) so there is no substring-overlap risk.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/SearchLibraryAnyNumberToBattlefieldEffectRule.cs)

### `SearchLibraryToBattlefieldEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Search your library for a &lt;filter&gt; permanent card with mana value N or less, put it onto the battlefield[tapped], then shuffle." Covers the activated tutor-to-battlefield pattern (e.g. Amrou Scout, Lin Sivvi, Deceptive Landscape, Contaminated Landscape). CR 701.23a: "To search for a card in a zone, look at all cards in that zone (even if it's a hidden zone) and find a card that matches the given description." Maps to a CompositeEffect containing: 1. SearchLibraryEffect (Destination = Battlefield or BattlefieldTapped) — the search and placement. 2. ShuffleEffect (Player = You) — the mandatory library shuffle. The ObjectFilter carries: - Subtypes: [subtype] when the qualifier is a creature-type (e.g. "Rebel") - Subtypes: [LandType1, LandType2, ...] when a basic-land subtype disjunction is present (e.g. "basic Plains, Swamp, or Forest" — CR 305.6: "The basic land types are Plains, Island, Swamp, Mountain, and Forest.") - CardTypes: ["permanent"] when a card-type constraint is present - ManaValueComparison: LessThanOrEqual N when "with mana value N or less"

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/SearchLibraryToBattlefieldEffectRule.cs)

### `SelfDealsDamageToAnyTargetEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

Two shapes — both map to self-as-source dealDamage:

"[Self] deals N damage to any target." → AnyTarget (CR 119.2: damage is dealt by the source; "any target" selects a creature, player, or planeswalker). "[Self] deals N damage to target creature." → Target with creature filter (CR 119.2: narrower form restricted to creatures only).

GUARD: does NOT match "each opponent" (family F17) — that shape has no "target" keyword.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/SelfDealsDamageToAnyTargetEffectRule.cs)

### `SelfDealsDamageToAttackingOrBlockingCreatureEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"[This creature|CardName] deals N damage to target attacking or blocking creature." — archer / ballista tap-to-deal-damage pattern. Both subject forms encode the source as `Self()`; the target is a creature with Characteristics = [Characteristic.InCombat(CombatState.AttackingOrBlocking)].

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/SelfDealsDamageToAttackingOrBlockingCreatureEffectRule.cs)

### `SelfDealsDamageToEachOpponentEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"This creature deals N damage to each opponent." — self-as-source dealDamage spreading N damage to every opponent simultaneously (CR 119.2: "Damage dealt to a player normally causes that player to lose that much life."). Guard: anchors on "each opponent"; does NOT match "any target" (handled by `SelfDealsDamageToAnyTargetEffectRule`) or "target attacking or blocking creature" (`SelfDealsDamageToAttackingOrBlockingCreatureEffectRule`).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/SelfDealsDamageToEachOpponentEffectRule.cs)

### `SelfDealsHalfLifeTotalDamageToEachPlayerEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"[Self] deals damage to each player equal to half that player's life total, rounded down." — the Heartless Hidetsugu activated-ability shape.

CR 602.1: "Activated abilities have a cost and an effect. They are written as '[Cost]: [Effect.]'" — this rule fires after the cost ({T}) is stripped, receiving only the effect clause.

The amount is a `CalculatedQuantity` wrapping a `DerivedQuantity` (`DerivedFrom=LifeTotal`) with `Operation="half"` and `Rounding="down"` (CR 107.1a: "If a spell or ability could generate a fractional number, the spell or ability will tell you whether to round up or down.").

CR 120.1: "Objects can deal damage to battles, creatures, planeswalkers, and players. This is generally detrimental to the object or player that receives that damage. An object that deals damage is the source of that damage."

GUARD: fully anchored (^ … $). Matches only the named-subject form ending in "each player equal to half that player's life total, rounded down" — does NOT match "each opponent" (handled by `SelfDealsDamageToEachOpponentEffectRule`).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/SelfDealsHalfLifeTotalDamageToEachPlayerEffectRule.cs)

### `SurveilEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Surveil N" as an activated-ability effect — e.g. "{4}, {T}: Surveil 1." CR 701.25a: "To 'surveil N' means to look at the top N cards of your library, then put any number of them into your graveyard and the rest on top of your library in any order."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/SurveilEffectRule.cs)

### `TakeExtraTurnEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Take an extra turn after this one." — schedules an additional full turn for the ability's controller to be taken immediately after the current turn.

CR 500.7 (verbatim): "Some effects can give a player extra turns. They do this by adding the turns directly after the specified turn." MAST records the verb and player reference; the turn-ordering bookkeeping is engine territory.

ANCHOR: pattern is anchored (^...$) to prevent partial matches inside longer effect text. This is the most-specific extra-turn form (controller takes a turn); target-player forms are handled by extending this rule or adding a sibling rule.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/TakeExtraTurnEffectRule.cs)

### `TapEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Tap [count] target [type]" — "Tap target creature", "Tap X target lands", "Tap two target creatures" (Rule 701.21). For variable-X abilities the X in cost and effect refer to the same chosen value (Rule 107.3b/c).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/TapEffectRule.cs)

### `TapPermanentsCostRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedCostRule

Tap-permanents cost: "Tap two untapped artifacts you control", "Tap an untapped creature you control", etc. — the spelled-out "Tap …" verb (not the {T} symbol) used as an activation cost (CR 118.3 / 602.5; CR 701.20 tap/untap). e.g. Whirler Rogue: "Tap two untapped artifacts you control: Target creature can't be blocked this turn." Emits a `TapPermanentsCost` whose `Filter` carries the tapped permanents' card type and controller. The "untapped" qualifier is implicit in the cost (a permanent must be untapped to be tapped to pay the cost — CR 701.20a), so it is not encoded as a separate axis; this matches the existing Conspire `TapPermanentsCost` idiom ("tap two untapped creatures you control").

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/TapPermanentsCostRule.cs)

### `TapSubtypesCostRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedCostRule

Tap-N-subtype cost: "Tap three untapped Elves you control", "Tap two untapped Merfolk you control", etc. — the spelled-out "Tap …" verb used as an activation cost where the permanents are identified by creature subtype rather than card type (CR 118.3 / 602.5; CR 701.20 tap/untap).

This rule handles the pattern where only a creature subtype (e.g. "Elf", "Elves", "Merfolk", "Goblin") appears in the tap cost — no explicit card-type word ("creature", "artifact") is printed. Oracle text capitalises creature subtypes (Rule 205.3m), so the regex requires an uppercase first letter, preventing "Tap three untapped lands you control" from matching here (handled by `TapPermanentsCostRule` which requires a card-type word).

The "untapped" qualifier is implicit in any tap cost (CR 701.20a: a permanent must be untapped to be tapped), so it is not encoded as a separate axis on the emitted `TapPermanentsCost`, matching the Conspire idiom.

Emits a `TapPermanentsCost` with:

`Filter` — CardTypes = ["creature"], Subtypes = [singularized subtype], Controller = You. `Quantity` — parsed from the number word (one/two/three/…) or digit in the cost text.

Priority 996 — above TapPermanentsCostRule (Priority 997 handles generic card-type tap costs; numbered lower = higher priority in the registry). Wait — Priority here is DESCENDING (higher number = higher priority per the convention in ActivatedCostRuleAttribute). Set to 998 so this fires before TapPermanentsCostRule (997), which would otherwise return null on subtype-only costs anyway (it requires a card-type keyword), making the order moot — but explicit priority ensures no future generic rule intercepts first.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/TapSubtypesCostRule.cs)

### `TransformSelfEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Transform this creature" / "Transform this permanent" — the activated transform-self ability on the front face of a double-faced card (e.g. "{6}: Transform this creature.").

CR 701.27a: "To transform a permanent, turn it over so that its other face is up. Only permanents represented by double-faced tokens and double-faced cards can transform. (See rule 712, 'Double-Faced Cards.')"

Maps to a `TransformEffect` with a `Self` subject. The activation cost (including Phyrexian hybrid mana such as {B/P} — CR 107.4f) is parsed upstream by the cost-component rules, the "Activate only as a sorcery" restriction is lifted by `ActivatedAbilityParser.ExtractActivationRestrictions`, and the parenthetical reminder explaining Phyrexian mana is stripped by `ActivatedAbilityParser.StripTrailingReminder` before this rule fires.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/TransformSelfEffectRule.cs)

### `UnattachNamedCostRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedCostRule
