"Unattach [card name]" activation cost — the Equipment detaches itself from the equipped creature as part of paying this cost. Paradigm card: KHM Toralf's Hammer, whose inner activated ability includes "Unattach Toralf's Hammer" in its cost.

Distinct from the keyword-action `UnattachEffect` (an effect that unattaches) and from the parameterless reconfigure unattach ability (CR 702.151a). This cost form names the Equipment explicitly, allowing the engine to locate the specific permanent to unattach without ambiguity (CR 201.4: a card's name in its own text is a self-reference).

The card name is captured verbatim from oracle text (e.g. "Toralf's Hammer") and stored in `CardName` for reference-not-resolution (ADR 0004 — MAST records the reference; the engine resolves the identity). Anchored (^…$) to prevent matching a standalone word in a multi-component cost.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/UnattachNamedCostRule.cs)

### `UntapAllControlledActivatedEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Untap all [filter] you control." — mass-untap of all permanents matching a filter under the controller's control. Activated-ability mirror of `UntapAllControlledRule`.

Handled patterns (via `TryMatch`):

"Untap all creatures you control." — Aggravated Assault "Untap all nonland permanents you control." "Untap all permanents you control." "Untap all artifacts you control."

Emits an `UntapEffect` whose `Target` has `Each` and the appropriate `ObjectFilter` with `Controller` = `You` (CR 701.19a: "to untap a permanent, rotate it back to the upright position from a sideways position. Only tapped permanents can be untapped.").

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/UntapAllControlledActivatedEffectRule.cs)

### `UntapAnotherOrCountTargetActivatedEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

Handles two "untap another/N other target" effect shapes that require both self-exclusion (`ExcludeSelf`) and optional cardinality on the target reference:

"Untap another target permanent." — CR 701.26b (untap); "another" excludes the source permanent. Produces a single-target `UntapEffect` with `ExcludeSelf = true` on the permanent card-type filter. "Untap [N] other target [Supertype] [CardType]s." — e.g. "Untap two other target legendary creatures." Produces a multi-target `UntapEffect` where the `Quantity` carries the literal count and `ExcludeSelf` = true excludes the source. The "another/other" marker encodes the self-exclusion axis (ExcludeSelf self-exclusion), separate from the counted target set size.

Runs at Priority 995 — above `UntapTargetCardTypeActivatedEffectRule` (994) and `UntapEffectRule` (993) — so these more-specific "another / N other" prefixes are dispatched here and never reach the generic single-target rules. The patterns are fully anchored (`^...$`) to avoid substring matching inside more-specific siblings. CR 701.26b: "To untap a permanent, rotate it back to the upright position from a sideways position. Only tapped permanents can be untapped." "Another"/"other" excludes the source object — modeled on the structured `ExcludeSelf` axis (no dedicated lettered subrule in the bundled CR data).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/UntapAnotherOrCountTargetActivatedEffectRule.cs)

### `UntapEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Untap target [subtype]" — "Untap target Forest.", "Untap target creature.", "Untap another target creature or land [you control]." First-cut single-token target recognition (Rule 701.20). Also handles self-reference form: "Untap this creature.", "Untap this artifact.", "Untap this permanent." — per CR 701.26b: "To untap a permanent, rotate it back to the upright position from a sideways position. Only tapped permanents can be untapped."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/UntapEffectRule.cs)

### `UntapSelfNameAndPutCounterEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule, IMultiActivatedEffectRule

"Untap [CardName] and put a +1/+1 counter on it." — the compound activated effect on Grimgrin, Corpse-Born (and similar self-referencing activated abilities). Expands to a flat two-element effect list:

`UntapEffect` targeting `Self` — "Untap [Name]" where the card refers to itself by name (CR 201.5 self-reference, equivalent to "Untap this permanent"). `PutCountersEffect` targeting `Self` with +1/+1 counter — "put a +1/+1 counter on it" where "it" is the pronoun back-referencing the untapped permanent (CR 701.26b; CR 122.1).

Implemented as `IMultiActivatedEffectRule` so the two effects appear as a flat sibling pair on `Effects`, following the same convention as `DrawThenSelfToTopOfLibraryRule`. `TryMatch` always returns null so the single-effect path never fires.

Priority 951 — above the generic `UntapEffectRule` (Priority 993) so this compound form is tried via the multi-effect path first. Pattern is anchored (^…$) to prevent false-positive substring matches on richer ability text.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/UntapSelfNameAndPutCounterEffectRule.cs)

### `UntapTargetCardTypeActivatedEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Untap target creature." / "Untap target artifact." / "Untap another target permanent." / etc. in an activated ability effect position — uses CardTypes (not Subtypes) for the card-type words that are CR card types rather than subtypes. Runs at higher priority than `UntapEffectRule` (Priority 994 > 993) so card-type words are routed here and do not land in `Subtypes` on the generic rule.

Also handles the "another target [type]" form (e.g. "Untap another target permanent."), where "another" excludes the source permanent (`ExcludeSelf = true`). CR 109.5: "'Another' means a different object." — the source is excluded from the legal targets.

CR 300.1 / 205.2a: the card types (artifact, creature, enchantment, instant, land, planeswalker, sorcery, battle, …) are distinct from subtypes (CR 205.3 / 205.3i). "Forest" is a land subtype; "creature" is a card type.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/UntapTargetCardTypeActivatedEffectRule.cs)

### `UntapUpToNTargetPermanentsRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Untap up to N target permanents." — the Teferi, Temporal Archmage −1 loyalty ability shape. Emits an `UntapEffect` whose target has an `UpToQuantity` and a permanent card-type filter.

CR 701.26b: "To untap a permanent, rotate it back to the upright position from a sideways position. Only tapped permanents can be untapped." The engine applies this; MAST records that up to N target permanents are untapped.

CR 115.1 (target keyword) — "target" makes these targeted choices, with the number of targets constrained to 0–N by the "up to N" phrasing (CR 107.3). "Permanent" is a CR card type covering all permanents on the battlefield (CR 110.1). The filter records `CardTypes: ["permanent"]`.

ANCHORED (`^…$`): "Untap up to" appears as a substring of more-specific sibling untap phrases only if followed by a different qualifier (e.g. "up to one target creature") — those would not match this rule's "permanents" suffix. Anchoring is the defensive convention regardless.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/UntapUpToNTargetPermanentsRule.cs)

### `UntilNextTurnGrantSorceryTimingEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Until your next turn, you may cast sorcery spells as though they had flash." — Teferi, Time Raveler's +1 loyalty ability. This is a duration-bounded flash grant that allows the controller to cast sorcery spells at instant speed (as though they had flash, per CR 702.8a) until the beginning of their next turn.

CR 702.8a: "'Flash' means 'You may play this card any time you could cast an instant.'" The grant here is scoped to sorcery-type spells only (the controller may cast them as though they had flash) and bounded to "until your next turn" — the beginning of the controller's next turn (CR 501.1: your upkeep step begins your turn, so "your next turn" ends at the start of your upkeep).

Modelled as a `TimingModificationEffect` with `Modification = Grant`, `Timing = Instant`, an `AppliesTo` `SpellReference` restricted to sorceries you control, and a `Duration = UntilTimeDuration.YourNextTurn`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/UntilNextTurnGrantSorceryTimingEffectRule.cs)

### `WhenTokenLeavesReturnExiledToHandRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"When that token leaves the battlefield, put the exiled card into your hand." — creates a `CreateDelayedTriggerEffect` that fires when the token created by the same ability leaves the battlefield, returning the face-down exiled card (exiled "with" this ability's source) to the controller's hand.

This is the second half of Ugin, the Ineffable's +1 linked-ability pair (CR 406.6: "An object may have one ability that causes one or more cards to be exiled, and another ability that refers either to 'the exiled cards' or to cards 'exiled with [this object]'. These abilities are linked."). The exile is produced by `ExileTopCardFaceDownAndLookRule`; the return is produced here as a delayed trigger keyed on the token's LTB event.

The trigger filter uses `IsToken: true` and `Controller: You` to scope the LTB event to the token just created. The return-to-hand target uses `ExiledWith: { Kind: Self }` to reference the card exiled by the same source (ADR 0004 reference-not-resolution).

Fully anchored (^…$). Priority 980 — below the other token-creation and exile rules so those run first; this rule handles the trailing delayed-trigger sentence.

CR 406.6 (linked abilities); CR 603.7 (delayed triggered abilities).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/WhenTokenLeavesReturnExiledToHandRule.cs)

### `WheneverCardPutIntoOpponentGraveyardThisTurnLoseLifeEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

Activated-ability effect that creates a turn-scoped delayed triggered ability (CR 603.7) watching for cards entering an opponent's graveyard: "Whenever a card is put into an opponent's graveyard from anywhere this turn, that player loses N life." The "this turn" clause establishes the delayed trigger's window (`EndOfTurn`). The trigger event is `PutIntoGraveyard` filtered to `Opponent`. The effect is `LoseLifeEffect` targeting `ThatPlayer` — the pronoun for the player whose graveyard received the card (CR 109.5).

CR rule citations:

CR 602.1: Activated abilities have a cost and an effect (cost:effect form). CR 603.7: A resolving effect may create a delayed triggered ability. CR 404 (graveyard) / 701.17: "put into a graveyard" / mill. CR 119.3: Life loss.

Representative card: Duskmantle Guildmage (GTC).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/WheneverCardPutIntoOpponentGraveyardThisTurnLoseLifeEffectRule.cs)

### `YouDrawCardAndLoseLifeEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule, IMultiActivatedEffectRule

"You draw a card and lose N life." — the Vraska planeswalker 0-ability shape.

A single "… and …"-joined sentence that expands to two sibling effects: `DrawCardsEffect` (you draw 1) and `LoseLifeEffect` (you lose N). Differs from the similar `TryParseYouDrawAndYouLoseLife` helper in TriggeredAbilityParser (which requires the second "you" — "you draw … and <b>you</b> lose …") and from `GainLifeAndDrawCardsEffectRule` (which goes gain-then-draw in the opposite order).

CR 121.1 (draw), CR 119.3 (lose life). Both clauses reference the controller ("you"), so both effects carry `You`.

ANCHORED (`^…$`): "draw a card and lose" appears as a SUBSTRING of no sibling rule, but the anchor prevents a future broader pattern from consuming this sentence and silently dropping the lose-life conjunct.

Implemented as `IMultiActivatedEffectRule` so the two effects sit as a flat sibling pair on `Effects` — not wrapped in a `CompositeEffect`. `TryMatch` always returns null so the single-effect path never claims the sentence.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/YouDrawCardAndLoseLifeEffectRule.cs)

### `YouLoseLifeThenImpulseEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule, IMultiActivatedEffectRule

"You lose N life. Look at the top M cards of your library. Put one of them into your hand and the rest into your graveyard." — the Professor Onyx +1 loyalty ability shape. Two coupled effects in three sentences: a controller life-loss followed by an impulse-draw (look at top M, keep one, rest to graveyard).

The three sentences cannot be dispatched independently because the second and third sentences ("Look at the top M cards … Put one of them …") form a single coupled `ImpulseEffect` — "them" in the third sentence is a back- reference to the cards revealed by the second sentence. This rule captures the full three-sentence text as a flat two-element sibling list:

`LoseLifeEffect` — the controller loses N life. `ImpulseEffect` — look at top M, put one in hand, rest to graveyard.

CR 119.3 (lose life); CR 701.9 (discard/graveyard).

Implemented as `IMultiActivatedEffectRule` so the two effects sit as a flat sibling pair on `Effects` — not wrapped in a `CompositeEffect`. `TryMatch` always returns `null` so the single-effect path never claims the text.

ANCHORED (`^…$`): this three-sentence combination is distinctive enough that a non-anchored match would not produce false positives, but anchoring is the defensive convention for all rules that could match a sibling as a substring. Priority 997 — ties with `DiscardCardsEffectRule` which is the most greedy existing rule. The name-based tiebreak puts this rule (YouLoseLife...) after DiscardCardsEffectRule alphabetically, so use 997 + 1 = 998 is occupied by the sacrifice rule. Use 997 to tie-break with alphabetical ordering placing this rule AFTER DiscardCardsEffectRule within the same band — but the anchored pattern will not match when DiscardCardsEffectRule's unanchored check fires first. Since DiscardCardsEffectRule fires on any "discard" text, this rule must be Priority 997 with TryMatch returning null (the single-effect path) so the multi-rule path is tried on the full 3-sentence text BEFORE sentence splitting. Actually: TryParseMultiRuleEffects is called AFTER TryParseMultiEffectSentences, which will fail (individual sentences don't parse). The multi-rule path then gets the full 3-sentence text and tries IMultiActivatedEffectRule implementations in priority order. DiscardCardsEffectRule only implements IActivatedEffectRule, not IMultiActivatedEffectRule, so the multi-rule loop only sees this rule. Priority 957 is safe for the multi-rule path.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/YouLoseLifeThenImpulseEffectRule.cs)

## `/Parsing/Parsers/Spell/`

### `IMultiSpellRule` — *interface*

Extension of `ISpellRule` for shapes that expand to a flat list of effects on `Effects` rather than a single `Effect`. Implement this alongside `ISpellRule` (returning `false` from `TryMatch` so the single-effect path never fires) when the oracle-text shape naturally produces multiple sibling effects that should be represented as a flat array, not a `CompositeEffect`.

Discovery: `SpellAbilityParser` picks this up automatically for any `SpellRuleAttribute`-decorated class that also implements this interface.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/IMultiSpellRule.cs)

### `ISpellRule` — *interface*

One spell-effect recognition rule. Each implementation maps a single oracle-text shape to its `Effect` AST node. Rules are discovered by reflection at `SpellAbilityParser` construction via the `SpellRuleAttribute` decoration and dispatched in descending `Priority` order — first match wins.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/ISpellRule.cs)

### `SpellRuleAttribute` — *sealed class* : Attribute, MagicAST.Parsing.Parsers.IPrioritizedRuleAttribute

Marks an `ISpellRule` implementation for reflection-based discovery by `SpellAbilityParser`. Each decorated rule contributes one oracle-text recognizer to the parser's effect cascade.

<b>Priority convention (range 0–100, default 50, higher = more specific = tried first):</b>

<b>80–100</b> — rules that supersede a more-general sibling (color-disjunction before type-disjunction, IfYouDo variants before plain variants, "this creature" before "a [type]"). Without the bump these would never fire because the more-general rule would shadow them. <b>50</b> — default. Non-overlapping rules whose regex anchors are mutually exclusive. <b>20–40</b> — catch-all / generic rules that should be tried last so a more-specific sibling gets a chance first.

The numeric scheme replaces the old source-order convention: each rule used to declare its priority implicitly by where it appeared in `TryParseEffect`'s cascade. Splitting rules into one-file-per-rule loses that ordering signal, so each rule now states its priority explicitly.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/SpellRuleAttribute.cs)

### `SpellRuleHelpers` — *static class*

Shared utilities used across multiple `ISpellRule` implementations. Lives outside the dispatcher so rules can be added without taking a dependency on `SpellAbilityParser` internals.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/SpellRuleHelpers.cs)

## `/Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/`

### `AddManaForEachCardInHandRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Add {R} for each card in target opponent's hand." — adds one mana of a specific color for each card in the targeted opponent's hand (Jeska's Will).

The amount is a `CountQuantity` over cards in the target's hand: `CountOf: { CardTypes: ["card"], Zone: Hand, Controller: Target }`. The "target opponent" is the player being targeted; `Target` identifies that the hand count belongs to the targeted player (CR 108.3 / 109.4 — in a hand, the controller/owner is the player whose hand it is).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/AddManaForEachCardInHandRule.cs)

### `AddManaForEachTappedLandOpponentsControlRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Add {mana} for each tapped land your opponents control." — adds one mana of a specific color per tapped land opponents control (e.g. Mana Geyser, P02/10E).

The amount is a `CountQuantity` over tapped lands opponents control: <c>CountOf: { CardTypes: ["land"], Zone: Battlefield, Controller: Opponent, Characteristics: [{ CharacteristicType: "tapped", Tapped: true }] }</c>. The "tapped" predicate is expressed as a `TappedStateCharacteristic` (CR 110.5a — a permanent is tapped if it's been turned sideways; the tapped/untapped state is engine territory, MAST records the filter predicate descriptively).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/AddManaForEachTappedLandOpponentsControlRule.cs)

### `AddManaSpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Add {mana}." — bare add-mana spell instruction (e.g., Infernal Plunge's main effect). Covers the same oracle pattern that ActivatedAbilityParser handles on the right-hand side of a mana ability cost, but here it appears as the whole spell body after the additional-cost prefix has been stripped by ClauseSplitter.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/AddManaSpellRule.cs)

### `AllMustBlockTargetRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"All creatures able to block target creature this turn do so." — Lure-type spell. Rule 509.1c forcing requirement applied transiently until end of turn.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/AllMustBlockTargetRule.cs)

### `ApproachOfTheSecondSunRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

Recognises the full Approach of the Second Sun spell text: "If this spell was cast from your hand and you've cast another spell named Approach of the Second Sun this game, you win the game. Otherwise, put Approach of the Second Sun into its owner's library seventh from the top and you gain 7 life."

This is a bespoke one-spell rule covering all three clauses atomically: the compound cast-history condition, the win-game then-branch, and the self-library-placement + life-gain else-branch. The compound condition ("cast from your hand AND you've cast another copy this game") has no general structured form yet — it lands in `OtherCondition` as a type-honest residual (ADR 0001/0007).

CR 104.2b (you win the game), CR 401.7 (Nth from the top), CR 601.2 (casting from the hand). Priority 95: must beat the general sentence-bundle dispatcher, which would try to split on ". " and choke on the "Otherwise, …" fragment.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ApproachOfTheSecondSunRule.cs)

### `BiteRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

Recognises "bite" spell oracle text — a one-directional fight where only the controlled creature deals damage:

"Target creature you control deals damage equal to its power to target creature you don't control." "Target creature you control deals damage equal to its power to target creature or planeswalker you don't control."

Both phrasings emit a `DealDamageEffect` where:

Source is the controlled target creature (Controller: You). Amount is `DerivedQuantity` Power sourced from "it". Target is an opponent-controlled creature (or creature/planeswalker for the broader form).

Distinct from `FightRule` (CR 701.14), which produces a symmetric `FightEffect` — both creatures deal damage to each other. Bite is asymmetric: only the source creature deals damage; the target does not retaliate.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/BiteRule.cs)

### `CantBeCounteredRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"This spell can't be countered." — encoded as a spell-level effect.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/CantBeCounteredRule.cs)

### `CastSpellThisTurnDelayedDrawRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Whenever you cast a[n] [type] spell this turn, draw a card." — a spell whose resolution creates a delayed triggered ability (CR 603.7) bounded to this turn: the trigger is an event (a spell of the given type is cast by you), the window is "this turn" (`untilTime` end of turn), the effect is a draw. ADR 0002/0004. Examples: Glimpse of Nature (creature), Beck.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/CastSpellThisTurnDelayedDrawRule.cs)

### `ChangeTargetRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Change the target of target spell with a single target." Rule 115.7 — the controller of this spell or ability may choose a new legal target for the targeted spell. Covers Divert, Misdirection, and functional equivalents.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ChangeTargetRule.cs)

### `CopyTargetSpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule, IMultiSpellRule

"Copy target instant or sorcery spell[, then return it to its owner's hand]. You may choose new targets for the copy." — the spell-side copy of an instant-or-sorcery on the stack (Reiterate; Narset's Reversal adds the bounce).

CR 707.10: "To copy a spell, activated ability, or triggered ability means to put a copy of it onto the stack; a copy of a spell isn't cast and a copy of an activated ability isn't activated." The copy source is a targeted spell on the stack — modelled as an `Target` reference whose filter pins the instant-or-sorcery card types in `Stack`. This is the spell-cast analog of `CopyTargetSpellTriggeredRule` (Dualcaster Mage's ETB trigger).

"You may choose new targets for the copy" is the structured retarget permission carried on `MayChooseNewTargets` — a rules-meaningful flag, not free text. Narset's Reversal's "then return it to its owner's hand" bounces the copied (originally targeted) spell and is emitted as a second sibling `ReturnToHandEffect` via `TryMatchMulti`; its `Target` is the same instant-or-sorcery spell reference the copy is taken from.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/CopyTargetSpellRule.cs)

### `CounterAbilityRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

Counters that target activated or triggered abilities (or both), or the full "spell, activated ability, or triggered ability" disjunction. Rule 701.6. Handles these oracle-text shapes:

"Counter target activated ability." "Counter target triggered ability." "Counter target activated or triggered ability." (Stifle) "Counter target spell, activated ability, or triggered ability." (Disallow)

The target filter uses `CardTypes` to record which stack-object categories are in scope: `"activatedAbility"`, `"triggeredAbility"`, and/or `"spell"` — parallel to the way `CounterSpellRule` uses `CardTypes: ["spell"]` for plain spell targets. Priority 80: more specific than `CounterSpellRule` (priority 50) and `CounterTargetTypeOrSubtypeSpellRule` (priority 80 by name-match conflict resolved by the "activated/triggered" literal tokens not being colour words, so no actual overlap there — but the explicit bump keeps ordering stable).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/CounterAbilityRule.cs)

### `CounterSpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Counter target spell." with optional color/type qualifiers and an optional "unless its controller pays {cost}" tail. Rule 701.6. Handles these filter dimensions in one consolidated surface: - color words (white, blue, black, red, green, colorless, multicolored) - non&lt;color&gt; predicates (nonblue, nonred, …) → Characteristics - bare card-type qualifier before "spell" (artifact, land, enchantment, …) → CardTypes - "with mana value N" → ManaValueComparison - "unless its controller pays {cost}" where cost is any mana expression ({1}, {X}, {1}{U}, etc.) — parsed via ManaCostParser

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/CounterSpellRule.cs)

### `CounterTargetTypeOrSubtypeSpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Counter target [Q1] or [Q2] spell." — Nullify ("creature or Aura"), Hisoka's Defiance ("Spirit or Arcane"). Token type classified by vocabulary, not position. Priority 80: overrides `CounterSpellRule` whose color-disjunction branch could otherwise capture two-token disjunctions.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/CounterTargetTypeOrSubtypeSpellRule.cs)

### `CounterUnlessPaysRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Counter target spell unless its controller pays {COST}." and typed variants: - "Counter target noncreature spell unless its controller pays {2}." (Spell Pierce) - "Counter target creature spell unless its controller pays {2}." - "Counter target spell unless its controller pays {3}." (Mana Leak) Pattern: "Counter target [TYPE] spell unless its controller pays {COST}." where TYPE is an optional spell-subset qualifier (creature, noncreature, instant, sorcery) and COST is any mana expression ({1}, {X}, {2}{U}, etc.). Priority 60: fires before `CounterSpellRule` (priority 50) so the unless-pays shape is owned by this rule. Rule 701.6: to counter a spell, it's put into its owner's graveyard; the unless clause (Rule 117.7) gives the target's controller an opportunity to pay a cost to prevent the counterspell from happening.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/CounterUnlessPaysRule.cs)

### `CreateCopyTokenRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Create a token that's a copy of target creature [you control|.]"

Maps the "create a token that's a copy of" pattern to `CopyEffect` with a `Target` reference. The token-vs-non-token distinction is engine territory (Rule 706.10); MAST records the copy-of-target relationship descriptively via `CopyEffect`.

Handles two variants:

"Create a token that's a copy of target creature you control." — Cackling Counterpart pattern "Create a token that's a copy of target creature." — bare target with no controller clause

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/CreateCopyTokenRule.cs)

### `CreateCopyTokenWithHasteAndEndStepExileRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

Recognises the token-copy-with-haste-and-end-step-exile pattern. CR 707.2: except-clause modifications are copiable values printed on the token. The trigger fires at the beginning of the end step (no "next" qualifier). Priority 67 -- above generic copy-token rules (65).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/CreateCopyTokenWithHasteAndEndStepExileRule.cs)

### `CreateCopyTokenWithTypeModificationSpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Create a token that's a copy of target [type1] or [type2] you control, except it's a [addedType] in addition to its other types." — Molten Duplication (DFT).

Maps a copy-a-token spell with a type-disjunction target and a `typeAdder` modification to a `CopyEffect` whose `Target` carries the disjunction as a multi-element `CardTypes` list and whose `Modifications` carry a single `TypeAdder` recording the added card type.

CR 707.2: "When copying an object, the copy acquires the copiable values of the original object's characteristics…" — the "except it's [X] in addition to its other types" clause overrides the copiable type values by adding one card type without removing existing ones. The `TypeAdder` modification encodes this additive type change structurally.

Priority 70: sits above the generic `CreateCopyTokenRule` (priority 65) so the more-specific type-modification form is claimed first.

Rule citations: CR 707.2 (copy — copiable values), CR 111.1 (token definition).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/CreateCopyTokenWithTypeModificationSpellRule.cs)

### `CreateCopyTokenYouControlWithHasteAndEndStepSacrificeRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

Recognises the token-copy-with-haste-and-end-step-sacrifice pattern for a creature the controller controls (Electroduplicate). CR 707.2: except-clause modifications are copiable values printed on the token. The trigger fires at the beginning of the end step (no "next" qualifier). Priority 68 — above generic copy-token rules (65) and the exile-variant (67).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/CreateCopyTokenYouControlWithHasteAndEndStepSacrificeRule.cs)

### `CreateTokenRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Create (a|X|&lt;num&gt;) &lt;P&gt;/&lt;T&gt; &lt;color&gt; [subtype ...] creature token(s) [with &lt;keyword&gt;]." Handles literal counts ("a"), variable counts ("X"), and numeric literals. Also handles predefined artifact tokens (Food, Treasure, Clue, Blood) which have no P/T and whose activated ability is reminder text only (Rule 107.10b). Reminder text is stripped by `SpellAbilityParser` before dispatch. The optional "with &lt;keyword&gt;" suffix captures a single granted keyword ability for the created token (e.g. "with flying", "with haste"). Multi-subtype tokens (e.g. "Knight Ally") are supported: the subtypes group captures all words between the color and "creature", which are split on whitespace to produce a `Subtypes` list. (CR 111.1: a token is a marker representing a permanent that isn't a card.)

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/CreateTokenRule.cs)

### `CreaturesCantBlockThisTurnRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

Handles two global "can't block this turn" shapes on spell oracle lines:

"Creatures without flying can't block this turn." (Falter, Cosmotronic Wave) — emits a `CantBlockEffect` whose `Target` references all creatures with `Characteristics = [Characteristic.Other("withoutFlying")]`. "Creatures can't block this turn." — emits a bare `CantBlockEffect` covering all creatures with no keyword filter.

Both shapes use `Each` (the restriction is not targeted; it applies to the whole population of creatures on the battlefield) and attach an `untilEndOfTurn` duration (Rule 509.1c — blocking restrictions resolve at the declare-blockers step of the current turn).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/CreaturesCantBlockThisTurnRule.cs)

### `CreaturesTargetPlayerGainKeywordRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Creatures target player/opponent controls gain [keyword] until end of turn." — spell-resolution mass keyword grant to all creatures controlled by a targeted player or opponent (e.g. Savage Alliance: "Creatures target player controls gain trample until end of turn."). The "target player/opponent" marks this as a one-shot imperative spell effect (Rule 113.3a) with a runtime-chosen controller axis, distinct from the declarative static form "Creatures you control have [keyword]." The controller filter is `Target` to record that the creatures' controller is determined at resolution from the targeting requirement.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/CreaturesTargetPlayerGainKeywordRule.cs)

### `DealDamageToAnyTargetAndGainLifeRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule, IMultiSpellRule

"[Self] deals N damage to any target and you gain N life." — burn+lifegain pattern in a single sentence (multi-effect-per-clause per MAST doctrine). Emits a flat [DealDamageEffect, GainLifeEffect] list via `TryMatchMulti`. CR 120.1: "Objects can deal damage to battles, creatures, planeswalkers, and players. This is generally detrimental to the object or player that receives that damage. An object that deals damage is the source of that damage." CR 119.3: "If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player's life total is adjusted accordingly." Representative card: Sacred Fire ({R}{W}, Instant with Flashback).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/DealDamageToAnyTargetAndGainLifeRule.cs)

### `DealDamageToEachCreatureTargetPlayerRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"[Subject] deals N damage to each creature target player/opponent controls." — spell-resolution sweeper damage to all creatures owned by a targeted player or opponent (e.g. Savage Alliance: "Savage Alliance deals 1 damage to each creature target opponent controls."). The "target player/opponent" marks this as a one-shot imperative spell effect (Rule 113.3a) whose damage scope is determined at resolution by the targeting requirement. The controller filter is `Target` to record that the creatures' controller is the targeted player/opponent rather than a static "opponent" or "you" axis.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/DealDamageToEachCreatureTargetPlayerRule.cs)

### `DealDamageToEachRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"[Self] deals N damage to each creature [and each player/opponent]." — sweeper damage. Covers three shapes:

"[Self] deals N damage to each creature" — creature-only wrath (Sweltering Suns) "[Self] deals N damage to each player [or opponent]" — player-only (Earthquake variants) "[Self] deals N damage to each creature and each player" — full sweeper (Rain of Embers)

The "each creature and each player" shape emits a `CompositeEffect` whose two children are `DealDamageEffect` nodes targeting `Each` (filtered to creature) and `EachPlayer` respectively. Single-population shapes emit a bare `DealDamageEffect`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/DealDamageToEachRule.cs)

### `DealDamageToTargetCreatureAndGainLifeRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule, IMultiSpellRule

"[Self] deals N damage to target creature and you gain N life." — burn+lifegain pattern targeting a creature specifically. Emits a flat [DealDamageEffect, GainLifeEffect] list via `TryMatchMulti`. Sibling of `DealDamageToAnyTargetAndGainLifeRule`, which covers the "any target" form. CR 120.1: "Objects can deal damage to battles, creatures, planeswalkers, and players. This is generally detrimental to the object or player that receives that damage. An object that deals damage is the source of that damage." CR 119.3: "If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player's life total is adjusted accordingly." CR 115.1: "Some spells and abilities require their controller to choose one or more targets for them. The targets are object(s) and/or player(s) the spell or ability will affect. These targets are declared as part of the process of putting the spell or ability on the stack." Representative card: Last Kiss ({2}{B}, Instant).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/DealDamageToTargetCreatureAndGainLifeRule.cs)

### `DestroyAllRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule, IMultiSpellRule

"Destroy all [filter]." — mass-destroy spell, with optional qualifier extensions.

Handled patterns (via `TryMatch`):

Single-token bare type: "Destroy all creatures." Single-token subtype: "Destroy all Plains." Conjunction of two card types: "Destroy all artifacts and enchantments." non- prefix + type: "Destroy all nonbasic lands.", "Destroy all nonland permanents." Color + type: "Destroy all white creatures."

Handled patterns (via `TryMatchMulti`):

"Destroy all [filter]. They can't be regenerated." — emits a single `DestroyEffect` with `CantBeRegenerated` set. The two-sentence form is not broken apart; regeneration is a modifier on the destruction event, not a sibling effect. (Wrath of God pattern.)

Emits a `DestroyEffect` whose `Target` has `Each` and the appropriate `ObjectFilter` slot populated.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/DestroyAllRule.cs)

### `DestroyMonocoloredCreatureRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Destroy target monocolored creature." — Ultimate Price. The "monocolored" qualifier surfaces on `IsMonocolored`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/DestroyMonocoloredCreatureRule.cs)

### `DestroyMulticoloredPermanentRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Destroy target multicolored permanent." — Null Elemental Blast. Sibling of `DestroyMonocoloredCreatureRule` on `IsMulticolored`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/DestroyMulticoloredPermanentRule.cs)

### `DestroyNTargetTypeRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Destroy {N} target {type}[s]." — exact-count targeted destroy spell. Examples:

"Destroy two target lands." (Rain of Salt) "Destroy three target artifacts."

The count lives on `Quantity` as a `LiteralQuantity`, not on the effect itself, because the oracle phrase is "{N} target {type}", not "destroy {type}, repeat N times". CR 701.8a: "To destroy a permanent, move it from the battlefield to its owner's graveyard." CR 115.1: "Some spells and abilities require their controller to choose one or more targets for them. The targets are object(s) and/or player(s) the spell or ability will affect. These targets are declared as part of casting..."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/DestroyNTargetTypeRule.cs)

### `DestroyTargetColorDisjunctionRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Destroy target [color1] or [color2] [type]." — destroy a target whose color matches any of the listed colors. Covers patterns such as:

"Destroy target green or white creature." (Deathmark) "Destroy target black or red creature that's attacking or blocking." (Surge of Righteousness)

The `Colors` field carries the "any of these" semantics (Rule 105.1–105.3): an object passes the filter if it has at least one of the listed colors. Strips reminder-text qualifiers (e.g. "that's attacking or blocking") into `Characteristics` so the core color+type shape always resolves.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/DestroyTargetColorDisjunctionRule.cs)

### `DestroyTargetOpponentControlledRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Destroy target [type] you don't control." — single-target destroy of a permanent controlled by an opponent.

Rule 701.8 (destroy) + CR 109.5 ("you don't control" = opponent-controlled). The "you don't control" qualifier maps to `Opponent` on the `ObjectFilter`. Examples: Vandalblast ("Destroy target artifact you don't control.").

This rule is anchored so it cannot shadow the broader `DestroyTargetSimpleRule` patterns (which require no controller qualifier). Any card type accepted by `DestroyCardTypeMap` is supported.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/DestroyTargetOpponentControlledRule.cs)

### `DestroyTargetSimpleRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Destroy target [filter]." — single-target destroy spell. Covers bare card-type targets (creature, artifact, land, …) and richer filter phrases via `ParseTargetFilter`:

Subtype-only: "Spirit", "Human" Color + card type: "black creature", "white creature" non- prefix + card type: "nonbasic land"

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/DestroyTargetSimpleRule.cs)

### `DestroyTargetStateQualifiedRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Destroy target [state] creature." — handles state-based qualifiers that describe a creature's current game status rather than its card-type or color.

"Destroy target tapped creature." — e.g., Vengeance "Destroy target attacking creature." — e.g., Immolating Glare "Destroy target blocking creature." "Destroy target attacking or blocking creature."

State words go into `Characteristics` per the established open-ended-qualifier convention (CR 109.3 — characteristics include any quality that can be true or false of a permanent at any moment).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/DestroyTargetStateQualifiedRule.cs)

### `DestroyTargetTypeDisjunctionRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Destroy target [type1] or [type2]." / "Destroy target [type1], [type2], or [type3]." Multi-element `CardTypes` as the disjunction. Also handles a keyword qualifier on the last element: "Destroy target [type1], [type2], or [typeN] with [keyword]." e.g., "Destroy target artifact, enchantment, or creature with flying." The qualifier is stored in `Characteristics`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/DestroyTargetTypeDisjunctionRule.cs)

### `DestroyTargetWithFilterRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Destroy target creature with [filter]." — destroy a single creature that satisfies an intrinsic-characteristic filter appended via "with". Handles two filter shapes:

<b>Keyword filter:</b> "with flying", "with defender", "with trample" → `Characteristics` gets the keyword (lowercased). Examples: Wing Snare ("Destroy target creature with flying."). <b>Power comparison:</b> "with power N or greater / N or less" → `PowerComparison` with `GreaterThanOrEqual` / `LessThanOrEqual`. Examples: Smite the Monstrous ("Destroy target creature with power 4 or greater."). <b>Toughness comparison:</b> "with toughness N or greater / N or less" → `ToughnessComparison`.

This rule fires before `DestroyTargetSimpleRule` would silently reject these phrases; `ParseTargetFilter` cannot parse multi-word "creature with X" phrases.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/DestroyTargetWithFilterRule.cs)

### `DiscardEachPlayerRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Each player [may] discard a card." — Death of Gwen Stacy first effect. Optional follow-up "Each player who doesn't loses N life." lands on `IfYouDoNot`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/DiscardEachPlayerRule.cs)

### `DiscardHandSpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule, IMultiSpellRule

Handles the "discard their hand" / "discard your hand" spell-effect surface, with an optional ", then draws [N] cards" continuation.

Covered oracle shapes:

"Each player discards their hand, then draws seven cards." (Wheel of Fortune) "Target player discards their hand, then draws four cards." (Wheel and Deal) "Each player discards their hand, then draws cards equal to the greatest number of cards a player discarded this way." (Windfall) Standalone variants without a draw continuation: "Each player discards their hand.", "Discard your hand."

The discard count is modelled as a `DerivedQuantity` with `CardsInHand` — descriptively, "hand" in oracle text means "all cards in your hand at the time the effect resolves."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/DiscardHandSpellRule.cs)

### `DiscardTargetPlayerRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Target player discards [a/N] card[s]." and "Target opponent discards [a/N] card[s]."

Covered oracle shapes:

"Target player discards a card." (Raven's Crime, Blackmail) "Target player discards two cards." (Mind Rot) "Target opponent discards a card." (Duress variants)

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/DiscardTargetPlayerRule.cs)

### `DiscardThenDrawSpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"You may discard [N] card(s). If you do, draw [M] card(s)." — Abandon Attachments. Head `DiscardCardsEffect` with IsOptional=true and IfYouDo continuation.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/DiscardThenDrawSpellRule.cs)

### `DoubleLifeTotalSpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Double target player's life total." — Beacon of Immortality's first clause.

Rule 701.10d: "To double a player's life total, the player gains or loses an amount of life such that their new life total is twice its current value." MAST records the oracle's phrasing as a `DoubleLifeTotalEffect`; the gain/lose arithmetic is engine territory.

GUARD: fully anchored (^ … $). Matches only the "Double target player's life total" shape. Does not match "double a creature's power" (different oracle template), "double [your] life total" (no "target"), or any substring of a compound sentence.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/DoubleLifeTotalSpellRule.cs)

### `DrawAndCreateTreasureRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule, IMultiSpellRule

"Draw [N] cards and create [N] Treasure tokens." — Unexpected Windfall / Pirate's Pillage pattern. Expands to a flat [`DrawCardsEffect`, `CreateTokenEffect`] pair so both effects sit as siblings on `Effects`.

Reminder text on the Treasure token ("They're artifacts with...") is stripped by `SpellAbilityParser` before dispatch, so this rule matches the bare "Draw two cards and create two Treasure tokens" form.

Rule 120.1 (draw), Rule 111.9 (Treasure token).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/DrawAndCreateTreasureRule.cs)

### `DrawCardsAtNextUpkeepRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Draw [N] card(s) at the beginning of the next turn's upkeep." Delayed draw shape: the draw effect carries an `AtBeginningOfNextUpkeepDuration` to record that it resolves at the start of the next turn's upkeep step (Rule 313.1). Covers literal and word counts (a, one, two …). Examples: Jolt, Ray of Erasure, Force Void, Blessed Wine, Gravebind, Bone Harvest.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/DrawCardsAtNextUpkeepRule.cs)

### `DrawCardsSimpleRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

Draw-cards spell-resolution rule. Handles three oracle clause forms:

"Draw [N] card(s)." — controller draws (Player = You). "Target player draws [N] card(s)." — targeted player draws (Player = Target + player filter). "Target opponent draws [N] card(s)." — targeted opponent draws (Player = Opponent).

Count tokens: literal words (one–ten), decimal digits, or variable slots (X/Y/Z).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/DrawCardsSimpleRule.cs)

### `DrawThenDiscardRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule, IMultiSpellRule

"Draw [N] card(s), then discard [M] card(s)." — draw-then-discard loot pattern. Examples:

"Draw four cards, then discard two cards." — Prying Eyes "Draw two cards, then discard a card." — Ghastly Discovery (first line)

Emits a flat `[DrawCardsEffect, DiscardCardsEffect]` list via `TryMatchMulti`. Both effects target the controller (Player = You). The single-effect `TryMatch` always returns false; the multi-effect path is the only active route.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/DrawThenDiscardRule.cs)

### `EachSacrificesRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Each [player|opponent] sacrifices [a|an|N] [type]." — distributed sacrifice addressed to all players or all opponents (Rule 701.21a — Sacrifice).

"Each player sacrifices a creature." (Innocent Blood) "Each player sacrifices two creatures." (Barter in Blood) "Each opponent sacrifices a creature." (edict variant) "Each opponent sacrifices an artifact." (Visions of Ruin variant) "Each opponent sacrifices a permanent." (generic)

Emits a `SacrificeEffect` whose `Target` carries `EachPlayer` or `EachOpponent` and an `ObjectFilter` restricting the sacrificed object type.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/EachSacrificesRule.cs)

### `ExileAllRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

Mass-exile spell rules. Two oracle shapes are handled:

"Exile all creatures." — removes every creature on the battlefield (the default game zone). `Zone` is left null (battlefield-scope is the implicit default for permanent references). Rule 701.13a (exile action) + Rule 400.1 (zones). "Exile all [type] cards from all graveyards." — graveyard-scope mass exile. Sets `Zone` to `Graveyard`. Covers Scavenging Ooze cousins and clean-the-graveyard spells. Rule 701.13a + Rule 400.2g (graveyard zone).

Both shapes emit an `ExileEffect` whose `Target` carries `Each` and the appropriate `ObjectFilter` slot populated.

Priority 60 — same band as `DestroyAllRule`. The leading "Exile all" anchor is mutually exclusive with all "Exile target" rules (priority 50–70), so no shadowing risk.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ExileAllRule.cs)

### `ExileAttackingCreatureUnlessRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Exile target attacking creature unless its controller pays {X}." — Excise.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ExileAttackingCreatureUnlessRule.cs)

### `ExileColorDisjunctionPermanentRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Exile target [color1] or [color2] permanent." — Celestial Purge. Priority 80: must override `ExileTypeDisjunctionRule` whose regex would otherwise greedily capture "black or red permanent" as three card types.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ExileColorDisjunctionPermanentRule.cs)

### `ExileGraveyardsRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Exile any number of target players' graveyards." — Death of Gwen Stacy ch. 3.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ExileGraveyardsRule.cs)

### `ExileMonocoloredPermanentRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Exile target monocolored permanent." — Vanishing Verse.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ExileMonocoloredPermanentRule.cs)

### `ExileSelfThenReturnTransformedRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule, IMultiSpellRule

"Exile this [permanent], then return it to the battlefield transformed under your control." — the final chapter of a transforming Saga (The Legend of Roku, chapter III). Returns the flat two-effect list [exile{Self}, returnToBattlefield].

The "it" in the second clause back-references the exiled permanent, which re-enters transformed (back face up, CR 712) under the controller's control. CR 701.13 (exile); CR 712 (transform); CR 715 (Saga). The exile-then-return is the standard flicker-to-transform idiom; the permanent's identity across the zone change is engine bookkeeping.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ExileSelfThenReturnTransformedRule.cs)

### `ExileTargetCardFromGraveyardRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Exile target card from a graveyard." / "Exile up to one target card from a graveyard." — single-card graveyard-exile spell effect (Rule 701.7). Handles both the bare "target card" form (Purify the Grave) and the "up to one target card" form (Heritage Reclamation). Priority 80: must override `ExileGraveyardsRule` which targets graveyards themselves (the prior agent's noted ordering pair).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ExileTargetCardFromGraveyardRule.cs)

### `ExileTargetControlledCreatureThenReturnOwnerRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Exile target creature you control, then return it to the battlefield under its owner's control." — the Ephemerate / blink-to-owner spell pattern.

This is the spell-wrapper of the classic blink (flicker) action where the returned permanent comes back under its <em>owner's</em> control rather than the controller's (contrast with `FlickerTargetPermanentsSpellRule`, which returns under "your control"). The oracle text uses a curly apostrophe (U+2019) in "owner’s", matched verbatim.

The composite shape mirrors the `FlickerTargetPermanentsSpellRule`: an `ExileEffect` on the chosen creature, followed by a `ReturnToBattlefieldEffect` whose target is the linked exiled card (CR 607.2 / ADR 0004 reference-not-resolution), addressed via `ExiledWith` = `{Kind: Self}`. "under its owner's control" maps to `Owner` on `UnderControl` (CR 400.6).

The pattern is anchored (^…$) to prevent substring matches inside more-specific sibling rules (e.g. the "non-Angel" or "another target" families). CR 701.13 (exile). CR 400.6 (owner/controller distinction).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ExileTargetControlledCreatureThenReturnOwnerRule.cs)

### `ExileTargetLandRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Exile target land." — Caustic Rain and similar sorceries that exile a single land permanent with no color, subtype, or disjunction qualifier. The bare "target land" shape is not covered by `ExileTypeDisjunctionRule` (which requires two or more types joined by "or") or the color/monocolored variants, so this rule handles the simplest case. Rule 701.13 (exile action) + Rule 205.3j (land as card type).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ExileTargetLandRule.cs)

### `ExileTargetQualifiedRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Exile target [state] creature" and "Exile target creature with power N or less/greater." Covers:

State qualifiers: "Exile target tapped creature", "Exile target attacking creature" Power filter (less-or-equal): "Exile target creature with power 3 or less" Power filter (greater-or-equal): "Exile target creature with power 4 or greater"

Priority 70 — runs before `ExileTargetSimpleRule` (50), which would otherwise claim the raw text via its FilterPattern fallback and then fail to produce a filter for the "with power N" or state-adjective shapes. Rule 701.13 (exile action). Power comparisons: Rule 208.1 (power is a characteristic of creature cards).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ExileTargetQualifiedRule.cs)

### `ExileTargetSimpleRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Exile target [filter]." — single-target exile spell. Covers bare card-type targets (creature, artifact, enchantment, permanent, …) and richer filter phrases via `ParseTargetFilter`:

Subtype-only: "Spirit", "Human" Color + card type: "black creature", "white artifact" non- prefix + card type: "nonbasic land"

Coexistence with `ExileTargetLandRule`: both rules dispatch at priority 50; alphabetical ordering puts ExileTargetLandRule ahead of ExileTargetSimpleRule, so the bare "land" shape is still claimed by the specialised rule. This rule handles all other card-type filters. Rule 701.13 (exile action) + Rule 205.3 (card types).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ExileTargetSimpleRule.cs)

### `ExileTopCardsPlayThisTurnRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Exile the top N cards of your library. You may play them this turn." — a current-turn-bounded impulse where all N exiled cards stay playable from exile until end of turn (Jeska's Will).

Modeled as a single `ImpulseEffect` with `RemainExiled` (none kept in hand; all N remain exiled) and an inherited `Duration` of `EndOfTurn`. The two oracle sentences are one game concept — "them" back-references the exiled pile — so they collapse into one node rather than a separate exile + permission. CR 406 (exile zone); CR 701.13 (exile). Sibling of `ExileTopCardsPlayUntilTimeRule` which uses "until the end of your next turn".

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ExileTopCardsPlayThisTurnRule.cs)

### `ExileTopCardsPlayUntilTimeRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Exile the top N cards of your library. Until [time], you may play those cards." — a duration-bounded impulse where all N exiled cards stay playable from exile for a stated window (The Legend of Roku, chapter I).

Modeled as a single `ImpulseEffect` with `RemainExiled` (none kept in hand; all N remain exiled) and an inherited `Duration` bounding the play window. The two oracle sentences are one game concept — "those cards" back-references the exiled pile — so they collapse into one node rather than a separate exile + permission (ADR 0004: impulse is a cohesive one-shot; the "cards playable from exile" cluster is recovered by consumer projection over {ImpulseEffect, MayPlayFromExile}). CR 406 (exile zone); CR 701.13 (exile).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ExileTopCardsPlayUntilTimeRule.cs)

### `ExileTypeDisjunctionRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Exile target [type1] or [type2]." — Gravkill etc. Multi-element CardTypes is the disjunction.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ExileTypeDisjunctionRule.cs)

### `FightRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

Recognises fight-spell oracle text in the form:

"Target creature you control fights target creature you don't control." "Target creature you control fights target creature an opponent controls."

CR 701.14 (Fight keyword action). Reminder text "(Each deals damage equal to its power to the other.)" is stripped by the parenthetical-removal pass before spell rules run, so it does not appear in the input text here.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/FightRule.cs)

### `FlickerTargetPermanentsSpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Exile [N] target [type list] you control, then return those cards to the battlefield under your control." — the spell-side flicker (blink) of Ghostly Flicker.

The line is a single composite (CR 603.2 governs the triggered sibling, Displacer Kitten; this is the spell wrapper of the same flicker effect): an `ExileEffect` on the chosen permanents, then a `ReturnToBattlefieldEffect` of the just-exiled cards. "those cards" is NOT free text — it is the linked exiled reference (CR 607.2 / ADR 0004 "reference, not resolution"): a `Designated` card in `Exile` exiled with this object (`ExiledWith = {Kind: Self}`), exactly the Petravark return shape. The two effects sit in one `CompositeEffect` because the oracle states them as a single "exile …, then return …" action.

"two" is a structured cardinality on the target reference's `Quantity` (a `LiteralQuantity`), not a literal repeated node. "artifacts, creatures, and/or lands you control" is a structured multi-type disjunction filter (<c>CardTypes = ["artifact", "creature", "land"]</c> + `Controller = You`) — any chosen permanent may be any of the three types. "under your control" rides on `UnderControl` as a `You` reference (CR 400.6).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/FlickerTargetPermanentsSpellRule.cs)

### `GainActivatedAbilitySpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

Recognises the "Until end of turn, target creature gains &lt;quoted activated ability&gt;." shape — i.e. an instant/sorcery that grants a target creature a full activated ability (cost + effect, quoted in oracle text) for the rest of the turn. Example: Retraction Helix (BNG) — "Until end of turn, target creature gains &quot;{T}: Return target nonland permanent to its owner's hand.&quot;" The granted ability is modelled as a nested `ActivatedAbility` inside a `GainAbilityEffect`; the duration is `untilEndOfTurn`. CR 613.1c (Layer 6 — ability-granting effects); CR 611 (continuous effects with duration); CR 113.6 — abilities granted by effects are still full-fledged abilities of the gaining permanent. Priority 85: must fire before the bare keyword-grant rules (TargetCreatureGainsKeywordRule, priority 50) since this rule is more specific (the quoted text inside the gains clause is a full activated ability, not a keyword).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/GainActivatedAbilitySpellRule.cs)

### `GainLifeSpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"You gain N life." — spell-resolution life-gain. Recuperate's first modal option. Also handles "You gain life equal to the life lost this way." — derived-quantity gain linked to a preceding LoseLifeEffect (Rule 119.3). Blood Tithe's second clause. Also handles "Target player gains N life." — life-gain whose subject is a targeted player rather than the controller (CR 119.3: "If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player's life total is adjusted accordingly."). Also handles "If this spell was kicked, you gain life equal to the life lost this way." — a conditioned life-gain gated on the kicker cost having been paid (CR 702.33d–e, Blood Tribute). Uses the `Condition` field (mirrors `Condition`). Representative card: Soothing Balm ({1}{W}, Instant).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/GainLifeSpellRule.cs)

### `InvestigateSpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Investigate." — Rule 701.28 keyword action. Creates a Clue token. Oracle lines containing only the keyword action (possibly with a reminder-text parenthetical, which is stripped by SpellAbilityParser before dispatch) produce a bare `InvestigateEffect`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/InvestigateSpellRule.cs)

### `ItGainsKeywordSpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"It gains [keyword] until end of turn." — a standalone sentence within a multi-sentence spell ability where "it" is a back-reference to an object named in the preceding sentence (e.g. the token created by a copy effect).

Emits a `GainAbilityEffect` targeting `It` with an `untilEndOfTurn` duration. The `It` reference is the standard MAST pronoun-back-reference (Rule 109.2 — game objects referenced by "it") and connects to the immediately preceding object in the same ability's effect list.

This rule handles the trailing "It gains haste until end of turn." sentence that appears in copy-token patterns (Molten Duplication) and similar spells. It is intentionally limited to a single keyword per sentence so the match is unambiguous and does not collide with the multi-effect `ThreatenRule` (which handles the full three-sentence Threaten / Act of Treason pattern as a unit).

Priority 55: above the generic `TargetCreatureGainsKeywordRule` (no priority = default 50) so this "it gains" shape is claimed before the "target … gains" shape could incorrectly fire.

Rule citations: CR 109.2 (pronoun reference), CR 611 (continuous effects with duration), CR 702.10 (haste).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ItGainsKeywordSpellRule.cs)

### `ItsControllerLosesLifeRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Its controller loses N life." — life-loss whose subject back-references the controller of a permanent named in a prior sentence (Rule 119.3: "If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player's life total is adjusted accordingly."). This rule handles the trailing sentence in patterns like: "Destroy target land. Its controller loses 2 life." (Spreading Rot) "Its controller" is modelled as `Controller`, which is an anaphoric reference to the controller of the previously mentioned object (the destroyed land). No runtime tracking is introduced — MAST describes the card, it does not execute it. CR 119.3 (verbatim): "If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player's life total is adjusted accordingly." CR 701.8a (verbatim): "To destroy a permanent, move it from the battlefield to its owner's graveyard."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ItsControllerLosesLifeRule.cs)

### `LookAtHandRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Look at target player's hand." — the Peek pattern (Rule 701.12). The controller looks at all cards in the named player's hand. "Their hand" means every card present, modelled as `CardsInHand` to mirror the `DiscardHandSpellRule` convention for "their hand" phrasing.

"Look at target player's hand." — Peek (INV) "Look at target opponent's hand." — Ostracize variant

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/LookAtHandRule.cs)

### `LookAtTopPutInHandRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

Recognises the Impulse/Strategic Planning pattern: "Look at the top N cards of your library. Put one of them into your hand and the rest into your graveyard." or the bottom-of-library variant: "Look at the top N cards of your library. Put one of them into your hand and the rest on the bottom of your library in any order." Both sentences are part of a single `ImpulseEffect`. The rule matches the full two-sentence text before the sentence-bundle path splits it, via the single-effect `TryMatch` path which receives the whole clause text. Examples:

"Look at the top three cards of your library. Put one of them into your hand and the rest into your graveyard." — Strategic Planning "Look at the top four cards of your library. Put one of them into your hand and the rest on the bottom of your library in any order." — Impulse

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/LookAtTopPutInHandRule.cs)

### `LoseLifeEachSpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Each player loses N life." and "Each opponent loses N life." — spell-resolution life-loss addressed to all players or all opponents (Rule 119.3, Rule 113.3a). Examples: Crushing Disappointment, Risky Shortcut (EachPlayer); Blood Tithe (EachOpponent).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/LoseLifeEachSpellRule.cs)

### `MaddeningCacophonyRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule, IMultiSpellRule

Kicker-gated mill-opponent-or-mill-half-library spell pattern. Handles the two-sentence oracle text: <code> Each opponent mills [N] cards. If this spell was kicked, instead each opponent mills half their library, rounded up. </code> Produces two sibling `MillEffect` nodes on `Effects`:

Base mill — count N, player EachOpponent, no condition. Kicked replacement mill — count = half opponent's library rounded up, player EachOpponent, condition = `KeywordCostPaidCondition` Kicker (CR 702.33d–e). The "instead" is expressed by the condition: when the condition holds the kicked effect supersedes the base.

The kicked count is a `CalculatedQuantity` (operation "half", rounding "up") over a `CountQuantity` of cards in the opponent's library.

CR 702.33 (Kicker): "Kicker is a static ability … 'Kicker [cost]' means 'You may pay an additional [cost] as you cast this spell.'" CR 702.33d: "a spell has been kicked" when its kicker cost was paid. CR 702.33e: effects gated on the kicked state are "if this spell was kicked" clauses.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/MaddeningCacophonyRule.cs)

### `MassAnthemSpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

Recognises the mass P/T-modification shape for creature subsets: "Creatures you control get +N/+M until end of turn." "Creatures you control get -N/-M until end of turn." "Creatures your opponents control get -N/-M until end of turn." "All creatures get +N/+M until end of turn." "All creatures get -N/-M until end of turn." "Attacking creatures get +N/+M until end of turn." "Blocking creatures get +N/+M until end of turn." This is the mass anthem/debuff version. The single-target "Target creature gets +N/+M ..." shape is handled separately by `ModifyPTSpellRule`. Examples:

"Creatures you control get +1/+1 until end of turn." (Charge) "Creatures you control get +0/+4 until end of turn." (Bar the Door) "All creatures get -1/-1 until end of turn." (Shrivel) "All creatures get -2/-2 until end of turn." (Infest) "All creatures get -4/-4 until end of turn." (Languish) "Attacking creatures get +2/+0 until end of turn." (Army of Allah) "Blocking creatures get +0/+3 until end of turn." (Piety) "Creatures your opponents control get -1/-1 until end of turn." (Cower in Fear) "Creatures your opponents control get -1/-1 until end of turn." (Make Obsolete)

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/MassAnthemSpellRule.cs)

### `MillSpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

Spell-resolution mill keyword action. Rule 701.17. Handles five oracle clause forms:

"Mill N cards." — controller (Player = You). "Target player mills N cards." — targeted player (Player = Target + player filter). "Target opponent mills N cards." — targeted opponent (Player = Opponent). "Target player mills half their library, rounded down." — half-library mill, rounded down (Traumatize). CR 701.17a: to mill a number of cards is to put that many cards from the top of the library into the graveyard. "Target player mills half their library, rounded up." — half-library mill, rounded up.

For the triggered-ability side, see `MillTriggeredRule`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/MillSpellRule.cs)

### `ModifyPTAndGainKeywordControlledSpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule, IMultiSpellRule

Recognises the controller-qualified composite buff shape: "Target creature you control gets +N/+M and gains &lt;keyword(s)&gt; until end of turn." This covers protective instants like Dive Down (XLN) where the caster targets only one of their own creatures to apply both a P/T modification and a keyword grant. The "you control" qualifier restricts the target to permanents the active player controls (CR 109.5 — controller filter on target). Emits a flat list via `TryMatchMulti`: `[ModifyPTEffect (Target=you-control creature), GainAbilityEffect (Target=It), …]`, one `GainAbilityEffect` per keyword, all sharing a single `UntilEndOfTurnDuration` instance.

The single-effect `TryMatch` always returns false so the flat-list path is the only active route.

CR citations: 702.11b — "Hexproof" on a permanent means "This permanent can't be the target of spells or abilities your opponents control." CR 613.1 — "The values of an object's characteristics are determined by starting with the actual object… Then all applicable continuous effects are applied in a series of layers in the following order:" (P/T and ability-granting are continuous effects).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ModifyPTAndGainKeywordControlledSpellRule.cs)

### `ModifyPTAndGainKeywordSpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule, IMultiSpellRule

Recognises the composite buff shape: "Target creature gets +N/+M and gains &lt;keyword(s)&gt; until end of turn." This covers any single-target creature buff that combines a P/T modifier with one or more keyword grants, all with until-end-of-turn duration. The keywords group is one or more keyword names joined by " and ". Examples:

"Target creature gets +2/+2 and gains trample until end of turn." "Target creature gets +3/+0 and gains first strike and reach until end of turn." "Target creature gets +1/+1 and gains flying and first strike until end of turn."

Emits a flat list via `TryMatchMulti`: `[ModifyPTEffect, GainAbilityEffect, …]`, one `GainAbilityEffect` per keyword, all sharing a single `UntilEndOfTurnDuration` instance.

The single-effect `TryMatch` always returns false so the flat-list path is the only active route.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ModifyPTAndGainKeywordSpellRule.cs)

### `ModifyPTAsLongAsSpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

Recognises the P/T-modification shape with an "as long as [condition]" duration: "Target creature gets +N/+M for as long as [condition]." "Target creature gets -N/-M for as long as [condition]." The condition text (e.g. "this artifact remains tapped") is stored verbatim on `Condition` — not parsed further. This mirrors how `TryParseAsLongAsStaticGrant` handles the same duration suffix on static ability grants. Examples:

"Target creature gets +1/+1 for as long as this artifact remains tapped." (Tawnos's Weaponry) "Target creature gets +0/+2 for as long as this artifact remains tapped." (Spirit Shield) "Target creature gets +0/+3 for as long as this artifact remains tapped." (Endoskeleton)

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ModifyPTAsLongAsSpellRule.cs)

### `ModifyPTConjunctionRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

Rookie-Mistake shape (single-effect dispatch path): "Until end of turn, target creature gets +0/+2 and another target creature gets -2/-0." Wrapped in a `CompositeEffect`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ModifyPTConjunctionRule.cs)

### `ModifyPTSpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

Recognises the bare P/T-modification shape (no keyword conjunction): "Target creature gets +N/+M until end of turn." "Target creature gets -N/-M until end of turn." "Target creature gets +X/+0 until end of turn." — variable X form "It gets +N/+M until end of turn." — pronoun back-reference form This is the simple single-effect version. The composite "gets +N/+M and gains &lt;keyword&gt; until end of turn" case is handled separately by `ModifyPTAndGainKeywordSpellRule` and is tried first at higher priority. Examples:

"Target creature gets +3/+3 until end of turn." (Giant Growth) "Target creature gets +4/+4 until end of turn." (Titanic Growth) "Target creature gets -2/-2 until end of turn." (Disfigure) "Target creature gets -3/-3 until end of turn." (Last Gasp) "Target creature gets +X/+0 until end of turn." (Howl from Beyond) "It gets +2/+4 until end of turn." (Inspirit — second sentence)

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ModifyPTSpellRule.cs)

### `MustAttackTargetRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Target creature attacks this turn if able." — Boiling Blood. Rule 508.1d.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/MustAttackTargetRule.cs)

### `MustBeBlockedTargetRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Target creature must be blocked this turn if able." — Irresistible Prey. Rule 509.1c.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/MustBeBlockedTargetRule.cs)

### `MustBlockTargetRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Target creature blocks this turn if able." — Culling Mark. Rule 509.1c.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/MustBlockTargetRule.cs)

### `PeerIntoTheAbyssRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule, IMultiSpellRule

"Target player draws cards equal to half the number of cards in their library and loses half their life. Round up each time." — the Peer into the Abyss draw-half-library-then-lose-half-life pattern.

Both quantities are `CalculatedQuantity` with `Operation="half"` and `Rounding="up"`. The draw count is based on a `CountQuantity` of cards in the target player's library (Zone=Library, Controller=Target). The life-loss amount is based on a `DerivedQuantity` with `DerivedFrom=LifeTotal` (the target player's life total is implicit from the `Player` reference).

CR 119.3: "If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player's life total is adjusted accordingly."

The trailing "Round up each time." sentence is a printed instruction clarifying rounding direction for both effects; it is encoded structurally as `Rounding="up"` on each `CalculatedQuantity` rather than as a separate effect.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/PeerIntoTheAbyssRule.cs)

### `PreventAllCombatDamageThisTurnRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Prevent all combat damage that would be dealt this turn." Fog-effect instants (Rule 615.1). Combat damage only; no target (blanket).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/PreventAllCombatDamageThisTurnRule.cs)

### `PreventDamageThisTurnRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Prevent the next N damage that would be dealt to target [type] this turn." (Rule 615.1) "Prevent the next N damage that would be dealt to any target this turn." (Rule 615.1)

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/PreventDamageThisTurnRule.cs)

### `ProliferateSpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule, IMultiSpellRule

Proliferate (Rule 701.27) in a spell or activated-ability context.

Handled patterns (via `TryMatch`):

"Proliferate." — standalone spell or activated effect.

Handled patterns (via `TryMatchMulti`):

"Destroy target [filter], then proliferate." — emits a flat [`DestroyEffect`, `ProliferateEffect`] list. The "then proliferate" suffix is a sequencing marker; both effects are siblings on the ability's Effects list. (Spread the Sickness pattern.)

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ProliferateSpellRule.cs)

### `PutAnyNumberFromGYOnTopRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Put any number of target creature cards from your graveyard on top of your library." Covers the unbounded multi-target graveyard-to-library-top zone change. Examples: Forever Young (ELD), Gravepurge (DTK), Footbottom Feast (CMD). Count is modelled as `UpToQuantity` with `MaxValue` maximum — the same convention used for "choose any number" modal abilities (Rule 700.2). Source zone on `Zone`; count on `Quantity`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/PutAnyNumberFromGYOnTopRule.cs)

### `PutCounterOnEachRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Put a [counter-type] counter on each [type] you control." — Titania's Boon. Count=1. Matches the mass-counter shape where the controller distributes one counter to every qualifying permanent they control, e.g.: "Put a +1/+1 counter on each creature you control."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/PutCounterOnEachRule.cs)

### `PutCounterOnSelfRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Put a [counter-type] counter on this [creature|permanent|...]." — the self-referential counter placement. Count=1.

This surfaces the put-counters-on-self shape on the SPELL rule chain so it is reachable when a modal option body is dispatched as a `SpellAbility`. The "When this creature enters, choose one — • Put a +1/+1 counter on this creature. • ..." family (Saurian Symbiote) routes each bullet body back through the classifier+registry, which classifies a bare "Put a … counter on this creature." imperative as a spell. The triggered `PutCountersTriggeredRule` already handles the same text on the triggered chain; this is its spell-chain sibling so the mode parses to its established `PutCountersEffect` shape rather than falling through to an unparsed ability.

CR 122.1: "A counter is a marker placed on an object or player that modifies its characteristics and/or interacts with a rule or effect." Handles both P/T notation counters ("+1/+1", "-1/-1") and named word counters. Distinct from `PutCounterOnTargetRule` only in subject: "this [permanent]" → a `Self` reference rather than a targeted one.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/PutCounterOnSelfRule.cs)

### `PutCounterOnTargetRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Put a [counter-type] counter on target [type]." — Boon of Safety, Battlegrowth. Count=1. Handles both word counters ("shield") and P/T notation counters ("+1/+1", "-1/-1").

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/PutCounterOnTargetRule.cs)

### `PutCountersTargetSubtypeDisjunctionRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Put N [counter type] counters on target [Subtype1] or [Subtype2] you control." Subtype-disjunction target (Rule 205.3) with an explicit Controller=You.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/PutCountersTargetSubtypeDisjunctionRule.cs)

### `PutOnTopOfLibraryRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Put target [type] on top of its owner's library." Covers single-target library-top zone change — the Time Ebb / Temporal Spring pattern.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/PutOnTopOfLibraryRule.cs)

### `ReturnCommanderToHandRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Put your commander into your hand from the command zone." — Road of Return.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ReturnCommanderToHandRule.cs)

### `ReturnFromGraveyardToHandRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Return target [filter] card from your graveyard to your hand." Also handles the untyped form: "Return target card from your graveyard to your hand." Source zone on `Zone`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ReturnFromGraveyardToHandRule.cs)

### `ReturnGraveyardToBattlefieldRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Return target [filter] card from your graveyard to the battlefield." Source zone on `Zone`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ReturnGraveyardToBattlefieldRule.cs)

### `ReturnKickedDrawRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule, IMultiSpellRule

Two-sentence compound spell: "Return target nonland permanent to its owner's hand. If this spell was kicked, draw a card."

Sentence 1 is a `ReturnToHandEffect` targeting "nonland permanent". Sentence 2 is a `DrawCardsEffect` guarded by `Condition.Text = "this spell was kicked"`. Both effects sit as siblings on `Effects`; the condition is descriptive, not a runtime branch — consistent with the MAST "describes cards, does not execute them" doctrine.

Rule 702.33 (kicker), Rule 120.1 (draw). Priority 80 so this supersedes the general sentence-bundle path, which would try to dispatch each sentence independently and fail on the conditional-draw fragment.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ReturnKickedDrawRule.cs)

### `ReturnMultipleFromGraveyardRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Return up to (one|two|three|…) target [filter] cards from your graveyard to your hand." Covers the bounded multi-target graveyard-retrieval pattern where the count is an "up to N" ceiling, e.g. Soul Salvage / March of the Returned. Source zone on `Zone`; count on `Quantity`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ReturnMultipleFromGraveyardRule.cs)

### `ReturnTargetSpellToHandRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Return target spell [you don't control|an opponent controls|you control] to its owner's hand." Handles the special case of returning a spell on the stack (Zone: Stack, CardTypes: ["spell"]) to its owner's hand. This differs from `ReturnTargetToHandRule` which handles permanent types (creature, artifact, land, etc.) but not spells. Rule 400.1 — zones; Rule 608.2b — spell on the stack is a game object with a controller. "A spell you don't control" means the spell is controlled by an opponent (CR 108.4).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ReturnTargetSpellToHandRule.cs)

### `ReturnTargetToHandRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Return [target|all] [modifier] [type] to [its owner's|their owners'|your] hand[s]."
