Covers single-target bounce, mass bounce ("Return all X"), "you own" ownership qualifier, "non*" prefix modifiers (e.g. "nonland permanent"), color predicates ("green permanents", "nonblue creatures"), and state adjectives ("tapped creature").

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ReturnTargetToHandRule.cs)

### `RevealChooseDiscardRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

Targeted reveal-choose-discard — the Thoughtseize / Coercion / Thought Erasure family: "Target opponent reveals their hand. You choose a nonland card from it. That player discards that card."

These three sentences are ONE coupled game action, not three independent effects: the card "you choose" from the revealed hand is the very card "that player discards". So this is a single `DiscardCardsEffect` capturing all three structured axes rather than a decomposed sentence-bundle —

`Player` (the discarder): `Target` opponent — mirrors `DiscardTargetPlayerRule`'s "target opponent discards" encoding; `Chooser`: "you" (`ObjectReferenceKind.You`) — distinct from the discarder; `Filter`: "a nonland card" → `CardTypes=["card"]` + `ExcludedCardTypes=["land"]`; `RevealHand=true`: the opponent reveals their hand first.

CR 701.9a (verbatim): "To discard a card, move it from its owner's hand to that player's graveyard."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/RevealChooseDiscardRule.cs)

### `SacrificeAtEndStepSpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

Recognises the delayed-sacrifice sentence:

"Sacrifice it at the beginning of the next end step." "Sacrifice it at end of turn."

This sentence appears as a trailing clause in multi-sentence spell abilities where a creature or token was obtained or created earlier in the same ability text — for example: "Gain control of target creature until end of turn. Untap that creature. It gains haste until end of turn. Sacrifice it at the beginning of the next end step." (Act of Treason family). The pronoun "it" refers to the object described in the preceding clause (Rule 109.2 — game objects). MAST records this as a `SacrificeEffect` whose `Target` is `It` and whose `Duration` is `AtBeginningOfNextEndStepDuration`. This is descriptive (Rule 701.21 — Sacrifice), not an engine action; the timing semantics are carried by the duration node per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine. Priority 70: must supersede generic sacrifice rules that match "sacrifice [it]" without the duration clause. Rule citations: 701.21 (Sacrifice), 513 (End Step), 603.7 (delayed triggers).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/SacrificeAtEndStepSpellRule.cs)

### `SawInHalfRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule, IMultiSpellRule

"Destroy target creature. If that creature dies this way, its controller creates two tokens that are copies of that creature, except their power is half that creature's power and their toughness is half that creature's toughness. Round up each time." — the Saw in Half destroy-and-create-halved-copy-tokens pattern (CLB).

Produces a flat two-element effect list:

A `DestroyEffect` targeting the creature. A `ConditionalEffect` whose condition is the `OtherCondition` "that creature dies this way" and whose `Then` branch is a `CopyEffect` — Count=2, Target=`{Kind:"It"}`, Player=`{Kind:"Controller"}`, and a single `PowerToughnessOverride` modification encoding "half [stat] rounded up" via `CalculatedQuantity`.

CR 701.7a ("To destroy a permanent, move it to its owner's graveyard") + CR 603.6 (the "if [preceding effect] [happened] this way" conditional — an effect-level gate checked after the destroy resolves). The "Round up each time" sentence is consumed structurally as `Rounding="up"` on each `CalculatedQuantity`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/SawInHalfRule.cs)

### `ScrySpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Scry N." — spell-side scry. Rule 701.18. No Player field (implicit you).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ScrySpellRule.cs)

### `SearchLibraryToBattlefieldRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Search your library for (a basic land card|up to N basic land cards), put (it|them) onto the battlefield tapped, then shuffle." Covers the sorcery ramp pattern (e.g. Rampant Growth, Beneath the Sands). Always non-optional (no "you may" prefix). Destination is always BattlefieldTapped.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/SearchLibraryToBattlefieldRule.cs)

### `SearchLibraryToHandRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

Spell-resolution rule for the tutor pattern: "Search your library for a [type] card, put (that card|it) into your hand, then shuffle." Handles:

"a card" — any card (Filter.CardTypes = ["card"]) "a [type] card" — typed filter, e.g. "a creature card", "a basic land card" (type word(s) parsed into CardTypes / Supertypes)

All variants map to a single `SearchLibraryEffect` with `Destination = Hand`. The put-to-hand and shuffle clauses are folded into the effect per the existing convention established by `SearchBasicLandTriggeredRule` and the Solemn Simulacrum fixture. Rule 701.23 (Search).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/SearchLibraryToHandRule.cs)

### `SearchLibraryToTopRevealRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

Spell-resolution rule for the Mystical Tutor pattern: "Search your library for an instant or sorcery card, reveal it, then shuffle and put that card on top." Handles:

"an instant or sorcery card" — two-type disjunction (Filter.CardTypes = ["instant", "sorcery"]) "a[n] [type] card" — single typed filter, e.g. "an artifact card", "a creature card" (type word parsed into CardTypes or Supertypes)

All variants map to a single `SearchLibraryEffect` with `Destination = TopOfLibrary` and `Revealed = true`. The reveal, shuffle, and put-on-top clauses are folded into the effect per the convention established by `SearchLibraryToTopRule` and `SearchLibraryToTopTriggeredRule`. Rule 701.23 (Search). Rule 701.20 (Reveal). Priority 60 — fires before `SearchLibraryToTopRule` (priority 50) because this pattern includes "reveal it," which is absent from the plain Vampiric Tutor form; both rules are fully anchored (^…$) so there is no substring collision between them.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/SearchLibraryToTopRevealRule.cs)

### `SearchLibraryToTopRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

Spell-resolution rule for the Vampiric Tutor pattern: "Search your library for a [type] card, then shuffle and put that card on top." Handles:

"a card" — any card (Filter.CardTypes = ["card"]) "a [type] card" — typed filter, e.g. "a creature card", "a basic land card" (type word(s) parsed into CardTypes / Supertypes)

All variants map to a single `SearchLibraryEffect` with `Destination = TopOfLibrary`. The shuffle and put-on-top clauses are folded into the effect per the convention established by `SearchLibraryToHandRule` and `SearchLibraryToBattlefieldRule`. Rule 701.23 (Search).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/SearchLibraryToTopRule.cs)

### `SelfDealsDamageToAnyTargetRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"[Self] deals N damage to any target." — bare self-by-name dealDamage with AnyTarget. Covers clean burn spells like Open Fire, Shock, Lightning Strike where the spell names itself as the source and targets "any target" (player, creature, or planeswalker) without any type filter. Distinct from `SelfDealsDamageToTypeDisjunctionRule` (which requires a specific type or type disjunction) and `SelfDealsDamageToFilteredCreatureRule` (which requires a trailing "with [characteristic]" qualifier).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/SelfDealsDamageToAnyTargetRule.cs)

### `SelfDealsDamageToAttackingOrBlockingCreatureRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"[CardName] deals N damage to target attacking or blocking creature." — spell analogue of the activated `SelfDealsDamageToAttackingOrBlockingCreatureEffectRule`. The source is the spell itself (named; encoded as `Self()`); the target is a creature filtered by Characteristics = [Characteristic.InCombat(CombatState.AttackingOrBlocking)]. Covers clean single-line instants/sorceries such as Divine Arrow and Cosmium Blast.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/SelfDealsDamageToAttackingOrBlockingCreatureRule.cs)

### `SelfDealsDamageToFilteredCreatureRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"[Self] deals N damage to target/each [type] with [characteristic]." — Take Down's modal options. Distinct from the bare type-disjunction shape: this requires a trailing "with [characteristic]" qualifier. (Source-order pair noted at SpellAbilityParser.cs:592 — both rules currently disjoint via the "with" anchor.)

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/SelfDealsDamageToFilteredCreatureRule.cs)

### `SelfDealsDamageToTypeDisjunctionRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"[Self] deals N damage to target [type] (or [type])?." — Ready to Rumble, Lava Axe ("target player or planeswalker"). Bare-target shape with no "with [characteristic]" suffix. "player" is listed alongside card types here because `CardTypes` encodes both card types and game entities (per CR 120.1: objects deal damage to battles, creatures, planeswalkers, and players).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/SelfDealsDamageToTypeDisjunctionRule.cs)

### `ShuffleIntoLibraryRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule, IMultiSpellRule

"Choose target [type-disjunction]. Its owner shuffles it into their library." Covers the Unravel the Aether pattern — a two-sentence oracle form where the first sentence targets an artifact or enchantment and the second sentence describes the zone-change with shuffle.

Handled patterns (via `TryMatchMulti`):

"Choose target artifact or enchantment. Its owner shuffles it into their library." (Unravel the Aether, BNG) "Choose target artifact or enchantment. Its owner shuffles it into their library." (any reprint of this pattern)

The two-sentence form is not split by the sentence-bundle path because neither sentence individually matches any rule. This rule implements `TryMatchMulti` to match the full two-sentence text (with the period separator present) and emits a single `ShuffleIntoLibraryEffect`. The "Choose target" sentence is a targeting sentence that is inseparable from the zone-change, so both sentences resolve into one effect (Rule 701.19, 701.20).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ShuffleIntoLibraryRule.cs)

### `ShuffleSelfIntoLibraryRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Shuffle [CardName] into its owner's library." — the self-referential library shuffle used on cards like Beacon of Immortality. The card refers to itself by name, instructing that it be shuffled into its owner's library on resolution.

Rule 701.24c: "If an effect would cause a player to shuffle one or more specific objects into a library, that library is shuffled even if none of those objects are in the zone they're expected to be in." Rule 701.24 governs shuffle.

MAST maps the card-name self-reference to `Self` (the card itself; CR 201.5). The owner-shuffles mechanic is an inherent part of the zone-change (CR 701.24). Emits a single `ShuffleIntoLibraryEffect` with `Target = Self`.

GUARD: fully anchored (^ … $). Matches ONLY "Shuffle [CapitalizedName] into its owner's library" — the name must begin with a capital letter and end before "into its owner's library". Does NOT match "Its owner shuffles it into their library" (handled by `ShuffleIntoLibraryRule`) or any substring of a broader clause.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ShuffleSelfIntoLibraryRule.cs)

### `SpellTapTargetRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Tap target [type]." with optional count and type-disjunction target.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/SpellTapTargetRule.cs)

### `SurveilSpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Surveil N." — standalone spell-side surveil. Rule 701.42. Matches the bare keyword invocation after reminder text has been stripped by the dispatcher. Covers cards like Deadly Visit, Consider, Sinister Sabotage, etc.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/SurveilSpellRule.cs)

### `SurveilThenDrawRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule, IMultiSpellRule

"Surveil N, then draw M cards." — Diresight, Cruel Truths, Risky Research pattern. Expands to a flat [`SurveilEffect`, `DrawCardsEffect`] pair so both effects sit as siblings on `Effects`.

The optional "You lose N life" suffix (sentence 2 in the spell's oracle text) is handled by the sentence-bundle dispatcher, which routes it through `YouLoseLifeSpellRule`. This rule only matches sentence 1.

Rule 701.42 (surveil), Rule 120.1 (draw). Surveil count and draw count are independent small integers and are allowed to differ (e.g. "Surveil 3, then draw three cards").

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/SurveilThenDrawRule.cs)

### `TapAndFreezeRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule, IMultiSpellRule

Two-sentence "tap then freeze" spell pattern. Handles both single-target and bounded-multi-target variants:

"Tap target creature. It doesn't untap during its controller's next untap step." (Ojutai's Breath, DTK) — single creature; back-reference via "It". "Tap up to two target creatures. Those creatures don't untap during their controller's next untap step." (Frost Breath, M13) — up-to-N creatures; back-reference via "Those creatures".

Implements both `ISpellRule` (for the freeze sentence when the sentence-bundle dispatcher has already consumed the tap sentence) and `IMultiSpellRule` (for the full two-sentence text as a dedicated pattern). The `TryMatch` path returns `false`; all matching goes through `TryMatch` for the freeze fragment or `TryMatchMulti` for the combined text.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/TapAndFreezeRule.cs)

### `TapOrUntapSpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"You may tap or untap target [type]." and "tap or untap target [type]." The controller chooses whether to tap or untap the named target. Covers single-type and disjunction-type targets (e.g. "artifact, creature, or land"). Rule 701.26 (Tap and Untap).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/TapOrUntapSpellRule.cs)

### `TapUpToNTargetsRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Tap up to N target creatures." — bounded-count tap-targets spell effect. Examples: "Tap up to two target creatures." / "Tap up to three target creatures." The quantity lives on `Quantity` (cardinality of the target set), not on `Count`, because the oracle phrase is "up to N target", not "tap N targets". Rule 115.1 / Rule 701.26.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/TapUpToNTargetsRule.cs)

### `TargetCantBeBlockedThisTurnRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

Handles "Target creature can't be blocked this turn." — emits a `CantBeBlockedEffect` targeting a single creature with an `untilEndOfTurn` duration. Rule 509.1b (evasion ability; the defending player may not declare any creature as a blocker for the named creature during the declare-blockers step of the current turn). Representative cards: Artful Dodge, Slip Through Space.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/TargetCantBeBlockedThisTurnRule.cs)

### `TargetCreatureGainsKeywordRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

Recognises the pure keyword-grant shape (no P/T modification): "Target creature gains &lt;keyword&gt; until end of turn." "Target permanent gains &lt;keyword&gt; until end of turn." This covers single-keyword combat tricks and protection spells whose entire oracle effect is a UEOT ability grant on a single target. Examples:

"Target creature gains flying until end of turn." (Jump) "Target creature gains haste until end of turn." (Unnatural Speed) "Target creature gains deathtouch until end of turn." (Lace with Moonglove) "Target creature gains shroud until end of turn." (Mage's Guile) "Target creature gains shadow until end of turn." (Shadow Rift) "Target creature gains undying until end of turn." (Undying Evil)

Multi-keyword forms ("gains flying and lifelink") and composite forms ("gets +N/+M and gains …") are handled by `ModifyPTAndGainKeywordSpellRule`; this rule is intentionally restricted to exactly one keyword so the regex is unambiguous. Rule citations: 613.1c (Layer 6 — ability-granting effects), 611 (continuous effects with duration).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/TargetCreatureGainsKeywordRule.cs)

### `TargetDealsDamageToSelfRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Target creature deals damage to itself equal to its power."

The target creature is both the damage source and the damage recipient. The amount is a `DerivedQuantity` with `Power`, sourced from the same object ("it"). Covers Repentance (WL Sorcery) and Justice Strike (GRN Instant).

Distinguished from `SelfDealsDamageToAnyTargetRule` (spell is the source, not the creature) and from combat-damage rules (this is a non-combat damage event resolving at sorcery/instant speed).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/TargetDealsDamageToSelfRule.cs)

### `TargetGainsProtectionColorRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

Recognises the "gains protection from the color of your choice UEOT" shape: "Target creature you control gains protection from the color of your choice until end of turn." This covers protection-grant instants such as Gods Willing (THS) and Shelter (INV) where the protected color is chosen by the controller at resolution, not fixed in the oracle text. The chosen color is modeled as `ChosenColor` — a declarative marker that the protection quality is controller-chosen at resolution time. MAST records the instruction to choose, not the chosen value (per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine; see ChooseColorEffect for the analogous standalone color-choice shape, which here has no entry timing — the choice is a spell effect, so no StaticTimingKind.AsThisEnters applies). Rule citations: 702.16 (Protection), 702.16e (protection from a player's choice), 613.1c (Layer 6 — ability-granting continuous effects), 611 (continuous effects with duration).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/TargetGainsProtectionColorRule.cs)

### `TargetOpponentLosesHalfLifeRoundedUpRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Target opponent loses half their life, rounded up." — the targeted half-life-loss spell pattern (Blood Tribute). The opponent is targeted; the amount is half their life total, rounded up.

CR 119.3: "If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player's life total is adjusted accordingly." The amount is a `CalculatedQuantity` with `Operation="half"`, `Rounding="up"`, and a `DerivedQuantity` base keyed on `LifeTotal` (the target's own life total is implicit from the `Player` reference).

The player reference is `Opponent` (the oracle text says "Target opponent"). "Target opponent" is a targeted reference to an opponent — represented as `Kind = Opponent` in MAST (see Bargain, HighwayRobber precedents).

Anchored (^…$) to prevent substring matches inside multi-sentence spell clauses (e.g. the full Blood Tribute clause bundles this sentence with the kicked gain-life).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/TargetOpponentLosesHalfLifeRoundedUpRule.cs)

### `TargetPlayerDrawsLosesLifeRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule, IMultiSpellRule

"Target player draws N cards and loses N life." / "Target opponent draws N cards and loses N life." This is the targeted draw-and-drain pattern. The draw count and life-loss amount use the same quantity token (they may differ in wording but represent the same value in all current oracle text instances). Both effects share the same target reference. Recognised shapes:

"Target player draws X cards and loses X life." — Damnable Pact "Target player draws two cards and loses 2 life." — hypothetical literal variant "Target opponent draws a card and loses 1 life." — opponent-targeted variant

Emits a flat `[DrawCardsEffect, LoseLifeEffect]` list via `TryMatchMulti`. The single-effect `TryMatch` always returns false.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/TargetPlayerDrawsLosesLifeRule.cs)

### `TargetPlayerLosesLifeYouGainRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule, IMultiSpellRule

"Target player loses N life and you gain N life." — the targeted drain pattern where the opponent-chosen player loses a fixed amount and the controller gains the same amount (CR 119.3: "If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player's life total is adjusted accordingly.").

Recognised shapes:

"Target player loses 4 life and you gain 4 life." — Absorb Vis

Emits a flat [`LoseLifeEffect`, `GainLifeEffect`] pair via `TryMatchMulti`. The two amounts are allowed to differ in oracle text; each is parsed independently. The drain amounts need not be equal for the rule to fire — the conjunction "and you gain N life" is what identifies the shape.

The single-effect `TryMatch` always returns false — this shape never reduces to a single Effect.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/TargetPlayerLosesLifeYouGainRule.cs)

### `TargetSacrificesRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Target [player|opponent] sacrifices a [type][.]" — targeted sacrifice. The named player chooses which qualifying permanent to sacrifice (Rule 701.21a).

"Target opponent sacrifices a creature." (Diabolic Edict, Cruel Edict) "Target player sacrifices a creature." (generic) "Target player sacrifices a creature of their choice." (older errata phrasing)

Emits a `SacrificeEffect` where:

`Target` carries `Target` with `Filter.CardTypes = ["opponent"]` or `["player"]` identifying the actor. `Filter` restricts the sacrificed permanent type.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/TargetSacrificesRule.cs)

### `ThatPlayerMayCopyThisSpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"That player may copy this spell and may choose a new target for that copy."

Chain of Smog's chain mechanic: the targeted player (that player) gets the option to copy the spell itself (Self), with permission to redirect the copy to a new target. CR 707.10: "To copy a spell … means to put a copy of it onto the stack; a copy of a spell isn't cast and a copy of an activated ability isn't activated. … If the spell has any targets, you may choose new targets for the copy." The "may copy" wraps the whole thing in an `OptionalEffect` (ADR 0005 — "you may" is composition, not a flag).

The `Player` field on `CopyEffect` records who creates the copy — "that player" (`ThatPlayer`), the targeted player from the preceding `DiscardCardsEffect`. The copy target is the spell itself (`Self`). `MayChooseNewTargets` captures "may choose a new target for that copy" (CR 707.10). CR 701.9a governs the preceding discard; this clause governs the optional propagation of the chain.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ThatPlayerMayCopyThisSpellRule.cs)

### `ThreatenRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule, IMultiSpellRule

Recognises the Threaten / Act of Treason pattern: "Gain control of target creature until end of turn. Untap that creature. It gains haste until end of turn." Three sequential effects share one target (the chosen creature), represented in the AST as:

`GainControlEffect` — target creature, until end of turn. `UntapEffect` — It (back-reference to the same creature). `GainAbilityEffect` — It gains haste until end of turn.

"That creature" in the second sentence is a back-reference to the target chosen for the first sentence; it maps to `It` (same pronoun semantics as "it" — Rule 109.2). Rule citations: CR 613.1a (Layer 2 — control-changing effects), CR 613.1c (Layer 6 — ability-granting effects), CR 702.10 (haste).

The single-effect `TryMatch` always returns false so the flat-list path is the only active route.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ThreatenRule.cs)

### `UntapAllControlledRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Untap all [filter] you control." — mass-untap spell targeting every qualifying permanent under the controller's control (CR 701.19a).

Handled patterns (via `TryMatch`):

"Untap all nonland permanents you control." — Dramatic Reversal "Untap all permanents you control." "Untap all creatures you control." "Untap all artifacts you control."

Emits an `UntapEffect` whose `Target` has `Each` and the appropriate `ObjectFilter` with `Controller` = `You`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/UntapAllControlledRule.cs)

### `UntapTargetRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

Untap spell rules — two shapes:

"Untap target [filter]." — single-target untap spell. "Untap it." — pronoun reference to an already-named target, used as the trailing clause of multi-sentence spells such as "Target creature gets +N/+M and gains &lt;keyword&gt; until end of turn. Untap it."

Covers the bare card-type target shapes most common in the corpus: creature and permanent.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/UntapTargetRule.cs)

### `YouLoseLifeSpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"You lose N life." — spell-resolution controller life-loss (Rule 119.3). Covers the trailing sentence in oracle texts like Diresight, Cruel Truths, and Risky Research ("Surveil N, then draw M cards. You lose N life.").

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/YouLoseLifeSpellRule.cs)

## `/Parsing/Parsers/Static/`

### `IStaticRule` — *interface*

One static-ability recognition rule. Each implementation maps a single oracle-text shape to the `Ability` list it produces (almost always a single `StaticAbility`, occasionally a keyword-list expansion). Rules are discovered by reflection at `StaticAbilityParser` construction via the `StaticRuleAttribute` decoration and dispatched in descending `Priority` order, with ordinal-name order breaking ties within a band — first non-null (non-empty) result wins.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/IStaticRule.cs)

### `StaticRuleAttribute` — *sealed class* : Attribute, MagicAST.Parsing.Parsers.IPrioritizedRuleAttribute

Marks an `IStaticRule` implementation for reflection-based discovery by `StaticAbilityParser`. Each decorated rule contributes one oracle-text recognizer to the parser's static-ability cascade.

<b>Priority convention (range 0–100, default 50, higher = more specific = tried first):</b>

<b>80–100</b> — rules that supersede a more-general sibling (color-disjunction before type-disjunction, IfYouDo variants before plain variants, "this creature" before "a [type]"). Without the bump these would never fire because the more-general rule would shadow them. <b>50</b> — default. Non-overlapping rules whose regex anchors are mutually exclusive. <b>20–40</b> — catch-all / generic rules that should be tried last so a more-specific sibling gets a chance first.

The numeric scheme replaces the old source-order convention: each rule used to declare its priority implicitly by where it appeared in `TryParse`'s cascade. Splitting rules into one-file-per-rule loses that ordering signal, so each rule now states its priority explicitly.

<b>Phase 3 migration note:</b> priorities are migrated ORDER-PRESERVING as `Priority = 1000 - (index in the legacy dispatch chain)`, so the original first-non-null-wins order of `StaticAbilityParser`'s legacy chain is reproduced exactly by construction. (The 0–100 band convention above describes the intended steady-state once the migration settles; until then the literal 1000-minus-index values keep behavior identical.)

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/StaticRuleAttribute.cs)

### `StaticRuleHelpers` — *static class*

Shared utilities used across multiple `IStaticRule` implementations. Lives outside the dispatcher so rules can be added without taking a dependency on `StaticAbilityParser` internals.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/StaticRuleHelpers.cs)

## `/Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/`

### `AbilityWordConditionalEntersWithCountersRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/AbilityWordConditionalEntersWithCountersRule.cs)

### `AbundanceDrawReplacementRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Recognises the Abundance optional draw-replacement shape: "If you would draw a card, you may instead choose land or nonland and reveal cards from the top of your library until you reveal a card of the chosen kind. Put that card into your hand and put all other cards revealed this way on the bottom of your library in any order."

This is a draw-replacement effect (CR 614.11) whose replacement action is optional ("you may instead") — the controller may let the draw proceed normally. The replacement action itself is the Abundance-specific "choose land or nonland + reveal until found + put rest on bottom in any order" atomic action, modelled as `AbundanceRevealEffect`.

Priority 981: one above `DrawReplacementRule` (980), so this more specific "may instead" variant is checked first and the general "draw N instead" rule is not reached for Abundance's oracle text.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/AbundanceDrawReplacementRule.cs)

### `AllMustBlockRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/AllMustBlockRule.cs)

### `AllObjectsAddTypeRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Parses the Mycosynth Lattice "all permanents are [type] in addition to their other types" oracle template: a static continuous effect that additively grants one or more card types to all permanents (or a broadly-scoped subject without controller qualification).

Handled patterns:

"All permanents are artifacts in addition to their other types." → `AddTypeEffect` with Target={Each, permanents}, AddedCardTypes=["artifact"].

CR 205.1b (verbatim): "Some effects change an object's card type, supertype, or subtype but specify that the object retains a prior card type, supertype, or subtype. In such cases, all the object's prior card types, supertypes, and subtypes are retained. This rule applies to effects that use phrases such as 'in addition to its other types' or that state that something is 'still a [type, supertype, or subtype].'"

Priority 969 — one below `NonlandCreatureTypeGrantRule` (970) which handles the more-specific controller-scoped shape, so this broader rule fires only when that rule declines (i.e., the subject is not "Nontoken creatures you control").

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/AllObjectsAddTypeRule.cs)

### `AllObjectsAreColorlessRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Parses the Mycosynth Lattice "all cards…, spells, and permanents are colorless" oracle template: a continuous layer-5 effect that makes all game objects colorless.

The oracle line enumerates three disjoint groups that together cover every game object: cards not on the battlefield (hand, library, graveyard, exile, command zone), spells (on the stack), and permanents (on the battlefield). Each group is modelled as a separate `ChangeColorEffect` with `Colors: []` (colorless) under a single `StaticAbility`, honouring the oracle sentence as written rather than collapsing it to a single "everything" filter.

CR 105.3 (verbatim): "Effects may change an object's color or give a color to a colorless object. If an effect gives an object a new color, the new color replaces all previous colors the object had (unless the effect said the object became that color 'in addition' to its other colors)." A layer-5 effect setting Colors = [] makes the object colorless (CR 105.2c: "A colorless object has no color.").

Priority 968 — below `AllObjectsAddTypeRule` (969) and `NonlandCreatureTypeGrantRule` (970).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/AllObjectsAreColorlessRule.cs)

### `AnthemModifyPTRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/AnthemModifyPTRule.cs)

### `AnyNumberInDeckRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"A deck can have any number of cards named [this]." — the self-referential exception to the four-of deck-construction rule (Relentless Rats, Rat Colony, Shadowborn Apostle, Persistent Petitioners, Dragon's Approach).

CR 100.2a: "In constructed play …, each deck has a minimum deck size of 60 cards. A constructed deck may contain any number of basic land cards and no more than four of any card with a particular English name other than basic land cards." This static line lifts that limit for cards sharing this card's name. It is a characteristic-defining-style declarative property of the card, so it lands as a `StaticAbility` — not an activated or triggered ability.

The "[this]" in "named [this]" always names the card the line appears on, so the emitted `AnyNumberInDeckEffect` carries no field: the self- reference is implicit. The regex tolerates any card name in that slot rather than threading the card's own name through (it is structurally always self).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/AnyNumberInDeckRule.cs)

### `AppliesToCostReductionRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Cost reductions that apply to a <i>class of other abilities/spells</i> the enchanted permanent is the target of — Strong Back's "Equip abilities you activate that target enchanted creature cost {3} less to activate." and "Aura spells you cast that target enchanted creature cost {3} less to cast." (ADR 0003 follow-up 1: the surviving keyword identity is what a reference filter matches on).

Distinct from `TypeSpellCostReductionRule` (self/spell-on-stack reductions carried via `AffectedObjects`): here the reduction targets a referenced <i>ability or spell</i> via `AppliesTo`, a typed `AbilityReference`. The "that target enchanted creature" clause becomes `TargetsObject` = `EnchantedOrEquipped`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/AppliesToCostReductionRule.cs)

### `AsLongAsAttachedMayPlayFromTopOfLibraryRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Recognises "As long as [X] is attached to a creature, you may play lands and cast spells from the top of your library." — a conditional static permission that allows the controller to play lands and cast spells from the top of their library while the source Equipment is attached to a creature. Paradigm card: The Reality Chip (NEO).

CR 702.151b: "Attaching an Equipment with reconfigure to another creature causes the Equipment to stop being a creature until it becomes unattached from that creature." CR 401.5: "Some effects … say that a player may look at the top card of their library." CR 604.2: "Static abilities create continuous effects … active as long as the permanent with the ability remains on the battlefield and has the ability."

The ability is conditional ("as long as [X] is attached to a creature") — the condition body is captured verbatim into an `OtherCondition` residual because no structured `IsAttachedToCreature` arm exists in the `Condition` union yet (debt — PB-7 structured-condition buckets). The effect itself is fully structured as a `MayPlayFromTopOfLibraryEffect` granting both `PlayLands` and `CastSpells`.

Priority 939 — fires before `MayPlayFromTopOfLibraryRule` (940) so the conditioned form is claimed before the unconditional Bolas's Citadel form. The pattern is anchored (^…$) to prevent substring collision with any sibling rule.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/AsLongAsAttachedMayPlayFromTopOfLibraryRule.cs)

### `AsLongAsControlledObjectsHaveAbilityRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Recognises the leading "As long as [condition], [card-types] you control have &quot;[quoted activated ability]&quot;." form — a conditional continuous grant of an activated ability to a set of controlled permanents.

The World Tree's third ability is the canonical example: "As long as you control six or more lands, lands you control have '{T}: Add one mana of any color.'"

Shape: the leading "As long as [condition]" block gates the grant duration (`AsLongAsDuration`). The main clause "[types] you control have [quoted ability]" becomes a `GainAbilityEffect` targeting each permanent of the listed card type(s) controlled by you. The quoted body is dispatched to `ActivatedAbilityParser` to produce a nested `ActivatedAbility`.

CR 604.1: "Static abilities do something all the time rather than being activated or triggered." CR 604.2: "Static abilities create continuous effects, some of which are … ability-granting effects." CR 611.1: "A continuous effect modifies characteristics of objects … for a fixed or indefinite period." CR 602.1: Activated abilities have cost and effect ("[Cost]: [Effect]").

Priority 969 — above `AsLongAsStaticGrantRule` (968) so this rule claims the "lands you control have [quoted]" clause before the generic rule attempts its suffix-pattern and fails. The regex anchors are ^…$ so there is no substring-overlap risk with sibling rules.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/AsLongAsControlledObjectsHaveAbilityRule.cs)

### `AsLongAsEnchantedColorConditionalRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"As long as enchanted creature is [color], it gets +N/+N and has [keyword]." "As long as enchanted creature is [color], it gets +N/+N and all creatures able to block it do so."

Produces a `StaticAbility` with the color condition on `Condition` (not encoded in a per-effect Duration) and `EnchantedOrEquipped` as the target of each effect. Both effects originate from one oracle clause gated by one condition (CR 611 — a static ability creates one continuous effect layer). The condition is stored at ability scope to avoid duplicating it across effects and to separate "what applies" (the effects) from "when it applies" (the condition).

Two oracle-text shapes map here:

PT-and-keyword grant: "it gets +1/+1 and has deathtouch" → `ModifyPTEffect` + `GainAbilityEffect` (both targeting `EnchantedOrEquipped`). PT-and-lure: "it gets +1/+1 and all creatures able to block it do so" → `ModifyPTEffect` + `AllMustBlockEffect` (Rule 509.1c; every creature that can legally block the enchanted creature must do so).

Sits at Priority 975 — above `AsLongAsPTAndKeywordGrantRule` (970) so the enchanted-creature shapes (which differ in target and condition placement) are claimed before that rule's `ObjectReference.Self()` path.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/AsLongAsEnchantedColorConditionalRule.cs)

### `AsLongAsPTAndKeywordGrantRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Leading-form conditional continuous effect bundling a P/T modifier AND a keyword grant under one shared `AsLongAsDuration`: "As long as &lt;cond&gt;, it/this creature gets +N/+M and has &lt;keyword&gt;."

Both effects live on a single `StaticAbility` because they originate from one oracle clause governed by one "as long as" condition (Rule 611 — the continuous effect is created by the static ability and applies for as long as the condition holds). Modelling them as one ability with two effects keeps the condition single-sourced rather than duplicating the static ability per effect.

Sits at a higher priority than `AsLongAsStaticGrantRule` (Priority 968) so the compound "gets +N/+M and has &lt;keyword&gt;" clause is recognised before that rule's single-effect sub-parsers, whose end-anchored regexes decline the compound text and would otherwise let the clause fall through to the fallback.

An ability-word prefix ("Threshold — …", Rule 207.2c) carries no rules meaning; the classifier captures it into `AbilityWord` and we record it as the descriptive label on the produced `StaticAbility`, peeling the em-dash prefix before matching the leading pattern.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/AsLongAsPTAndKeywordGrantRule.cs)

### `AsLongAsStaticGrantRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/AsLongAsStaticGrantRule.cs)

### `AssignDamageAsToughnessRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"Each creature you control assigns combat damage equal to its toughness rather than its power." — High Alert, Doran-the-Siege-Tower family. Rule 510.1a (verbatim): "Each attacking creature and each blocking creature assigns combat damage equal to its power." This static ability establishes a global replacement that substitutes toughness for power in the combat-damage assignment step, governed by CR 614 (replacement effects) and CR 613 (layer ordering).

The pattern is fully anchored (^ … $) to prevent substring-matching another oracle line that contains this phrase embedded inside a larger clause.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/AssignDamageAsToughnessRule.cs)

### `AssignDamageAsUnblockedRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"You may have this creature assign its combat damage as though it weren't blocked." (Pride of Lions). Rule 510.1c: a blocked creature normally assigns its combat damage to the creatures blocking it; this static grants the optional substitution to assign all combat damage as though unblocked. Distinct from Trample (excess only) — see `AssignDamageAsUnblockedEffect`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/AssignDamageAsUnblockedRule.cs)

### `BareKeywordGrantRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/BareKeywordGrantRule.cs)

### `BareKeywordPairGrantRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Recognises the static keyword-pair grant on an attached permanent: "(Enchanted|Equipped) creature has [kw1] and [kw2]."

This is a <b>static</b> continuous effect (CR 611.1: "A continuous effect modifies characteristics of objects … for a fixed or indefinite period."; CR 611.3: "A continuous effect may be generated by the static ability of an object."). The grant persists for as long as the Aura or Equipment remains attached — there is no Duration node (distinct from the activated "gains [kw] until end of turn" shape).

CR 702.5a: "Enchant is a static ability … The enchant ability restricts what an Aura spell can target and what an Aura can enchant." The enchanted/equipped subject maps to `EnchantedOrEquipped`.

Per the MAST multi-effect-per-clause doctrine, "has [kw1] and [kw2]" on one oracle line is bundled into a single `StaticAbility` whose `Effects` list carries two independent `GainAbilityEffect` nodes — one per keyword. No `CompositeEffect` wrapper is used: the flat Effects list is the idiomatic multi-effect encoding for a static ability.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/BareKeywordPairGrantRule.cs)

### `BlitzStaticRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Decomposes a "Blitz [cost]" oracle line into the three abilities defined by CR 702.152a (the sacrifice-variant sibling of `DashStaticRule`; mirroring the `ReconfigureStaticRule` multi-ability precedent).

CR 702.152a (verbatim): "Blitz represents three abilities: two static abilities that function while the card with blitz is on the stack, one of which may create a delayed triggered ability, and a static ability that functions while the object with blitz is on the battlefield. 'Blitz [cost]' means 'You may cast this card by paying [cost] rather than its mana cost,' 'If this spell's blitz cost was paid, sacrifice the permanent this spell becomes at the beginning of the next end step,' and 'As long as this permanent's blitz cost was paid, it has haste and \"When this permanent is put into a graveyard from the battlefield, draw a card.\"' Casting a spell for its blitz cost follows the rules for paying alternative costs in rules 601.2b and 601.2f-h."

Priority 1001 — fires before `KeywordListRule` (priority 1000) so the three-ability decomposition takes precedence over the single-ability keyword combinator path. The combinator in `BlitzKeyword` remains live as a fallback, emitting only the primary alternative-cast static ability (the keyword expander returns a single `Ability`; Blitz decomposes into three).

Differs from Dash only in the second and third abilities: Blitz's end-step delayed trigger <em>sacrifices</em> the permanent (rather than returning it to hand), and its conditional battlefield static grants TWO abilities — haste plus a "put into a graveyard from the battlefield, draw a card" triggered ability ("put into a graveyard from the battlefield" is the `Dies` event, CR 700.4).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/BlitzStaticRule.cs)

### `BlockAdditionalRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/BlockAdditionalRule.cs)

### `BoastTwicePerTurnRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"Creatures you control can boast [N] times during each of your turns rather than once."

CR 702.142a: "A boast ability is a special kind of activated ability. 'Boast - [Cost]: [Effect]' means '[Cost]: [Effect]. Activate only if this creature attacked this turn and only once each turn.'" This static ability modifies that default once-per-turn limit for controller-owned creatures.

ANCHORED (^…$): the surface phrase "can boast … times during each of your turns rather than once" is distinctive and will not match as a substring of any other static rule.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/BoastTwicePerTurnRule.cs)

### `BuybackStaticRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Decomposes a "Buyback [cost]" oracle line into the two static abilities defined by CR 702.27a (mirroring the `DashStaticRule` / `ReconfigureStaticRule` multi-ability precedent).

CR 702.27a (verbatim): "Buyback appears on some instants and sorceries. It represents two static abilities that function while the spell is on the stack. 'Buyback [cost]' means 'You may pay an additional [cost] as you cast this spell' and 'If the buyback cost was paid, put this spell into its owner's hand instead of into that player's graveyard as it resolves.'"

Priority 1001 — fires before `KeywordListRule` (priority 1000) so the two-ability decomposition takes precedence over the single-ability keyword combinator path. The combinator in `BuybackKeyword` remains live as a fallback, emitting only the primary additional-cast-cost static ability (the keyword expander returns a single `Ability`; Buyback decomposes into two).

Ability 2 is a zone-change replacement effect (CR 614 / 702.27a): the spell would go from the stack to the graveyard as it resolves, but instead goes to its owner's hand. Modeled with the existing replacement primitives — `ReplacementEffect` over a `ZoneChangeEvent` (origin stack, destination graveyard) with `OriginalEventOccurs = false` ("instead") and the `ReturnToHandEffect` as the replacement action — gated on `KeywordCostPaidCondition` for Buyback.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/BuybackStaticRule.cs)

### `CanAttackIgnoringDefenderRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"Creatures you control can attack as though they didn't have defender." — High Alert, Assault Formation family. Rule 702.3b (verbatim): "A creature with defender can't attack." This static ability overrides that restriction, allowing creatures with Defender to be declared as attackers.

The pattern is fully anchored (^ … $) to prevent substring-matching another oracle line that embeds this phrase inside a larger clause. The "they" pronoun in High Alert's text ("as though they didn't have defender") is a plural referring to the earlier "Creatures you control" subject; both forms are matched.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/CanAttackIgnoringDefenderRule.cs)

### `CanBeYourCommanderStaticRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"[Card name] can be your commander." — printed on non-default-legal commander cards (Teferi, Temporal Archmage; The Prismatic Bridge; etc.) to declare that the card is usable as a commander even though it lacks the Legendary supertype on its own type line or is otherwise excluded from the default commander pool.

Teferi, Temporal Archmage (C14) is Legendary and a Planeswalker, but carries this clause because the Commander format at that time required separate printed permission for planeswalkers to be commanders. Current Oracle errata still preserves the text.

CR 903.3: "Each player chooses a legendary creature card or Legendary Planeswalker card as their commander." The printed permission here expands (or confirms) that the named card qualifies. MAST records this as a `CommanderDesignationEffect` on a `StaticAbility` — the designation is a static fact about the card, not a one-shot action (no zone-change, no event required).

ANCHORED (`^…$`): the phrase "can be your commander" does not appear as a substring of any other standard oracle-text clause. Anchoring is the defensive convention.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/CanBeYourCommanderStaticRule.cs)

### `CanBlockOnlyRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/CanBlockOnlyRule.cs)

### `CanOnlyAttackAloneRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Static restriction: "This creature can only attack alone." The "only alone" qualifier restricts the creature to attacking only as the sole attacker — it cannot be declared as an attacker alongside other creatures.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/CanOnlyAttackAloneRule.cs)

### `CantAttackOrBlockAloneRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Static restriction: "This creature can't attack or block alone." The "alone" qualifier conditions both the attack and the block restriction on this object being the sole attacker / sole blocker — the restriction is lifted whenever at least one other creature is also declared.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/CantAttackOrBlockAloneRule.cs)

### `CantAttackUnlessDefendingControlsRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/CantAttackUnlessDefendingControlsRule.cs)

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/CantBeBlockedRule.cs)

### `CantBeCastRestrictionRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/CantBeCastRestrictionRule.cs)

### `CantBlockRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/CantBlockRule.cs)

### `CantCastMoreThanNSpellsRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Recognises the spell-cast-cap static "Each player can't cast more than one spell each turn." (Eidolon of Rhetoric, Arcane Laboratory). A rules-of-the-game continuous effect (CR 611.1) that caps cast events (CR 601.2) per player per turn — NOT a per-spell "can't be cast" restriction. Emits a single `StaticAbility` carrying one `CantCastMoreThanNSpellsEffect`; the cap, scope, and counting window are structured fields, never free text.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/CantCastMoreThanNSpellsRule.cs)

### `CastAsFlashWithConsequenceRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/CastAsFlashWithConsequenceRule.cs)

### `CastFromExileRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"You may cast this card from exile." — an unconditional static permission to cast this card from the exile zone (Eternal Scourge, EMN).

CR 601.3e (verbatim, excerpt): "Some rules and effects state that an alternative set of characteristics or a subset of characteristics are considered to determine if a card or copy of a card is legal to cast…" Here the alternative set of characteristics is the <i>zone</i> the card may be cast from — exile rather than only its hand. The permission does not replace the mana cost, so the produced `AlternativeCastEffect` has `FromZone = Exile` and a null `Cost` (cast for its own mana cost) and a null `Condition` (unconditional).

This is not a keyword ability, so the ability carries no `KeywordSource`. Priority 983 places it just below `CastFromGraveyardConditionalRule` (985) and `CastFromMultipleZonesRule` (984), which handle overlapping oracle phrasings — multi-zone permission and conditional graveyard permission respectively. A narrower anchor (^…$) ensures this rule does not match either sibling as a substring.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/CastFromExileRule.cs)

### `CastFromGraveyardConditionalRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"You may cast this card from your graveyard as long as [condition]." — the written-out conditional graveyard-recursion permission (Gravecrawler: "You may cast this card from your graveyard as long as you control a Zombie.").

CR 601.3e (verbatim, excerpt): "Some rules and effects state that an alternative set of characteristics or a subset of characteristics are considered to determine if a card or copy of a card is legal to cast…" Here the alternative set of characteristics is the <i>zone</i> the card may be cast from — its graveyard rather than only its hand. The permission does not replace the mana cost (contrast the alternative-cost keywords Flashback/Escape/Madness), so the produced `AlternativeCastEffect` has `FromZone = Graveyard` and a null `Cost` (cast for its own mana cost).

The "as long as [condition]" gate is structured through `ConditionParser` (ADR 0007) — "you control a Zombie" becomes a `CountCondition` over Zombies you control (≥1), not a free-text residual — and rides on `Condition`, the effect-level precondition home shared with the conditional alternative-cast keywords (Surge/Spectacle/Mayhem). This is not a keyword, so the ability carries no `KeywordSource`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/CastFromGraveyardConditionalRule.cs)

### `CastFromMultipleZonesRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"You may cast this card from your graveyard or from exile." — an unconditional permission to cast this card from two zones (Squee, the Immortal).

CR 601.3e (verbatim, excerpt): "Some rules and effects state that an alternative set of characteristics or a subset of characteristics are considered to determine if a card or copy of a card is legal to cast…" Here the permission grants two separate zone exceptions — graveyard and exile — without an alternative cost and without any condition. Each zone becomes its own `AlternativeCastEffect` inside a single `StaticAbility` (the conjunction of the two permissions is expressed by the single oracle sentence).

The produced `AlternativeCastEffect` nodes each have a null `Cost` (cast for the card's own mana cost) and a null `Condition` (unconditional).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/CastFromMultipleZonesRule.cs)

### `CastNoncreatureSpellsAsFlashRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"You may cast noncreature spells as though they had flash." — Valley Floodcaller and similar cards that grant flash specifically to noncreature spells. This is a continuous static permission (CR 604.2) that allows the controlling player to cast noncreature spells at instant speed, modelled as a `TimingModificationEffect` whose `AppliesTo` targets noncreature spells the controller casts.

CR 702.8a: "Flash is a static ability that functions in any zone from which you could play the card it's on. 'Flash' means 'You may play this card any time you could cast an instant.'" The global grant here extends that permission to every noncreature spell the controller casts. The filter encodes the noncreature restriction via `ExcludedCardTypes = ["creature"]` on the `SpellReference` (parallel to the `SpellCastConditionRule` "noncreature spell" encoding).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/CastNoncreatureSpellsAsFlashRule.cs)

### `CastSpellsAsFlashRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"You may cast spells as though they had flash." — Vedalken Orrery and similar global flash-grant static abilities. This is a continuous static permission (CR 604.2) that allows the controlling player to cast any spell at instant speed, modelled as a `TimingModificationEffect` whose `AppliesTo` targets all spells you cast.

CR 702.8a: "Flash is a static ability that functions in any zone from which you could play the card it's on. 'Flash' means 'You may play this card any time you could cast an instant.'" The global grant here extends that permission to every spell the controller casts rather than to a single card.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/CastSpellsAsFlashRule.cs)

### `CasualtyStaticRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Decomposes a "Casualty N" oracle line into the two abilities defined by CR 702.153a (mirroring the `ReplicateStaticRule` / `SquadStaticRule` multi-ability precedent).

CR 702.153a (verbatim): "Casualty is a keyword that represents two abilities. The first is a static ability that functions while the spell with casualty is on the stack. The second is a triggered ability that functions while the spell with casualty is on the stack. Casualty N means 'As an additional cost to cast this spell, you may sacrifice a creature with power N or greater,' and 'When you cast this spell, if a casualty cost was paid for it, copy it. If the spell has any targets, you may choose new targets for the copy.' Paying a spell's casualty cost follows the rules for paying additional costs in rules 601.2b and 601.2f-h."

Priority 1001 — fires before `KeywordListRule` (priority 1000) so the two-ability decomposition takes precedence over the single-ability keyword combinator path. The combinator in `CasualtyKeyword` remains live as a fallback, emitting only the primary additional-cost static ability (the keyword expander returns a single `Ability`; Casualty decomposes into two).

"you may choose new targets for the copy" is recorded on the `CopyEffect` via `MayChooseNewTargets` = `true` per the CR. The decision to retarget is engine territory, but the PERMISSION is oracle-stated and descriptively meaningful — not omitted (contrast Replicate where the same clause is engine-territory only per its rule text).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/CasualtyStaticRule.cs)

### `ChooseCardTypeOnEntryRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"As this [permanent] enters, choose artifact, creature, enchantment, instant, or sorcery." — recognizes the as-enters card-type-choice declaration (Cloud Key shape) and emits a composite `StaticAbility` carrying `AsThisEnters` plus a plain `ChooseCardTypeEffect`.

The oracle text lists the options as a disjunction ("artifact, creature, enchantment, instant, or sorcery") — the specific enumeration is reminder context, not structured data, so no `Options` field is needed: the rule captures any mention of those permanent types in a disjunctive list. A loose anchored pattern is used (does not require the exact five-type enumeration) so that future printings with different subsets still parse correctly.

Sibling of `ChooseCreatureTypeOnEntryRule` and `ChooseColorOnEntryRule`; priority 940 is below both siblings so they claim their distinct noun phrases first and this rule does not shadow them.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/ChooseCardTypeOnEntryRule.cs)

### `ChooseColorOnEntryRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/ChooseColorOnEntryRule.cs)

### `ChooseCreatureTypeOnEntryRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"As this [permanent] enters, choose a creature type." — recognizes the as-enters creature-type-choice declaration (Unclaimed Territory shape) and emits a composite `StaticAbility` carrying `AsThisEnters` plus a plain `ChooseCreatureTypeEffect`. Sibling of `ChooseColorOnEntryRule`; the two regexes are disjoint ("choose a color" vs "choose a creature type"), so dispatch priority relative to the color rule is immaterial.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/ChooseCreatureTypeOnEntryRule.cs)

### `ChosenTypeAnthemModifyPTRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"Creatures you control of the chosen type get +X/+Y." — a continuous P/T anthem (CR 613.1: an object's characteristics are recomputed in layers, the anthem being a P/T-modifying continuous effect) whose affected set is filtered by the creature type chosen as this permanent entered.

The "of the chosen type" clause is the structured consumer half of a CR 607 linked ability — CR 607.1: "An object may have two abilities printed on it such that one of them causes actions to be taken or objects or players to be affected and the other one directly refers to those actions, objects, or players. If so, these two abilities are linked: the second refers only to actions that were taken or objects or players that were affected by the first, and not by any other ability." The producer is the "choose a creature type" effect under `StaticAbility.When = AsThisEnters`. It is encoded with the structured `ChosenCharacteristic` = `CreatureType` reference, never free-text.

Distinct from `TribalAnthemModifyPTRule`, whose affected set is a fixed named subtype ("Other Goblin creatures you control get …"). Here the subtype is runtime-chosen rather than printed, so the subtype axis is the chosen-characteristic reference, not `Subtypes`. No "Other " prefix — the source enchantment is not a creature, so it never appears in the affected set.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/ChosenTypeAnthemModifyPTRule.cs)

### `ChosenTypeSpellCostReductionRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"Spells you cast of the chosen type cost {N} less to cast." — a cost-reduction static ability whose affected set is filtered by the card type chosen as this permanent entered (Cloud Key shape).

This is the consumer half of a CR 607 linked ability pair: the producer is the `ChooseCardTypeEffect` under `StaticAbility.When = AsThisEnters` (Cloud Key, line 1). The "of the chosen type" clause is encoded as `ChosenCharacteristic` = `CardType`, never free-text.

Distinct from `TypeSpellCostReductionRule` (a fixed printed card type like "Creature spells you cast cost {1} less") and from `ChosenTypeAnthemModifyPTRule` (which reads a chosen creature type for a P/T buff, not a chosen card type for a cost reduction). The pattern anchors on "of the chosen type" to distinguish it from the fixed-type family.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/ChosenTypeSpellCostReductionRule.cs)

### `ColoredCreatureSpellCostReductionRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"[Color] creature spells you cast cost {N} less to cast." — the Amonkhet Monument cycle (Oketra's Monument, Bontu's Monument, Kefnet's Monument, Rhonas's Monument, Hazoret's Monument). Each reduces the cost of spells that are both a specific colour AND a creature spell.

CR 118.7: "What a player actually needs to do to pay a cost may be changed or reduced by effects." CR 118.7a: "Effects that reduce a cost by an amount of generic mana affect only the generic mana component of that cost."

Sits above `TypeSpellCostReductionRule` (priority 984) and `ConjunctiveTypeSpellCostReductionRule` (priority 983) because the two-word colour+type qualifier would otherwise never reach those rules. Anchored (^…$) to prevent false-positive substring matches inside longer oracle lines.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/ColoredCreatureSpellCostReductionRule.cs)

### `ColorlessCreatureAnthemRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"Colorless creatures you control get +N/+M." — continuous P/T anthem (CR 613.1, layer 7c) scoped to colorless creatures the controller owns. Colorless is not a color (Rule 105.1: "Colorless is not a color. Effects that specifically affect colorless permanents or spells use the term 'colorless'.") so the filter encodes colorlessness via `IsColorless` rather than the Colors axis. This rule runs at priority 970 (above `LordPTBuffRule` at 969) to intercept the "Colorless creatures" filter noun before LordPTBuffRule can misclassify "Colorless" as a creature subtype. LordPTBuffRule's [Subtype] creatures branch would treat "Colorless" as a subtype name (producing Subtypes: ["Colorless"]) rather than the correct IsColorless: true filter.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/ColorlessCreatureAnthemRule.cs)

### `ConspireStaticRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Decomposes a "Conspire" oracle line into the two abilities defined by CR 702.78a (mirroring the `DashStaticRule` / `ReconfigureStaticRule` multi-ability precedent).

CR 702.78a (verbatim): "Conspire is a keyword that represents two abilities... 'Conspire' means 'As an additional cost to cast this spell, you may tap two untapped creatures you control that each share a color with it' and 'When you cast this spell, if its conspire cost was paid, copy it. If the spell has any targets, you may choose new targets for the copy.'"

Priority 1001 — fires before `KeywordListRule` (priority 1000) so the two-ability decomposition takes precedence over the single-ability keyword combinator path. The combinator in `ConspireKeyword` remains live as a fallback, emitting only the primary additional-cost static ability (the keyword expander returns a single `Ability`; Conspire decomposes into two).

The "each share a color with it" predicate on the tapped creatures is a relational color filter (the creature shares a color with the spell being cast), modeled via `SharesColorWith` keyed on `{Kind: Self}` (the conspire spell). "Untapped" is creature-state derived from the rules and stays engine. The trigger's "you may choose new targets for the copy" clause is engine territory and is omitted (copy once, no `Count`).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/ConspireStaticRule.cs)

### `CreatureSpellsCantBeCounteredRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Parses "Creature spells you control can't be countered." — a static continuous effect on an enchantment (or other permanent) that grants uncounterability to all creature spells the controller casts. This is the Rhythm of the Wild shape.

CR 113.6g (verbatim): "An object's ability that states it can't be countered or can't be copied functions on the stack."

The affected objects are creature spells on the stack controlled by the source's controller. `CardTypes: ["spell", "creature"]` follows the established MAST convention for stack objects that are both spells and creatures (parallel to Maskwood Nexus "creature spells you control" — CardTypes: ["spell", "creature"], Controller: You). The `CantBeCounteredEffect` records the restriction; its presence on a `StaticAbility` with `AffectedObjects` scoping to creature spells is the structured form of the "creature spells can't be countered" lock.

Priority 972 — above `CantBeCastRestrictionRule` (971) and well above the generic counter/spell rules (50–80 band). Anchored (^...$) to prevent substring matches against other static lines.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/CreatureSpellsCantBeCounteredRule.cs)

### `CreaturesAreEveryCreatureTypeRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Parses the Maskwood Nexus oracle template: a static continuous effect that grants every creature type (the Changeling characteristic-defining ability) to creatures you control, creature spells you control, and creature cards you own that aren't on the battlefield.

Oracle text (verbatim): "Creatures you control are every creature type. The same is true for creature spells you control and creature cards you own that aren't on the battlefield."

Three scopes are modelled as three sibling `GainAbilityEffect` nodes in a single `StaticAbility`, each granting the Changeling characteristic-defining ability:

Creatures you control — CardTypes: ["creature"], Controller: You (permanents) Creature spells you control — CardTypes: ["spell", "creature"], Controller: You (on stack) Creature cards you own not on battlefield — CardTypes: ["creature"], Owner: You (non-permanent zones)

CR 702.73a (verbatim): "Changeling is a characteristic-defining ability. 'Changeling' means 'This object is every creature type.' This ability works everywhere, even outside the game. See rule 604.3."

ANCHORED (^…$): the two-sentence oracle paragraph is matched in full to prevent this rule from firing on any substring of a more-specific sibling. Priority 971 — above `NonlandCreatureTypeGrantRule` (970) since this shape covers a broader set of zones and is more specific in oracle intent.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/CreaturesAreEveryCreatureTypeRule.cs)

### `DamagePreventionAndMillReplacementRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Damage-prevention-plus-mill replacement effect (The Mindskinner family): "If a source you control would deal damage to an opponent, prevent that damage and each opponent mills that many cards." CR 615.1: some continuous effects are prevention effects; they watch for a damage event that would happen and completely prevent the damage that would be dealt. CR 615.1: effects that use the word "prevent" are prevention effects. CR 615.5: some prevention effects also include an additional effect, which may refer to the amount of damage that was prevented. CR 701.17: to mill a number of cards is to put that many cards from the top of a player's library into their graveyard. Structure: a `StaticAbility` with a `ReplacementEffect` that:

Watches for a `DamageEvent` where the source is controlled by You (`Source.Controller = You`) and the target is an opponent (`AffectedObjects.Controller = Opponent`). Replaces the damage with a `CompositeEffect` of: (1) a `PreventDamageEffect` targeting the opponent (All = true, recording the prevention), and (2) a `MillEffect` targeting each opponent with a `DerivedQuantity(DamageDealt)` — "that many cards" = the damage amount that would have been dealt (CR 615.5).

ANCHORED (^...$): the surface phrase "a source you control would deal damage to an opponent, prevent that damage and each opponent mills that many cards" is highly specific and does not appear as a substring of any other standard oracle-text clause.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/DamagePreventionAndMillReplacementRule.cs)

### `DashStaticRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Decomposes a "Dash [cost]" oracle line into the three abilities defined by CR 702.109a (mirroring the `ReconfigureStaticRule` multi-ability precedent).

CR 702.109a (verbatim): "Dash represents three abilities: two static abilities that function while the card with dash is on the stack, one of which may create a delayed triggered ability, and a static ability that functions while the object with dash is on the battlefield. 'Dash [cost]' means 'You may cast this card by paying [cost] rather than its mana cost,' 'If this spell's dash cost was paid, return the permanent this spell becomes to its owner's hand at the beginning of the next end step,' and 'As long as this permanent's dash cost was paid, it has haste.' Casting a spell for its dash cost follows the rules for paying alternative costs in rules 601.2b and 601.2f-h."

Priority 1001 — fires before `KeywordListRule` (priority 1000) so the three-ability decomposition takes precedence over the single-ability keyword combinator path. The combinator in `DashKeyword` remains live as a fallback, emitting only the primary alternative-cast static ability (the keyword expander returns a single `Ability`; Dash decomposes into three).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/DashStaticRule.cs)

### `DefinePTDerivedRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

A characteristic-defining ability (CDA) that sets a creature's power and/or toughness to a `DerivedQuantity` — a hand or graveyard size, not a board-object count. Handles the oracle pattern:

"[Name]'s power and toughness are each equal to the number of cards in your hand." → `Both`, `CardsInHand` "[Name]'s power is equal to the number of cards in your hand." → `Power`, `CardsInHand` "[Name]'s toughness is equal to the number of cards in your hand." → `Toughness`, `CardsInHand`

Rule 604.3: "A characteristic-defining ability defines a characteristic value for the object it's on." Rule 107.3: the value of * in a CDA box is determined by an ability (layer 7a, Rule 613.1a). The value here is derived from the controller's hand size — a game-state quantity evaluated continuously.

Priority 976 is higher than `DefinePTRule` (Priority 975) so this derived-value branch fires first; if neither derived pattern matches, control falls through to `DefinePTRule`'s board-count branch.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/DefinePTDerivedRule.cs)

### `DefinePTRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/DefinePTRule.cs)

### `DevotionConditionalLoseCreatureTypeRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"As long as your devotion to [color] is less than [N], [card name] isn't a creature." — the Theros God template (CR 205.1a / CR 700.5a). Continuous layer-4 type-loss conditional on the controller's devotion to a given colour falling below a threshold.

CR 700.5a: "A player's 'devotion to [color]' is the number of mana symbols of that color among the mana costs of permanents that player controls." MAST records the condition as a `quantityComparison` (`DevotionQuantity` &lt; threshold) and the effect as a `loseType` (`LoseTypeEffect` targeting Self, removing the "creature" card type from the permanent's type line). The StaticAbility carries no Duration: the effect persists for as long as the condition holds (modelled via an `AsLongAsDuration` wrapping the condition on the effect).

Priority 974 — above the generic `AsLongAsStaticGrantRule` (968) and below the keyword-list rules (980+), so this dedicated shape wins before the generic fallback sees it.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/DevotionConditionalLoseCreatureTypeRule.cs)

### `EachNonlandGraveyardHasEscapeRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Recognises the Underworld Breach (THB) two-sentence static paragraph:

"Each nonland card in your graveyard has escape. The escape cost is equal to the card's mana cost plus exile three other cards from your graveyard. (You may cast cards from your graveyard for their escape cost.)"

This is a continuous static ability (CR 604) that grants escape (CR 702.139) to every nonland card in the controller's graveyard, with the escape cost defined as the card's own mana cost plus exiling three other graveyard cards. The parenthetical is standard Escape reminder text (Rule 207.2) and is captured into `Reminder`.

The granted ability is modelled as a `GainAbilityEffect` whose `Target` is each nonland card in your graveyard and whose `GainedAbility` is a `StaticAbility` with `EscapeEffect`. The escape cost's mana half is a `SelfManaCost` (the card's own mana cost — a self-referential reference, not a fixed mana cost); the exile half is represented by `CardsToExile` = 3.

Rule citations: CR 604 (static abilities), CR 702.139 (Escape), CR 202.1 (mana cost), CR 207.2 (reminder text).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/EachNonlandGraveyardHasEscapeRule.cs)

### `EachOpponentSorcerySpeedRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"Each opponent can cast spells only any time they could cast a sorcery." — Teferi, Time Raveler and similar effects that restrict opponents to casting spells only at sorcery speed. This is a continuous static effect (CR 604.2 / CR 611.1) that modifies the rules of the game for all opponents: they may only cast spells during their own main phase when the stack is empty, rather than at instant speed.

CR 116.1b: "A player can cast an instant spell any time they have priority. A player can cast a noninstant spell during their main phase when the stack is empty." This restriction locks opponents to the latter (sorcery-speed) window, preventing instant-speed interaction.

Modelled as a `TimingModificationEffect` with `Modification = Restrict` and `Timing = Sorcery`, where `AppliesTo` is a `SpellReference` constraining opponents' spells (`Controller = Opponent`).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/EachOpponentSorcerySpeedRule.cs)

### `EnchantedPTAndGrantedAbilityRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"(Enchanted|Equipped) creature gets +N/+M and has "[quoted ability]"" — the P/T buff plus a granted <em>quoted ability</em> (Bear Umbra: <c>gets +2/+2 and has "Whenever this creature attacks, untap all lands you control."</c>). Sibling of `EnchantedPTAndKeywordRule`, which grants a bare <em>keyword</em>; this rule grants the quoted body the Aura confers (CR 303.4 / 702.5 — the enchanted permanent gains the quoted ability).

The quoted body is dispatched to the `TriggeredAbilityParser` first (the recurring Aura shape grants a triggered ability), falling back to the `ActivatedAbilityParser` (mirroring `GrantedAbilityRule`'s hand-off). The self-reference inside the quoted body ("this creature") resolves to the granted ability's own source (CR 109), so it is marked `IsSelf` by `ParseObjectFilter` — NOT to a separate "enchanted creature" filter.

Emits a `CompositeEffect` of [`ModifyPTEffect`, `GainAbilityEffect`] both targeting `EnchantedOrEquipped` — the exact shape of `EnchantedPTAndKeywordRule`, with a parsed ability in place of the keyword's static expansion. Priority 967 (above `EnchantedPTAndDualKeywordRule` at 966 and `EnchantedPTAndKeywordRule` at 965) so the quoted form is recognised before the keyword forms attempt (and fail) to map the quoted body.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/EnchantedPTAndGrantedAbilityRule.cs)

### `EnchantedPTForEachAttachedRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"Enchanted creature gets +N/+M for each &lt;subtype&gt; [and &lt;subtype&gt;] attached to it." — Strong Back's "+2/+2 for each Aura and Equipment attached to it". The counted set is the relational "attached to it" axis (CR 303/301 Aura/Equipment attachment), and the per-item increment exceeds 1, so the dynamic amount is a `CalculatedQuantity` with a structured `Operand` ("multiply the attached-count by N").

Sibling to `EnchantedPTForEachRule` (which handles the "you control" board count at increment 1) and `SelfPTForEachAuraAttachedRule` (the "This creature" subject). This rule covers the Enchanted/Equipped subject over an attached-to-it relational count at increment &gt; 1.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/EnchantedPTForEachAttachedRule.cs)

### `EnchantedPermanentIsVehicleArtifactRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"Enchanted permanent is a Vehicle artifact with crew N and it loses all other card types." — the Swift Reconfiguration static ability (Aura from NEO) that transforms any enchanted permanent into a Vehicle artifact and strips its non-artifact card types.

The oracle clause decomposes into three simultaneous continuous effects (all targeting `EnchantedOrEquipped`):

<b>SetCardTypes</b> (layer 4, CR 613.1d) — sets the permanent's card types to `["artifact"]`, which together with "it loses all other card types" establishes the complete type picture. CR 205.2: artifact is a card type. <b>ChangeSubtype</b> (layer 4, CR 613.1d) — sets the permanent's subtype to `["Vehicle"]`. CR 301.7: Vehicle is a subtype of artifact. <b>GainAbility</b> (layer 6, CR 613.1f) — grants `Crew N` (CR 702.122), structured as a `StaticAbility` carrying a `CrewEffect` with the printed power threshold, mirroring the native Crew encoding on Vehicle cards (e.g. Bomat Bazaar Barge, Ripclaw Wrangler).

The parenthetical reminder "(It's not a creature unless it's crewed.)" is stripped by `StripReminderText` before matching. The reminder text is not modelled: the rule engine applies it via CR 702.122a (Crew's becomes-artifact-creature clause). The `Parenthetical` is preserved in the emitted `Reminder` field for round-tripping.

CR 702.122 (Crew): "Crew N" means "Tap any number of other untapped creatures you control with total power N or greater: This permanent becomes an artifact creature until end of turn." CR 301.7 (Vehicles): "Some artifacts have the subtype 'Vehicle.'" CR 205.1a: an effect that sets card types replaces existing types not listed.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/EnchantedPermanentIsVehicleArtifactRule.cs)
