### `EquippedCreatureHasQuotedAbilityRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"Equipped creature has "[quoted ability]"" — grants a quoted activated ability to the equipped creature with no accompanying P/T modifier. Paradigm card: KHM Toralf's Hammer — "Equipped creature has '{1}{R}, {T}, Unattach Toralf's Hammer: It deals 3 damage to any target. Return Toralf's Hammer to its owner's hand.'"

This is a <b>static</b> continuous effect (CR 611.1). The grant persists for as long as the Equipment remains attached — there is no Duration node.

The quoted body is dispatched to `ActivatedAbilityParser` first (the common Equipment grant shape), falling back to `TriggeredAbilityParser`. Returns null (surfacing the gap honestly) when neither recognises the body.

Priority 964 — below `EnchantedPTAndGrantedAbilityRule` (967) and `EquippedPTKeywordAndGrantedAbilityRule` (968) so those compound forms are recognised first. Only the bare "Equipped creature has [quoted]" form falls through to this rule.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/EquippedCreatureHasQuotedAbilityRule.cs)

### `EquippedPTAndCantBlockRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Parses a static Equipment/Aura line of the form "Equipped creature gets +N/+N and can't block."

The P/T modification is a layer-7c continuous effect (CR 613.4c: "Effects and counters that modify power and/or toughness (but don't set power and/or toughness to a specific number or value) are applied."). The blocking restriction is a CR 509.1a constraint (CR 509.1a: "The defending player chooses which creatures they control, if any, will block. The chosen creatures must be untapped and they can't also be battles..."). Both effects are always-on (static, no Duration) and apply to the equipped/enchanted permanent (ObjectReferenceKind.EnchantedOrEquipped).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/EquippedPTAndCantBlockRule.cs)

### `EquippedPTAndProtectionRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"Equipped creature gets +N/+M and has protection from [quality] and from [quality]." Also handles the Enchanted form and single-quality protection.

The P/T buff is a layer-7c continuous effect (CR 613.4c). The protection grant is a static continuous effect (CR 702.16 — protection from a quality). Both apply as long as the Aura or Equipment remains attached (static, no Duration).

The protection may be from one or more qualities, each preceded by "from" (or "and from" for additional qualities), e.g. "protection from black and from green" → two Color qualities (B, G).

CR 613.4c: "Effects and counters that modify power and/or toughness (but don't set power and/or toughness to a specific number or value) are applied." CR 702.16a: "Protection is a static ability … this permanent can't be blocked, targeted, dealt damage, enchanted, or equipped by anything [with the quality]."

Priority 968 — above `EnchantedPTAndDualKeywordRule` (966) so the "protection from" phrase is recognised before the dual-keyword rule attempts to split it into two bare keyword names.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/EquippedPTAndProtectionRule.cs)

### `EquippedPTAsLongAsConditionRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"Equipped creature gets +N/+M as long as [condition]." — a conditional P/T modifier applied to the equipped creature. Paradigm card: KHM Toralf's Hammer — "Equipped creature gets +3/+0 as long as it's legendary."

This is a <b>static</b> continuous effect with an `AsLongAsDuration`: the P/T modification applies only while the stated condition holds (CR 611.2 — "A continuous effect generated by a static ability has no defined end; it lasts as long as the permanent with that ability remains on the battlefield and the condition holds"). The condition is parsed by `ConditionParser`; conditions not yet structured emit an `OtherCondition` residual.

Emits a single `StaticAbility` with one `ModifyPTEffect` targeting `EnchantedOrEquipped` and an `AsLongAsDuration` carrying the condition. Both the P (power) and T (toughness) modifiers accept a '+'/'-' sign and a digit so "+3/+0" and "+0/+3" round-trip correctly.

Priority 963 — below `EquippedCreatureHasQuotedAbilityRule` (964) and below the existing "Equipped creature gets … and has …" compound rules (965-968) so those more-specific forms are consumed first. Sits above the generic `AsLongAsStaticGrantRule` (968) priority so it is tried before the leading/suffix forms in that rule (which require "it/this creature" as the subject rather than "Equipped creature").

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/EquippedPTAsLongAsConditionRule.cs)

### `EquippedPTKeywordAndGrantedAbilityRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"Equipped creature gets +N/+M and has [keyword] and "[quoted ability]"" — the P/T buff plus a bare keyword grant and a quoted ability grant on the equipped creature.

Canonical example: The Reaver Cleaver — "Equipped creature gets +1/+1 and has trample and 'Whenever this creature deals combat damage to a player or planeswalker, create that many Treasure tokens.'" This is a three-part continuous ability:

P/T modification (layer 7c — CR 613.4c) Keyword ability grant (layer 6 — CR 613.1f) — the bare keyword (e.g. trample) Triggered ability grant (layer 6 — CR 613.1f) — the quoted triggered ability

All three effects apply to the equipped creature (`EnchantedOrEquipped`) and are always-on (static, no Duration).

Rule 702.6 (Equipment): an Equipment's static abilities apply to the equipped creature. Rule 613.1 (layer ordering): P/T and keyword/ability grants are applied in strict layer order; MAST records the oracle text descriptively — layer ordering is engine territory.

The quoted body is dispatched to the `TriggeredAbilityParser` first, falling back to `ActivatedAbilityParser`, mirroring the sibling `EnchantedPTAndGrantedAbilityRule`. Priority 968 (above `EnchantedPTAndGrantedAbilityRule` at 967) so the three-part keyword+quoted form is recognised before the two-part quoted form could attempt to match it.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/EquippedPTKeywordAndGrantedAbilityRule.cs)

### `EquippedPTKeywordColorSubtypeRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Parses the compound static Equipment line: "Equipped creature gets +N/+N, has [keyword], and is a [color] [Subtype]."

Nim Deathmantle (SOM) is the canonical example: "Equipped creature gets +2/+2, has intimidate, and is a black Zombie." This is a four-part continuous ability:

P/T modification (layer 7c — CR 613.4c) Keyword ability grant (layer 6 — CR 613.1f) Color change to [color] (layer 5 — CR 613.1e) Subtype change to [Subtype] (layer 4 — CR 613.1d)

All four effects target the equipped creature (`EnchantedOrEquipped`) and persist for as long as the Equipment is attached (no Duration — always-on static).

Rule 702.6 (Equipment): an Equipment's static abilities apply to the equipped creature. Rule 613.1 (layer ordering): type, color, and P/T changes are applied in strict layer order; MAST records the oracle text descriptively — layer ordering is engine territory.

Reminder text is stripped by `StripReminderText` before matching, following the convention of sibling Equipment static rules such as `EquippedPTAndCantBlockRule`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/EquippedPTKeywordColorSubtypeRule.cs)

### `EvasionRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/EvasionRule.cs)

### `EvokeStaticRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Decomposes an "Evoke [cost]" oracle line into the two abilities defined by CR 702.74a — a static alternative-cast ability and an ETB-sacrifice triggered ability.

CR 702.74a (verbatim): "Evoke represents two abilities: a static ability that functions in any zone from which the card with evoke can be cast and a triggered ability that functions on the battlefield. 'Evoke [cost]' means 'You may cast this card by paying [cost] rather than paying its mana cost' and 'When this permanent enters, if its evoke cost was paid, its controller sacrifices it.' Casting a spell for its evoke cost follows the rules for paying alternative costs in rules 601.2b and 601.2f-h."

Priority 1001 — fires before `KeywordListRule` (priority 1000) so the two-ability decomposition takes precedence over the single-ability keyword combinator path. "if its evoke cost was paid" is the generalized cost-paid reference node `KeywordCostPaidCondition` keyed on Evoke (reference-not-resolution, ADR 0004); both abilities carry `Evoke` as their `KeywordSource` (keyword-inherent, mirroring the Reconfigure precedent where both abilities carry their KeywordSource).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/EvokeStaticRule.cs)

### `ExertStaticRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Decomposes an Exert paragraph into the two linked abilities defined by CR 701.43d: a static ability (the optional cost to attack) and a triggered ability ("When you do").

CR 701.43d (verbatim): "'You may exert [this creature] as it attacks' is an optional cost to attack (see rule 508.1g). Some objects with this static ability have a triggered ability that triggers 'when you do' printed in the same paragraph. These abilities are linked. (See rule 607.2h.)"

Combat Celebrant's oracle text (with trailing reminder stripped): <c>"If this creature hasn't been exerted this turn, you may exert it as it attacks. When you do, untap all other creatures you control and after this phase, there is an additional combat phase."</c>

Returns two abilities in order:

A `StaticAbility` with `KeywordSource = "Exert"`, a `OtherCondition` capturing the "hasn't been exerted this turn" gate, and an `ExertEffect` recording the optional cost to attack. A `TriggeredAbility` with `KeywordSource = "Exert"`, `Trigger.Event = TriggerEvent.Exerted` ("When you do"), and the combined untap + additional-combat-phase effects.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/ExertStaticRule.cs)

### `FoodTokenAugmentationReplacementRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Token-augmentation replacement effect (Peregrin Took family): "If one or more tokens would be created under your control, those tokens plus an additional Food token are created instead." CR 614.1: A replacement effect watches for a particular event — "If [event] would happen, [modified result] instead" — and modifies that event. The original tokens are still created (`OriginalEventOccurs` is true), and one additional Food token is created alongside them. Distinct from `TokenAugmentationReplacementRule` (Chatterfang family), which adds a creature token matching the count of the original tokens ("that many"), and from `TokenDoublingReplacementRule` (Doubling Season family), which doubles the quantity. This family adds a single named token subtype regardless of the original token count. The trailing parenthetical "(It's an artifact with ...)" is reminder text for the Food token (CR 207.2) and is preserved on `Reminder`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/FoodTokenAugmentationReplacementRule.cs)

### `FoodsHaveActivatedAbilitiesOfExiledCreaturesRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"Foods you control have all activated abilities of all creature cards exiled with this creature." — the Hazel's Brewmaster continuous static ability that copies a dynamic set of activated abilities from exiled creature cards onto Foods the controller controls.

<b>CR 613.1f</b> (layer 6 — ability-granting continuous effects): this static ability continuously grants abilities to the target permanents while the source is on the battlefield.

Anchored (^…$) to prevent matching substrings of longer ability lines. Must run before `BareKeywordGrantRule` (which tries to match "Foods you control have …") to claim the more-specific "all activated abilities" surface before the keyword-grant fallback can attempt it. Priority 996 (above BareKeywordGrantRule at 967).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/FoodsHaveActivatedAbilitiesOfExiledCreaturesRule.cs)

### `GainControlOfEnchantedRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/GainControlOfEnchantedRule.cs)

### `GrantAlternativeCostRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Parses "You may pay {cost} rather than pay the mana cost for [type] [subtype] creature spells you cast." — the Rooftop Storm shape.

CR 118.9 (verbatim): "Some spells have alternative costs. An alternative cost is a cost listed in a spell's text, or applied to it from another effect, that its controller may pay rather than paying the spell's mana cost. Alternative costs are usually phrased, 'You may [action] rather than pay [this object's] mana cost,' or 'You may cast [this object] without paying its mana cost.'"

The surface phrase "You may pay {0} rather than pay the mana cost for" is fully anchored (^…$) because Rooftop Storm's full oracle text is a single ability line; no sibling trigger or effect shares this opening. The filter noun phrase "<em>subtype</em> creature" (or just "<em>subtype</em>") before "spells you cast" is captured and routed through `BuildTypeSpellFilter(string)` to emit a structured `ObjectFilter`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/GrantAlternativeCostRule.cs)

### `GrantEquipInstantTimingRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"You may activate equip abilities any time you could cast an instant." — Leonin Shikari. Grants instant-speed timing (CR 601/602 activation timing) to a referenced class of abilities. The affected class is a typed `ActivatedAbilityReference` keyed on the surviving `Equip` identity (ADR 0003): the keyword that survives decomposition is the same identity this timing grant filters on.

Shares the `AbilityReference` value type with `AppliesToCostReductionRule`; here it is carried on `AppliesTo` rather than a cost effect.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/GrantEquipInstantTimingRule.cs)

### `GrantedAbilityRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/GrantedAbilityRule.cs)

### `HistoricSpellCostReductionRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Parses "Historic spells you cast cost {N} less to cast." — the static cost-reduction ability granted by cards like Jhoira's Familiar (DOM). CR 700.6: "The term historic refers to an object that has the legendary supertype, the artifact card type, or the Saga subtype." Historic is a named game quality (not a printed card type or supertype), so the filter uses `IsHistoric` rather than the Supertypes/CardTypes axes. Priority 985 — fires before `TypeSpellCostReductionRule` (priority 984) so the "Historic" token is claimed before the generic single-noun type rule misclassifies it as a creature subtype. The pattern is anchored (^…$) after stripping reminder text; the "Historic" match is case-sensitive (oracle text always uses title case for the quality name) to prevent false positives.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/HistoricSpellCostReductionRule.cs)

### `KeywordListRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/KeywordListRule.cs)

### `KickedCountEntersWithCountersRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"This [type] enters with a [counterType] counter on it for each time it was kicked." (Skitter of Lizards). The kicked-count-scaled sibling of `EntersWithCountersRule` (a literal/X count): the number of counters is the number of times the spell was kicked, not the article "a".

The count is a `KeywordCostPaidCountQuantity` keyed on `Kicker` — a multikicker cost is a kicker cost, so "for each time it was kicked" references Kicker. Reference-not-resolution (ADR 0004): the count names the producing keyword's identity, NOT a variable threaded from the Multikicker `AdditionalCastCostEffect` producer on the same card (mirrors CreateTokenRule's Wolfbriar handling of the same "for each time it was kicked" phrase).

Rules: 614.1c (enters-with replacement is a static ability applied as the permanent enters), 702.33d ("If a spell's controller declares the intention to pay any of that spell's kicker costs, that spell has been 'kicked.' If a spell has two kicker costs or has multikicker, it may be kicked multiple times.").

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/KickedCountEntersWithCountersRule.cs)

### `KickerConditionalEntersWithCountersRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/KickerConditionalEntersWithCountersRule.cs)

### `KickerTapPermanentsStaticRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Decomposes a "Kicker—Tap an untapped [Subtype] you control." oracle line (em-dash kicker with a tap-permanents non-mana cost, CR 702.33a) into a single `StaticAbility` carrying an `AdditionalCastCostEffect` with a `TapPermanentsCost`.

CR 702.33a (verbatim): "Kicker is a static ability that functions while the spell with kicker is on the stack. 'Kicker [cost]' means 'You may pay an additional [cost] as you cast this spell.' Paying a spell's kicker cost(s) follows the rules for paying additional costs in rules 601.2b and 601.2f-h."

The em-dash separator ("Kicker—") is the Wizards printing convention for non-mana kicker costs (e.g. Blood Tribute: "Kicker—Tap an untapped Vampire you control."). The `KickerKeyword` combinator handles the mana-symbol form ("Kicker {cost}") via the tokenised Superpower pipeline; this rule intercepts the em-dash + tap-permanents shape before `KeywordListRule` fires so the full cost is preserved as a typed `TapPermanentsCost`.

Priority 1001 — fires before `KeywordListRule` (priority 1000). The pattern is anchored (^…$) to avoid substring-matching inside a more-specific sibling. Requires the cost phrase to match "Tap an untapped [Subtype] you control" exactly (case-insensitive); the subtype is captured and used as the filter criterion.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/KickerTapPermanentsStaticRule.cs)

### `LandCardsInGraveyardHaveDredgeRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Recognises the Necrobloom static paragraph: "Land cards in your graveyard have dredge N. ([reminder])"

This is a continuous static ability (CR 604) that grants Dredge N (CR 702.52) to every land card in the controller's graveyard. The parenthetical is standard Dredge reminder text (CR 207.2) and is captured into `Reminder`.

The granted ability is modelled as a `GainAbilityEffect` whose `Target` is each land card in your graveyard (Filter: CardTypes=["land"], Controller=You, Zone=Graveyard) and whose `GainedAbility` is a `StaticAbility` with `DredgeEffect` and `KeywordSource` = "Dredge". Mirrors `EachNonlandGraveyardHasEscapeRule` (Underworld Breach) over the land-type axis and with Dredge in place of Escape.

Rule citations: CR 604 (static abilities), CR 702.52 (Dredge), CR 207.2 (reminder text), CR 404/404.1 (graveyard zone).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/LandCardsInGraveyardHaveDredgeRule.cs)

### `LandsAreEveryBasicLandTypeRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Parses the Dryad-of-the-Ilysian-Grove oracle template: a static continuous effect that additively grants every basic land type to all lands the controller controls, "in addition to their other types."

Oracle text (verbatim): "Lands you control are every basic land type in addition to their other types."

This is a layer-4 (CR 613.1d) continuous type-changing effect. Per CR 305.7: "If a land gains one or more land types in addition to its own, it keeps its land types and rules text, and it gains the new land types and mana abilities." The five basic land types (CR 305.6: Plains, Island, Swamp, Mountain, Forest) are all granted additively via an `AddTypeEffect` with `AddedSubtypes` containing all five.

CR 305.6 (verbatim): "The basic land types are Plains, Island, Swamp, Mountain, and Forest. If an object uses the words 'basic land type,' it's referring to one of these subtypes."

CR 305.2 (verbatim): "A player can normally play one land during their turn; however, continuous effects may increase this number." (context for the first ability on the Dryad, handled by `PlayAdditionalLandRule`).

ANCHORED (^…$): the full oracle sentence is matched exactly to prevent this rule from firing on any substring of a more-specific sibling. Priority 971 — above `NonlandCreatureTypeGrantRule` (970) since this shape is dedicated to the "every basic land type" template and the controller-scoped land subject.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/LandsAreEveryBasicLandTypeRule.cs)

### `LandsYouControlEnterUntappedRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"Lands you control enter untapped." — a static continuous replacement effect (CR 614.1d) that causes all lands the controller controls to enter the battlefield untapped rather than tapped. Canonical card: Spelunking.

CR 614.1d (verbatim): "Continuous effects that read '[This permanent] enters . . .' or '[Objects] enter [the battlefield] . . .' are replacement effects." This oracle sentence matches the second template exactly: "[Lands you control] enter [untapped]." — a replacement that suppresses any tapped-entry for controller-owned lands.

Modelled as a `StaticAbility` with `AsObjectEnters` — the same timing used by the "Creatures your opponents control enter tapped" arm of `EntersTappedRule` — carrying a `UntapEffect` targeting each land the controller controls. The parallel with `EntersTappedRule` is intentional: the structure is "as each [object] enters, [tap/untap] it"; only the direction (untap instead of tap) and the filter (lands you control instead of creatures opponents control) differ.

ANCHORED (^…$): fully anchored to prevent substring-matching any sibling oracle line that mentions lands or untapped. Priority 972 — one above `LandsAreEveryBasicLandTypeRule` (971) so both land-static shapes are tried before the generic fall-through.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/LandsYouControlEnterUntappedRule.cs)

### `LegendRuleSuppressionRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/LegendRuleSuppressionRule.cs)

### `LookAtTopCardAnyTimeRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Recognises "You may look at the top card of your library any time." and produces a `StaticAbility` with a `LookAtTopCardAnyTimeEffect`.

This line appears on Sphinx of Jwar Isle, Bolas's Citadel, and similar cards that grant the controller a continuous permission to inspect their library's top card. It was previously misrouted to the spell parser; this rule intercepts it in the static pipeline where it belongs (Rule 604.3 — continuous effects).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/LookAtTopCardAnyTimeRule.cs)

### `LordPTBuffRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/LordPTBuffRule.cs)

### `MarvinHasAllActivatedAbilitiesRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"Marvin has all activated abilities of creatures you control that don't have the same name as this creature." — the Marvin, Murderous Mimic continuous static ability that grants all activated abilities from other creatures the controller controls to itself.

<b>CR 613.1f</b> (layer 6 — ability-adding continuous effects): the static ability continuously grants abilities to Marvin while it is on the battlefield.

<b>CR 602.1</b>: "An activated ability is the only kind of ability that can be activated." The class of abilities granted is identified as "activated."

Anchored (^…$) to prevent matching substrings of longer ability lines. The card name prefix ("[Name] has all activated abilities …") is matched by a general pattern so this rule generalises to other mimic-style cards with the same oracle shape. Priority 996 (above generic keyword-grant rules at 967) so this specific shape is claimed before a keyword-grant fallback attempts it.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/MarvinHasAllActivatedAbilitiesRule.cs)

### `MayCastExiledWithSelfRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"During your turn, you may cast cards exiled with [this object] and you may cast them as though they had flash. Mana of any type can be spent to cast those spells." — the persistent play-from-exile permission of a CR 406.6 linked pair (Azula, Cunning Usurper).

This is the second, separate static ability of the linked pair: it refers to "cards exiled with [object]" via the `ExiledWith` reference rather than threading a binding from the exile trigger (ADR 0004 "reference, not resolution"). It is a separate static permission — not a one-shot bundled inside the exile ability — so a window exists (ADR 0004 "topology, not annotation"). A card referring to itself by name resolves to the `Self` source (CR 201.4 self-reference). CR 406.6 (linked exile); CR 702.8 (flash timing).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/MayCastExiledWithSelfRule.cs)

### `MayCastFromTopOfLibraryRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Recognises "You may cast artifact spells and colorless spells from the top of your library." and produces a `StaticAbility` with two `MayPlayFromLibraryEffect` instances — one for artifact spells and one for colorless spells.

The oracle line is a single sentence but names two disjoint eligibility classes: artifact spells (CardTypes=["artifact"]) and colorless spells (IsColorless=true). MAST models these as two sibling `MayPlayFromLibraryEffect` nodes under one `StaticAbility`: the pair expresses "you have permission to cast from the top if the card is an artifact OR if the card is colorless". The effects sit on the same ability rather than two separate abilities because the oracle sentence is one continuous permission grant, not two discrete abilities (CR 604.2 — topology not annotation).

CR 604.2: "Static abilities create continuous effects … These effects are active as long as the permanent with the ability remains on the battlefield."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/MayCastFromTopOfLibraryRule.cs)

### `MayPlayFromTopOfLibraryRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Recognises the Bolas's Citadel two-sentence permission: "You may play lands and cast spells from the top of your library. If you cast a spell this way, pay life equal to its mana value rather than pay its mana cost."

This is a static ability (Rule 604 — continuous effect) that grants two simultaneous permissions: (a) play lands from the top of the library, and (b) cast spells from the top of the library, with (c) an alternative cost rider that replaces the mana cost of any spell cast this way with "pay life equal to its mana value" (CR 118.9b — alternative cost; CR 202.3 — mana value). MAST describes the permission; evaluation of what the top card is at a given moment is engine territory (ADR 0003 describe-not-execute).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/MayPlayFromTopOfLibraryRule.cs)

### `MayPlayLandsFromGraveyardRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"You may play lands from your graveyard." — a static permission granting the controller the ability to play land cards from their graveyard.

CR 305.1: "A player who has priority may play a land card from their hand during a main phase of their turn when the stack is empty. Playing a land is a special action; it doesn't use the stack." This rule extends that permission so eligible land cards may come from the graveyard. The description is static (it persists as long as the source is on the battlefield — CR 604.2); MAST models the permission, not the execution. Crucible of Worlds and Ramunap Excavator share this exact oracle line.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/MayPlayLandsFromGraveyardRule.cs)

### `MayPlayLandsFromTopOfLibraryRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Parses "You may play lands from the top of your library." and produces a `StaticAbility` with a `MayPlayFromTopOfLibraryEffect` whose `Actions` list contains only `PlayLands`.

CR 305.2: "A player can normally play one land during their turn; however, continuous effects may increase this number." This rule extends the play-land permission so eligible land cards may come from the top of the library rather than (or in addition to) the hand. The "You may" preamble makes the permission elective — the controller is not required to play lands from the top.

The effect node is `MayPlayFromTopOfLibraryEffect` (not `OptionalEffect` wrapping a separate effect) because "may" is semantically built into the effect type — it is a permission grant, not a wrapped optional action. This follows the same convention as `MayPlayLandsFromGraveyardRule`, which uses `MayPlayFromGraveyardEffect` directly without an `OptionalEffect` wrapper.

The pattern is fully anchored (^…$) so it does not misfire on "You may play lands and cast spells from the top of your library." (Bolas's Citadel), which is handled by `MayPlayFromTopOfLibraryRule` and contains additional text beyond this shape.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/MayPlayLandsFromTopOfLibraryRule.cs)

### `MillDoublingReplacementRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Mill-doubling replacement effect (Bruvac the Grandiloquent): "If an opponent would mill one or more cards, they mill twice that many cards instead." CR 614.1: replacement effects apply continuously as events happen and watch for a particular event — "If [event] would happen, [modified event] instead" is a replacement, NOT a triggered ability. CR 701.17a: to mill a number of cards is to put that many cards from the top of a library into a graveyard. Structure mirrors `TokenDoublingReplacementRule`/`CounterDoublingReplacementRule`: the replaced event is a `MillEvent` by an opponent ("one or more" → MinimumQuantity 1), and the doubling is a structured `ReplacementModifier{ Type: "double" }` ("twice that many"), not a free-text description. The trailing mill reminder (CR 207.2) carries no rules meaning and is preserved on `Reminder`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/MillDoublingReplacementRule.cs)

### `ModularStaticRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Decomposes a "Modular N" oracle line into the two abilities defined by CR 702.43a (mirroring the `DashStaticRule` / `ReconfigureStaticRule` multi-ability precedent).

CR 702.43a (verbatim): "Modular represents both a static ability and a triggered ability. 'Modular N' means 'This permanent enters with N +1/+1 counters on it' and 'When this permanent is put into a graveyard from the battlefield, you may put a +1/+1 counter on target artifact creature for each +1/+1 counter on this permanent.'"

Priority 1001 — fires before `KeywordListRule` (priority 1000) so the two-ability decomposition takes precedence over the single-ability keyword combinator path. The combinator in `ModularKeyword` remains live as a fallback, emitting only the PRIMARY enters-with-counters static ability (the keyword expander returns a single `Ability`; Modular decomposes into two).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/ModularStaticRule.cs)

### `NoncombatDamageDoublingReplacementRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Noncombat-damage-doubling replacement effect (Solphim, Mayhem Dominus): "If a source you control would deal noncombat damage to an opponent or a permanent an opponent controls, it deals double that damage to that player or permanent instead." CR 614.1a: effects using "instead" are replacement effects. This effect watches for a noncombat-damage event from a source the controller owns and replaces the damage quantity with double. Structure mirrors `MillDoublingReplacementRule`: the replaced event is a `DamageEvent` with Source controlled by You and DamageType "noncombat", directed at an opponent-controlled recipient, and the doubling is a structured `ReplacementModifier{ Type: "double" }` ("double that damage"), not a free-text description. ANCHORED (^...$): the phrase "noncombat damage to an opponent or a permanent an opponent controls … double that damage … instead" is highly specific and does not appear as a substring of any other standard oracle-text clause.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/NoncombatDamageDoublingReplacementRule.cs)

### `NonlandCreatureTypeGrantRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Parses the Ashaya-family oracle template: a static continuous effect that additively grants one or more card types and subtypes to a group of permanents "in addition to their other types."

Handled pattern:

"Nontoken creatures you control are Forest lands in addition to their other types." → `AddTypeEffect` with Target={Each, nontoken creatures you control}, AddedCardTypes=["land"], AddedSubtypes=["Forest"].

CR 205.1a (verbatim): "Some effects change an object's card type, subtype, and/or supertype but specify that the object retains a prior card type, subtype, and/or supertype. In such cases, all the object's prior card types, subtypes, and supertypes are retained, and the effect causes the object to gain or lose other card types, subtypes, and/or supertypes."

Priority 970 — below the P/T-defining rules and keyword lists (976–1000) and above the broad grant/anthem fallbacks (967–968), so this dedicated shape fires first against the "nontoken creatures ... are [type]" template.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/NonlandCreatureTypeGrantRule.cs)

### `NontokenCreaturesHaveKeywordRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Parses "Nontoken creatures you control have [keyword]." — a static continuous effect that grants a keyword ability to every nontoken creature the controller controls. This is the Rhythm of the Wild shape.

CR 702.136a (verbatim): "Riot is a static ability. 'Riot' means 'You may have this permanent enter with an additional +1/+1 counter on it. If you don't, it gains haste.'"

The "nontoken" qualifier maps to `IsToken = false` on the `ObjectFilter` (CR 111.1: a token is not a card). The grant is a continuous effect with no explicit duration — a static ability's continuous effect lasts for as long as the source remains on the battlefield.

Priority 969 — above `BareKeywordGrantRule` (967) so this more-specific nontoken-scoped shape fires before the broader filter arm would mis-match "Nontoken creatures" as a subtype "Nontoken". Anchored pattern prevents substring matches against more-specific siblings.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/NontokenCreaturesHaveKeywordRule.cs)

### `ObjectActivatedAbilityCostReductionRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Static cost-reduction ability that lowers the activation cost for all activated abilities belonging to a category of permanents the controller controls, with an optional mana floor.

Canonical pattern (Forensic Gadgeteer, CR 602.1): "Activated abilities of `ObjectFilter` you control cost {N} less to activate[. This effect can't reduce the mana in that cost to less than one mana]."

CR 602.1c: "An activated ability is the only kind of ability that can be activated." The reference here is to all activated abilities on permanents matching the type filter, not to a specific keyword's activated ability (contrast `AppliesToCostReductionRule` which keys on a keyword identity). Encoded as `PermanentFilter` on a `CostReductionEffect`.

The optional trailing sentence "This effect can't reduce the mana in that cost to less than one mana." becomes `MinimumManaCost` = 1.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/ObjectActivatedAbilityCostReductionRule.cs)

### `OpeningHandRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/OpeningHandRule.cs)

### `OpponentArtifactsActivatedAbilitiesLockedRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"Activated abilities of artifacts your opponents control can't be activated." — Karn, the Great Creator's static ability. A continuous restriction (CR 604.2 / CR 611.1) that prevents any activated ability on an artifact controlled by an opponent from being put on the stack (CR 602.5: "A player can't begin to activate an ability that's prohibited from being activated.").

Modelled as a `StaticAbility` whose `AffectedObjects` is an `ObjectFilter` scoping the restriction to artifacts controlled by opponents (`CardTypes=["artifact"], Controller=Opponent`), and whose `Effects` list contains a single `CantActivateAbilitiesEffect` with no `Target` (the affected-objects filter on the ability already names the locked objects). This matches the pattern used for global activation locks (cf. `EnchantedCantAttackOrBlockRule` for the per-Aura form where Target is set to `EnchantedOrEquipped`).

The pattern is anchored (^…$) so it cannot fire as a substring of a longer clause such as a reminder parenthetical.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/OpponentArtifactsActivatedAbilitiesLockedRule.cs)

### `OtherCreaturesAreSubtypeAndHaveAbilityRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Parses the Ygra-family static ability template: "Other creatures are [Subtype] [card-types] in addition to their other types and have &quot;[quoted activated ability]&quot;."

This oracle template combines a layer-4 additive type grant (CR 205.1a) with a layer-6 ability grant (CR 613.1f) in a single printed sentence. MAST represents this as ONE `StaticAbility` carrying two effects in order:

`AddTypeEffect` — grants the named card types and subtypes. `GainAbilityEffect` — grants the quoted activated ability.

Both effects share the same target filter ("Other creatures" = all creatures excluding the source, `ExcludeSelf` = true, no controller restriction — this affects ALL creatures on the battlefield except the source).

Example oracle text: "Other creatures are Food artifacts in addition to their other types and have &quot;{2}, {T}, Sacrifice this permanent: You gain 3 life.&quot;" (Ygra, Eater of All, BLB).

CR 205.1a (verbatim): "Some effects change an object's card type, subtype, and/or supertype but specify that the object retains a prior card type, subtype, and/or supertype." CR 613.1d (layer 4 — type-changing effects), CR 613.1f (layer 6 — ability-granting). CR 111.10b: "A Food token is a colorless Food artifact token with '{2}, {T}, Sacrifice this token: You gain 3 life.'" (The CR definition of the Food subtype.)

Priority 968 — below the controller-scoped type-grant rules (969–970) and above the fallback anthem rules so this dedicated shape fires before the generic type-grant rules decline it (they require "you control"). Anchored (^…$) to prevent substring overlap.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/OtherCreaturesAreSubtypeAndHaveAbilityRule.cs)

### `PainlandRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/PainlandRule.cs)

### `PanharmoniconTriggerDoublingRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"If an artifact or creature entering causes a triggered ability of a permanent you control to trigger, that ability triggers an additional time."

This models Panharmonicon's trigger-multiplication static ability. Like Echoes of Eternity (see `TriggerDoublingStaticRule`), the effect is a `ReplacementEffect` over an `AbilityTriggerEvent`. The critical difference: Panharmonicon is scoped to triggered abilities whose cause is "an artifact or creature entering the battlefield" — encoded via `CausedByEntering` with a two-type card-type filter.

CR 603.2d (verbatim from rules-structure.json): "An ability may state that a triggered ability triggers additional times. In this case, rather than simply determining that such an ability has triggered, determine how many times it should trigger, then that ability triggers that many times."

`AffectedObjects` = <c>{ CardTypes:["permanent"], Controller: You }</c> — only triggered abilities of <em>permanents you control</em> are doubled. `CausedByEntering` = <c>{ CardTypes: ["artifact", "creature"] }</c> — only when the ETB entering object was an artifact or creature.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/PanharmoniconTriggerDoublingRule.cs)

### `PayLifeForColoredManaRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Parses the K'rrik, Son of Yawgmoth static ability: "For each {X} in a cost, you may pay N life rather than pay that mana."

This is a continuous static permission (CR 604) that allows the controller to substitute N life for each instance of the named mana color in any cost. The mechanic is a generalization of the Phyrexian mana rule (CR 107.4f) applied universally to all costs paid by the controller rather than only to Phyrexian mana symbols on the card itself.

Pattern: "For each {COLOR} in a cost, you may pay N life rather than pay that mana." Anchored (^…$) to prevent substring matches in sibling trigger/effect contexts. No other static rule in the registry matches this surface phrase — the anchor is belt-and-suspenders against future rules that happen to contain the words "life" and "cost" (the #1 FAIL class per the vertical-slice contract).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/PayLifeForColoredManaRule.cs)

### `PhantomDamagePreventionRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/PhantomDamagePreventionRule.cs)

### `PlayAdditionalLandRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Parses "You may play [an | N] additional land[s] on each of your turns." CR 305.2: "A player can normally play one land during their turn; however, continuous effects may increase this number."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/PlayAdditionalLandRule.cs)

### `PlayWithTopRevealedRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Parses "Play with the top card of your library revealed." and produces a `StaticAbility` with a `PlayWithTopRevealedEffect`.

CR 701.18c: "Some effects instruct a player to 'play' with a certain aspect of the game changed, such as 'Play with the top card of your library revealed.' 'Play' in this sense means to play the Magic game." CR 401.5 governs how the revealed top-card rule interacts with spells being cast; CR 401.6 governs card-object identity when the top card changes.

This is a continuous, non-optional static effect: the controller must keep the top card of their library face-up at all times while the source permanent is on the battlefield (CR 604.2). The effect is NOT wrapped in `OptionalEffect`. The pattern is fully anchored (^…$) to prevent matching "Play with …" substrings inside longer ability lines.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/PlayWithTopRevealedRule.cs)

### `PlayersSpendManaAsAnyColorRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Parses the Mycosynth Lattice "Players may spend mana as though it were mana of any color" oracle template: a continuous static permission that lets all players pay costs with any mana as though it were colored.

CR 609.4b (verbatim): "If an effect allows a player to spend mana 'as though it were mana of any [type or color],' this affects only how the player may pay a cost. It doesn't change that cost, and it doesn't change what mana was actually spent to pay that cost."

The "Players" subject identifies all players globally. Future variants with "You may spend mana" would emit the same node type with `Beneficiary = "You"`.

Priority 967 — below `AllObjectsAreColorlessRule` (968).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/PlayersSpendManaAsAnyColorRule.cs)

### `ReconfigureStaticRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Decomposes a "Reconfigure [cost]" oracle line into two `ActivatedAbility` nodes, one per ability defined by CR 702.151a.

CR 702.151a (verbatim): "Reconfigure represents two activated abilities. Reconfigure [cost] means "[Cost]: Attach this permanent to another target creature you control. Activate only as a sorcery" and "[Cost]: Unattach this permanent. Activate only if this permanent is attached to a creature and only as a sorcery."

Priority 1001 — fires before `KeywordListRule` (priority 1000) so the two-ability decomposition takes precedence over the single-ability keyword combinator path. The activation condition "only if this permanent is attached to a creature" is a runtime-state guard with no existing structured node; it is omitted per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/ReconfigureStaticRule.cs)

### `ReplicateStaticRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Decomposes a "Replicate [cost]" oracle line into the two abilities defined by CR 702.56a (mirroring the `ReconfigureStaticRule` / `DashStaticRule` multi-ability precedent).

CR 702.56a (verbatim): "Replicate is a keyword that represents two abilities... 'Replicate [cost]' means 'As an additional cost to cast this spell, you may pay [cost] any number of times' and 'When you cast this spell, if a replicate cost was paid for it, copy it for each time its replicate cost was paid. If the spell has any targets, you may choose new targets for any of the copies.'"

Priority 1001 — fires before `KeywordListRule` (priority 1000) so the two-ability decomposition takes precedence over the single-ability keyword combinator path. The combinator in `ReplicateKeyword` remains live as a fallback, emitting only the primary additional-cost static ability (the keyword expander returns a single `Ability`; Replicate decomposes into two).

"you may choose new targets for any of the copies" is engine territory (target reselection) and is omitted per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/ReplicateStaticRule.cs)

### `RevealOrEntersTappedRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Reveal-or-enters-tapped land ("Snarl" cycle, Strixhaven): "As this land enters, you may reveal a [TypeA] or [TypeB] card from your hand. If you don't, this land enters tapped."

Modelled descriptively as the checkland/fastland shape — the composite "as this enters, tap it" (`When = StaticTimingKind.AsThisEnters` on the `StaticAbility`, a plain `TapEffect` targeting self) gated by `Condition`. The condition names the predicate under which the land enters UNtapped (the controller revealing a qualifying card): the land enters tapped whenever that does not happen. The "you may reveal" choice is recorded as the condition's text rather than as a game action — MAST describes the card, it does not execute the reveal (descriptive-not-engine doctrine).

The reveal is a pure entry gate carrying no quantified resource, so it collapses cleanly onto the `Condition` axis. This differs from the painland/shockland template (`PainlandRule`) where the optional action is a life payment — a quantified cost that warrants its own `PayLifeEffect`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/RevealOrEntersTappedRule.cs)

### `SelfNameEntersTappedDoesntUntapRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"[Name] enters tapped and doesn't untap during your untap step." — the compound static restriction printed on Grimgrin, Corpse-Born and similar permanents that name themselves rather than using "This [type]".

Produces two `StaticAbility` nodes from one oracle sentence:

`AsThisEnters` + `TapEffect` targeting `Self` — the entry-tapped replacement (CR 603.6d: "Such text is a static ability—not a triggered ability—whose effect occurs as part of the event that puts the permanent onto the battlefield."). `DoesntUntapEffect` with `WhoseUntapStep = "your"` — the continuous skip-untap effect (CR 502.3: "effects can keep one or more of a player's permanents from untapping.").

Priority 963 — just above `EntersTappedRule` (Priority 962) so this compound form is matched first; both rules handle different surface shapes (self-by-name compound vs. "This [type] enters tapped" simple). Pattern is anchored (^…$) so it cannot consume a plain "enters tapped" clause that the `EntersTappedRule` already handles correctly.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/SelfNameEntersTappedDoesntUntapRule.cs)

### `SelfPTForEachAuraAttachedRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"This creature gets +N/+N for each Aura attached to it." — the Kor Spiritdancer family. Distinct from `SelfPTForEachRule` in two ways: (1) the counted set is "Aura attached to it" (an attachment relationship, not a "you control" board count), and (2) the per-Aura increment may exceed 1 (Spiritdancer is +2/+2 per Aura), which `SelfPTForEachRule` explicitly defers to a "distinct family shape" by returning null on multiplier &gt; 1.

The dynamic amount is "N × (number of Auras attached)". A per-each increment of 1 reuses a bare `CountQuantity` (matching the single-increment convention); an increment &gt; 1 wraps that count in a `CalculatedQuantity` with `Operation = "multiply"`, mirroring the established CalculatedQuantity-wrapping convention (e.g. the "negate" wrapper in `ActivatedRuleHelpers.ParseSignedModifier`). No new `Quantity` subtype is introduced — both discriminators (`count`, `calculated`) already exist.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/SelfPTForEachAuraAttachedRule.cs)

### `SelfPTForEachRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/SelfPTForEachRule.cs)

### `SiegeReminderStaticRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Standalone Siege type-reminder line, identical on every "Battle — Siege" front face (the March-of-the-Machine Invasions): "(As a Siege enters, choose an opponent to protect it. You and others can attack it. When it's defeated, exile it, then cast it transformed.)".

Emits a `StaticAbility` carrying a field-less `SiegeEffect` marker, with the verbatim parenthetical preserved on `Reminder` via the existing reminder mechanism (CR 207.2 — italic text with no game function). This honors no-silent-drop: the recognized reminder is captured, not discarded, and the marker gives it a structured host that anchors the Siege mechanic on the card's rules text.

MAST describes, it does not execute. The defeat → exile → cast-transformed life cycle (CR 310.7 — a battle with defense 0 leaves the battlefield as a state-based action) and the casting of a battle as a spell (CR 310.1) are engine territory, summarized in the captured reminder rather than modeled. The Siege's defense value is absent from the MAST `Input`, so it is out of scope.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/SiegeReminderStaticRule.cs)

### `SkipUntapRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/SkipUntapRule.cs)

### `SpellCopyAugmentationReplacementRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"If you would copy a spell one or more times, instead copy it that many times plus an additional time. You may choose new targets for the additional copy." — Twinning Staff's spell-copy augmentation static ability.

CR 707.10: "To copy a spell, activated ability, or triggered ability means to put a copy of it onto the stack; a copy of a spell isn't cast…" This replacement effect intercepts any event where the controller would copy a spell one or more times and adds one additional copy to that total. The original copies still occur (`OriginalEventOccurs` = true); the modifier (Type = "plusOne") specifies one extra copy beyond the original count. The retarget permission (`MayChooseNewTargets = true`) applies specifically to the additional copy, not the original copies.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/SpellCopyAugmentationReplacementRule.cs)

### `SpellsTargetingCostMoreRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/SpellsTargetingCostMoreRule.cs)

### `SquadStaticRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Decomposes a "Squad [cost]" oracle line into the two linked abilities defined by CR 702.157a (mirroring the `DashStaticRule` / `ReconfigureStaticRule` multi-ability precedent).

CR 702.157a (verbatim): "Squad is a keyword that represents two linked abilities... 'Squad [cost]' means 'As an additional cost to cast this spell, you may pay [cost] any number of times' and 'When this creature enters, if its squad cost was paid, create a token that's a copy of it for each time its squad cost was paid.'"

Priority 1001 — fires before `KeywordListRule` (priority 1000) so the two-ability decomposition takes precedence over the single-ability keyword combinator path. The combinator in `SquadKeyword` remains live as a fallback, emitting only the primary additional-cost static ability (the keyword expander returns a single `Ability`; Squad decomposes into two).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/SquadStaticRule.cs)

### `SupertypeAnthemAndKeywordGrantRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Combined supertype anthem + keyword grant: "[Supertype] creatures you control get +N/+N and have [keyword]." e.g. Bloodsworn Steward — "Commander creatures you control get +2/+2 and have haste."

Emits ONE `StaticAbility` carrying TWO effects in its list (the MAST multi-effect-per-clause encoding): a `ModifyPTEffect` (+N/+N) and a `GainAbilityEffect` (the keyword), BOTH targeting the same supertype-filtered subject "[Supertype] creatures you control". One oracle clause generates one continuous effect from one static ability, so the two effects share a single ability rather than being split into two abilities (CR 611.3 — "A continuous effect may be generated by the static ability of an object. Example: A permanent with the static ability 'All white creatures get +1/+1' generates an effect that continuously gives +1/+1 to each white creature on the battlefield.").

The subject's supertype qualifier (e.g. "Commander", CR 903 — a commander designation that behaves as a supertype on the affected set) is recorded on `Supertypes`, NOT as a name or subtype: it categorises the affected objects rather than naming a specific card or its creature-type line.

The granted keyword is itself a static ability (e.g. CR 702.10a — "Haste is a static ability."), modelled as a recursive `GainedAbility` via the shared `MapKeywordToStaticAbility` mapping. No duration is carried — a static ability's continuous effect lasts for as long as the source remains on the battlefield, not until a stated time.

Distinct from `AnthemModifyPTRule` (the bare anchor-target "Enchanted/ Equipped creature gets +N/+M." shape, no keyword grant and no supertype subject) and from `TribalAnthemModifyPTRule` (the named-SUBTYPE anthem with no keyword conjunction). This rule is the supertype-subject compound (pump AND keyword grant).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/SupertypeAnthemAndKeywordGrantRule.cs)

### `TriggerDoublingStaticRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"If a triggered ability of a colorless spell you control or another colorless permanent you control triggers, that ability triggers an additional time."

This models the trigger-multiplication static ability on Echoes of Eternity. The effect is a `ReplacementEffect` over an `AbilityTriggerEvent`: the replaced event is "a triggered ability of [colorless thing you control] fires", and the replacement is that it fires one additional time (`OriginalEventOccurs` = true, `Modifier` Type = "additionalTime").

The "another colorless permanent" excludes the Echoes card itself from the source set (modeled via `ExcludeSelf` = true on the AffectedObjects filter). "Colorless spell you control" covers spells on the stack; "another colorless permanent you control" covers permanents on the battlefield. The zone distinction and the exact "another" scope are engine territory; MAST records the colorless + controller-you + exclude-self scope.

CR 603 (triggered abilities trigger whenever the event they watch for occurs). Rule 105.1: "Colorless is not a color." The IsColorless filter axis encodes colorlessness rather than the Colors axis.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/TriggerDoublingStaticRule.cs)

### `TypeSpellCostReductionRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/TypeSpellCostReductionRule.cs)

### `UnleashStaticRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Decomposes a bare "Unleash" oracle line into the two static abilities defined by CR 702.98a (mirroring the `DashStaticRule` / `ReconfigureStaticRule` multi-ability precedent).

CR 702.98a (verbatim): "Unleash is a keyword that represents two static abilities. 'Unleash' means 'You may have this permanent enter with an additional +1/+1 counter on it' and 'This permanent can't block as long as it has a +1/+1 counter on it.'"

Priority 1001 — fires before `KeywordListRule` (priority 1000) so the two-ability decomposition takes precedence over the single-ability keyword combinator path. The combinator in `UnleashKeyword` remains live as a fallback, emitting only the primary enters-with-counter static ability (the keyword expander returns a single `Ability`; Unleash decomposes into two).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/UnleashStaticRule.cs)

### `VorinclexCounterReplacementRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Parses Vorinclex-style counter-placement replacement effects: 1. "If you would put one or more counters on a permanent or player, put twice that many of each of those kinds of counters on that permanent or player instead." — the controller doubles counter placement on any target (permanent or player). Actor = you (Controller: {Kind:You}); Modifier = double. CR 614 (replacement effects). 2. "If an opponent would put one or more counters on a permanent or player, they put half that many of each of those kinds of counters on that permanent or player instead, rounded down." — opponents halve counter placement on any target, rounded down. Actor = opponent (Controller: {Kind:Opponent}); Modifier = halveRoundDown. CR 614 (replacement effects). AffectedObjects is null on both (permanent or player = all valid counter recipients; no restriction expressed by the oracle text beyond exhaustive enumeration).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/VorinclexCounterReplacementRule.cs)

### `WardKeywordRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Parses Ward keyword abilities in three cost forms:

Mana cost: "Ward {N}" — e.g. "Ward {2}" Life cost: "Ward—Pay N life." — e.g. "Ward—Pay 7 life." Sacrifice cost: "Ward—Sacrifice a [filter]." — e.g. "Ward—Sacrifice a Food."

CR 702.21a: "Ward is a triggered ability. Ward [cost] means 'Whenever this permanent becomes the target of a spell or ability an opponent controls, counter that spell or ability unless that player pays [cost].'"

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/WardKeywordRule.cs)

### `WithKeywordAnthemModifyPTRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Recognises the "Other creatures you control with [keyword] get +X/+Y" anthem shape. A continuous P/T modification (CR 613.1, layer 7c) scoped to permanents the controller owns that carry a particular keyword. The keyword predicate is a "has [keyword]" filter encoded on `Characteristics` (e.g. `"flying"`), consistent with how game-state predicates like "tapped" and "with a +1/+1 counter" are encoded by sibling rules. Example oracle text: "Other creatures you control with flying get +1/+0." → ObjectFilter { CardTypes: ["creature"], Controller: You, ExcludeSelf: true, Characteristics: ["flying"] }

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/WithKeywordAnthemModifyPTRule.cs)

### `YouHaveKeywordRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Handles the "You have [keyword]." static ability shape, where the permanent grants a keyword to its controller (a player, not a permanent). CR 702.11a: "Hexproof is a static ability." Oracle examples: "You have hexproof." (Spirit of the Hearth) "You have shroud." (Teferi's Protection effect) The subject "You" maps to `You` — the controller player, NOT the permanent itself (not Self). The granted ability is embedded as a full `StaticAbility` node via `GainAbilityEffect`. Reminder text (the parenthetical) is stripped before matching; the caller places it on the `Reminder` field.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/YouHaveKeywordRule.cs)

## `/Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/`

### `ITriggerConditionRule` — *interface*

One trigger-condition recognition rule (the event + filter half of a triggered ability). Implementations are discovered by reflection via `TriggerConditionRuleAttribute` and dispatched by `TriggeredAbilityParser` in descending `Priority` order, then ordinal name for tie-breaking; the first non-null result wins.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/ITriggerConditionRule.cs)

### `ITriggeredRule` — *interface*

One triggered-effect recognition rule. Dispatched by `TriggeredAbilityParser` in priority order (descending). The `text` input is the post-trigger effect fragment, already split off by the trigger/effect boundary detection in the dispatcher.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/ITriggeredRule.cs)

### `TriggerConditionRuleAttribute` — *sealed class* : Attribute, MagicAST.Parsing.Parsers.IPrioritizedRuleAttribute

Marks an `ITriggerConditionRule` implementation for reflection-based discovery by `TriggeredAbilityParser`. Mirrors `TriggeredRuleAttribute` for the trigger-condition side.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/TriggerConditionRuleAttribute.cs)

### `TriggeredRuleAttribute` — *sealed class* : Attribute, MagicAST.Parsing.Parsers.IPrioritizedRuleAttribute

Marks an `ITriggeredRule` implementation for reflection-based discovery by `TriggeredAbilityParser`. See `SpellRuleAttribute` for the shared priority convention (0–100, default 50, higher = more specific = tried first).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/TriggeredRuleAttribute.cs)

### `TriggeredRuleHelpers` — *static class*

Shared utilities used across `ITriggeredRule` implementations.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/TriggeredRuleHelpers.cs)

## `/Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/`

### `ActivateAbilityConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever you activate an ability" — the bare activated-ability trigger condition (Rings of Brighthearth). Emits `AbilityActivated` with a `Controller = You` filter (the ability you activate).

CR 603.2 / CR 602.1: an activated ability is "[cost]: [effect]" put on the stack by its controller; "whenever you activate an ability" is a triggered ability that fires on that act. The intervening-if "if it isn't a mana ability" (CR 605) is handled separately by `ConditionParser` as a `TriggeringAbilityIsManaCondition`; this rule owns only the trigger event + subject filter.

ANCHORED (`^…$`) deliberately: "you activate an ability" recurs as a SUBSTRING of more-specific siblings ("…activate an ability of a creature", "…activate an ability that targets only…", "…activate a loyalty ability"). An unanchored matcher would mislabel those, dropping their qualifiers — the #1 overfit FAIL class. The bare form (the post-split `triggerPart` is exactly "Whenever you activate an ability") matches; every qualified sibling carries extra words after "ability" and falls through to its own rule.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ActivateAbilityConditionRule.cs)

### `AddAdditionalManaRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"add an additional {X}" — triggered mana-doubling effect. This is the effect clause of a mana-doubling trigger (e.g. Forsaken Monument: "Whenever you tap a permanent for {C}, add an additional {C}"). The word "additional" distinguishes this from the baseline `AddManaRule` which handles "add {X}" directly. Rule 106: "To add mana, a player puts that mana into their mana pool." The "additional" qualifier is descriptive context for the trigger (you get one more mana of that type on top of what the tapping already produced); MAST models what the oracle text says — the effect is an addMana node whose `Mana` field holds the mana symbol string.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/AddAdditionalManaRule.cs)

### `AddManaRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"add {...}" / "add one mana of any color" — Rule 106. Note: triggered mana production is NOT a Rule 605 mana ability, so no IsManaAbility flag is set.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/AddManaRule.cs)

### `AddManaThenIfSecondTimeRevealUntilRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

Recognises the Nissa, Resurgent Animist Landfall effect: "add one mana of any color. Then if this is the second time this ability has resolved this turn, reveal cards from the top of your library until you reveal an Elf or Elemental card. Put that card into your hand and the rest on the bottom of your library in a random order."

The full effect is a single coherent resolution: unconditional mana, then a conditional library-reveal. The "Then if" clause gates the reveal on an ordinal history predicate; "that card" in the Put sentence back-references the card found in the reveal. This rule captures all three sentences as one match and emits them as a `CompositeEffect` containing:

`AddManaEffect` — add one mana of any color (unconditional). `ConditionalEffect` — if this is the second time the ability has resolved this turn (`OtherCondition` residual per ADR 0001, as no structured history-ordinal condition exists yet), perform the `RevealUntilEffect` atomic reveal-until-found action.

The "second time this ability has resolved this turn" predicate does not yet have a structured `Condition` variant; it is recorded as `OtherCondition` — the typed IResidual residual (acceptable per ADR 0001).

Priority 95: must beat generic sentence-bundle dispatch.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/AddManaThenIfSecondTimeRevealUntilRule.cs)

### `AdditionalCombatPhaseTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"After this phase, there is an additional combat phase." — triggered-ability mirror of `AdditionalCombatPhaseEffectRule`.

Emits an `AdditionalCombatPhaseEffect`, which inserts exactly one additional combat phase into the current turn without an accompanying main phase (distinct from the Aggravated Assault pattern). This variant is used inside a triggered ability (e.g. Godo, Bandit Warlord's second ability), where the sentence bundle dispatcher routes each sentence through `ITriggeredRule` implementations rather than through activated rules.

Pattern anchored (^...$) so it cannot match as a substring of a longer phrase. CR 506 (combat phase); CR 500.1 (turn structure).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/AdditionalCombatPhaseTriggeredRule.cs)

### `AmassTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"amass [Subtype] N" / "amass N" — the Amass keyword action as a triggered effect.

CR 701.47a: "Amass [subtype] N means 'Either put N +1/+1 counters on an Army you control, or create a 0/0 black [Subtype] Army creature token, then put N +1/+1 counters on it.'"

Matches both the modern typed form ("amass Orcs 1", which specifies a subtype) and the legacy untyped form ("amass 1", from War of the Spark before subtype- specific amass was templated). Handles an optional leading "Then " prefix (from multi-sentence oracle text like "...deals 1 damage. Then amass Orcs 1.") so the rule fires correctly when the sentence-bundle splitter produces a "Then amass" fragment.

MAST records the keyword-action and its parameters descriptively. The army- selection logic, token creation, and counter placement are engine territory.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/AmassTriggeredRule.cs)

### `AnotherColorCreatureEntersConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"another [color] creature you control enters" — color-filtered creature ETB trigger, excluding the source permanent (the "another" qualifier).

CR 603.2: "Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability's trigger event, that ability automatically triggers." The "another" qualifier (CR 109.5) excludes the source permanent from the matching set; the color filter narrows which entering creatures match (e.g. "another green creature you control enters" — only green creatures controlled by the trigger's controller, not the source itself).

Uses `Colors` (not `IsColorless`) per Rule 105.1: "Colorless is not a color" — the five named MTG colors (white, blue, black, red, green) are encoded as single-letter codes (W, U, B, R, G) in the Colors list.

Running at priority 996 — tried after `AnotherColorlessCreatureEntersConditionRule` (997) and before the general `EntersConditionRule` (990).

Example: Ivy Lane Denizen — "Whenever another green creature you control enters, put a +1/+1 counter on target creature."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/AnotherColorCreatureEntersConditionRule.cs)

### `AnotherColorlessCreatureEntersConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"another colorless creature you control enters" — trigger condition for watching colorless creature ETB events from the same controller, excluding the source itself.

CR 603.2: "Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability's trigger event, that ability automatically triggers." The "another" qualifier (CR 109) excludes the source permanent from the matching set; the "colorless creature" filter (CR 105.1: "Colorless is not a color") encodes colorlessness via `IsColorless` rather than `Colors` (which encodes the five named colors). Running at priority 997 to be tried after `ColorlessSpellCastConditionRule` (999) but before the general `EntersConditionRule` (990).

Example: Glaring Fleshraker — "Whenever another colorless creature you control enters, this creature deals 1 damage to each opponent."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/AnotherColorlessCreatureEntersConditionRule.cs)

### `AnotherLegendaryCreatureDiesConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"another legendary creature you control dies" — the Ratadrabik of Urborg trigger. Fires on `Dies` filtering for legendary creatures under the controller's control, excluding the source permanent itself.

CR 702.20a: "Vigilance is a static ability…" — vigilance is the keyword context for this card. CR 603.2: a triggered ability fires whenever its event occurs. The filter narrows the match to legendary creatures you control (not the source).

Rule 205.4: "Legendary is a supertype. It is placed before the card's type." Supertypes on `Supertypes` encode the positive-match requirement (CR 205.4 — legendary must be among the permanent's supertypes).

The "another" qualifier ("another" = any object other than this source; plain-language English, no dedicated CR rule number in the bundled data) maps to `ExcludeSelf` = true, consistent with the DiesConditionRule and AnotherSubtypeEntersConditionRule conventions.

ANCHORED (^…$): the pattern targets a highly specific surface phrase and is fully anchored to prevent matching inside broader sibling trigger texts. Priority 994 (above the generic DiesConditionRule at 991, below IsSelfByNameTrigger-based rules) so this more-specific legendary filter is claimed first.

Rule citations: CR 603.2 (triggered ability), CR 205.4 (Legendary supertype), CR 700.4 ("dies" → moved to graveyard from battlefield). ("another" is plain-language exclusion — no dedicated CR rule — modeled on the ExcludeSelf axis.)

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/AnotherLegendaryCreatureDiesConditionRule.cs)

### `AnotherSubtypeEntersConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"another [Subtype] you control enters" — subtype-filtered enters trigger. Handles the Ally-rally pattern and analogous mechanics where the trigger fires when another permanent of a specific creature subtype enters the battlefield under your control. Rule 603.1: triggered abilities use "when," "whenever," or "at" to watch for zone changes; the subtype filter narrows which entering permanents cause the ability to trigger. Examples: "Whenever another Ally you control enters, put a +1/+1 counter on this creature."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/AnotherSubtypeEntersConditionRule.cs)

### `AttachTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"attach it to target [filter] you control" — an explicit attach instruction inside a triggered ability. Covers the ETB auto-attach pattern common on Equipment: "When this Equipment enters, attach it to target creature you control."

Rule 701.3: "To take an Aura, Equipment, or Fortification from where it currently is and put it onto a specified object or player." MAST records the oracle-text instruction descriptively; the legality checks, zone-change details, and resulting continuous effects are engine territory.

Distinct from `EquipEffect`, which models the Equip activated-ability keyword (Rule 702.6). This rule handles explicit "attach it to target" instructions in triggered ability effects.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/AttachTriggeredRule.cs)

### `AttackWithAndAnotherConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever you attack with [Name] and another [qualifier] creature" (Merry shape) — models the companion-attack trigger (Rule 508). Emits `Attacks` with a companion-creature filter carrying the structured ExcludeSelf self-exclusion plus any supertype qualifier.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/AttackWithAndAnotherConditionRule.cs)

### `AttacksAnOpponentConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever [CardName] attacks an opponent" — emits `AttacksAnOpponent` (CR 508 — Declare Attackers Step; CR 102.2 — opponent = player not on the controller's team).

Distinguished from the plain `AttacksConditionRule` (Event: Attacks) by the presence of "an opponent" after "attacks". The "attacks an opponent" phrasing constrains the attack target to a player (not a planeswalker or battle), so the trigger fires only when the creature is declared as attacking a player who is an opponent of its controller.

Priority 990 — above `AttacksConditionRule` (Priority 987) so the opponent-specific form is matched first and never falls through to the generic "attacks" rule.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/AttacksAnOpponentConditionRule.cs)

### `AttacksConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever [CardName] attacks" / "Whenever this creature attacks" / "Whenever a creature you control attacks" — emits `Attacks` (Rule 508 — Declare Attackers). The filter shape is shared with dies/enters via `ParseObjectFilter`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/AttacksConditionRule.cs)

### `AttacksFirstTimeEachTurnConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever [subject] attacks for the first time each turn" — a once-per-turn attack trigger that fires only on the subject's first attack in a given turn.

Produces a `TriggerCondition` with `Attacks`, `Ordinal` = 1, and `PerTurn` = true, matching the encoding used for other first-N-per-turn ordinal triggers (e.g. Lat-Nam Adept's second draw each turn). CR 603.2: the trigger fires on the first event that matches the condition within the qualifying window; subsequent attacks that turn do not re-trigger.

Priority 988 — above the generic `AttacksConditionRule` (Priority 987) so this more-specific ordinal form is matched first. Both rules share the same surface verb "attacks"; the ordinal qualifier "for the first time each turn" distinguishes them. The pattern is anchored (^...$) so it cannot silently consume a sibling trigger that merely contains "attacks for the first time" as a substring of a longer phrase.

CR 603.2: triggered abilities trigger every time the event they specify occurs; the Ordinal + PerTurn qualification narrows which occurrence of that event actually fires the ability, recorded descriptively — the per-turn counter is engine territory.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/AttacksFirstTimeEachTurnConditionRule.cs)

### `AttacksOrBlocksConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever [subject] attacks or blocks" — combined combat trigger (Rule 508/509), modelled as a single `AttacksOrBlocks` event. Tried before the individual Attacks/Blocks rules (higher priority) so the disjunction isn't partially matched.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/AttacksOrBlocksConditionRule.cs)

### `AttacksPlayerAndIsntBlockedConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

Compound trigger condition: "this creature attacks a player and isn't blocked" — fires when the creature attacks and the compound condition holds. Produces a plain `Attacks` trigger on self; the compound qualifier "attacks a player and isn't blocked" is communicated as a pending `PendingInterveningIf` for `TriggeredAbilityParser` to pick up.

Rule 508.1: "The active player declares attackers" — attack target is a player (not a planeswalker or battle). Rule 509 (declare-blockers step): "isn't blocked" is the state immediately after blockers are declared. Together they form the compound condition governing when this trigger fires.

Must be tried at higher priority (Priority = 990) than `AttacksConditionRule` (Priority = 987) so this specific compound form is matched before the generic "attacks" rule consumes the text and drops the "a player and isn't blocked" qualifier. The pattern is anchored (^...$) so it cannot silently match as a substring.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/AttacksPlayerAndIsntBlockedConditionRule.cs)

### `AuraDealsDamageToPlayerOrPlaneswalkerRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"this Aura deals N damage to that player or a planeswalker that player controls." — the upkeep-damage trigger of a player-enchanting Curse (Curse of the Pierced Heart). The "or" is a single chooser-selected reference, so the damage Target is a `Choice` over two options: the enchanted player ("that player", on a player Aura = the enchanted player) and a planeswalker that player controls.

Rule 120 (dealing damage); Rule 702.5 (Enchant player — the enchanted player is the Aura's enchanted object). The chooser picks exactly one option at resolution, so a single Choice reference is correct rather than two targets.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/AuraDealsDamageToPlayerOrPlaneswalkerRule.cs)

### `AuraLosesGainsEnchantReturnAndAttachRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

Recognises the multi-effect sentence produced by Animate Dead's ETB resolution: <c>it loses "enchant creature card in a graveyard" and gains "enchant creature put onto the battlefield with this Aura." Return enchanted creature card to the battlefield under your control and attach this Aura to it.</c>

This sentence is the first sentence in the complex ETB ability's effect body. It produces four effects in sequence:

`LoseAbilityEffect` — "it" (the Aura, `Self`) loses the quoted enchant ability text (captured on the free-text `AbilityText` field, which is a typed residual per ADR 0001). `GainAbilityEffect` — "it" gains a new static `EnchantRestrictionEffect` targeting any creature (the revised enchant restriction once the Aura is on the battlefield). `ReturnToBattlefieldEffect` — returns the enchanted creature card (`EnchantedOrEquipped`) to the battlefield under the controller's control. `AttachEffect` — attaches this Aura to "it" (`It` — the creature just returned).

CR 702.5 (Enchant — Aura ability); CR 303.4 (Aura attachment to a permanent); CR 400.6 (entering under a specific player's control); CR 701.3 (Attach). CR 614.1 (replacement effects for the new enchant restriction on entry).

The overall pattern: <c>^it loses "(?&lt;lost&gt;.+?)" and gains "enchant creature put onto the battlefield with this (?:Aura|enchantment)\.?" Return enchanted creature card to the battlefield under your control and attach this Aura to it\.?$</c> This is narrow enough to be safely anchored — the combination of "loses" + quoted text + "gains" + "enchant creature put onto the battlefield with this Aura" is unique to the Animate Dead / Dance of the Dead family.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/AuraLosesGainsEnchantReturnAndAttachRule.cs)

### `BecomeMonarchTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you become the monarch." — Rule 725. The controller of the source permanent becomes the monarch game-state designation. MAST records the descriptive instruction; the draw-at-end-step and combat-damage-transfer rules (725.2–725.6) are engine territory.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/BecomeMonarchTriggeredRule.cs)

### `BecomesBlockedConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever this creature becomes blocked" — Rule 509 (Declare Blockers Step). Fires on the attacking creature when one or more blockers are assigned to it. The `BecomesBlocked` event is distinct from `Blocks` (the blocking creature's trigger).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/BecomesBlockedConditionRule.cs)

### `BecomesCrewedConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever this Vehicle becomes crewed [for the first time each turn]" — Rule 702.122 trigger.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/BecomesCrewedConditionRule.cs)

### `BecomesTargetConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"When this creature becomes the target of a spell or ability" — triggered-ability machinery: Rule 603.1-603.2; "becomes the target" relationship: Rule 115.1. The subject "this creature" is the source permanent; only the self-reference shape is modelled here.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/BecomesTargetConditionRule.cs)
