### `BlocksConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever this creature blocks" / "Whenever [CardName] blocks" / "Whenever this creature blocks a creature" — Rule 509 (Declare Blockers Step). Excludes "becomes blocked" (BecomesBlocked event), which also contains "blocks".

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/BlocksConditionRule.cs)

### `BlocksOrBecomesBlockedConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever this creature blocks or becomes blocked" — combined combat trigger (CR 702.45 Bushido). Fires from the perspective of BOTH the blocking creature (Rule 509 — it declared as a blocker) AND the attacked creature (Rule 509 — a blocker was assigned to it). Modelled as a single `BlocksOrBecomesBlocked` event. Tried before the individual Blocks/BecomesBlocked rules (higher priority) so the disjunction is not partially matched.

CR 702.45 (verbatim): "Bushido is a triggered ability. 'Bushido N' means 'Whenever this creature blocks or becomes blocked, it gets +N/+N until end of turn.'"

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/BlocksOrBecomesBlockedConditionRule.cs)

### `CantBlockThisTurnTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

Handles the triggered-ability effect shape "target [filter] can't block this turn." CR 603.6a: Enters-the-battlefield abilities trigger when a permanent enters the battlefield. Written "When [this object] enters, ..." or "Whenever a [type] enters, ..." CR 509.1 (declare-blockers step): a "can't block" restriction applies during the declare-blockers turn-based action. The effect is durative — it expires at the end of the current turn (`untilEndOfTurn`).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CantBlockThisTurnTriggeredRule.cs)

### `CardPutIntoOpponentGraveyardConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

Recognises "a [type] card is put into an opponent's graveyard from anywhere" — the trigger event that fires whenever any card moves to an opponent's graveyard from any zone (Bloodchief Ascension, ZEN).

CR 603.2: "Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability's trigger event, that ability automatically triggers." The matched event is a card entering a graveyard (CR 701.7 — "put into a graveyard"). The destination is specifically an opponent's graveyard rather than the controller's own graveyard (CR 102.2 — "opponent" is any player not on the same team). The "from anywhere" clause omits a source-zone restriction.

The controller axis (`Opponent`) on the filter identifies whose graveyard receives the card, mirroring the `You` convention in the sibling `PutIntoGraveyardConditionRule`.

Representative card: Bloodchief Ascension (ZEN) — "Whenever a card is put into an opponent's graveyard from anywhere, if this enchantment has three or more quest counters on it, …" Rule citations: CR 603.2 (Triggered Abilities), CR 701.7 (Put into a graveyard), CR 102.2 (Opponent).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CardPutIntoOpponentGraveyardConditionRule.cs)

### `CastAuraSpellConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever you cast an Aura spell" (Kor Spiritdancer family). Aura is an enchantment <em>subtype</em> (Rule 205.3h), so the cast filter is recorded as `Subtypes = ["Aura"]` rather than a card-type characteristic — the distinction that `SpellCastConditionRule` cannot express (its per-word loop matches card types like "creature"/"noncreature", and it has no subtype channel). Sits above `SpellCastConditionRule` (priority 998) so the Aura subtype qualifier is captured instead of being silently dropped by the generic spell-cast recognizer.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CastAuraSpellConditionRule.cs)

### `CastOrCopyInstantOrSorceryConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever you cast or copy an instant or sorcery spell" — the Magecraft ability-word trigger condition (CR 207.2c: "magecraft" is listed as an ability word with no rules meaning of its own; the trigger text is the mechanical definition).

CR 707.10: "To copy a spell … means to put a copy of it onto the stack; a copy of a spell isn't cast." The disjunction "cast or copy" therefore covers both the casting event and the copy event, which is why this rule produces `CastOrCopySpell` rather than `SpellCast`.

ANCHORED (`^…$`) on the trigger text after the timing word so that the matcher cannot fire as a substring inside a more-specific sibling (e.g. a bare "cast a spell" trigger). Priority 999 — above `SpellCastConditionRule` (998) so the more-specific "cast or copy" disjunction is recognised before the plain "cast" rule can claim the text.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CastOrCopyInstantOrSorceryConditionRule.cs)

### `ColorlessSpellCastConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"you cast a colorless spell" — trigger condition for watching colorless spell casts. Distinct from the general `SpellCastConditionRule` (priority 998) which handles color-word qualifiers (white, blue, black, red, green) but not "colorless". Colorless is not a color (Rule 105.1: "Colorless is not a color."), so it is encoded via `IsColorless` rather than the `Colors` axis. Running at priority 999 ensures this rule is tried before the general rule. Examples: Forsaken Monument — "Whenever you cast a colorless spell, you gain 2 life."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ColorlessSpellCastConditionRule.cs)

### `CommitsCrimeConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever you commit a crime" — the crime-mechanic trigger (CR 700.13).

CR 700.13: "A player commits a crime as that player casts a spell, activates an ability, or puts a triggered ability on the stack and that spell or ability targets at least one opponent; at least one permanent, spell, or ability an opponent controls; and/or at least one card in an opponent's graveyard."

The trigger fires on the controller ("you"), encoded as `You` on the filter. The crime definition is purely a rules term (CR 700.13); its expansion is engine territory. MAST records only the canonical event label `CommitsACrime` and the controller constraint.

The phrase "Whenever you commit a crime" is unique and cannot appear as a substring of a more-specific trigger phrase, so no priority elevation is needed. The anchor on `_pattern` is full-string (^…$) to guard against future sibling triggers that might extend the phrase.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CommitsCrimeConditionRule.cs)

### `ConditionalPaySubtypeCounterTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

Handles "you may pay {COST}. If you do, put a +1/+1 counter on it. If it's a [Subtype], put two +1/+1 counters on it instead." — the optional-pay triggered ability with a subtype-conditional counter boost (Emiel the Blessed pattern).

The effect is an `OptionalEffect` wrapping a `ConditionalPayEffect`; the `IfYouDo` consequence is a `ConditionalEffect` whose `Condition` is an `ObjectHasSubtypeCondition` (CR 205.3m — creature subtypes). When the subtype matches, two +1/+1 counters are placed (the "instead" branch — the `Then`); otherwise one counter (the `Else`). "instead" signals a replacement of the base action for the qualified subtype — modelled as an if-then-else rather than a sequential pair so the engine knows that the one-counter placement does NOT happen for Unicorns.

Priority 85: higher than `ConditionalPayTriggeredRule` (Priority 80) so this specific subtype-counter shape is claimed first; the generic conditional-pay handler never sees this text. Anchored sub-patterns guard against substring capture on sibling rules that match on "{counter} counter(s)".

CR 122.1 (verbatim): "A counter is a marker placed on an object or player that modifies its characteristics and/or interacts with a rule, ability, or effect." CR 205.3m (verbatim): "Creature subtypes are always a single word and are listed after a long dash on the card's type line." CR 602.1 / CR 603.2: activated/triggered ability structure.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ConditionalPaySubtypeCounterTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ConditionalPayTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

Handles "you may pay {COST}. If you do, [effect]." conditional-pay triggers and the discard-as-cost loot variant "you may discard a card. If you do, draw a card."

Two shapes:

<b>Mana-pay shape</b>: "you may pay {N}. If you do, [EFFECT]." → `ConditionalPayEffect` with the mana cost on `Cost` and the parsed consequent on `IfYouDo`. Example: "Whenever a creature dies, you may pay {1}. If you do, you gain 1 life." (Deathgreeter, ALA). <b>Discard-loot shape</b>: "you may discard [N] card(s). If you do, draw [M] card(s)." → `DiscardCardsEffect` with `IsOptional = true` and `IfYouDo = DrawCardsEffect`. Mirrors the spell-level `DiscardThenDrawSpellRule`.

Priority 80: both shapes contain the trigger text "you may [action]. If you do," as a prefix. Without elevated priority the discard-loot shape would race against the plain `EachOpponentDiscardsRule`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ConditionalPayTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ControlNoLandTypeConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"When you control no [LandSubtype]" — state-triggered ability (Rule 603). Fires when the controlling player transitions to controlling no lands of the named basic-land subtype (e.g. "When you control no Islands, sacrifice this creature."). Expanded oracle for the obsolete Islandhome family. The land subtype is stored in Filter.Subtypes; CardTypes=["land"], Controller=You.

Supported subtypes: Island, Forest, Swamp, Mountain, Plains.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ControlNoLandTypeConditionRule.cs)

### `ControllerMayDrawIfGreatestPowerTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"its controller may draw a card if its power is greater than each other creature's power" — Selvala, Heart of the Wilds ETB trigger pattern.

The entering creature's controller gets an optional draw, but only when the entering creature has strictly greater power than every other creature on the battlefield. CR 603.5: an optional triggered effect ("may") goes on the stack regardless of whether the controller intends to exercise it; the choice is made on resolution. CR 121.1: drawing a card is placing the top card of the library into hand.

AST shape: `ConditionalEffect` whose `Condition` is an `OtherCondition` residual (the relative-maximum-power predicate "its power is greater than each other creature's power" has no structured `GreatestPowerCondition` node — PB-7 debt) and whose `Then` is an `OptionalEffect` wrapping a `DrawCardsEffect` targeting the creature's controller (`ThatPlayer`).

"Its controller" is the controller of the entering creature (the trigger's subject), not the ability's controller ("you"). Encoded as `ThatPlayer` following the Smothering Tithe convention (CR 109.5 — the controller of an object is the player who controls it).

The rule is fully anchored (`^…$`) so it cannot match as a substring inside a more-specific sibling's text.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ControllerMayDrawIfGreatestPowerTriggeredRule.cs)

### `CopyAllSpellsAndAbilitiesTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

Handles the Ulalek, Fused Atrocity triggered-ability effect: "you may pay {COST}. If you do, copy all spells you control, then copy all other activated and triggered abilities you control. You may choose new targets for the copies."

The effect decomposes as:

`OptionalEffect` (the "you may" wrapper) `ConditionalPayEffect` as the `Inner` — the mana cost the controller may optionally pay. `CompositeEffect` as the `IfYouDo` — two `CopyEffect` nodes with `MayChooseNewTargets = true`: "copy all spells you control" → `Each` + filter `CardTypes=["spell"], Controller=You, Zone=Stack` "copy all other activated and triggered abilities you control" → `Each` + filter <c>CardTypes=["activatedAbility","triggeredAbility"], Controller=You, Zone=Stack, ExcludeSelf=true</c> — the word "other" excludes the source ability itself (this triggered ability of Ulalek), CR 109.5. Both copies carry `MayChooseNewTargets = true` because the retarget grant in "You may choose new targets for the copies" applies to all copies produced by this ability. CR 707.10.

Priority 90: must run BEFORE `ConditionalPayTriggeredRule` (priority 80), whose `TryParseIfYouDoEffect` cannot handle a composite copy-all consequent and would reject the full text, leaving the ability unparsed. This rule takes priority because it is more specific.

CR 707.10: "To copy a spell, activated ability, or triggered ability means to put a copy of it onto the stack." The copies are placed onto the stack; the controller then may choose new targets for each copy independently (CR 707.10b / CR 117.7).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CopyAllSpellsAndAbilitiesTriggeredRule.cs)

### `CopyItForEachPrecedingSpellTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"copy it for each other instant and sorcery spell you've cast before it this turn. You may choose new targets for the copies." — Thousand-Year Storm's triggered effect.

The copy count equals the number of other instant and sorcery spells the controller cast earlier this turn (the "storm count" for instants/sorceries). Each copy represents one preceding spell in the cast sequence. The count is expressed as a `CountQuantity` over an `ObjectFilter` with `CastThisTurnPredicate` (Caster=You) and `ExcludeSelf` = true (the "other" qualifier, excluding the triggering spell itself, CR 109.5).

"Before it this turn" — the temporal ordering qualifier restricting the count to spells cast earlier in the turn rather than the full "this turn" set — is engine-execution semantics (CR 603.1: a triggered ability fires once the triggering event occurs; the storm-count state is tracked by the engine, not by the AST descriptor). MAST records the "other [filter] spells cast this turn" reference (ExcludeSelf + CastThisTurnPredicate); the ordering is implied by the trigger event itself and not separately encoded.

CR 707.10: "To copy a spell, activated ability, or triggered ability means to put a copy of it onto the stack; a copy of a spell isn't cast." CR 702.40a: "Storm" is the keyword for the analogous "copy for each spell cast before it this turn" mechanic on a spell itself; Thousand-Year Storm is a standing enchantment that applies a Magecraft-style storm effect to instants and sorceries only. CR 603.1: "Triggered abilities have a trigger condition and an effect."

"You may choose new targets for the copies" is the structured retarget permission carried on `MayChooseNewTargets` — a rules-meaningful flag, not free text (CR 707.10c).

Priority 76: must run BEFORE `CopyTriggeringSpellTriggeredRule` (priority 74), which handles the shorter "copy it. You may choose new targets for the copy." body and would fail to match this longer clause, but to be safe this more-specific rule runs first. The pattern is ANCHORED (^…$) so it cannot match as a substring of a more-specific sibling.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CopyItForEachPrecedingSpellTriggeredRule.cs)

### `CopyTargetSpellTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"copy target instant or sorcery spell. You may choose new targets for the copy." — the resolution clause of Dualcaster Mage's ETB trigger (and the broader "copy a spell on the stack" family).

CR 707.10: "To copy a spell, activated ability, or triggered ability means to put a copy of it onto the stack; a copy of a spell isn't cast and a copy of an activated ability isn't activated." The copy source is a targeted spell on the stack — modelled as an `Target` reference whose filter pins the instant-or-sorcery card types in `Stack`.

The optional second sentence "You may choose new targets for the copy" is the structured retarget permission carried on `MayChooseNewTargets` — a rules-meaningful flag, not free text. The whole two-sentence body is matched as one effect so the retarget permission rides the copy rather than splitting into a spurious second effect.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CopyTargetSpellTriggeredRule.cs)

### `CopyTriggeringAbilityConditionalPayTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

Rings of Brighthearth's conditional-pay copy-the-triggering-ability effect: "you may pay {COST}. If you do, copy that ability. You may choose new targets for the copy."

Decomposes as the canonical "you may pay [cost]. If you do, [Y]" shape (Nim Deathmantle pattern):

`OptionalEffect` — the "you may" wrapper. `ConditionalPayEffect` as `Inner` — the mana cost the controller may optionally pay. `CopyEffect` as `IfYouDo` — the copy of the triggering ability. `Target` is `TriggeringAbility` ("that ability" — the activated/triggered ability on the stack named by the `AbilityActivated` trigger, CR 113), and `MayChooseNewTargets = true` ("You may choose new targets for the copy", CR 707.10b).

Priority 90: must run BEFORE the generic `ConditionalPayTriggeredRule` (priority 80), whose consequent delegation cannot structure "copy that ability" into the `TriggeringAbility` reference and would leave a residual. This rule is more specific (it owns the full anchored clause).

ANCHORED (`^…$`): the clause is matched in full so no sibling conditional-pay text is mislabeled.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CopyTriggeringAbilityConditionalPayTriggeredRule.cs)

### `CopyTriggeringSpellTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"copy it. You may choose new targets for the copy." — the resolution clause of triggers that copy the triggering spell (e.g. Echoes of Eternity: "Whenever you cast a colorless spell, copy it. You may choose new targets for the copy.").

Distinct from `CopyTargetSpellTriggeredRule` (which handles "copy target instant or sorcery spell") — here the copy target is "it": a pronoun referring to the colorless spell named by the trigger condition (i.e. the spell that caused the trigger to fire). The reference is `It` rather than an explicit targeted filter.

CR 707.10: "To copy a spell, activated ability, or triggered ability means to put a copy of it onto the stack." The reminder text "(A copy of a permanent spell becomes a token.)" is engine semantics carried on the enclosing `Reminder`, not modeled in the effect node.

"You may choose new targets for the copy" is the structured retarget permission carried on `MayChooseNewTargets` — a rules-meaningful flag, not free text. The whole two-sentence body is matched as one effect so the retarget permission rides the copy rather than splitting into a spurious second effect.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CopyTriggeringSpellTriggeredRule.cs)

### `CounterPutOnCreaturePassiveConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"a -1/-1 counter is put on a creature" — passive counter-placement trigger (Flourishing Defenses).

CR 122.1: "A counter is a marker placed on an object or player that modifies its characteristics and/or interacts with a rule or effect." This trigger fires whenever any player places a -1/-1 counter on any creature (passive voice: "is put on"). Unlike `PutNegativeCountersOnCreatureConditionRule` which covers "you put one or more -1/-1 counters on a creature" (controller-scoped, "one or more" quantity), this rule covers the broader "a [counter] is put on a creature" (any player, single counter, passive). The event is still `CounterPlaced` with `CounterType="-1/-1"` and `MinimumCount=1` (any single counter placement fires this trigger).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CounterPutOnCreaturePassiveConditionRule.cs)

### `CreateColorlessTokenWithSacrificeManaAbilityRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"create a [P/T] colorless [Subtype(s)] creature token with 'Sacrifice this token: Add {C}.'" — colorless creature token whose only ability is a sacrifice-for-mana activated ability.

This covers Eldrazi Spawn and Eldrazi Scion tokens (CR 701.6: "Create" puts a token on the battlefield; CR 111.1: a token is a marker representing a permanent that isn't a card). The token has a single activated mana ability (CR 605.1a: an activated ability that doesn't require a target and could add mana is a mana ability): "Sacrifice this token: Add {C}." The sacrifice cost targets the token itself (CR 701.21a), encoded via `IsSelf` = `true` on the `Filter`.

Runs at priority 75, above the generic `CreateTokenRule` (default 50), so this specific shape is matched first and the generic rule never overwrites the token's structured ability with an empty ability list.

Example: Glaring Fleshraker — "create a 0/1 colorless Eldrazi Spawn creature token with \"Sacrifice this token: Add {C}.\""

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CreateColorlessTokenWithSacrificeManaAbilityRule.cs)

### `CreateCopyOfEquippedCreatureTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"create a token that's a copy of equipped creature, except the token isn't legendary. That token gains haste." — Helm of the Host's combat trigger resolution. Produces a single `CopyEffect` whose `Target` is the equipped creature and whose `Modifications` carry both "except"-style overrides on the copy: it isn't legendary (`SupertypeRemover`) and it gains haste (`AbilityAdder`).

CR 111.1: "A token is a marker used to represent any permanent that isn't represented by a card." CR 707.2: "When copying an object, the copy acquires the copiable values of the original object's characteristics…" — the two modifications override copiable values the token would otherwise inherit. The trailing "That token gains haste." sentence is folded into the same `CopyEffect` as a haste modification (one oracle line → one copy with its modifications), not split into a separate gain-ability effect. "equipped creature" is the `EnchantedOrEquipped` reference (CR 301.5c).

Priority 70 — more specific than the generic create-token rules so the "copy of equipped creature" phrasing is claimed here first. The rule matches the whole two-sentence body; the dispatcher's sentence-bundle pre-pass bails (the "That token gains haste" sentence has no standalone rule), so the full body reaches this rule intact.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CreateCopyOfEquippedCreatureTriggeredRule.cs)

### `CreateCopyOfThatCreatureNotLegendaryWithTypeAndColorTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"create a token that's a copy of that creature, except it's not legendary and it's a [P]/[T] [color] [Subtype] in addition to its other colors and types." The Ratadrabik of Urborg pattern: a copy of the dying creature with three structured "except"-clauses applied in order:

`SupertypeRemover` strips Legendary (CR 704.5j — legend rule would otherwise cull the token copy if the original creature is still on the battlefield). `PowerToughnessOverride` fixes the token at 2/2 regardless of the original's power/toughness (CR 707.2 copiable values). `ColorAdder` adds black "in addition to its other colors" (CR 105.3 additive colour change — the token keeps the original's colours). `TypeAdder` adds Zombie "in addition to its other types" (CR 205.1b — subtype addition preserves all existing subtypes).

"that creature" back-references the object named by the trigger's filter (the dying legendary creature), encoded as `ThatCreature`.

Rule citations: CR 707.2 (copy semantics), CR 105.3 (additive colour change), CR 205.1b (subtype addition), CR 704.5j (legend rule), CR 111.1 (token creation).

ANCHORED (^…$): the full surface phrase is anchored to prevent substring matches against any sibling triggered rule. Priority 76 — above the general copy-of-this-creature rules (70–75), well above the generic token rule (50).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CreateCopyOfThatCreatureNotLegendaryWithTypeAndColorTriggeredRule.cs)

### `CreateCopyOnCombatDamageTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"create a token that's a copy of this creature" — token-copy effect that fires as the resolution clause of a `DealsCombatDamageToPlayer` trigger.

Rule 707.2: A token that is a copy of an object copies the copiable values of that object. Rule 111.8: Such tokens are created on the battlefield under their creator's control. The copy source is the card itself ("this creature"), modelled as `Self`.

Priority 70 — more specific than the generic `CreateTokenRule` (50) so this rule is tried first and the generic rule never sees the "copy of this creature" phrasing.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CreateCopyOnCombatDamageTriggeredRule.cs)

### `CreateEmblemTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

Matches "you get an emblem with [quoted abilities]" — creates an emblem in the command zone whose abilities are parsed from the quoted oracle-text fragment (CR 114.2).

The quoted string is extracted and re-parsed via `OracleParser` so the emblem abilities receive the same full triggered/static/activated parsing as any other oracle text. The resulting abilities become `Abilities`.

ANCHORED (^...$): matches only the full "you get an emblem with ..." clause. Priority 90: runs before generic keyword or residual rules.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CreateEmblemTriggeredRule.cs)

### `CreateTappedAttackingCopyOfNontokenCreatureRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"create a tapped and attacking token that's a copy of up to one other target nontoken creature you control" — Satya, Aetherflux Genius's attack-trigger copy effect (CR 111.1: token creation; CR 508.1k: token enters as an attacking creature declared in the same attack step; CR 707.2: the token copies the copiable values of the target).

"Up to one" is encoded as `UpToQuantity` on the `Quantity` (maximum 1, minimum 0). "Other" excludes the source creature (`ExcludeSelf` = true). "Nontoken" is the `IsToken` = false axis (CR 111: a token is not a card; "nontoken" means the object is not a token).

The "tapped and attacking" qualifier is contextually implicit from the attack trigger and is not serialised as a separate field on `CopyEffect` — matching the convention established by Kari Zev, Skyship Raider's gold fixture, where the same phrasing on a `CreateTokenEffect` is also omitted.

ANCHORED (^…$): prevents spurious matches inside a longer copy-token sentence that happens to contain this phrase as a substring. Priority 75 — above the generic `CreateCopyOnCombatDamageTriggeredRule` (70) and well above the `CreateTokenRule` fallback (50); specific enough to be tried before any broader "create a … copy" path.

Rule citations: CR 111.1 (token creation), CR 508.1k (attacking token), CR 707.2 (copy semantics), CR 107.14 (energy), CR 603.7 (delayed triggered ability).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CreateTappedAttackingCopyOfNontokenCreatureRule.cs)

### `CreateTappedPredefinedTokenRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"create a tapped [Subtype] token" — creates a predefined artifact token that enters the battlefield tapped (Rule 111 — Tokens; CR 302.6 / 110.6b: a permanent enters tapped when an effect instructs it to do so). Handles the "create a tapped Treasure token" form produced by cards such as Nuka-Cola Vending Machine (PIP). The "tapped" qualifier is recorded on the `EntersTapped` field so consumers know the token enters already tapped rather than untapped. This is a descriptive datum, not an engine tap action (MAST describes what oracle text says, not engine execution). Priority 96: sits above `CreateTokenRule` (default priority 50) so the tapped variant is matched before the plain vanilla path, which would fail anyway because it doesn't recognise "tapped" between the article and the subtype word. CR 701.21a (Sacrifice), CR 111 (Tokens), CR 302.6 (enters tapped), CR 603.2 (triggered ability event matching).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CreateTappedPredefinedTokenRule.cs)

### `CreateThatManyTokensRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"create that many [P/T] [color] [Subtype] creature tokens" — token creation where the quantity is derived from the triggering event's counter count (Nest of Scarabs).

"That many" is an anaphoric reference to the number of counters placed in the triggering event — the antecedent of "that many" is the count from a `CounterPlaced` trigger (CR 122.1: counters are markers placed on objects; the count placed in a single event is the antecedent). Modelled as `DerivedQuantity` keyed on `CountersPlaced`, the counter-placement analog of `DamageDealt` and `LifeLost`.

Rule 111.1: "A token is a marker used to represent any permanent that isn't represented by a card." Rule 603.2: the triggering event fires the ability; the effect clause creates tokens equal to the number of counters placed.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CreateThatManyTokensRule.cs)

### `CreateThatManyTreasureTokensRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"create that many Treasure tokens." — token creation where the quantity is derived from the damage dealt by the triggering combat-damage event (The Reaver Cleaver: "Whenever this creature deals combat damage to a player or planeswalker, create that many Treasure tokens.").

"That many" is an anaphoric reference to the amount of combat damage dealt in the triggering event (CR 510.1 — combat damage step). Modelled as `DerivedQuantity` keyed on `DamageDealt`, the same derived quantity used by "you gain that much life" lifelink analogs. The predefined Treasure token is as specified in CR 111.10a: "A Treasure token is a colorless Treasure artifact token with '{T}, Sacrifice this token: Add one mana of any color.'"

Rule 111.1: "A token is a marker used to represent any permanent that isn't represented by a card." Rule 603.2: the triggering event fires the ability; the effect clause creates tokens equal to the damage dealt.

ANCHORED (^…$): the full effect clause is matched to prevent this rule from misfiring inside a broader effect sentence. Runs as a standard `[TriggeredRule]` — lower priority than the predefined-token patterns in `CreateTokenRule` but named uniquely so it does not collide with the creature-token "that many" shape in `CreateThatManyTokensRule`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CreateThatManyTreasureTokensRule.cs)

### `CreateTokenChoiceRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"create a [TokenA] token or a [TokenB] token" — a one-of-two CHOICE between creating one predefined artifact token or another (Tireless Provisioner: "create a Food token or a Treasure token").

"X or Y" is a modal choice the controller makes on resolution, not two creations — Rule 700.2 (modal). It is modelled as a `ModalEffect` with `ChooseOne` whose two modes each wrap a single `CreateTokenEffect` in a `SpellAbility`, mirroring the bullet-modal shape produced for "choose one —" triggers (Saurian Symbiote).

Both options name a predefined artifact token whose colorless, P/T-less body is fully specified by its subtype: Treasure (Rule 111.10a) and Food (Rule 111.10b). Their activated abilities are reminder text (stripped to the ability's Reminder field upstream), so each token reuses the predefined `TokenDefinition` factory rather than re-parsing the body.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CreateTokenChoiceRule.cs)

### `CreateTokenOrInsteadIfConditionRule` — *static class*

Recognises the two-sentence conditional-token-creation shape: "create a P1/T1 color1 Subtype1 creature token. If [condition], create a P2/T2 color2 Subtype2 creature token instead."

Rule 111 (tokens), CR 603 (triggered abilities). The word "instead" signals that the second creation REPLACES the first (not in addition to it). Modelled as a `ConditionalEffect`:

`Condition` — the mid-resolution board-state predicate; `Then` — the token created when the condition is true (the "instead" token from the second sentence); `Else` — the token created when the condition is false (the default token from the first sentence).

The Necrobloom (BIG) is the canonical example: "create a 0/1 green Plant creature token. If you control seven or more lands with different names, create a 2/2 black Zombie creature token instead." CR 207.2c: "Landfall" is a CR ability word with no special rules meaning. CR 603: triggered abilities fire "Whenever a land you control enters". The condition "you control seven or more lands with different names" is an `OtherCondition` residual (ADR 0001) because `ObjectFilter` does not yet have a "different names" axis.

Called as a composite path from `TriggeredAbilityParser` BEFORE the sentence bundle splitter, so the two-sentence shape is not incorrectly split into two independent token creation effects.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CreateTokenOrInsteadIfConditionRule.cs)

### `CreateTokenRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"create [article] [P/T] [colors] [subtypes] creature token [with ...]" — Rule 111. Also handles predefined artifact tokens (Food, Treasure, Clue, Blood) which have no P/T and whose activated ability is reminder text only (Rule 107.10b). Handles the optional "you may" prefix (Rule 116.1b) by setting IsOptional = true on the resulting CreateTokenEffect.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CreateTokenRule.cs)

### `CreateTokensEqualToDieResultTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"You create a number of [PredefinedToken] tokens equal to the result." — creates predefined artifact tokens (Treasure, Food, Clue, Blood) in a count equal to the result of a preceding die roll (CR 706.4).

CR 706.4: "Some abilities that instruct a player to roll one or more dice do not include a results table. The text of those abilities will indicate how to use the results of the die rolls, if at all." This sentence is the "how to use" clause: the count of created tokens equals the die-roll result, modelled as `DieRollResultQuantity` (reference-not-resolution, ADR 0004).

CR 111.10a: "A Treasure token is a colorless Treasure artifact token with '{T}, Sacrifice this token: Add one mana of any color.'"

ANCHORED (`^…$`): the "equal to the result" tail is distinctive enough to not match siblings, but anchoring is applied per the standing contract. Priority 72: above the generic `CreateCopyOnCombatDamageTriggeredRule` (70) and above the generic CreateTokenRule (50) so this more-specific pattern is tried first.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CreateTokensEqualToDieResultTriggeredRule.cs)

### `CreateTreasureForEachOpponentDealtDamageRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you create a Treasure token for each opponent dealt damage" — the Malcolm, Keen-Eyed Navigator effect pattern. The controller creates one Treasure token per opponent who was dealt damage by the triggering event.

The quantity "for each opponent dealt damage" is modelled as a `CalculatedQuantity` with `Expression = "for each opponent dealt damage"` — an anaphoric reference to the set of opponents hit in the triggering `DealsDamageToOpponents` event. There is no structured `CountQuantity` for "opponents dealt damage" (that set is a dynamic game-state query outside `ObjectFilter` scope); the free-text expression is the type-honest residual per ADR 0001.

The predefined Treasure token is as specified in CR 111.10a: "A Treasure token is a colorless Treasure artifact token with '{T}, Sacrifice this token: Add one mana of any color.'" Rule 111.1: "A token is a marker used to represent any permanent that isn't represented by a card." Rule 603.2: the triggering event fires the ability; the effect clause creates tokens equal to the number of opponents dealt damage.

ANCHORED (^…$): the full effect clause is matched to prevent this rule from misfiring inside a broader effect sentence.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CreateTreasureForEachOpponentDealtDamageRule.cs)

### `CreatureDealtDamageConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever a creature is dealt damage" — emits `CreatureDealtDamage` with a Filter of `{CardTypes: ["creature"]}` (Rule 120 — Damage; Rule 603.2 — triggered abilities). Covers the Repercussion trigger pattern: any creature (not just a specific one) receiving damage fires the ability.

CR 120.1: "Any time an object, player, or battle would be dealt damage, that damage is dealt instead to the appropriate target." CR 120.3: "Damage can be dealt to creatures, planeswalkers, battles, and players." CR 603.2: "Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability's trigger event, that ability automatically triggers." The filter carries the creature predicate so the trigger's subject is clearly a creature (not a player or planeswalker).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CreatureDealtDamageConditionRule.cs)

### `CreatureWithKeywordEntersConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"a creature you control with [keyword] enters" — an enters trigger on a creature-type filter that additionally requires a keyword ability. Handles Dragon Tempest's first ability: "Whenever a creature you control with flying enters, …" Rule 603.6a: enters-the-battlefield abilities trigger when a permanent enters the battlefield. The keyword constraint ("with flying") narrows which entering creatures fire the ability. The constraint is structured on the `Characteristics` axis as a `KeywordCharacteristic`, not free text (ADR 0001: no free text for structurable concepts). Sits below `EntersConditionRule` (Priority 990) so the plain "a creature you control enters" shape is handled first; this rule fires only when a "with [keyword]" qualifier is present.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CreatureWithKeywordEntersConditionRule.cs)

### `CreatureWithoutKeywordDiesConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

Dies trigger on a creature a player controls that lacks a specific keyword ability — the Luminous Broodmoth family. Oracle text: "a creature you control without flying dies", "a creature you control without flying or reach dies", etc.

The filter encodes the keyword absence as a typed `OtherCharacteristic` residual ("withoutFlying", "withoutFlanking", etc.) per the convention established by Falter (M10). `OtherCharacteristic` is `IResidual`, not `IUnparsed` — a deliberate scope deferral (ADR 0001), not a parse failure.

"without flying" is not a structured negation of the keyword-presence axis on `Characteristics` because there is currently no `NegatedKeywordCharacteristic` node; the "withoutX" residual is the approved frontier encoding. See: Falter (M10), Cosmotronic Wave (GRN).

CR 700.4 ("dies" = moves from battlefield to graveyard, see also TriggerEvent.Dies). Priority above generic DiesConditionRule (991) so this more specific guard fires first.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CreatureWithoutKeywordDiesConditionRule.cs)

### `DealThatMuchDamageToControllerRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"[source] deals that much damage to that creature's controller" — the Repercussion effect: a named source deals damage equal to the amount just dealt to the triggering creature, targeting that creature's controller.

The antecedent of "that much" is the damage dealt to the creature in the triggering event (`DamageDealt`), matching the `CreatureDealtDamage` trigger (CR 120 — Damage; CR 603.2). The target is the controller of the creature that was dealt damage (`Controller`); the source is the enchantment itself (`Self`).

CR 120.1: "Any time an object … would be dealt damage, that damage is dealt to the appropriate target." CR 120.3: damage can be dealt to players. This triggered effect redirects an equivalent amount to the damaged creature's controller, who is a player (not the creature). The source is "this enchantment" — a self-reference carried on the `Source` field rather than inferred, so the interact-oracle operator can distinguish the damage source.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/DealThatMuchDamageToControllerRule.cs)

### `DealsCombatDamageConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever [subject] deals combat damage to (a player|an opponent|any player|a player or planeswalker)" — emits `DealsCombatDamageToPlayer` for player-only recipients and `DealsCombatDamageToPlayerOrPlaneswalker` for the broader "player or planeswalker" form (The Reaver Cleaver, Sword of Sinew and Steel). Rule 510 — Combat Damage Step; Rule 603.6 — triggered abilities. The recipient class is encoded in the event enum value; the Filter captures the subject.

"a creature you control with [keyword] deals combat damage to a player" — the "with [keyword]" suffix on the subject noun phrase is a keyword-ability characteristic constraint on the matched object (CR 702 — keyword abilities). Modelled as `Characteristics` carrying a structured `KeywordCharacteristic` so the filter is fully typed with no free-text residual.

CR 510.1 (Combat Damage Step): "First, the active player announces how each attacking creature assigns its combat damage … This turn-based action doesn't use the stack." CR 603.2: "Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability's trigger event, that ability automatically triggers."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/DealsCombatDamageConditionRule.cs)

### `DealsCombatDamageToCreatureConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever [subject] deals combat damage to a creature" — emits `DealsCombatDamageToCreature`.

CR 510.1 (Combat Damage Step): "First, the active player announces how each attacking creature assigns its combat damage, then the defending player announces how each blocking creature assigns its combat damage. This turn-based action doesn't use the stack." The combat-damage assignment is the game event; CR 603.2 — "Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability's trigger event, that ability automatically triggers."

The recipient class (a creature) is implied by the enum value; the Filter captures the subject — the thing dealing the damage ("this creature", self-by-name, etc.). Sits above `DealsCombatDamageConditionRule` (player recipient, Priority 985) and `DealsDamageConditionRule` (any damage, Priority 984) so the creature-recipient variant is recognised before the broader shapes; those sibling rules guard on "to a player" / exclude "deals combat damage" respectively, so there is no overlap.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/DealsCombatDamageToCreatureConditionRule.cs)

### `DealsDamageConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever [subject] deals damage" — any damage (Rule 120), not only combat damage. Emits `DealsDamage`. Covers lifelink-analog oracle text printed before the lifelink keyword existed. The combat variant is handled by `DealsCombatDamageConditionRule` (higher priority), so this excludes it.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/DealsDamageConditionRule.cs)

### `DealsDamageToOpponentConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever [subject] deals damage to an opponent" — any damage (combat or non-combat, Rule 120) specifically to an opponent (not any player, Rule 102.2). Emits `DealsDamageToOpponent`. The Filter captures the subject (the thing dealing the damage).

Distinct from `DealsCombatDamageConditionRule` (combat damage only) and `DealsDamageConditionRule` (any damage, unspecified recipient). The guard excludes "deals combat damage" so combat-damage text routes to `DealsCombatDamageConditionRule` (higher priority), and requires "to an opponent" to distinguish from the generic unrecipient-qualified `DealsDamageConditionRule`.

Rule 120: a source deals damage when it assigns damage to a player or permanent. Rule 102.2: an opponent is any player not on the same team as the controller. Rule 603.2: triggered abilities fire automatically on matching game events.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/DealsDamageToOpponentConditionRule.cs)

### `DealsThatMuchDamageToEachOtherOpponentRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"it deals that much damage to each other opponent." — reflexive combat-damage spread (Grenzo's Ruffians pattern). Paired with a DealsCombatDamageToPlayer trigger; the "that much" refers to the amount of combat damage dealt to the trigger's original recipient, and "each other opponent" is every opponent other than that recipient. Rule 120.1: a source deals damage. Rule 510 (Combat Damage Step): the trigger fires when combat damage is dealt to an opponent. "Each other opponent" is the set of opponents excluding the one already hit; this is recorded descriptively as an EachOpponent reference with Characteristics: ["other"] — the "other" predicate's runtime resolution (relative to the trigger's opponent) is engine territory per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine. Rule 702.121 (Melee) lists Grenzo's Ruffians as a canonical example of this pattern. The rule encodes "that much" as a CalculatedQuantity with Expression="that much" and Operation="match", matching the convention established for Chatterfang's "that many" token-augmentation replacement.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/DealsThatMuchDamageToEachOtherOpponentRule.cs)

### `DestroyDefendingCreatureThenPutCounterRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"destroy target creature defending player controls, then put a +1/+1 counter on [Name]." — the compound triggered attack-effect on Grimgrin, Corpse-Born and similar attack-triggered destroy-then-counter abilities.

Produces a single `CompositeEffect` containing two sibling effects (consistent with the BloodArtist/multi-effect triggered convention):

`DestroyEffect` targeting a `Target` creature with `DefendingPlayer` — "destroy target creature defending player controls" (CR 701.8: destroy means put the permanent into its owner's graveyard; CR 508.1b: the defending player is whoever is being attacked). `PutCountersEffect` targeting `Self` with one +1/+1 counter — "then put a +1/+1 counter on [Name]" where [Name] is a self-reference by the card's own name (CR 201.5; CR 122.1).

The pattern is anchored (^…$) to prevent substring collision with `DestroyTargetTriggeredRule`, which would otherwise consume the "destroy target creature" prefix and silently drop the ", then put" clause. Priority 60 — above `PutCountersTriggeredRule` (default 50) so this anchored compound form is tried first; it will not match unless both "destroy ... defending player" and "then put a +1/+1 counter" are present.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/DestroyDefendingCreatureThenPutCounterRule.cs)

### `DestroyTargetTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"destroy target [filter]." / "you may destroy target [filter]." — single-target destroy on the triggered side. Delegates single-noun filter parsing to `ParseTargetFilter` (same lexical surface as `DestroyTargetSimpleRule`). Handles type-disjunction filters ("artifact or enchantment", "creature, artifact, or enchantment") via `SplitTypeDisjunction`, matching the spell-side `DestroyTargetTypeDisjunctionRule`. Covers: land, artifact, enchantment, creature, permanent, and richer filter phrases (color + type, non- prefix, etc.), as well as any two-or-more-type disjunction. The optional "you may" prefix sets `IsOptional` = <see langword="true"/>.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/DestroyTargetTriggeredRule.cs)

### `DiesConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"dies" triggers: "this creature dies", "a creature dies", "another creature dies", etc. "dies" is modern oracle; the longform "is put into a graveyard from the battlefield" (Rule 700.4) describes the same event and normalises to the same TriggerEvent.Dies.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/DiesConditionRule.cs)

### `DiscardAndUntapAllLandsRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"that player discards a card and you untap all lands you control" — the combined triggered effect of a `DealsCombatDamageToPlayer` trigger (Rule 510 — Combat Damage Step) where the recipient discards a card AND the controller untaps all their lands.

Sword of Feast and Famine is the canonical card for this pattern. The two effects are conjunctive in a single clause, wrapped in a `CompositeEffect` mirroring the single-sentence-conjunction convention used elsewhere in triggered resolution text (Rule 701.9 — Discard; Rule 701.26 — Untap).

"That player" refers back to the player named in the trigger condition (Rule 603.2 — "that player" in a triggered ability resolution resolves to the player identified by the trigger event). "All lands you control" references the controller's entire land permanents (CR 301 — Artifacts; CR 701.26a — untapping a permanent).

Priority 62 — sits above the generic single-effect rules (which would fail on the conjoined clause) but below more specific multi-effect rules.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/DiscardAndUntapAllLandsRule.cs)

### `DiscardOnCombatDamageTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"that player discards a card" — discard effect imposed on the player who received combat damage. Fires as the effect clause of a `DealsCombatDamageToPlayer` trigger (Rule 510 — Combat Damage Step). "That player" refers back to the player mentioned in the trigger condition (Rule 701.9 — Discard).

Covers the canonical single-card form. The N-card variant ("that player discards N cards") is also handled: count is parsed from a word-or-digit group so both shapes land on the same rule.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/DiscardOnCombatDamageTriggeredRule.cs)

### `DrawCardConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever you draw a card" — draw-card trigger (Rule 121: Drawing a Card). Fires whenever the controller draws a card by any means. Controller defaults to You.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/DrawCardConditionRule.cs)

### `DrawCardsTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"draw a card" with optional "unless that player pays {N}" tail.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/DrawCardsTriggeredRule.cs)

### `DrawNthCardEachTurnConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever you draw your second card each turn" — ordinal-qualified draw trigger (Rule 121: Drawing a Card). Unlike the unqualified `DrawCardConditionRule` ("you draw a card"), this shape names a specific occurrence within the turn ("your <i>second</i> card") and a per-turn counting window ("each turn"). MAST records those two qualifiers descriptively on the `TriggerCondition` (`Ordinal` and `PerTurn`) rather than encoding any turn-state counting machinery — Rule 603.2 makes the event-match the trigger; the ordinal merely narrows which match counts. Controller defaults to You.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/DrawNthCardEachTurnConditionRule.cs)

### `DrawThatManyCardsLifeLostRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"draw that many cards" (imperative form) — draw effect where the count is derived from life just LOST by the triggering player. Backs the `LosesLife` trigger on Vilis, Broker of Blood: "Whenever you lose life, draw that many cards."

CR 119.3: "If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player's life total is adjusted accordingly." The antecedent of "that many" is the amount of life lost in the triggering event, keyed on `LifeLost`.

Distinct from `YouDrawThatManyCardsTriggeredRule` (which handles the "you draw that many cards" surface and keys the count on `DamageDealt`): Vilis's effect text omits the "you" subject (imperative form) and the triggering event is life loss, not damage dealt. This rule is NOT reflection-discovered into the generic effect loop (no `[TriggeredRule]` attribute); the `TriggeredAbilityParser` dispatches to it directly only when the resolved trigger event is `LosesLife`, preventing the "that many" surface from ever being resolved as `DamageDealt` under a life-loss trigger.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/DrawThatManyCardsLifeLostRule.cs)

### `DrawThenMayPutLandFromHandRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"draw a card, then you may put a [filter] card from your hand onto the battlefield"

Handles the Spelunking ETB pattern: a two-step action within a single sentence where the controller draws a card and then optionally puts a qualifying card from their hand onto the battlefield. The two sub-effects are emitted as a `CompositeEffect` so the sentence-bundle dispatcher's one-effect-per-sentence invariant is preserved while the full semantics of the sentence are captured.

Rule citations:

CR 121.1: "A player draws a card by putting the top card of that player's library into that player's hand." CR 603.1: "Triggered abilities have a trigger condition and an effect." CR 400.7: "If an object in the exile zone or a face-up object in the command zone is put into another zone, it ceases to exist in its current zone and enters the new zone." (zone-change for the hand-to-battlefield move)

ANCHORED (^…$): the full sentence is matched exactly to prevent this rule from firing as a substring of any more-specific sibling. Priority 97 — higher than the plain `DrawCardsTriggeredRule` (default 50) and `PutFromHandOntoBattlefieldTriggeredRule` (63) so neither grabs a portion of this composite sentence before the full form is tried.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/DrawThenMayPutLandFromHandRule.cs)

### `EachOpponentBlightsTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"each opponent blights N" — triggered effect where each opponent performs the blight keyword action (CR 701.68). Emits a `BlightEffect` with `Player = EachOpponent`.

CR 701.68a (verbatim): "To 'blight N' means to put N -1/-1 counters on a creature you control." When an opponent blights, they put the counters on a creature THEY control (not the active player's creature). CR 701.68b: A player who controls no creatures cannot blight.

Pattern: "^each opponent blights \d+$" — anchored so "each opponent blights 1" cannot match inside a broader clause such as hypothetical multi-clause text. Reminder text "(They each put a -1/-1 counter on a creature they control.)" is stripped by the triggered-ability parser before this rule is called.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/EachOpponentBlightsTriggeredRule.cs)

### `EachOpponentDiscardsRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"each opponent discards a card" — ETB discard effect imposed on all opponents simultaneously. Rule 701.9 (Discard). MAST records the discard event and the player scope (EachOpponent); the simultaneous discard order for multiplayer is engine territory, not described by the oracle text.

This rule covers the bare single-card form only. Counts other than one (e.g., "each opponent discards two cards") are a separate shape handled by extension once corpus pressure warrants.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/EachOpponentDiscardsRule.cs)

### `EachOpponentLosesLifeTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"each opponent loses N life" — symmetric drain trigger (Marauding Blight-Priest pattern). Covers triggered effects where all opponents lose a fixed life amount simultaneously.

Rule 119.3: "If a player would lose life, that player's life total is reduced by that amount." Rule 102.2: "An opponent is each other player." In multiplayer, this effect applies to each opponent simultaneously; the simultaneous-resolution ordering is engine territory, not described by oracle text.

This rule handles the bare N-life form only. The compound drain pattern "each opponent loses N life and you gain N life" is handled by `TryParseEachOpponentLoseAndYouGainLife` in the multi-effect composite path.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/EachOpponentLosesLifeTriggeredRule.cs)

### `EachPlayerDrawsTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"each player draws a card" / "each opponent draws a card" — draw effect applied to all players simultaneously or to all opponents simultaneously. Rule 121 (Drawing a Card). MAST records the draw event and the player scope (EachPlayer or EachOpponent); the simultaneous draw order for multiplayer is engine territory, not described by the oracle text.

This rule covers the bare single-card form only. The composite "each player draws a card and loses N life" (Stormfist Crusader shape) is handled by the `TryParseEachPlayerDrawAndLoseLife` orchestrator in `TriggeredAbilityParser`, which runs before this rule and takes priority.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/EachPlayerDrawsTriggeredRule.cs)

### `EnchantedPlayerMillsXCardsWhereXIsGraveyardCountRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"enchanted player mills X cards, where X is the number of cards put into their graveyard from anywhere this turn." — the Fraying Sanity pattern (CR 701.17: "To mill N cards, a player puts the top N cards of their library into their graveyard."; CR 702.5: "Enchant player" — the enchanted player is the Aura's enchanted object).

X is a `CountQuantity` over cards owned by the enchanted player with a `PutIntoGraveyardThisTurnPredicate` history predicate, encoding the backward-looking "cards put into their graveyard from anywhere this turn" phrase faithfully per ADR 0004 (reference-not-resolution). The milling subject is the `EnchantedOrEquipped` reference (the enchanted player). The `FromZone` field on the predicate is set to `Anywhere` for the explicit "from anywhere" qualifier.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/EnchantedPlayerMillsXCardsWhereXIsGraveyardCountRule.cs)

### `EntersAndCreatureControllerDealsCombatDamageToPlayerConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

Compound trigger: "When [SelfName] enters and whenever a creature you control deals combat damage to a player" — Derevi, Empyrial Tactician (C13). A single triggered ability that fires on EITHER of two events: (1) when this permanent (self) enters the battlefield, or (2) whenever a creature the controller controls deals combat damage to a player.

CR 603.2: a triggered ability triggers whenever any of its listed events occurs. The "and" in oracle text ("When A and whenever B") combines two disjoint trigger conditions into one ability; the ability fires whenever EITHER condition is met. The primary condition (ETB self-by-name) is returned from `Match`; the secondary (creature you control deals combat damage) is signalled via `PendingAdditionalTrigger` so `TriggeredAbilityParser` can promote it to `TriggeredAbility.AdditionalTrigger`.

Rule 603.6a (ETB triggers): "Enters-the-battlefield abilities trigger when a permanent enters the battlefield." The self-by-name ETB primary (CR 201.5) is encoded as `IsSelf=true` on the filter (not `Controller=You`), because it refers to this permanent itself, not any creature the controller controls.

Rule 510 (Combat Damage Step): combat damage is dealt; CR 603.2 triggers fire. The secondary condition is `DealsCombatDamageToPlayer` with `Controller=You` on the filter — "a creature you control" means any creature the ability's controller controls (CR 109.5).

Anchored (`^...$`) to prevent substring-matching a broader trigger phrase. Must be tried at higher priority than the generic `EntersConditionRule` and `DealsCombatDamageConditionRule` so the compound phrase is matched as a unit before either sub-phrase claims it.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/EntersAndCreatureControllerDealsCombatDamageToPlayerConditionRule.cs)

### `EntersAndOpponentDrawsNotFirstConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

Compound trigger: "When this creature enters and whenever an opponent draws a card except the first one they draw in each of their draw steps" — Orcish Bowmasters (LTR). This is a single triggered ability that fires on EITHER of two events: (1) when this creature enters the battlefield, or (2) whenever an opponent draws any card that is not the first card drawn in their draw step.

CR 603.2: a triggered ability triggers whenever any of its listed events occurs. The "and" in oracle text ("When A and whenever B") combines two disjoint trigger conditions into one ability; the ability fires whenever EITHER condition is met. The primary condition (ETB) is encoded on `TriggerCondition` (Trigger); the secondary condition (opponent-draws-not-first) is encoded on `AdditionalTrigger`. Both share the same effect list.

The "except the first one they draw in each of their draw steps" qualifier is captured on the secondary condition's `ExceptFirstDrawStep` flag (CR 121.1: "a player draws a card"; CR 603.2: the qualifier narrows which draw events match). This is descriptive — the engine determines whether a given draw event is the first draw of the step; MAST records that the exclusion exists.

Must be tried at higher priority than the generic Enters and OpponentDrawsCard rules so the full compound phrase is matched before either sub-phrase.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/EntersAndOpponentDrawsNotFirstConditionRule.cs)

### `EntersConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"enters" triggers: "this creature enters", "a creature enters", etc.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/EntersConditionRule.cs)

### `EntersOrAttacksConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"enters or attacks" combined triggers — fires on either zone-change/combat event. Oracle form: "Whenever this creature enters or attacks, [effect]." Rule 603: "enters" (ETB, CR 400.4) and "attacks" (Declare Attackers step, CR 508) are distinct game events; a single triggered ability may name both via the "or" conjunction, triggering on whichever event occurs. Must be tried before the individual Enters/Attacks rules (higher priority = 993) so the disjunction isn't split and misclassified.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/EntersOrAttacksConditionRule.cs)

### `EntersOrDiesConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"enters or dies" compound triggers — fires on either zone-change event. Rule 603: "When [subject] enters or dies, [effect]." The oracle phrase "enters or dies" (always in that order) denotes a single triggered ability watching for the permanent either entering the battlefield or dying. Must be tried before the individual Enters/Dies rules (higher priority) so the disjunction isn't split and misclassified.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/EntersOrDiesConditionRule.cs)

### `EtbTeamPumpTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"Creatures you control get +N/+M [and gain &lt;keyword&gt;] until end of turn." ETB team-pump pattern: the triggering creature buffs the whole team. The keyword grant is optional — both shapes are covered by one rule:

"Creatures you control get +1/+0 until end of turn." "Creatures you control get +1/+1 and gain haste until end of turn."

When only P/T is present the rule emits a bare `ModifyPTEffect`. When a keyword follows "and gain" the rule wraps both effects in a `CompositeEffect`, matching the conjunctive-modifier gold convention used on the static side (Rule 611 — continuous effects applied simultaneously). Recognised keywords (the set that appears in existing oracle corpus): haste, trample, flying, first strike, double strike, reach, lifelink, vigilance, deathtouch, menace, indestructible, hexproof, shroud.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/EtbTeamPumpTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ExcessNoncombatDamageDealtConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever a creature or planeswalker an opponent controls is dealt excess noncombat damage" — the Toralf trigger condition (KHM: Toralf, God of Fury). Fires when a creature or planeswalker controlled by an opponent receives noncombat damage that exceeds lethal damage or the permanent's loyalty (CR 120.10).

The filter carries `CardTypes = ["creature", "planeswalker"]` with `Controller = Opponent`. Both card types share one filter because the oracle text unifies them with "or" — no separate arms for each type.

CR 120.10 (verbatim): "Some triggered abilities check whether a permanent has been dealt excess damage. These abilities check after the permanent has been dealt damage by one or more sources. If those sources together dealt an amount of damage to a creature greater than lethal damage, excess damage equal to the difference was dealt to that creature. If those sources together dealt an amount of damage to a planeswalker greater than that planeswalker's loyalty before the damage was dealt, excess damage equal to the difference was dealt to that planeswalker."

Anchored (^…$) and scoped to the trigger clause to prevent matching inside a broader clause that contains "dealt excess noncombat damage" as a substring. Runs at priority 987 — above `SourceDealsNoncombatDamageConditionRule` (986) so the "excess noncombat" specialisation is claimed before the more-general "deals noncombat damage" rule.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ExcessNoncombatDamageDealtConditionRule.cs)

### `ExileInstantFromHandImprintTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you may exile an instant card with mana value 2 or less from your hand" — the Imprint triggered-ability effect (Isochron Scepter, Mirrodin / MRD:200).

CR 702.38 (Imprint): an imprint ability exiles a card from the controller's hand onto this permanent so a second ability may reference it. The exile is a one-shot effect (Rule 701.13) from Zone.Hand: the controller chooses an eligible card from their hand (not a targeted card — the word "target" does not appear) and exiles it. The choice is modelled as `Any` (controller-choice reference, CR 109.5) with Zone.Hand so the interaction layer can distinguish "from hand" from "on the battlefield". The "you may" gate is modelled as an `OptionalEffect` per ADR 0005.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ExileInstantFromHandImprintTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ExileSelfTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"exile it" / "exile this creature" / "exile this permanent" — a self-exile effect where the triggered ability's source exiles itself (or the trigger subject). Maps to an `ExileEffect` with `It` (the trigger subject / source).

Rule 701.13a (verbatim): "To exile an object, move it to the exile zone from wherever it is." MAST records the target as It (the subject of the trigger, i.e., the source permanent); the engine performs the zone change.

Examples: Eternal Scourge (EMN): "exile this creature."

Rule citations: 701.13a (Exile action), 109.2 (object reference), 603.1-603.2 (triggered ability machinery).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ExileSelfTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ExileTargetTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"exile target [filter]." — single-target permanent exile on the triggered side. Delegates filter parsing to `ParseTargetFilter` (same lexical surface as `ExileTargetSimpleRule`). Covers bare card-type targets (creature, permanent, artifact, enchantment, planeswalker) and richer filter phrases via `ParseTargetFilter`:

Subtype-only: "Spirit", "Human" Color + card type: "black creature", "white artifact" non- prefix + card type: "nonblack creature", "nontoken creature"

This rule handles PERMANENT exile (no "until [this] leaves the battlefield" suffix). The `ExileUntilLeavesTriggeredRule` handles the temporary Oblivion Ring shape. Rule 701.13 (exile action) + Rule 205.3 (card types).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ExileTargetTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ExileThenReturnFlickerTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

ETB "flicker" (blink) effect: "you may exile [target] you control, then return that card to the battlefield under [its owner's / your] control." — the resolution half of an ETB trigger (Felidar Guardian, Restoration Angel).

The "you may" is a structured `OptionalEffect` (CR 117.7), not a flag: the controller may choose to perform the whole exile-then-return action. The action itself is a `CompositeEffect` of two ordered effects — an `ExileEffect` followed by a `ReturnToBattlefieldEffect`. "then return that card" is the LINKED exiled reference (the Petravark / Azula precedent), modelled via `ExiledWith` as a `{Zone: Exile, ExiledWith: {Kind: Self}}` reference (ADR 0004 reference-not-resolution) rather than a threaded runtime binding or free text: the just-exiled card and this return are the same one resolving ability.

The exile target keeps its printed filter — "another target permanent you control" (`ExcludeSelf`, CR 109.5) or "target non-Angel creature you control" (`ExcludedSubtypes`). "under its owner's control" rides on `UnderControl` as an `Owner` reference; "under your control" as a `You` reference (CR 400.6).

CR 603.6a (enters-the-battlefield abilities) — this is the resolution half; the trigger condition ("When this creature enters") is recognised separately. CR 603.2 (the event-match is the trigger).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ExileThenReturnFlickerTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ExileUntilLeavesTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"exile target nonland permanent an opponent controls until this [type] leaves the battlefield." — the Oblivion Ring / Hieromancer's Cage ETB exile shape. The effect is a temporary exile: the permanent returns when this [type] leaves the battlefield (Rule 611 — continuous effects with duration). MAST records the exile action descriptively (Rule 701.13 — Exile) with an `UntilLeavesBattlefieldDuration` whose `Object` is the literal self-reference phrase from oracle text ("this enchantment", "this creature", etc.). The LTB return ability is engine territory (Rule 603.7d — linked triggered abilities); MAST does not model it (descriptive-not-engine doctrine).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ExileUntilLeavesTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ExileUpToOneGraveyardAndCreateFoodRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"exile up to one target card from a graveyard and create a Food token" — the compound triggered-effect pattern from Hazel's Brewmaster. Produces a `CompositeEffect` of an `ExileEffect` (up to one target graveyard card) followed by a `CreateTokenEffect` (Food).

CR 701.13a: "To exile an object, move it to the exile zone from wherever it is." CR 111.10b: "A Food token is a colorless Food artifact token with '{2}, {T}, Sacrifice this token: You gain 3 life.'" CR 602 / 603: the exile and token-creation are bundled into a single effect body separated by "and"; both effects resolve together at ability resolution.

Anchored (^…$) to prevent matching inside a longer "exile…and…" body that carries additional clauses a more-specific rule should handle.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ExileUpToOneGraveyardAndCreateFoodRule.cs)

### `ExploreTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"it explores." — Rule 701.44a keyword action on the triggered side. Matches the canonical ETB-explore pattern where the entering creature (referred to as "it") performs the Explore keyword action.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ExploreTriggeredRule.cs)

### `FlickerUpToOnePermanentTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"exile up to one target nonland permanent you control, then return that card to the battlefield under its owner's control" — the cast-trigger flicker (blink) of Displacer Kitten ("Avoidance — Whenever you cast a noncreature spell, …").

This is the triggered-side wrapper of the same flicker effect the spell-side `FlickerTargetPermanentsSpellRule` (Ghostly Flicker) builds: an `ExileEffect` on the chosen permanent, then a `ReturnToBattlefieldEffect` of the just-exiled card. The cast trigger itself ("Whenever you cast a noncreature spell") is recognised by `SpellCastConditionRule` per CR 603.2 (a game event matching the trigger event triggers the ability); this rule only builds the effect.

"that card" is NOT free text — it is the linked exiled reference (CR 607.2 / ADR 0004 "reference, not resolution"): a `Designated` card in `Exile` exiled with this object (`ExiledWith = {Kind: Self}`), the Petravark return shape. "up to one" is a structured `UpToQuantity` on the target reference's `Quantity`, not a literal. "nonland permanent you control" is a structured filter (`CardTypes = ["permanent"]` + <c>ExcludedCardTypes = ["land"]</c> + `Controller = You`). "under its owner's control" rides on `UnderControl` as a `Owner` reference (CR 400.6). The two effects sit in one `CompositeEffect` because the oracle states them as a single "exile …, then return …" action.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/FlickerUpToOnePermanentTriggeredRule.cs)

### `GainControlTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"gain control of target [filter] [until end of turn]" — single-target control-change on the triggered side (ETB, dies, upkeep, etc.). Delegates single-noun filter parsing to `ParseTargetFilter`. Also handles the controller qualifier "an opponent controls" after the filter phrase, which is the canonical way oracle text restricts steal effects to opposing permanents (Rule 109.5 — control relationship). Duration is optional:

"until end of turn" → `UntilEndOfTurnDuration` No duration clause → <see langword="null"/> (permanent control change)

The optional "you may" prefix sets `IsOptional` = <see langword="true"/>. Recognized filter nouns (via `ParseTargetFilter`): creature, permanent, artifact, enchantment, planeswalker, land, and richer filter phrases (color + type, non- prefix, etc.). Rule 701.3 (control-changing effects) + Rule 115.1 (target).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/GainControlTriggeredRule.cs)

### `GainEnergyTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you get {E}", "you get {E}{E}", ... — energy-counter gain triggered effect. Rule 107.14: Energy counters are a player resource represented by the {E} symbol. Each "{E}" symbol in the oracle text represents one energy counter; we count the symbols to derive the literal amount.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/GainEnergyTriggeredRule.cs)

### `GainProtectionFromEverythingTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you gain protection from everything until your next turn" — The One Ring's ETB consequent.

A player gains protection from everything (CR 702.16 — protection; CR 702.16e — a player can have protection). Modeled as a `GainAbilityEffect` granting the controller (`You`) a static `Protection` ability whose quality is `Everything`, for `YourNextTurn` (CR 611 — continuous effect with a duration). The "if you cast it" gate is a separate intervening-if (`CastThisObjectCondition`) handled by the triggered-ability parser, not this effect.

ANCHORED (`^…$`) on the full clause so no sibling protection-grant is mislabeled.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/GainProtectionFromEverythingTriggeredRule.cs)

### `GainsLifeConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever you gain life" — life-gain trigger. Controller defaults to You, flips to Opponent when the oracle text names an opponent.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/GainsLifeConditionRule.cs)

### `HauntCompoundConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"When this creature enters or the creature it haunts dies" — Haunt compound trigger (Rule 702.55). Fires in two situations: (1) when this creature enters the battlefield, and (2) when the creature this card haunts dies. Both events are modelled as the single `EntersOrHauntedCreatureDies`. Must be tried before the generic enters/dies rules (higher priority) — this trigger contains both words.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/HauntCompoundConditionRule.cs)

### `IfPutSubtypeOntoBattlefieldThisWayGainLifeRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"If you put a [Subtype] onto the battlefield this way, you gain [N] life."

Handles the Spelunking pattern: a conditional effect sentence (CR 603.12 — reflexive triggered ability) that fires based on whether the card moved to the battlefield by the preceding optional zone-change in the same ability had a particular subtype. Modelled as a `ConditionalEffect` whose `Condition` is an `OtherCondition` (the causation-gate "this way" plus a subtype filter is a compound shape without a dedicated structured arm yet — ADR 0001 residual) and whose `Then` is a `GainLifeEffect`.

CR 603.12 (verbatim): "A resolving spell or ability may allow or instruct a player to take an action and create a triggered ability that triggers 'when [a player] [does or doesn't]' take that action or 'when [something happens] this way.' These reflexive triggered abilities follow the rules for delayed triggered abilities (see rule 603.7), except that they're checked immediately after being created and trigger based on whether the trigger event or events occurred earlier during the resolution of the spell or ability that created them."

CR 119.3 (verbatim): "If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player's life total is adjusted accordingly."

ANCHORED (^…$): fully anchored to prevent false-positive substring matches. Priority 97 — same band as `DrawThenMayPutLandFromHandRule` so both Spelunking sentences are processed before less-specific rules.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/IfPutSubtypeOntoBattlefieldThisWayGainLifeRule.cs)

### `InvestigateTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"investigate." — Rule 701.16a keyword action on the triggered side. Matches the bare keyword action when it appears as the entire effect body of a triggered ability (e.g. Thraben Inspector's ETB trigger). CR 701.16a: "Investigate" means "Create a Clue token." See rule 111.10f. CR 603.6a: Enters-the-battlefield abilities trigger when a permanent enters the battlefield. These are written, "When [this object] enters, . . . "... REUSE-ONLY: emits the existing `InvestigateEffect` node. No new AST types are introduced.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/InvestigateTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ItDealsCountDamageToAnyTargetRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"it deals X damage to any target, where X is the number of [Subtype]s you control." — the entering permanent deals damage equal to a count of a subtype you control to any legal target. Covers Dragon Tempest's second ability: "it deals X damage to any target, where X is the number of Dragons you control." Rule 603: triggered abilities resolve by executing their effects. Rule 120.1– 120.2: a source deals damage to a permanent or player. Rule 115.4: "any target" may be any player, creature, planeswalker, or battle. "It" is the anaphoric pronoun (CR 113.8b) referring to the entering Dragon — the permanent the trigger's filter matched. Modelled as `It`. The amount "X" is a `CountQuantity` — the count of permanents matching the filter (subtype + controller = You). "Where X is the number of…" is a definitional clause naming the quantity; the variable letter X has no independent identity here — the structured `CountQuantity` replaces it. Distinct from `SelfDealsDamageToAnyTargetTriggeredRule`, which handles a fixed literal amount ("it deals 3 damage").

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ItDealsCountDamageToAnyTargetRule.cs)

### `ItGainsKeywordUntilEndOfTurnRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"it gains [keyword] until end of turn." — grants a keyword ability to the entering object (referred to as "it") for the remainder of the turn. Covers Dragon Tempest's first ability resolution clause: "it gains haste until end of turn." Rule 603: triggered abilities resolve by executing their effects. Rule 611.1: effects that modify characteristics (such as keyword abilities) are continuous effects with a duration. "Until end of turn" is the most common duration for triggered keyword grants. The target "it" refers anaphorically (CR 113.8b) to the permanent named by the trigger's filter — the entering creature. Modelled as `It` (the generic anaphoric pronoun). Distinct from `TeamGainKeywordTriggeredRule`, which targets "creatures you control" (an `Each` reference), not the entering creature itself.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ItGainsKeywordUntilEndOfTurnRule.cs)

### `LeavesTheBattlefieldConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"leaves the battlefield" triggers: "this creature leaves the battlefield", "a creature you control leaves the battlefield", etc. (CR 603.2 — a game event matching a triggered ability's trigger event causes it to trigger).
