This is the LTB half of a CR 607.2 linked pair on cards like Petravark, whose ETB exiles a permanent and whose LTB returns the card exiled by that same ability. MAST records the trigger event descriptively; the linkage to the exiled card is carried on the effect side via the `ExiledWith` reference, not threaded here (ADR 0004 reference-not-resolution).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/LeavesTheBattlefieldConditionRule.cs)

### `LookAtTopCardsTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"look at the top N cards of your library, then put them back in any order." — The Crystal Seer ETB pattern. Rule 701.12 (look) + unconditional reorder. The controller looks at the top N cards then returns all of them in any chosen order. Distinct from scry (top/bottom choice per card) and surveil (graveyard option).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/LookAtTopCardsTriggeredRule.cs)

### `LoseLifeDerivedRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"target opponent loses that much life" — Vito's drain shape. Encodes the derived quantity (LifeGained antecedent) and the OPPONENT recipient.

The recipient is `Opponent` — a singular opponent (CR glossary "Opponent"), matching the corpus convention for opponent-player references (Eroded Canyon, Jagged Barrens, Lonely Arroyo). It is NOT a generic `Target {{ CardTypes = ["player"] }}`: dropping the opponent constraint flattens "an opponent" to "any player" (which could be you), which is rules-wrong and downstream costs interaction recall — the operator cannot certify the loser is an opponent, so a life-drain loop (Vito × Exquisite Blood) floors to a needless AMBER. The opponent reference is the parse-layer precision that lets that hop tier GREEN (see libs/mast-interaction/docs/adding-a-flow-arm.md).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/LoseLifeDerivedRule.cs)

### `LoseLifePerNamedCounterTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you lose 1 life for each [counter] counter on [self]" — The One Ring's upkeep drain ("you lose 1 life for each burden counter on The One Ring").

You lose life equal to the number of named counters on the source (1 per counter). Modeled as a `LoseLifeEffect` whose `Amount` is a `CounterCountQuantity` over the named counter on `Self` (reference-not-resolution, ADR 0004 — the engine reads the live counter count). CR 122 (counters); the "1 … for each" is the unit per-counter multiplier, i.e. the count itself.

The self target is "it" or the card naming itself (a capitalized name → self-reference, CR 201.5); a reference to ANOTHER permanent's counters uses "that"/"target"/lowercase and is not matched here. ANCHORED on the full clause.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/LoseLifePerNamedCounterTriggeredRule.cs)

### `LosesLifeConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever an opponent loses life" / "Whenever you lose life" — life-loss trigger (Exquisite Blood, Bloodthirsty Conqueror). The controller axis is carried on the trigger's filter: `Opponent` when the oracle text names an opponent, else `You`.

CR 603.2: "Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability's trigger event, that ability automatically triggers." The matched event is a player losing life (CR 119.3: "If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player's life total is adjusted accordingly."). The amount of life lost is referenced by a downstream "that much" effect (a derived quantity); this rule records only the event + the player class.

Mirrors `GainsLifeConditionRule` (the `GainsLife` sibling): same controller-default-flip shape, the opposite life event.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/LosesLifeConditionRule.cs)

### `ManaPersistsUntilEndOfTurnRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"Until end of turn, you don't lose this mana as steps and phases end." — the mana-persistence clause on Birgi, God of Storytelling.

CR 106.4 / CR 500.5: normally mana empties at the end of each step and phase. This sentence is the oracle-text signal that the mana produced in the same triggered ability persists until end of turn instead, overriding the turn-based mana-emptying action.

ANCHORED (^…$): the surface phrase "don't lose this mana as steps and phases end" is highly specific and cannot match as a substring of another triggered rule. Anchoring is maintained as a defensive convention (ADR anchor rule).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ManaPersistsUntilEndOfTurnRule.cs)

### `MayHaveThatPlayerLoseLifeYouGainRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you may have that player lose N life. If you do, you gain N life." — an optional triggered drain where the controller optionally applies life-loss to the player who triggered the ability (a "that player" back-reference), and if they do, the controller gains life (Bloodchief Ascension, ZEN).

The "you may" makes the life-loss optional (CR 117.7: "if you do" clauses are optional branches). The controller chooses whether to have "that player" (CR 109.5 — the player identified by the trigger condition's filter, e.g. the opponent whose graveyard received a card) lose life. If the controller opts in, they also gain an equal amount of life.

Modelled as an `OptionalEffect` whose `Inner` is `loseLife` (player = `ThatPlayer`) and whose `IfYouDo` is `gainLife` (player = You). The "If you do" follow-up is the canonical structured `IfYouDo` slot of `OptionalEffect` (ADR 0005 — clause modifiers are composition).

Representative card: Bloodchief Ascension (ZEN) — "… you may have that player lose 2 life. If you do, you gain 2 life." Rule citations: CR 117.7 (optional effects / "if you do"), CR 119.3 (life totals), CR 109.5 ("that player").

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/MayHaveThatPlayerLoseLifeYouGainRule.cs)

### `MayPayUntapAttackingAndAdditionalCombatRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you may pay {COST}. If you do, untap all attacking creatures and after this phase, there is an additional combat phase." — the Hellkite Charger combat-reset pattern.

Produces an `OptionalEffect` whose `Inner` is a `ConditionalPayEffect` carrying the mana cost, and whose `IfYouDo` is a `CompositeEffect` containing:

An `UntapEffect` targeting all attacking creatures (CR 701.26: untap; CR 508: attacking creatures). An `AdditionalCombatPhaseEffect` (CR 500.8: adding phases to a turn).

Pattern is anchored (^...$) to prevent matching as a substring of a more-specific sibling. Only the COST is variable; "untap all attacking creatures" and "after this phase, there is an additional combat phase" are fixed. This rule deliberately handles only this exact clause shape rather than extending the general `ConditionalPayTriggeredRule` to avoid broad combinatorial coupling.

CR references: CR 500.8 (adding a phase to a turn); CR 508.3a (attacks trigger); CR 701.26 (tap/untap); CR 117.3 (paying costs optionally).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/MayPayUntapAttackingAndAdditionalCombatRule.cs)

### `MillTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"mill N cards." / "mill a card." — Rule 701.17 keyword action on the triggered side. The implicit subject (controller) is encoded as Player = You. Also handles "target player mills N cards." (Constellation pattern such as Sage of Mysteries / Thassa's Devourer) where the target is encoded as Player = Target + player filter (Rule 115.1). Also handles "defending player mills half their library, rounded up/down." (Terisian Mindbreaker) where the defending player (CR 508.1b) mills a calculated half of their own library, rounded in the named direction. The `DefendingPlayer` reference already exists in the reference system; this rule composes it with a `CalculatedQuantity` using `CardsInLibrary` as the base (mirroring the Heartless Hidetsugu pattern where LifeTotal is used without a source — context is provided by the Player field of MillEffect).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/MillTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ModifyPTTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"it gets +N/+N until end of turn" — triggered P/T modification on the creature that fired the trigger (Rule 508 attack trigger pattern). The subject "it" refers to the attacking or otherwise-triggering creature and maps to `It`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ModifyPTTriggeredRule.cs)

### `MutatesConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever this creature mutates" — mutate trigger (Rule 702.140). Fires each time the creature successfully mutates. The Filter captures the subject ("this creature") as a creature-type filter.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/MutatesConditionRule.cs)

### `NamedSourceDealsDamageEqualToExcessToAnyTargetRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"[CardName] deals damage equal to the excess to any target [other than that permanent]." — named-source ping where the damage amount equals the excess from an `ExcessNoncombatDamageDealt` trigger (KHM: Toralf, God of Fury). The subject starts with a capital letter (a card name, CR 201.4: a card's name in its own text refers to itself). MAST resolves the self-reference to `Self`.

The amount "equal to the excess" is modelled as `DerivedQuantity { DerivedFrom = DerivedKind.DamageDealt }`: the triggering event's excess is the distinct quantum of damage dealt beyond lethal (CR 120.10 — "excess damage equal to the difference"). `DamageDealt` is the canonical derived-quantity axis for damage dealt in a triggering event, reused here for excess damage since there is no separate `ExcessDamage` kind.

The trailing "other than that permanent" exclusion is dropped: no `ObjectReferenceKind` carries a "ThatPermanent" exclusion; the engine resolves the targeting constraint via the existing AnyTarget + CR 115.7 legality. This matches the precedent in PawpatchRecruit ("target creature you control other than that creature" → unfiltered creature target).

Anchored (^…$) to prevent matching inside a broader clause. Priority 61 — just above `NamedSourceDealsDamageToAnyTargetTriggeredRule` (Priority 60) so the "equal to the excess" shape is claimed before the literal-amount form attempts to parse the non-numeric "equal to the excess" amount.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/NamedSourceDealsDamageEqualToExcessToAnyTargetRule.cs)

### `NamedSourceDealsDamageToAnyTargetTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"[CardName] deals N damage to any target." — named-source self-ping triggered effect. Covers cards whose triggered ability effect reads "[CardName] deals N damage to any target." where the card refers to itself by its own printed name (Rule 201.4 — a card's name refers to itself when used in its own text). MAST resolves the self-reference to `Self`. Rule 120.1-120.2: dealing damage (a source deals damage to a permanent/player). Rule 115.4: "any target" — a target may be any player, creature, planeswalker, or battle unless otherwise restricted. Distinct from `SelfDealsDamageToAnyTargetTriggeredRule` (which handles the pronoun "it deals N damage" shape — same event, different oracle phrasing). Both map to Source=Self; the difference is naming convention only.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/NamedSourceDealsDamageToAnyTargetTriggeredRule.cs)

### `NamedSourceDealsDamageToThatPlayerTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"[CardName] deals N damage to that player." — named-source self-ping effect directed at the trigger's named player. Covers triggered abilities whose effect reads "[CardName] deals N damage to that player." where the card refers to itself by its own printed name (CR 201.5 — a card's name in its own text refers to itself). MAST resolves the self-reference to `Self`. "That player" back-references whichever player caused the trigger to fire (CR 603.2: the event-match player).

CR 120.1: "Objects can deal damage to battles, creatures, planeswalkers, and players." CR 603.1: "Triggered abilities have a trigger condition and an effect."

Anchored (^…$) to prevent substring matches. Priority 62 — just above `NamedSourceDealsDamageToAnyTargetTriggeredRule` (Priority 60) so the more-specific "that player" target is claimed before the "any target" form. Distinct from `SelfDealsDamageToThatPlayerRule`, which handles the pronoun form "this creature/it deals N damage to that player/them".

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/NamedSourceDealsDamageToThatPlayerTriggeredRule.cs)

### `NoCombatDamageThisCombatRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"This creature assigns no combat damage this combat." — the source creature is excluded from assigning combat damage for the remainder of the current combat.

Rule 510.1: "Each attacking creature and each blocking creature assigns combat damage." This effect records the explicit oracle override preventing the named creature from assigning any combat damage in the current combat phase. Descriptive only; the zero-damage assignment is engine territory.

Canonical use: Master of Cruelties — the triggered ability that sets an attacked player's life total to 1 also prevents the creature from dealing combat damage, so the combined effect is: reduce to 1 life without also dealing the 1/4 base damage. Oracle text: "This creature assigns no combat damage this combat."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/NoCombatDamageThisCombatRule.cs)

### `NontokenCreatureToGraveyardFromBattlefieldConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

Trigger condition: "a nontoken creature is put into your graveyard from the battlefield."

This is the long-form "dies" trigger (CR 700.4: "a creature, planeswalker, or battle is put into a graveyard from the battlefield") qualified with two restrictions:

<b>nontoken</b> — the creature is not a token (CR 111.1: a token is not a card; the `IsToken = false` axis on `ObjectFilter` encodes this). <b>your graveyard</b> — the creature entered a graveyard you own (`Controller = You` on the filter).

Maps to `Dies` because "put into your graveyard from the battlefield" is definitionally the Dies event (CR 700.4), with the controller constraint naming whose graveyard receives it. The nontoken qualifier is the standard creature-token distinction used throughout the corpus (CR 111.1).

Canonical card: Nim Deathmantle (SOM) — "Whenever a nontoken creature is put into your graveyard from the battlefield, you may pay {4}. If you do, return that card to the battlefield and attach this Equipment to it."

CR 700.4 (verbatim): "The word 'dies' means 'is put into a graveyard from the battlefield.'" CR 111.1 (tokens are not cards).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/NontokenCreatureToGraveyardFromBattlefieldConditionRule.cs)

### `OneOrMoreSubtypeDealsDamageToOpponentsConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever one or more [Subtype] you control deal damage to your opponents" — the group-damage-to-multiple-opponents trigger condition (Malcolm, Keen-Eyed Navigator pattern).

Emits `DealsDamageToOpponents` (plural). The Filter carries the creature subtype and controller (You). Distinct from `DealsDamageToOpponentConditionRule` (singular "an opponent" / single source) and `DealsCombatDamageConditionRule` (combat damage only).

Rule 120.1: "Objects can deal damage to battles, creatures, planeswalkers, and players. This is generally detrimental to the object or player that receives that damage." Rule 102.2: an opponent is any player not on the same team as the controller. Rule 603.2: triggered abilities fire automatically on matching game events.

ANCHORED (^…$): matched against the full trigger clause (after timing-word strip) to prevent misfiring on broader trigger phrases that include this as a substring. Priority 987: above `DealsDamageToOpponentConditionRule` (986) so the more specific "your opponents" (plural) shape is tried before the singular form.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/OneOrMoreSubtypeDealsDamageToOpponentsConditionRule.cs)

### `OpenAttractionTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"open an Attraction." — keyword action (Rule 701.51) on the triggered side. Matches the bare keyword action when it is the entire effect body of a triggered ability (e.g. Seasoned Buttoneer's ETB trigger). The trailing parenthetical reminder is stripped by the dispatcher before this rule runs (Rule 207.2). CR 701.51b: "To open an Attraction, move the top card of your Attraction deck off the Attraction deck, turn it face up, and put it onto the battlefield under your control." CR 603.6a: Enters-the-battlefield abilities trigger when a permanent enters the battlefield. These are written, "When [this object] enters, . . ." Emits the `OpenAttractionEffect` node.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/OpenAttractionTriggeredRule.cs)

### `OpponentDrawsCardConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever an opponent draws a card" — draw-card trigger scoped to an opponent (CR 121.1: "A player draws a card by putting the top card of their library into their hand"; CR 603.2: the event-match fires the ability whenever any opponent performs that action). The Filter Controller = Opponent narrows the trigger to draws performed by an opponent rather than the controller.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/OpponentDrawsCardConditionRule.cs)

### `PhaseTriggerConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"At the beginning of your upkeep" / "...first main phase" / "...draw step" / "...end step" / "...combat on your turn". Maps the phase/step word to a `TriggerEvent`. The "your" / "each opponent's" possessive lands on the filter as a `Controller`. Only fires for At-timing triggers.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/PhaseTriggerConditionRule.cs)

### `ProliferateTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"proliferate." — Rule 701.27 keyword action on the triggered side. Matches the bare proliferate instruction that appears after the trigger comma (reminder text has already been stripped by the time this rule is called).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ProliferateTriggeredRule.cs)

### `PutCountersTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"put a +1/+1 counter on this creature" / "put a -1/-1 counter on target creature" / "that player gets N [type] counters" (poison/energy/etc. given to a player on combat damage — Fynn, the Fangbearer; Blighted Agent; etc.).

CR 122.1: "A counter is a marker placed on an object or player that modifies its characteristics and/or interacts with a rule or effect." Poison counters are placed on players; the "gets" verb in oracle text means the player receives those counters. Distinct from "put a counter on [permanent]" only in subject (player vs. permanent) and verb ("gets" vs. "put … on"). MAST models both as `putCounters` effect — the counter placement is the same action regardless of verb.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/PutCountersTriggeredRule.cs)

### `PutFromHandOntoBattlefieldTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you may put a[n] &lt;filter&gt; card from your hand onto the battlefield tapped and attacking that opponent"

Handles the Kaalia of the Vast pattern: an optional zone-change from hand to battlefield where the card enters tapped and attacking the opponent named by the enclosing trigger. The "you may" wrapper is materialised as `OptionalEffect` around the inner `PutFromHandOntoBattlefieldEffect`.

Rule citations:

CR 508.1b — "attacks an opponent" trigger; the card enters attacking that same opponent. CR 400.7 — zone change creates a new object. CR 110.5b — permanent enters tapped. CR 603.1 — triggered ability trigger + effect.

Priority 63 — above the generic battlefield-search rules and sibling hand-to-play rules so the "from your hand onto the battlefield" form is matched before any library-search fallback. The pattern is fully anchored (^...$) so it cannot match as a substring of a more-complex sibling effect.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/PutFromHandOntoBattlefieldTriggeredRule.cs)

### `PutIntoGraveyardConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

Recognises the "one or more [type] cards are put into your graveyard from anywhere" trigger condition (Rule 603 — Handling Triggered Abilities; Rule 701.7 — "Put into a graveyard"). Maps to `PutIntoGraveyard` with a filter that captures the card type and the controller whose graveyard is the destination.

The "one or more" qualifier is a threshold condition on the trigger event: the ability fires once per batch-triggering event, regardless of whether one or many qualifying cards entered the graveyard simultaneously. MAST records this descriptively (the filter names the type; the threshold semantics are engine territory).

"from anywhere" omits a source-zone restriction; the filter carries only the destination (owner's graveyard). A future zone-of-origin axis on the filter could refine this when needed, but the current corpus does not require it.

Representative cards: The Gitrog Monster (SOI) — "Whenever one or more land cards are put into your graveyard from anywhere, draw a card." Rule citations: CR 603 (Triggered Abilities), CR 701.7 (Put into a graveyard).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/PutIntoGraveyardConditionRule.cs)

### `PutNegativeCountersOnCreatureConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"you put one or more -1/-1 counters on a creature" — counter-placement trigger (Nest of Scarabs).

CR 122.1: "A counter is a marker placed on an object or player that modifies its characteristics and/or interacts with a rule or effect." This trigger fires whenever the controller places one or more -1/-1 counters on any creature. The filter carries the target card type (creature) and the controller (You) so the interaction layer knows whose action triggered. The counter type is encoded in a companion `CounterType` field on the `TriggerCondition` Filter-as-written (the creature being the object that receives the counters).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/PutNegativeCountersOnCreatureConditionRule.cs)

### `PutPermanentCardByManaValueFromHandRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you may put a permanent card with equal or lesser mana value from your hand onto the battlefield"

Handles the Kodama of the East Tree pattern: an optional zone-change from hand to battlefield for a permanent card whose mana value is less than or equal to the mana value of a referenced object (typically the permanent that triggered the enclosing triggered ability — represented as `It`).

The "equal or lesser mana value" phrase is a relative comparison (CR 202.3 — mana value is the sum of the mana symbols in a card's mana cost) against the triggering permanent's mana value, encoded as a `Comparison` with `RelativeTo` set to `It` and `RelativeCharacteristic` set to `ManaValue`.

Rule citations:

CR 202.3 — mana value (formerly converted mana cost) is the sum of mana symbols. CR 400.7 — zone change creates a new object. CR 603.1 — triggered ability trigger + effect.

Priority 64 — above `PutFromHandOntoBattlefieldTriggeredRule` (priority 63) so this more-specific mana-value-comparison form is matched first. Fully anchored (^...$) to prevent substring matches against sibling effects.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/PutPermanentCardByManaValueFromHandRule.cs)

### `PutPlusCountersOnThisCreatureConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"one or more +1/+1 counters are put on this creature" — passive counter-placement trigger on the source creature itself (Scurry Oak, Spike Feeder variants).

CR 122.1: "A counter is a marker placed on an object or player that modifies its characteristics and/or interacts with a rule or effect." This trigger fires whenever one or more +1/+1 counters are placed on the ability's source creature (passive voice: "are put on this creature"). The filter carries `IsSelf = true` to record that the recipient is the source permanent, not an arbitrary creature, so the interaction layer can distinguish "counters placed on this specific permanent" from "counters placed on any creature". The counter type is encoded in `CounterType` and the minimum quantity in `MinimumCount`.

Rule 603.2: each event matching the trigger condition fires the ability; "one or more" constrains the per-event count to at least 1 (MinimumCount = 1). The event fires on any +1/+1 counter placement — by the controller or any other source — as long as the recipient is this creature.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/PutPlusCountersOnThisCreatureConditionRule.cs)

### `RemoveCounterTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"remove a +1/+1 counter from it" / "remove a charge counter from it" / "remove a -1/-1 counter from this creature" as a triggered effect. Covers counter-removal as the primary triggered action, e.g.: "At the beginning of your upkeep, remove a -1/-1 counter from Aboroth." "At end of combat, remove a +1/+1 counter from it." "At the beginning of your upkeep, remove a charge counter from this artifact." Counter type resolution mirrors `PutCountersTriggeredRule`: "+1/+1" and "-1/-1" are matched directly; all other types are captured from the "a(n) &lt;type&gt; counter" pattern (Rule 122.1 — named counters). Target resolution order: 1. "from it" → ObjectReference.It() 2. "from this creature/permanent/artifact/enchantment" → ObjectReference.Self() 3. Named self-reference fallback (card name in text) → ObjectReference.Self()

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/RemoveCounterTriggeredRule.cs)

### `RemoveCountersTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"remove a +1/+1 counter from it at end of combat" — Phantom-creature counter-removal pattern (Judgment set). Fires as the resolution effect of an attacks-or-blocks triggered ability; "it" is the pronoun for the creature that declared as an attacker or blocker.

Rule 122.3: removing a counter from a permanent means taking one counter of the specified type off that permanent. Rule 511: the end of combat step is the last step of the combat phase; delayed removal effects scheduled "at end of combat" resolve here.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/RemoveCountersTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ReturnEnchantedOnDeathTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"return that card to the battlefield under your control" — the Animate Dead / Unhallowed Pact pattern. This effect appears as the resolution clause of a "When enchanted creature dies" trigger on Auras that reanimate their host. "That card" is a pronoun back-reference (Rule 113.8b) to the enchanted creature that just moved from the battlefield to the graveyard. MAST models it as `It` — the generic anaphoric reference kind ("it", "that card", "the creature" all denote the same game object). The "under your control" clause is captured in `UnderControl = You` on the `ReturnToBattlefieldEffect`. Rule 400.6: an object that returns to the battlefield under a specific player's control enters under that player's control as a replacement on zone-change resolution. Variant: "return it to its owner's hand" is handled by `ReturnSelfToHandOnDeathTriggeredRule` — the effect text is identical regardless of whether the trigger subject is "this Aura" or "enchanted creature", so no separate rule is needed for the hand-return variant. Rule 303.4 (Aura attachment); Rule 700.4 ("dies"); Rule 400.6 (enters under control).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ReturnEnchantedOnDeathTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ReturnExiledCardOnLeaveTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"return the exiled card to the battlefield under its owner's control." — the LTB resolution clause of a CR 607.2 linked pair (Petravark): the card a prior linked exile ability put in exile is returned to the battlefield. "the exiled card" is NOT free text — it is a linked reference (CR 607.2). The returned object is identified by the `ExiledWith` reference (the same axis Azula's play-from-exile permission uses): a `Designated` card in the `Exile` zone exiled with this object (`ExiledWith = {Kind: Self}`). This is a reference, not a threaded runtime binding (ADR 0004 reference-not-resolution); the ETB exile ability and this LTB return ability are two separate triggered abilities, linked by CR 607.2. "under its owner's control" rides on `UnderControl` as an `Owner` reference (CR 400.6 — an object returning under a specific player's control enters under that control).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ReturnExiledCardOnLeaveTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ReturnInstantOrSorceryFromGraveyardRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"return target instant or sorcery card from your graveyard to your hand" Models the Izzet Chronarch / Archaeomancer family (Rule 701.9: Return). The oracle text always targets a card by type-disjunction ("instant or sorcery") in the controller's graveyard and moves it to the controller's hand. Zone = Graveyard encodes the source zone stated in oracle text; Controller = You encodes "your graveyard". CardTypes = ["instant", "sorcery"] encodes the disjunction (Rule 700.4). Priorities above the generic ReturnToHandRule (default 50) because this rule is more specific (graveyard source + type-disjunction shape).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ReturnInstantOrSorceryFromGraveyardRule.cs)

### `ReturnItToBattlefieldWithCounterRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"return it to the battlefield under its owner's control with a [type] counter on it" — the Luminous Broodmoth / Persist / Undying-style counter-on-return shape where the return and the counter placement are written as a single clause (CR 400.6 / CR 122.1).

This complements `ReturnSelfToBattlefieldUnderOwnerControlRule` (bare "return it … under its owner's control", no counter suffix). Priority is higher (71) so the more specific counter-bearing form is tried before the bare form.

The counter is encoded on `WithCounters` — the same field used by the Undying/Persist keyword expansions (UndyingKeyword, PersistKeyword) — so the interaction layer treats it identically. Counter type is lowercased to match the `CounterType` convention ("flying", "+1/+1", "-1/-1", "time", etc.).

CR 400.6 (enters under owner's control); CR 113.8b ("it" = the triggering object); CR 122.1 (counters); CR 702.9a (flying counter).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ReturnItToBattlefieldWithCounterRule.cs)

### `ReturnSelfFromGraveyardToBattlefieldRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"return this card from your graveyard to the battlefield" — graveyard self-reanimation as a triggered effect (Forsaken Miner, CR 700.13 / CR 603).

The source zone "your graveyard" is encoded on the Target's ObjectFilter.Zone axis (`Graveyard`) and Controller axis (`You`). The card re-enters the battlefield NOT tapped (no "tapped" qualifier); Tapped = false.

This is the un-tapped counterpart to the activated-ability rule `ReturnSelfFromGraveyardToBattlefieldEffectRule` (which handles the tapped form). The regex is fully anchored (^…$) so it cannot match inside a longer effect clause.

CR 113.6 example (Reassembling Skeleton): an ability on a card in the graveyard that returns the card to the battlefield functions from that zone. This rule models the equivalent triggered-effect form.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ReturnSelfFromGraveyardToBattlefieldRule.cs)

### `ReturnSelfFromGraveyardTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you may return this card from your graveyard to your hand" — graveyard-based self-retrieval triggered from the graveyard zone (Rule 603.6a: a triggered ability on a card in a graveyard functions from that zone). The Eidolon family (Dissension) is the canonical corpus for this pattern. The source zone (Graveyard) is encoded on the Target filter's Zone axis rather than on the ability itself — MAST describes what the oracle text says, not where the object resides at runtime (Rule 400 series). Example corpus patterns: "you may return this card from your graveyard to your hand"

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ReturnSelfFromGraveyardTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ReturnSelfToBattlefieldUnderOwnerControlRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"return it to the battlefield under its owner's control" — the Enduring Tenacity / Glimmer-creature dies-and-returns pattern. The returning permanent is described by the pronoun "it" (back-reference to the triggering object, CR 113.8b — "it" in an ability always refers to the object the trigger is about), and it re-enters under its owner's control.

"under its owner's control" rides on `UnderControl` as an `Owner` reference (CR 400.6 — an object that returns to the battlefield under a specific player's control enters under that player's control). The target is `It` (the triggering object), matching the Persist / Undying / Animate Dead family of return effects.

Distinct from `ReturnEnchantedOnDeathTriggeredRule` ("return that card to the battlefield under your control" — Aura reanimation where the controller gains control) and `ReturnExiledCardOnLeaveTriggeredRule` ("return the exiled card to the battlefield under its owner's control" — exile–return pairs). This rule handles the bare "return it … under its owner's control" form used for non-keyword self-return triggers.

CR 400.6 (enters under owner's control); CR 113.8b ("it" refers to the trigger subject).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ReturnSelfToBattlefieldUnderOwnerControlRule.cs)

### `ReturnSelfToHandOnDeathTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"return it to its owner's hand" — self-referential bounce that appears on Aura (and occasionally other) cards whose death trigger returns themselves to hand. The pronoun "it" back-references the triggering object named in the trigger condition ("this Aura", "this artifact", etc.). There is no formal target (Rule 115.1 — "target" keyword absent); the card simply moves zone-change without targeting. Example corpus patterns: "return it to its owner's hand"

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ReturnSelfToHandOnDeathTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ReturnThisToHandTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"return this [type] to its owner's hand" — a self-referential bounce where the card names its own type word ("this enchantment", "this creature", "this artifact"...) to refer to itself. Resolves to `Self` — the card moving its own zone with no target (Rule 115.1 — "target" keyword absent, so no target is chosen). Higher priority than `ReturnToHandRule` so the self-reference "this [type]" is recognised as Self rather than mis-read as an indefinite "a [type] you control" choice (ObjectReferenceKind.Any). The "this [object]" phrasing is the oracle convention for a card referring to itself; modelling it as Self keeps the AST descriptively faithful to what the text says. Example corpus pattern: "return this enchantment to its owner's hand"

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ReturnThisToHandTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ReturnToHandRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"return ... to its owner's hand" — type-disjunction, you-control, optional "you may"/"up to N", and non-prefix qualifiers (e.g. "nonland permanent", "nontoken creature").

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ReturnToHandRule.cs)

### `ReturnToHandWithIfYouDoGainLifeRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

Niambi shape: "you may return ... to its owner's hand. If you do, you gain life equal to that creature's mana value." Priority 80: must override `ReturnToHandRule` so the IfYouDo continuation isn't dropped.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ReturnToHandWithIfYouDoGainLifeRule.cs)

### `RingTemptsTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"the Ring tempts you." — the Rule 701.54 keyword action on the triggered side. Matches the bare keyword action when it is the entire effect body of a triggered ability (e.g. Uruk-hai Berserker's ETB trigger). CR 701.54a: "Certain spells and abilities have the text 'the Ring tempts you.' Each time the Ring tempts you, choose a creature you control. That creature becomes your Ring-bearer until another creature becomes your Ring-bearer or another player gains control of it." REUSE-ONLY emits the existing `RingTemptsEffect` node.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/RingTemptsTriggeredRule.cs)

### `RollDieTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"roll a dN" — emits a `RollDieEffect` for the canonical die-roll instruction (CR 706.1).

CR 706.1: "An effect that instructs a player to roll a die will specify what kind of die to roll and how many of those dice to roll." CR 706.1a: the die has N equally likely outcomes numbered 1 to N. This rule handles the single-die form ("roll a d20") without a results table (CR 706.4).

ANCHORED (`^…$`): "roll a d20" is a short phrase that could appear as a substring of a longer instruction (e.g. a results-table card that also says "roll a d20 and consult …"). Anchoring prevents this rule from matching inside a more-specific sibling that the corpus would need its own rule for. Priority 60: above the generic fallback (default 50) and below other combat- damage specialisations (70+).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/RollDieTriggeredRule.cs)

### `SacrificeAnotherCreatureDrawCardRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you may sacrifice another creature. If you do, draw a card." — optional sacrifice-a-different-creature then draw (Sephiroth, Fabled SOLDIER ability 1).

Decomposes as an `OptionalEffect`:

`Inner` — `SacrificeEffect` targeting any creature the controller controls other than the source (ExcludeSelf = true, Kind = `Any`). "Another creature" in oracle text is the standard self-exclusion phrasing (CR 201.5 — the name or "this" refers to the source; "another" excludes it). The sacrifice is a player choice at resolution, not a target (CR 115.1 — "target" keyword only), so Kind = Any not Target. `IfYouDo` — `DrawCardsEffect` for exactly one card (CR 120.1 — "draw a card" = one card from the top of the library). Player = `You`.

Priority 95 — must run BEFORE `DrawCardsTriggeredRule` (priority 50), which matches any text containing "draw a card" and would fire on the full "you may sacrifice … If you do, draw a card" text first (producing an incorrect OptionalEffect wrapping only the draw). This rule is anchored (`^…$`) so it cannot fire on a sibling draw-only clause.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SacrificeAnotherCreatureDrawCardRule.cs)

### `SacrificeAnyCreatureTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"sacrifice a creature" — triggered player-choice sacrifice. The controller picks any creature they control at resolution; there is no formal targeting declaration (Rule 115.1 — only the "target" keyword creates a target), so the reference kind is `Any`. Covers the classic upkeep-tax pattern used on cards like Necrite (FEM/ME2). Distinct from `SacrificeTriggeredRule`, which handles the pronoun forms "sacrifice it / this creature / this permanent" where the object to sacrifice is already determined by the trigger context.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SacrificeAnyCreatureTriggeredRule.cs)

### `SacrificeAnyNumberOtherCreaturesDrawThatManyRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you may sacrifice any number of other creatures. If you do, draw that many cards." — optional mass-sacrifice-then-draw-that-many (Sephiroth, One-Winged Angel ability 3).

Decomposes as an `OptionalEffect`:

`Inner` — `SacrificeEffect` targeting any number of other creatures. "Any number of other creatures" → Kind = `Any`, Filter.ExcludeSelf = true (the card's "other" clause, CR 201.5 self-exclusion), Count = `AnyAmountQuantity` (the "any number of" upper-unbounded player choice, CR 107.3). The sacrifice is a player choice, not a target (CR 115.1 — no "target" keyword). `IfYouDo` — `DrawCardsEffect` for "that many" cards. "That many" is an anaphoric reference to the number of creatures sacrificed in the Inner — backed by `Other` (the sacrificed- creature count is not a named `DerivedKind` variant; Other is the structured residual per ADR 0001). Player = `You`.

Priority 95 — must run BEFORE `YouDrawThatManyCardsTriggeredRule` (default priority 50) and `DrawCardsTriggeredRule` (50), which would fire on the "draw that many cards" tail and produce an incorrect shape. ANCHORED (`^…$`) so it cannot fire on a standalone "draw that many cards" clause.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SacrificeAnyNumberOtherCreaturesDrawThatManyRule.cs)

### `SacrificeAtEndStepTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

Recognises the delayed-sacrifice sentence within a triggered ability effect body:

"Sacrifice it at the beginning of the next end step."

This sentence appears as a trailing clause in multi-sentence triggered ability effects — typically after a token-creation effect. For example: "Whenever this creature attacks, create a 1/1 red Goblin creature token. Sacrifice it at the beginning of the next end step." The pronoun "it" refers to the token or creature created/obtained by the preceding clause (Rule 109.2 — game objects). MAST records this as a `SacrificeEffect` whose `Target` is `It` and whose `Duration` is `AtBeginningOfNextEndStepDuration`. Descriptive, not engine-executable (descriptive-not-engine doctrine). The trigger timing semantics are carried by the duration node. Priority 70: must supersede `SacrificeTriggeredRule` (plain "sacrifice it") so the more-specific duration-bearing form fires first. Rule citations: 701.21 (Sacrifice), 513 (End Step), 603.7 (delayed triggers).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SacrificeAtEndStepTriggeredRule.cs)

### `SacrificeConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever you sacrifice a [subtype]" — player-sacrifice trigger (Rule 701.21 Sacrifice; Rule 603). Handles oracle text of the form "Whenever you sacrifice a [Subtype]", where [Subtype] is a named permanent subtype such as Food, Treasure, Clue, or Blood. The filter carries the sacrificed object's subtype and the controller (You), so "you sacrifice a Food" maps to TriggerEvent.Sacrifices + Filter{ Subtypes:["Food"], Controller:You }. CR 701.21a: "To sacrifice a permanent, its controller moves it from the battlefield directly to its owner's graveyard. A player can't sacrifice something that isn't a permanent, or something that's a permanent they don't control." CR 603.2: "Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability's trigger event, that ability automatically triggers."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SacrificeConditionRule.cs)

### `SacrificeItAtEndStepUnlessPayEnergyEqualToManaValueRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"At the beginning of the next end step, sacrifice that token unless you pay an amount of {E} equal to its mana value." — Satya, Aetherflux Genius's delayed-trigger energy-buyback pattern (CR 603.7: delayed triggered ability; CR 107.14: energy counters; CR 701.21: sacrifice).

Oracle structure: this sentence encodes a `CreateDelayedTriggerEffect` that schedules a one-shot trigger at the beginning of the next end step. When that trigger resolves its controller must either pay energy equal to the token's mana value or sacrifice it. Modelled as `PreventableEffect` wrapping `SacrificeEffect` with an `UnlessClause` whose cost is `PayEnergyCost` with a `DerivedQuantity` referencing `ManaValue` (the mana value of the token = "it").

"That token" / "it" — both pronouns refer to the token created by the preceding copy-effect sentence in the same triggered ability. MAST models this as `It` (the conventional back-reference pronoun, matching `SacrificeAtEndStepTriggeredRule` and `SacrificeUnlessPayTriggeredRule`).

ANCHORED (^…$): the "at the beginning of the next end step" phrase could match inside a broader sentence on a sibling trigger, so the anchor prevents substring collisions. Priority 73 — above `SacrificeAtEndStepTriggeredRule` (70) so the more-specific energy-buyback form is claimed here first.

Rule citations: CR 603.7 (delayed triggered ability), CR 107.14 (energy symbol), CR 513 (end step), CR 701.21 (sacrifice), CR 117.7 (unless clause).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SacrificeItAtEndStepUnlessPayEnergyEqualToManaValueRule.cs)

### `SacrificeOneOrMoreSubtypeConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever you sacrifice one or more [Subtype]s" — player-sacrifice trigger for a batch of named-subtype permanents (Rule 701.21; Rule 603).

Handles oracle text of the form "Whenever you sacrifice one or more [Subtype]s,", where [Subtype] is a named permanent subtype (e.g. Food, Treasure, Clue). The "one or more" qualifier sets `MinimumCount` = 1, matching the CR convention that the ability fires on any positive-count sacrifice event regardless of exact quantity. The filter carries the sacrificed object's subtype and the controller (You), so "you sacrifice one or more Foods" maps to `Sacrifices` + Filter{ Subtypes:["Food"], Controller:You } with MinimumCount = 1.

Distinct from `SacrificeConditionRule` which handles the singular "you sacrifice a [Subtype]" form (no MinimumCount qualifier). Both rules share the same TriggerEvent; the MinimumCount distinguishes "one or more" (batch) from the singular form (any single instance).

CR 701.21a: "To sacrifice a permanent, its controller moves it from the battlefield directly to its owner's graveyard." CR 603.2: "Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability's trigger event, that ability automatically triggers."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SacrificeOneOrMoreSubtypeConditionRule.cs)

### `SacrificeSelfTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"sacrifice this [enchantment|artifact|land|sorcery|instant|planeswalker|…]" — a self-sacrifice effect where the oracle text names the source's own card type (e.g. "sacrifice this enchantment") rather than the generic "sacrifice this permanent" form. Maps to a `SacrificeEffect` with `Self`.

Rule 701.21a: "To sacrifice a permanent, its controller moves it from the battlefield directly to its owner's graveyard." MAST records the target as Self (the source of the ability); the engine performs the move.

This rule supersedes any broader "sacrifice this permanent" fallback by Priority = 65 (above the default 50 but below the 70 used by the `SacrificeAtEndStepTriggeredRule` which handles the delayed form). Examples: Underworld Breach (THB): "sacrifice this enchantment."

Rule citations: 701.21a (Sacrifice), 201.4 (self-name reference).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SacrificeSelfTriggeredRule.cs)

### `SacrificeSelfUnlessPayRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"sacrifice this creature unless you pay {COST}" — the upkeep-tax pattern on creature cards (Rule 701.21a — Sacrifice; Rule 117.7 — unless clause).

Oracle text split by `TriggeredAbilityParser`: trigger = "At the beginning of your upkeep" effect = "sacrifice this creature unless you pay {B}"

The pronoun form "this creature" is a self-reference (Rule 109.2): the creature bearing the ability is the object that would be sacrificed. MAST models this as `Self`, distinct from the "sacrifice it" ETB-land pattern handled by `SacrificeUnlessPayTriggeredRule` which uses `It`.

Produces a `SacrificeEffect` with Target = Self and an `UnlessClause` whose Player is You and whose Cost is the parsed mana expression.

Representative cards: Whipstitched Zombie (NEM), Wild Leotau (CON), Spindrift Drake (WTH), Molting Harpy (UDS). Rule citations: 701.21 (Sacrifice), 117.7 (unless clause).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SacrificeSelfUnlessPayRule.cs)

### `SacrificeSelfUnlessSacrificePermanentRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"sacrifice [this creature / this permanent / CardName] unless you sacrifice a [type]" — the upkeep-tax pattern where the cost to prevent self-sacrifice is sacrificing another permanent of a given type (Rule 701.21a — Sacrifice; Rule 117.7 — unless clause).

Oracle text split by `TriggeredAbilityParser`: trigger = "At the beginning of your upkeep" effect = "sacrifice [this creature|The Gitrog Monster] unless you sacrifice a land"

The self-reference ("this creature", "this permanent", or a named card like "The Gitrog Monster") is the source object itself (CR 201.4 / CR 109.2) — MAST models this as `Self`. The preventive cost is a `SacrificeCost` whose Filter restricts to the stated permanent type.

Representative cards: The Gitrog Monster (SOI), Yavimaya Ants (ALL). Rule citations: 701.21 (Sacrifice), 117.7 (unless clause), 201.4 (self-name reference).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SacrificeSelfUnlessSacrificePermanentRule.cs)

### `SacrificeTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"sacrifice it" — triggered self-sacrifice on the creature that fired the trigger (Rule 701.21a — Sacrifice). The pronoun "it" refers back to the trigger subject and maps to `It`, matching the pronoun- reference convention used elsewhere in triggered rules.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SacrificeTriggeredRule.cs)

### `SacrificeUnlessPayTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"sacrifice it unless you pay {COST}" — the Karoo/bounce-land ETB sacrifice pattern (Rule 701.21a — Sacrifice; Rule 117.7 — unless clause).

Oracle text split by `TriggeredAbilityParser`: trigger = "When this land enters" effect = "sacrifice it unless you pay {1}"

Produces a `SacrificeEffect` with Target = It and an `UnlessClause` whose Player is You and whose Cost is the parsed mana expression. "It" refers back to the land named as the trigger subject — matching the pronoun-reference convention used in `SacrificeTriggeredRule`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SacrificeUnlessPayTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ScryOrSurveilConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever you scry or surveil" — fires on either keyword action (Rule 701.18 / 701.43).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ScryOrSurveilConditionRule.cs)

### `ScryTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"scry N." — Rule 701.18 keyword action on the triggered side.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ScryTriggeredRule.cs)

### `SearchBasicLandTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you may search your library for a basic land card, [reveal it,] put [it|that card] onto the battlefield tapped / into your hand, then shuffle." Covers the ETB-search-land pattern (Rule 701.23: Search). Two destination variants: - put it into your hand (Destination=Hand, Revealed=true) - put that card onto the battlefield tapped (Destination=BattlefieldTapped, Revealed=false)

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SearchBasicLandTriggeredRule.cs)

### `SearchForNamedCardTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you may search your library [and/or graveyard] for a card named [Name], reveal it, [and] put it into your hand. [If you search your library this way, shuffle.]" Covers the ETB named-card tutor pattern (Rule 701.23: Search), e.g. Sorin's Guide. Distinct from `SearchLibraryToHandTriggeredRule` in two ways the older rule cannot express: 1. The search target is pinned by exact name ("a card named X") rather than a card-type/subtype category — recorded on `Name`. 2. The search may span multiple source zones ("library and/or graveyard") — recorded on `Sources`. A library-only search leaves Sources null (the default Rule 701.23 case). The "you may" prefix is optional; its presence sets IsOptional=true. The reveal clause sets Revealed=true. The trailing conditional-shuffle sentence ("If you search your library this way, shuffle.") is rules-inferred bookkeeping and is folded into the effect rather than modelled separately — the same convention SearchLibraryToHandTriggeredRule uses for its "then shuffle" tail. Priority 70 (above SearchLibraryToHandTriggeredRule's 60) so the named/ multi-zone shape is recognised before the generic category-tutor rule.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SearchForNamedCardTriggeredRule.cs)

### `SearchLibraryToBattlefieldTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you may search your library for a[n] &lt;filter&gt; card, put it onto the battlefield, then shuffle." — optional triggered tutor directly to the battlefield.

Handles the pattern found on Godo, Bandit Warlord and similar ETB tutors that place the found card directly onto the battlefield (not into the hand or top of library). The "you may" prefix is optional; its presence wraps the effect in `OptionalEffect`.

Maps to a `CompositeEffect` containing:

`SearchLibraryEffect` with Destination = `Battlefield` (or `BattlefieldTapped` when "tapped" is present). `ShuffleEffect` for the mandatory library shuffle.

CR 701.23 (Search); CR 701.26 (Tap and Untap for tapped variant).

Priority 62 — above the generic Hand-destination rule (Priority 60) so this battlefield-specific rule is tried before the hand rule, and the two cannot cross-match because their destination clauses are anchored differently.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SearchLibraryToBattlefieldTriggeredRule.cs)

### `SearchLibraryToHandTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you may search your library for a [filter] card, reveal it, put it into your hand, then shuffle." Handles the non-basic-land ETB tutor pattern (Rule 701.23: Search). Supports three filter shapes: 1. Plain card type — "a creature card", "an artifact card", etc. 2. Subtype (tribal) — "an Elf card", "a Goblin card", etc. 3. Card type + mana value comparison — "a creature card with mana value 6 or greater" The "you may" prefix is optional; its presence sets IsOptional=true. Only the Hand destination is covered here (reveal → hand → shuffle). The SearchBasicLandTriggeredRule handles basic-land searches at higher priority. Priority 60 (above default 50) so this is tried before generic fallback rules but after any higher-priority specialised rules.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SearchLibraryToHandTriggeredRule.cs)

### `SearchLibraryToTopTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you may search your library for a [filter] card, reveal it, then shuffle and put that card on top." The ETB tutor-to-top pattern (the Lorwyn "Harbinger" cycle and kin), e.g. Boggart Harbinger ("a Goblin card") and Dune Mover ("a basic land card"). Distinct from the existing ETB tutor rules (`SearchLibraryToHandTriggeredRule`, `SearchBasicLandTriggeredRule`) only in its destination: the found card goes on top of the library (`TopOfLibrary`) rather than into the hand or onto the battlefield. The spell-resolution analogue is `SearchLibraryToTopRule`. Rule 701.23a (Search). The "you may" prefix is optional; its presence sets IsOptional=true via `Optional`. The reveal clause sets Revealed=true. The "shuffle and put that card on top" tail is folded into the effect (Destination=TopOfLibrary) rather than modelled separately — the same convention every sibling tutor rule uses for its shuffle clause. The filter group is partitioned into Supertypes / CardTypes / Subtypes the same way `SearchLibraryToTopRule` partitions it, so "basic land" → Supertypes=[Basic] CardTypes=[land] and a bare subtype word ("Goblin") → Subtypes=[Goblin].

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SearchLibraryToTopTriggeredRule.cs)

### `SelfDealsDamageToAnyTargetTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"it deals N damage to any target." — ETB self-ping targeting any legal target (creature, player, or planeswalker). Covers cards whose triggered ETB ability reads "When this [creature|artifact] enters, it deals N damage to any target." Rule 603: triggered abilities (When/Whenever/At). Rule 120.1-120.2: dealing damage (a source deals damage to a permanent/player). Rule 115.4: "any target" — a target may be any player, creature, planeswalker, or battle unless otherwise restricted. Distinct from `SelfDealsDamageToYouRule` (targets the controller only) and the spell-side `SelfDealsDamageToAnyTargetRule` (subject is the spell itself, not a triggered pronoun).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SelfDealsDamageToAnyTargetTriggeredRule.cs)

### `SelfDealsDamageToEachCreatureAndPlaneswalkerRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

Triggered board-sweep: "[it] deals N damage to each creature and each planeswalker." — the Siege-ETB sweeper (Invasion of Karsus). The source fires N damage simultaneously into both the creature population and the planeswalker population.

CR 120.1: "Objects can deal damage to battles, creatures, planeswalkers, and players. … An object that deals damage is the source of that damage." Each population is a distinct `DealDamageEffect` targeting `Each` filtered to the respective card type; the two are bundled in a `CompositeEffect` mirroring the spell-side "each creature and each player" sweeper (`DealDamageToEachRule`).

Triggered-side companion to that spell-side rule — the ETB self-reference is "it" (the permanent named by the trigger condition) rather than the substituted card name, so `It` is the source.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SelfDealsDamageToEachCreatureAndPlaneswalkerRule.cs)

### `SelfDealsDamageToEachOpponentTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

Triggered self-as-source spread: "this creature deals N damage to each opponent." — the cast-trigger drain on Refraction Elemental ("Whenever you cast a spell, this creature deals 2 damage to each opponent.").

CR 603.2: "Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability's trigger event, that ability automatically triggers." (Here the trigger is the spell-cast event, parsed by the condition side.) CR 120.1: "Objects can deal damage to … players. … An object that deals damage is the source of that damage." The source is the permanent itself ("this creature" → `Self`); the target population is every opponent (`EachOpponent`), dealt N simultaneously.

Triggered-side companion of the activated/spell each-opponent damage rules (`SelfDealsDamageToEachOpponentEffectRule`); the effect node is identical, only the dispatch context differs.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SelfDealsDamageToEachOpponentTriggeredRule.cs)

### `SelfDealsDamageToOpponentRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"it deals N damage to target opponent." — ETB self-ping targeting a single opponent. Covers cards whose triggered ETB ability reads "When this [land|creature|artifact] enters, it deals N damage to target opponent." Rule 603: triggered abilities (When/Whenever/At). Rule 120.1-120.2: dealing damage (a source deals damage to a player). Rule 115.1: "target" creates a target. Rule 102.2: an opponent is a player not on the same team as the controller. Distinct from `SelfDealsDamageToAnyTargetTriggeredRule` (which targets any legal target — creature, player, or planeswalker — per Rule 115.4) and from `SelfDealsDamageToYouRule` (which targets the controller).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SelfDealsDamageToOpponentRule.cs)

### `SelfDealsDamageToThatPlayerRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"this creature deals N damage to them" — the effect clause of an upkeep trigger whose recipient is the player named by the trigger condition (Prickle Faeries: "At the beginning of each opponent's upkeep, …, this creature deals 2 damage to them"). "Them" is the singular pronoun for that opponent — the player whose upkeep fired the trigger — and maps to `ThatPlayer` (the same back-reference `ThatPlayerLosesLifeRule` uses). The source is the card itself, `Self`.

CR 603.2: the event-match (the opponent's upkeep beginning) is the trigger. The damage is dealt by the source to that player (CR 120.1-120.2). Distinct from `SelfDealsDamageToYouRule` (recipient = the controller) and `SelfDealsDamageToOpponentRule` ("it deals … to target opponent", a fresh targeted opponent rather than the trigger's named player).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SelfDealsDamageToThatPlayerRule.cs)

### `SelfDealsDamageToYouRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"it deals N damage to you" / "this creature deals N damage to you" — reflexive damage.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SelfDealsDamageToYouRule.cs)

### `SelfOrAnotherArtifactEntersConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"[CardName] or another artifact you control enters" — self-by-name disjunction enters trigger on artifacts. The oracle pattern "X or another artifact you control enters" means the trigger fires when ANY artifact the controller controls enters — including the source card itself (named in the "X" half) and any other artifact.

This is the artifact-type analogue of the creature disjunction handled in `ParseObjectFilter` (the Blood Artist shape: "this creature or another creature"). The correct filter is artifact + Controller=You, with NO ExcludeSelf (the disjunction explicitly includes the source). Setting ExcludeSelf = true would be rules-wrong: the card itself does trigger the ability when it enters.

Example: Gonti's Aether Heart — "Whenever Gonti's Aether Heart or another artifact you control enters, you get {E}{E}."

Rule 603.1: triggered abilities use "when", "whenever", or "at" to watch for events. Rule 603.2: the trigger fires each time the condition is met. Rule 107.14: energy counters ({E}) are a player resource.

ANCHOR: the pattern is anchored (^...$) and requires "or another artifact you control" to prevent collision with the generic EntersConditionRule on simpler artifact triggers. Runs at Priority 992 — above EntersConditionRule (990) and AnotherSubtypeEntersConditionRule (995), below the most-specific rules.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SelfOrAnotherArtifactEntersConditionRule.cs)

### `SetCardTypesTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"It's an enchantment." / "It's a creature." / "It's a land." — a layer-4 (CR 613.1d) card-type declaration sentence that sets the subject's card types. Typically follows a return-to-battlefield sentence to declare the returned permanent's new type (e.g. Enduring Tenacity: "… return it to the battlefield under its owner's control. It's an enchantment.").

"It" is the generic anaphoric pronoun (CR 113.8b) back-referencing the triggering object — the same object named by the preceding effect sentence. MAST models it as `It` on the `Subject` field.

CR 613.1d: "Layer 4: Type-changing effects are applied." An "It's an X" oracle sentence sets the object's card types to the declared set, removing any types not listed (e.g. removing the creature type from a creature/enchantment). The parenthetical "(It's not a creature.)" is a clarifying reminder printed by WotC for card types that lose creature status; it is not a separate effect and is stripped by the reminder-extraction pass before this rule fires. This rule handles the imperative declaration that remains.

CR 205.2 (card types list); CR 613.1d (type-changing layers); CR 113.8b ("it" pronoun reference).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SetCardTypesTriggeredRule.cs)

### `SourceDealsNoncombatDamageConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever a source you control deals noncombat damage to an opponent" — noncombat-damage trigger gated to sources the controller owns (CR 609.7: a source is any object, spell, or ability that deals damage; CR 510.1: combat damage is dealt only in the Combat Damage Step). Emits `NoncombatDamageDealt`.

The filter records `CardTypes = ["source"]` with `Controller = You`: "source" is the CR 609.7 rules term for any damage-dealing entity that a player controls, modelled as a CardTypes singleton so it is structurally parallel to "a creature you control" and "a permanent you control" filters.

Runs at priority 986 — above `DealsCombatDamageConditionRule` (985) and `DealsDamageConditionRule` (984) — so "deals noncombat damage" is claimed before those rules consume the more-general "deals damage" surface.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SourceDealsNoncombatDamageConditionRule.cs)

### `SpellCastConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever you cast a spell" / "Whenever an opponent casts a spell" / "Whenever you cast a spell that's white, blue, black, or red" / etc. Encodes the caster and any inline spell-color/type qualifiers on the filter.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SpellCastConditionRule.cs)

### `SubtypeEntersConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"a [Subtype] you control enters" — a subtype-filtered enters trigger keyed on the indefinite article "a"/"an" rather than "another". The triggering object is some OTHER permanent (not this card) that shares the named subtype; the subtype is the most precise type word the oracle text gives, so the filter carries it on the Subtypes axis with no CardTypes constraint. Rule 603.6a: enters-the-battlefield abilities trigger when a permanent enters the battlefield, written "When [this object] enters, ..." or "Whenever a [type] enters, ...". Rule 603.2: when a game event matches a triggered ability's trigger event, that ability automatically triggers. This is distinct from a this-object ETB trigger (handled by `EntersConditionRule` via the "this [type]" self-reference) and from the creature-subtype "another [Subtype] you control enters" shape (`AnotherSubtypeEntersConditionRule`, which keys on "another" and pins CardTypes to creature). Here the subtype may be any permanent subtype — e.g. the Aura subtype Cartouche — so no card-type is asserted. Examples: "When a Cartouche you control enters, ..." Sits just below `EntersConditionRule` (Priority 990) so the generic enters recogniser gets first refusal on plainer shapes ("a creature you control enters", "this enchantment enters"); this rule only fires once that generic recogniser has declined, then narrows on the proper- noun subtype.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SubtypeEntersConditionRule.cs)

### `SubtypeListPumpTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"[SubtypeA], [SubtypeB], [SubtypeC], and [SubtypeD] you control get +N/+N until end of turn." Handles the multi-subtype tribal pump pattern: a comma-and-"and"-joined list of creature subtypes that the controller's creatures of those types get a temporary P/T boost. The "you control" controller filter is required. Covers both two-item ("[A] and [B] you control") and three-or-more-item ("[A], [B], and [C] you control") forms.

The subtypes list uses OR semantics on the `Subtypes` axis (a creature qualifies if it has ANY of the listed subtypes — CR 205.3m). The boost is recorded as a `ModifyPTEffect` with `Duration` = `untilTime{Turn, End}`.

Rule 613.1c (Layer 6, ability-granting effects) and 613.4c (Layer 7c, P/T-modification effects): temporary boosts are layer-7c continuous effects. CR 205.3m: creature subtypes list.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SubtypeListPumpTriggeredRule.cs)

### `SubtypePutIntoGraveyardConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

Recognises the "a [Subtype] is put into a graveyard from the battlefield" trigger condition — the longform "dies" trigger (CR 700.4) qualified by an artifact subtype rather than a card type (e.g. "Food", "Treasure", "Blood", "Clue").

CR 700.4 (verbatim): "The word 'dies' means 'is put into a graveyard from the battlefield.'" Subtype-qualified dies triggers fire on the same event as creature dies triggers, but the filter names an artifact subtype (e.g. Food, CR 111.10b) rather than the card type "creature". MAST maps to `Dies` because the zone-change is identical; the filter carries `Subtypes` to record the named subtype faithfully.

Canonical card: Ygra, Eater of All (BLB) — "Whenever a Food is put into a graveyard from the battlefield, put two +1/+1 counters on Ygra." CR 700.4 (Dies = put into graveyard from battlefield). CR 111.10b (Food token is a colorless Food artifact token).

Priority 993 — above `DiesConditionRule` (991) and above `NontokenCreatureToGraveyardFromBattlefieldConditionRule` (992) so this more-specific subtype-qualified shape fires before the generic "dies" fallback. Anchored pattern prevents substring matches against longer, more-specific siblings.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SubtypePutIntoGraveyardConditionRule.cs)

### `SubtypeSpellCastConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever you cast an Eldrazi spell" / "Whenever you cast a Zombie spell" — the subtype-qualified spell-cast condition family. The subtype is a creature subtype (Rule 205.3m), NOT a card type, so it lands on `Subtypes` while the card type remains `"spell"`.

Sits above `SpellCastConditionRule` (priority 998) so the subtype qualifier is captured first. `CastAuraSpellConditionRule` (priority 1001) handles the Aura enchantment-subtype specifically; this rule handles all other subtypes and sits between them at priority 1000.

CR 205.3m: subtypes appear after the em-dash on the type line and are distinct from the broad card types. "An Eldrazi spell" is a spell whose subtype includes "Eldrazi".

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SubtypeSpellCastConditionRule.cs)

### `SupportTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"support N" — Rule 701.41 keyword action on the triggered side.

Rule 701.41a: "\"Support N\" on a permanent means \"Put a +1/+1 counter on each of up to N other target creatures.\"" This rule covers the permanent form (the "other" variant); a permanent's "support N" never targets itself.

The keyword action is unpacked into its literal meaning per the rule (MAST describes what the keyword says, not how it resolves): a single +1/+1 counter placed on each of an up-to-N choice of OTHER target creatures. The "up to N" is the multiplicity of the target reference (Rule 115.1 — "up to N target"), not the per-target counter count, which is one. Hence `Count` is the literal 1 and the "up to N" lives on the reference's `Quantity`.

The reminder text "(Put a +1/+1 counter on each of up to two other target creatures.)" has already been stripped by the dispatcher before this rule is called, so the matched fragment is the bare "support N".

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SupportTriggeredRule.cs)

### `SurveilTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"surveil N." and "you may surveil N." — Rule 701.42 keyword action on the triggered side. The "you may" prefix produces an optional `SurveilEffect` (IsOptional = true).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SurveilTriggeredRule.cs)

### `SyrKonradTripleOrConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

Compound triple-OR trigger for Syr Konrad, the Grim (ELD): "Whenever another creature dies, or a creature card is put into a graveyard from anywhere other than the battlefield, or a creature card leaves your graveyard, …"

This is a single triggered ability with THREE disjoint firing events (CR 603.2: "Whenever a game event … matches a triggered ability's trigger event, that ability automatically triggers"):

Primary: another creature dies (Dies event, ExcludeSelf filter). Secondary: a creature card is put into a graveyard from anywhere other than the battlefield (PutIntoGraveyard event — covers hand/library/exile/stack origins; the "other than battlefield" restriction distinguishes it from the Dies clause which already covers battlefield→graveyard transitions via CR 700.4). Tertiary: a creature card leaves your graveyard (LeavesGraveyard event, Controller=You — covers cards removed from graveyard by any means).

The primary condition is returned normally; the secondary is signalled via `AdditionalTrigger`'s normal single-pending mechanism (piggy-backed on `PendingAdditionalTrigger` — we use our own static field here to avoid coupling). The tertiary is stored in `PendingAdditionalTriggers` so `TriggeredAbilityParser` can populate `AdditionalTriggers` (the list form introduced for 3+ events).

CR 700.4 (verbatim): "The term dies means 'is put into a graveyard from the battlefield.'" CR 603.2 (verbatim): "Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability's trigger event, that ability automatically triggers." CR 701.17a (verbatim, mill): "For a player to mill a number of cards, that player puts that many cards from the top of their library into their graveyard."

Priority 998 — must fire before the generic `DiesConditionRule` (991) or `PutIntoGraveyardConditionRule` (985) see the first clause in isolation.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SyrKonradTripleOrConditionRule.cs)

### `TakeExtraTurnTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"Take an extra turn after this one." — schedules an additional full turn for the ability's controller when used as a triggered effect.

CR 500.7: "Some effects can give a player extra turns. They do this by adding the turns directly after the specified turn." MAST records the verb and player reference; the turn-ordering bookkeeping is engine territory.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/TakeExtraTurnTriggeredRule.cs)

### `TakeInitiativeRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you take the initiative." — Rule 726. The player takes the initiative game-state designation. Parameterless; mirrors AscendEffect doctrine.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/TakeInitiativeRule.cs)

### `TapAndFreezeBackReferenceTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

Single-sentence "tap then freeze" back-reference effect, as it appears on the resolution half of a triggered ability: "tap that creature and it doesn't untap during its controller's next untap step." (Kashi-Tribe Warriors — the combat-damage-to-a-creature trigger family).

Two conjoined instructions on one sentence: a `TapEffect` on the back-referenced creature ("that creature") and a `DoesntUntapEffect` on that same creature ("it"). Both back-references resolve to `It` — the creature the trigger condition just named (the one dealt combat damage). The pair is wrapped in a single `CompositeEffect`, matching the single-sentence-conjunction convention already used on the triggered side (`EtbTeamPumpTriggeredRule` for "get +N/+M and gain &lt;keyword&gt;").

CR 502.3 (Untap Step): "Third, the active player determines which permanents they control will untap. Then they untap them all simultaneously... Normally, all of a player's permanents untap, but effects can keep one or more of a player's permanents from untapping." The "next untap step" wording is recorded on `WhoseUntapStep` as "its controller's next".

Mirrors `TapAndFreezeRule` (the two-sentence spell form, "Tap target creature. It doesn't untap...") but covers the one-sentence "and"-joined back-reference form that surfaces in triggered-ability resolution text, where the creature is not re-targeted.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/TapAndFreezeBackReferenceTriggeredRule.cs)

### `TapEnchantedTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"tap enchanted [type]" — Aura enters-the-battlefield tap effect. Fires when an Aura's triggered ability instructs it to tap the enchanted permanent. The target is always the permanent the Aura is attached to, modelled as `EnchantedOrEquipped`. Rule 701.26 (Tap and Untap); Rule 303.4 (Aura attachment).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/TapEnchantedTriggeredRule.cs)

### `TapForManaConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

Trigger condition for "tap a [nonland] permanent for [mana/{C}]" shapes. Handles two variants:

Specific-symbol: "you tap a permanent for {C}" (Forsaken Monument) — the produced mana symbol is recorded in `ProducedMana`. Controller: You when "you tap"; Any when "a player taps". Any-mana: "you tap a nonland permanent for mana" (Kinnan, Bonder Prodigy) — the trigger fires on any mana production by tapping a nonland permanent. No specific symbol is recorded (`ProducedMana` is null). The nonland qualifier is expressed as `ExcludedCardTypes: ["land"]` on the filter.

Rule 605.1a: An activated ability is a mana ability if it could add mana to a player's mana pool when it resolves and it doesn't require a target. Tapping a permanent for mana is such an ability; this rule encodes the trigger that fires when that activation occurs.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/TapForManaConditionRule.cs)

### `TapUntapTargetTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

Triggered-ability rule for tap, untap, and tap-or-untap effects on a named target. Covers three surface patterns:

"tap target [filter]" → `TapEffect` "untap target [filter]" → `UntapEffect` "(you may) tap or untap target [filter]" → `TapOrUntapEffect`

The filter phrase may be a single card type ("creature") or a disjunction ("artifact or creature", "creature or land", "artifact, creature, or land"). Rule 701.26 (Tap and Untap).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/TapUntapTargetTriggeredRule.cs)

### `TargetOpponentDiscardsTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"target opponent discards [a/N] card[s]" as the effect clause of a triggered ability — the Invasion of Eldraine ETB shape ("When this Siege enters, target opponent discards two cards"). The discarder is a targeted opponent (CR 115.1 — "target" in oracle text creates a targeting requirement); the count is the literal quantity of cards moved from hand to graveyard (CR 701.9a: "To discard a card, move it from its owner's hand to that player's graveyard"). CR 603.2: the event-match (the Siege entering) is the trigger.

The spell-speed sibling for this surface is `DiscardTargetPlayerRule` ("Target opponent discards two cards." as a standalone spell effect); this rule is the triggered-side counterpart, dispatched after the trigger/effect split has peeled the "When this Siege enters," prefix. The all-opponents variant is `EachOpponentDiscardsRule`; this rule is the single-targeted-opponent shape, so the Player reference is `Target` filtered to "opponent" rather than `EachOpponent`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/TargetOpponentDiscardsTriggeredRule.cs)

### `TargetPlayerLoseAndYouGainLifeRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"target player loses N life and you gain N life" — Blood Artist / drain-on-death shape.

Distinct from `TryParseTargetOpponentLoseAndYouGainLife` (which requires "target opponent") — this rule handles the more general "target player" form, which allows targeting any player (including yourself). Blood Artist uses this phrasing because the drain applies to any chosen player, not constrained to an opponent.

The two effects (lose life, gain life) are wrapped in a `CompositeEffect` because `ITriggeredRule` returns a single `Effect`; the composite preserves both effects as structured children rather than collapsing to free text. CR 700.4: "The term dies means 'is put into a graveyard from the battlefield.'" CR 603.2 (triggered ability event matching).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/TargetPlayerLoseAndYouGainLifeRule.cs)

### `TeamGainKeywordTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"Creatures you control gain &lt;keyword&gt; until end of turn." — the bare team keyword-grant (no power/toughness modifier), as on the Rally/Ally rallying creatures (Chasm Guide, Ondu Champion).

Distinct from `EtbTeamPumpTriggeredRule`, which requires a leading "get +N/+M" P/T modifier. Here the whole resolution clause is a keyword grant: the entering creature grants the named keyword to the whole team for the turn. CR 611.1 — the grant is a continuous effect with a fixed duration (until end of turn).

The granted keyword is structured, not free text: each keyword resolves to a `GainAbilityEffect` whose `GainedAbility` is a `StaticAbility` carrying the keyword (mirroring the existing team-pump and modal grant golds — e.g. Flame-Kin Zealot, Savage Alliance). Multiple keywords joined by "and" each become their own grant effect, wrapped in a `CompositeEffect`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/TeamGainKeywordTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ThassasOracleRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

Recognises the Thassa's Oracle ETB effect: "Look at the top X cards of your library, where X is your devotion to blue.
