Put up to one of them on top of your library and the rest on the bottom of your library in a random order. If X is greater than or equal to the number of cards in your library, you win the game."

The whole three-sentence fragment is a single coherent game action: the variable X is defined in the first sentence and referenced by both the disposition sentence and the win-condition. Splitting on sentences would break the X-binding, so this rule captures all three sentences as one match and emits them as two structured effects:

`OracleTopLookEffect` — the look-and-reorder action. `ConditionalEffect` — the QuantityComparisonCondition → WinTheGameEffect gate.

CR 700.5 (devotion); CR 701.12 (look); CR 104.3a (you win the game).

Priority 95: must beat generic sentence-bundle dispatch and the plain LookAtTopCards rules.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ThassasOracleRule.cs)

### `ThatPlayerDrawsAdditionalCardTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"that player draws an additional card" — a draw-step trigger effect that makes the player whose draw step is occurring draw one extra card. "That player" is the pronoun for the trigger's named player (CR 603.2: "at the beginning of" fires for the active player; "that player" back-references whoever owns that step). The "additional" qualifier is oracle flavour; mechanically this is identical to drawing one card (CR 121.1: "A player draws a card by putting the top card of their library into their hand.").

Anchored (^…$) to prevent substring matches inside longer clauses. Distinct from `DrawCardsTriggeredRule` (which handles "draw a card" directed at the controller "you", not "that player") and `EachPlayerDrawsTriggeredRule` (which handles "each player draws a card").

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ThatPlayerDrawsAdditionalCardTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ThatPlayerLifeTotalBecomesRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"that player's life total becomes [N]" — sets the triggering player's life total to a fixed value.

Rule 119.5: "If an effect sets a player's life total to a specific number, the player gains or loses the necessary amount of life to end up with the new total." "That player" refers to the player identified by the trigger condition (CR 603.2); it maps to `ThatPlayer`.

Canonical use: Master of Cruelties — "Whenever this creature attacks a player and isn't blocked, that player's life total becomes 1." The effect reduces (or in unusual situations raises) the attacked player's life total to exactly 1.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ThatPlayerLifeTotalBecomesRule.cs)

### `ThatPlayerLosesLifeRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"that player loses N life" — symmetry-drain trigger on an opponent-controlled-ETB trigger (Suture Priest pattern). "That player" is the pronoun for the player whose action fired the trigger (Rule 109.5) and maps to `ThatPlayer`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ThatPlayerLosesLifeRule.cs)

### `ThatPlayerMayPayYouCreateTreasureRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

Smothering Tithe pattern: "that player may pay {COST}. If the player doesn't, you create a Treasure token." (CR 603 — triggered on opponent draw; CR 117.7 — the paying player may pay; the IfYouDoNot branch creates for you).

Decomposes as:

`OptionalEffect` — the "may pay" wrapper. `ConditionalPayEffect` as `Inner` — the cost "that player" may pay. `Player` is `ThatPlayer` (the opponent who drew the card, per the trigger condition). `CreateTokenEffect` as `IfYouDoNot` — "you create a Treasure token" when the opponent declines to pay. Player is You (the ability controller), Count = 1, Token = `Treasure()`.

ANCHORED (`^…$`): the full effect clause is matched so that no sibling "that player may pay" text in a different pattern is mis-labelled. Priority 91: must run BEFORE the generic `ConditionalPayTriggeredRule` (priority 80) and after the Rings-of-Brighthearth rule (priority 90), since all three open with a conditional-pay surface but differ in consequent shape.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ThatPlayerMayPayYouCreateTreasureRule.cs)

### `ThatPlayerMillsThatManyRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"that player mills that many cards" — the Mindcrank family: whenever an opponent loses life, that player mills that many cards.

The antecedent of "that many" is the life lost by the opponent in the triggering event (CR 119.3: "If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player's life total is adjusted accordingly."). The amount is encoded as a `DerivedQuantity` keyed on `LifeLost`. "That player" is a back-reference to the player identified by the `LosesLife` trigger's filter (CR 603.2: the trigger fires whenever the event matches), encoded as `ThatPlayer`.

CR 701.17 defines "mill" as a keyword action: "To mill N cards, a player puts the top N cards of their library into their graveyard." The player mills cards equal to the amount of life lost in the triggering event.

This rule carries NO `[TriggeredRule]` attribute — it must NOT enter the reflection-discovered generic effect pool. The identical surface text "that player mills that many cards" appears on combat-damage triggers (Crosstown Courier, Captain Nghathrod) where "that many" is the damage dealt (`DamageDealt`), not life lost. Dispatching this rule for any trigger event would mislabel those cards. Instead, `TriggeredAbilityParser` dispatches to it directly only when the resolved trigger event is `LosesLife` — mirroring `YouGainThatMuchLifeLostRule`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ThatPlayerMillsThatManyRule.cs)

### `TheyLoseLifeRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"they lose N life" — triggered life-loss where "they" is the player who performed the triggering action (CR 119.3: if an effect causes a player to lose life, that player's life total is adjusted accordingly; CR 603.2: the trigger fires whenever the event matches). "They" is a pronoun back-reference to the player identified by the trigger's filter (e.g. "an opponent draws a card, they lose N life") — that opponent is `ThatPlayer`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/TheyLoseLifeRule.cs)

### `ThisCreatureDealsDamageToAnyTargetTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"this creature deals N damage to any target" — self-ping triggered effect that targets any legal target (creature, player, planeswalker, or battle).

Covers cards like Orcish Bowmasters whose compound-trigger effect reads "this creature deals 1 damage to any target." — where the pronoun is "this creature" rather than "it" (the `SelfDealsDamageToAnyTargetTriggeredRule` pronoun). The source is the creature itself (CR 109); the target is any legal target (CR 115.4: "any target" — player, creature, planeswalker, or battle).

Rule 120.1–120.2: dealing damage (a source deals damage to a permanent or player). Rule 603.2: triggered abilities (When/Whenever/At).

Distinct from `SelfDealsDamageToAnyTargetTriggeredRule` (which matches the "it deals" pronoun form); both emit the same `DealDamageEffect` with `Self` as the source and `AnyTarget` as the target.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ThisCreatureDealsDamageToAnyTargetTriggeredRule.cs)

### `TransferCountersOnDeathTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"put its counters on target creature you control" — counter-transfer shape that moves all counters from the dying creature to a target controlled creature. The phrase "its counters" is an anaphoric reference to the triggering creature's current counter set (all types and amounts); MAST describes this as `CounterType = "all"` (the sentinel for "every counter type present on the source") with a `DerivedQuantity` source of "its", meaning the count is whatever is on the dying permanent rather than a fixed number (Rule 122.1 — counters are game objects; their quantity is a game state). Oracle text shape (after trigger/effect split): "put its counters on target creature you control" Example: "When Goobling dies, put its counters on target creature you control." (ONE) Priority 60: more specific than the generic `PutCountersTriggeredRule` (priority 50) which requires an explicit counter type ("a +1/+1 counter", "a charge counter", etc.) and would fall through on "its counters" anyway. The higher priority ensures this rule is tried first for the "its counters" surface without ambiguity.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/TransferCountersOnDeathTriggeredRule.cs)

### `TransformTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

Matches "transform this creature" / "transform this permanent" effect text. Classic Innistrad werewolves carry explicit transform triggered abilities, e.g.:

"At the beginning of each upkeep, if a player cast two or more spells last turn, transform this creature." (human-to-wolf) "At the beginning of each upkeep, if no spells were cast last turn, transform this creature." (wolf-to-human)

The "if [condition]" intervening-if clause is stripped by `TriggeredAbilityParser` before this rule receives the effect fragment. This rule handles the remaining "transform this creature" / "transform this permanent" text.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/TransformTriggeredRule.cs)

### `TransformsIntoConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"As this creature transforms into [Name]" — transform-into trigger condition. CR 603.6: an ability that uses "as [permanent] transforms into [Name]" fires when the permanent transforms; it is functionally a "When" trigger with timing simultaneous to the transformation event.

Produces `Transforms` with Filter carrying the source type and `IsSelf = true` (the object being transformed is the source itself, CR 201.5 — "this [type]" in oracle text is a self-reference). Timing is always `When` (the "As" keyword maps to When by CR 603.6; the caller supplies the timing having already detected the "As" prefix via the raw-text intercept in `TriggeredAbilityParser`).

ANCHORED (`^…`): the guard checks for the exact "As this (creature|permanent) transforms into" prefix so the rule cannot misfire on a sibling trigger.

Priority 999: transform-into triggers are highly specific and must run before any generic `Transforms` rule that might match on "transforms" alone.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/TransformsIntoConditionRule.cs)

### `TurnedFaceUpConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"When this creature is turned face up" — Rule 702.37 (Morph/Megamorph) face-up trigger. Fires when the face-down permanent's morph or megamorph cost is paid and the card becomes face up (Rule 702.37e/f). Subject is always "this creature".

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/TurnedFaceUpConditionRule.cs)

### `UntapAllTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"untap all [types] you control" — mass-untap triggered effect. Covers patterns such as:

"untap all lands you control" (Bear Umbra) "untap all creatures you control" "untap all permanents you control"

Produces an `UntapEffect` whose Target is `Each` filtered to the named card type with `You` when "you control" is present, or no controller filter otherwise (e.g. "untap all creatures"). Rule 701.26 (Tap and Untap).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/UntapAllTriggeredRule.cs)

### `UntapSelfAndAllSubtypeTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"untap it and all [Subtype] you control" — the compound untap form that untaps the source permanent (referred to by "it") AND every permanent of a named subtype the controller controls.

Produces a `CompositeEffect` containing two `UntapEffect` instances:

`untap it` — self-reference (`Self`) `untap all [Subtype] you control` — `Each` with `Controller` = `You` and the named subtype on `Subtypes`.

CR 701.26b: "To untap a permanent, rotate it back to the upright position from a sideways position. Only tapped permanents can be untapped."

Priority 55 — above the generic `UntapSelfRule` (Priority 50) and above `UntapAllTriggeredRule` (Priority 50) so this compound form is matched first. The pattern is anchored (^...$) so it cannot silently consume a text that only partially matches (e.g. "untap it" alone).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/UntapSelfAndAllSubtypeTriggeredRule.cs)

### `UntapSelfRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"untap this artifact" / "untap it" with optional gate forms: - "you may pay {X}. If you do, untap this artifact." — Mana Vault style (CR 602) - "you may untap this [type]" — Grinding Station style (CR 117.7 "you may")

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/UntapSelfRule.cs)

### `UntapThemTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"Untap them." — a trailing sentence in a multi-sentence triggered ability resolution that untaps a group of permanents named in the preceding sentence.

The pronoun "them" is a plural anaphoric back-reference (CR 109.2 — game objects) to the set of permanents named by the prior effect clause in the same triggered ability. Because MAST is descriptive, not imperative (ADR-0003), and `TryParseSentenceBundleEffects` dispatches each sentence independently, this rule models the untap target using the `It` pronoun reference — the universal anaphoric stand-in for "the set just named" (the distinction between singular "it" and plural "them" is a linguistic surface form; the reference kind is the same structured back-reference slot).

Rule 701.20 (Tap and Untap): "To untap a permanent, rotate it back to the upright position from a sideways position. Only tapped permanents can be untapped." Rule 109.2 (Objects): an object is a card, token, spell, or ability; pronouns in oracle text refer to objects in the resolution context.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/UntapThemTriggeredRule.cs)

### `UntapUpToNLandsTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"untap up to N lands" — bounded non-targeted land-untap triggered effect.

CR 603.6a: "Enters-the-battlefield abilities trigger when a permanent enters the battlefield. These are written, 'When [this object] enters, …'" — the trigger condition is handled by `EntersConditionRule`; this rule handles only the effect clause.

Examples: "untap up to five lands" (Peregrine Drake). The count "up to N" is an `UpToQuantity` on the target reference — the player-chosen, non-targeted set of lands (no "target" keyword in oracle, so `Any` rather than `Target`). No controller restriction is stated in the text, so none is applied. Rule 701.26 (Tap and Untap).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/UntapUpToNLandsTriggeredRule.cs)

### `WhenThisAuraLeavesSacrificeItDelayedRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

Recognises the "When this Aura leaves the battlefield, that creature's controller sacrifices it." delayed-trigger sentence produced by the Animate Dead / Dance of the Dead family.

This sentence appears as the second sentence in the complex ETB ability's effect body, AFTER the composite lose-gain-return-attach sentence. It creates a `CreateDelayedTriggerEffect` whose `DelayedTriggeredAbility` fires when this Aura leaves the battlefield (CR 603.7 — a delayed triggered ability created by a resolving effect).

The sacrifice target is the enchanted creature ("it" — the anaphoric reference to the creature previously returned to the battlefield by the preceding effects). "That creature's controller sacrifices it" — the sacrificer is that creature's controller, not necessarily the Aura's controller. MAST records the sacrifice effect descriptively via `SacrificeEffect` with `It`; the "controller" subject is engine-derived from the triggered ability's implicit actor (CR 603.3 — a triggered ability's controller is the player who controlled the ability's source when it triggered, but here oracle specifies the enchanted creature's controller — the engine resolves this per CR 303.4e). Descriptive, not engine-executable.

CR 702.5a (Enchant); CR 303.4 (Aura); CR 603.7 (delayed triggered abilities); CR 701.21 (Sacrifice).

This rule is tightly anchored (^...$) and keyed on the conjunction of "this Aura leaves the battlefield" + "controller sacrifices it" so it cannot match as a substring of a broader trigger or effect. Priority 95 ensures it fires before the generic `SacrificeTriggeredRule`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/WhenThisAuraLeavesSacrificeItDelayedRule.cs)

### `YouCreateTokenConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever you create a token" — token-creation trigger (CR 111.1; CR 603.2). Fires whenever the ability's controller creates one or more tokens. The filter carries Controller=You so that a "whenever any player creates a token" variant (if ever added) is distinguishable. The anchor is tight: ^you\s+create\s+a\s+token$ to avoid matching "you create a token for each…" compound forms (different shape) or occurrences embedded in a broader trigger body. CR 111.1: "Some effects put tokens onto the battlefield. A token is a marker used to represent any permanent that isn't represented by a card." CR 603.2: "Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability's trigger event, that ability automatically triggers."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/YouCreateTokenConditionRule.cs)

### `YouCycleThisCardConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"When you cycle this card" — the cycling trigger condition (CR 702.29c).

CR 702.29c (verbatim): "'When you cycle this card' means 'When you discard this card to pay an activation cost of a cycling ability.' These abilities trigger from whatever zone the card winds up in after it's cycled."

The trigger fires on `Cycled` with `IsSelf` = true, identifying that it is THIS card that is being cycled. Anchored check prevents matching broader discard or draw triggers.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/YouCycleThisCardConditionRule.cs)

### `YouDiscardCardConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever you discard a card" — discard-card trigger (CR 701.9a: "To discard a card, move it from its owner's hand to that player's graveyard.").

Fires whenever the controller discards a card by any means — from cost payment, from a discard effect, from a forced discard, etc. (CR 603.2: the trigger fires on the event, regardless of cause). The controller defaults to You.

CR 603: "Triggered abilities have a trigger condition and an effect. They are written as '[When/Whenever/At] [trigger condition or event], [effect].'" CR 701.9a defines the discard action. Event = DiscardsCard, Filter.Controller = You.

Anchored check prevents matching "an opponent discards a card" or other subject-prefixed discard forms — those route to `EachOpponentDiscardsRule` or `TargetOpponentDiscardsTriggeredRule`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/YouDiscardCardConditionRule.cs)

### `YouDrawThatManyCardsTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you draw that many cards" — draw effect where the count is derived from the triggering event (CR 120: damage dealing; "that many" is an anaphoric reference to the amount of damage dealt). Backed by `DamageDealt`.

Covers Niv-Mizzet, Visionary and similar cards whose trigger fires on damage dealt and whose effect draws cards equal to the damage amount.

CR 121.1: "A player draws a card by putting the top card of their library into their hand." The derived count records <i>how many</i> cards are drawn; the "that many" antecedent is the damage dealt in the triggering event (`DamageDealt`), resolved by the engine (ADR 0004 reference-not-resolution).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/YouDrawThatManyCardsTriggeredRule.cs)

### `YouGainLifePerSpellCastThisTurnRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you gain 1 life for each spell you've cast this turn" — triggered effect on Aetherflux Reservoir. The life amount equals the count of spells you have cast in the current turn (CR 601.2 — casting a spell puts it on the stack; the count includes the spell currently being cast when the trigger resolves). The `CountQuantity` carries an `ObjectFilter` with `CastThisTurnPredicate` to express the backward-looking "cast this turn" restriction.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/YouGainLifePerSpellCastThisTurnRule.cs)

### `YouGainLifeRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you gain N life" — ETB lifegain trigger effect. Rule 701.20: "To gain life, a player adds the indicated amount to their life total."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/YouGainLifeRule.cs)

### `YouGainThatMuchLifeLostRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you gain that much life" where "that much" is the life a player just LOST — the gain-life half of a `LosesLife` trigger (Exquisite Blood, Bloodthirsty Conqueror).

CR 119.3: "If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player's life total is adjusted accordingly." The antecedent of "that much" is the amount of life lost in the triggering event, so the gained amount is a `DerivedQuantity` keyed on `LifeLost`.

The effect surface ("you gain that much life") is identical to the lifelink/ damage family handled by `YouGainThatMuchLifeRule`, which keys the derived amount on `DamageDealt`. The two differ ONLY by the triggering event — the antecedent of "that much" is the trigger's own event. This rule is therefore NOT reflection-discovered into the generic effect loop (it carries no `[TriggeredRule]`); the `TriggeredAbilityParser` dispatches to it directly only when the resolved trigger event is `LosesLife`, so the two "that much" surfaces never collide.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/YouGainThatMuchLifeLostRule.cs)

### `YouGainThatMuchLifeRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you gain that much life" — lifelink-analog triggered effect where the amount is derived from the damage dealt by the source in the triggering event. Rule 120 (Damage): a source deals damage; the amount is "that much" referencing the damage dealt in the triggering event. Rule 701.20: "To gain life, a player adds the indicated amount to their life total." Distinct from `YouGainLifeRule` (literal N life) — this rule models a derived amount ("that much") backed by `DamageDealt`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/YouGainThatMuchLifeRule.cs)

### `YouLoseLifeRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you lose N life" — Deadpool's upkeep tax shape.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/YouLoseLifeRule.cs)

### `YouMayCreateTokenCopyOfItTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you may create a token that's a copy of it" — the optional constellation-style token-copy effect found on cards such as Ondu Spiritdancer.

The pronoun "it" is a back-reference to the object named by the trigger condition — the entering enchantment. MAST models this as `It`, consistent with other "copy it" rules such as `CopyTriggeringSpellTriggeredRule`.

The "you may" prefix means the controller may choose not to copy. MAST wraps the `CopyEffect` in an `OptionalEffect` per ADR 0005 (clause-modifier composition). No `IfYouDo` is present (the copy is the sole consequence).

CR 707.1: "Some effects create a token that's a copy of another object." CR 111.2: "The player who creates a token is its owner." CR 603.2h: "A triggered ability may have an instruction followed by 'Do this only once each turn.'" — the restriction is peeled from the effect text by `TriggeredAbilityParser` before this rule is invoked; this rule handles only the single-sentence body.

ANCHORED (^…$): prevents matching inside a more-specific sibling whose text also contains "create a token that's a copy of it" as a substring. Priority 72 — above the generic `CreateCopyOnCombatDamageTriggeredRule` (70) and well above the vanilla token rule (50); specific enough to be tried before any broader "you may create" path.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/YouMayCreateTokenCopyOfItTriggeredRule.cs)

### `YouMayPayReturnAndAttachEquipmentRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you may pay {COST}. If you do, return that card to the battlefield and attach this Equipment to it." — the conditional-pay reanimation-and-attach pattern.

This is the canonical Nim Deathmantle (SOM) shape: a player may pay a mana cost; if they do, the triggering card (a nontoken creature that just died) is returned to the battlefield AND this Equipment is attached to it. The two consequent actions are a composite one-shot: return + attach are sequential but inseparable per the oracle sentence structure.

AST structure: <c>OptionalEffect { Inner: ConditionalPayEffect { Cost }, IfYouDo: CompositeEffect [ ReturnToBattlefieldEffect { Target: It }, AttachEffect { Target: It } ] }</c> The "that card" pronoun in "return that card to the battlefield" is the creature that triggered the ability — encoded as `It` (CR 113.8b: "it" in an ability refers to the object the trigger is about). "attach this Equipment to it" refers to the same returned creature, also encoded as `It`.

Priority 81 — higher than `ConditionalPayTriggeredRule` (priority 80) so this more-specific rule fires first and the generic handler does not attempt to dispatch the "return and attach" consequence through its limited if-you-do vocabulary.

CR 117.7 ("you may"); CR 701.3 (attach); CR 400.6 (enters under owner's control). CR 113.8b ("it" — the pronoun back-reference).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/YouMayPayReturnAndAttachEquipmentRule.cs)

## `/Parsing/Tokens/`

### `OracleTokenizer` — *sealed class* : Tokenizer<OracleToken>

Tokenizer for Magic: The Gathering oracle text. Transforms character streams into token streams for parsing. Structural keywords are discovered via reflection at startup from classes decorated with `StructuralKeywordAttribute`. To add a new keyword, create a new file under `Parsing/Tokens/Keywords/`; no edits to this file are required.

[Source](Parsing/Tokens/OracleTokenizer.cs)

### `OracleToken` — *enum*

Token types recognized in Magic: The Gathering oracle text. These represent the lexical units that the tokenizer produces.

[Source](Parsing/Tokens/OracleToken.cs)

## `/Parsing/Tokens/Keywords/`

### `StructuralKeywordAttribute` — *sealed class* : Attribute

Marks a class as the registration site for a structural keyword recognized by `OracleTokenizer`. The class body is conventionally empty — the attribute is the entire registration. Each tagged class is discovered at runtime by `StructuralKeywordRegistry`.

[Source](Parsing/Tokens/Keywords/StructuralKeywordAttribute.cs)

### `StructuralKeywordRegistry` — *static class*

Discovers all `StructuralKeywordAttribute`-tagged types in the MagicAST assembly and exposes a case-insensitive text -> token lookup. Discovery runs once on first access and is cached for the process lifetime. Adding a new structural keyword means creating a new file decorated with `StructuralKeywordAttribute` — no edits to this registry or to `OracleTokenizer` are required.

[Source](Parsing/Tokens/Keywords/StructuralKeywordRegistry.cs)

## `/Serialization/`

### `PolymorphicTypeAttribute` — *abstract class* : Attribute

Abstract base for attributes that register a derived concrete type into a polymorphic AST hierarchy. Each polymorphic base has its own concrete subclass (e.g., `OracleAbilityAttribute`, `OracleEffectAttribute`) so that the attribute name signals which hierarchy the type belongs to. Discovered at startup by `PolymorphicReflectionConverter{TBase}`.

[Source](Serialization/PolymorphicTypeAttribute.cs)

### `PolymorphicBaseAttribute` — *sealed class* : Attribute

Marks an abstract record/class as the root of a polymorphic AST hierarchy serialized via `PolymorphicReflectionConverter{TBase}`. The named discriminator property is injected by the converter on write and consumed (then stripped) by the converter on read. The discriminator value itself is supplied per-derived-type by a `PolymorphicTypeAttribute` (or one of its subclasses such as `OracleAbilityAttribute`).

[Source](Serialization/PolymorphicBaseAttribute.cs)

### `PolymorphicConverterFactory` — *sealed class* : JsonConverterFactory

A `JsonConverterFactory` that produces a `PolymorphicReflectionConverter{TBase}` for any type marked with `PolymorphicBaseAttribute`. The set of recognised base types is discovered at startup by scanning the MagicAST assembly. Adding a new polymorphic hierarchy means:

Decorate the abstract base with `PolymorphicBaseAttribute`. Author a per-base `PolymorphicTypeAttribute` subclass (e.g., `OracleAbilityAttribute`). Decorate concrete derived types with that attribute.

No edits to this factory, to `MagicASTJsonOptions`, or to any existing base class are required.

[Source](Serialization/PolymorphicConverterFactory.cs)
